# Changelog — 1.0.1

Archived release section of [docs/CHANGELOG.md](../CHANGELOG.md). The current work and the
release the server is running stay in that file; every earlier release is one file here.

## 1.0.1 — 2026-09-11

### Fixed
- **The attendance screen no longer sends people to a screen that could not help them.** A login with
  no employee behind it is told so by Employee Attendance, and the message named Administration →
  Users as the place to fix it — where nothing could set the link. It now names the ⋮ menu entry that
  actually does it
  ([EmployeeAttendancePage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/EmployeeAttendancePage.js)).
- **Reseeding no longer resets the Super Admin's password to the one in this repository.**
  `prisma/seed.js` carried `password` in the `update` half of its upsert, so every run put that
  account back on the password written at the top of the file. That is not a first-install-only
  step: adding a permission to `seeds/iam-roles-permissions.js` **and reseeding** is how a new
  module or feature becomes grantable at all (Rule 4), so the studio's own documented workflow
  quietly returned its highest-privilege login to a published credential on the VPS, and nothing
  said so. The password is set when the account is created and never again; the username, role,
  status and branch are still asserted on every run, because those really are the seed's to keep
  true. The seed's closing check asserts the stored credential is a bcrypt hash rather than that it
  matches the seed's own password — that comparison is false by design once somebody has chosen
  their own, and a seed that threw at them would have been "fixed" by putting the overwrite back.
  Guarded by [seed-super-admin-password.test.js](../../backend/test/seed-super-admin-password.test.js)
  ([seed.js](../../backend/prisma/seed.js)).
- **The nightly deploy no longer fails at the health check the moment the backend answers it.**
  Every SSH step passed `script_stop: true` to `appleboy/ssh-action`, which does not simply stop on
  failure: it appends an exit-code check after *every* line of the script. In an `if` whose `then`
  sits on the next line — how all five conditions in the health check are written — that check lands
  inside the condition list, so it sees the status of the bare test rather than of the finished `if`
  statement and exits the deploy on a test that merely evaluated false. `[ -z "${RESPONSE}" ]`
  returning 1 *is* the backend answering, so the step died one line after printing the healthy
  response, with no error and no logs — the healthier the backend, the more certain the failure.
  The retry loop was dead for the same reason: a backend not yet listening aborted the step on the
  first attempt instead of polling ten times. `set -euo pipefail`, which every one of these scripts
  already starts with, is what was doing the real work, so the option is gone and a comment at each
  step says why it must not come back ([deploy.yml](../../.github/workflows/deploy.yml)).

  **This is why the app reports `1.0.0`.** The tag job only runs after a green health check, so the
  baseline tag `v1.0.0` was never created and every release since has computed to build 0. The first
  deploy that now passes creates it, and the build number starts counting
  ([Versioning](../project-state/DEPLOYMENT-WORKFLOW.md#versioning)).

### Docs
- **The deploy workflow no longer recommends a required reviewer on the `production` environment.**
  It is scheduled, so an approval gate would hold the 23:00 run until somebody noticed it — which
  [DEPLOYMENT-WORKFLOW.md](../project-state/DEPLOYMENT-WORKFLOW.md) has said since the nightly train
  replaced deploy-on-merge, while the comment in the workflow still advised the opposite. The
  comment now points at the setting that environment actually needs — **Deployment branches and
  tags** restricted to `main`, so that a `workflow_dispatch`, which GitHub offers on every branch,
  cannot ship an unmerged branch to the VPS ([deploy.yml](../../.github/workflows/deploy.yml)).

### Changed
- **Settings → Fields holds its edits until Save, with Cancel to put the list back.** It wrote every
  add, rename and removal the moment it was made, which is right for a list nobody is composing and
  wrong beside Table Settings, where the same group's other screen stages its work behind the same
  two buttons. Adds, renames, removals **and** the questions a few lists carry — which settlement
  means money comes back, which task types ask what the work was — are now held on a copy of the
  list and written together by **Save**; **Cancel** throws the copy away, and neither button is
  offered anything to press while nothing is staged. The section reports to the settings rail like
  every other staged screen, so leaving it, closing the overlay or changing Module, Screen or Field
  asks first instead of discarding the work in silence. The order Save writes in is deliberate:
  removals, then renames, then additions, so a name freed by either can be taken in the same Save,
  and a list's flags are set after a reload — a row added moments ago has no id to set one against
  until then. A refusal the server alone can make — removing a designation forty employees hold —
  stops the rest, and the list reloads onto what actually landed rather than onto what was asked
  for. **Quotation → Shoot Types is untouched** and goes on saving each change as it is made: the
  staging is a flag its caller passes, not a change to the shared editor's behaviour. The pair sits
  at the end of the **Module / Screen / Field** row, which is where Table Settings keeps its own
  Cancel and Save, rather than beside the Add box — the same row, the same weights, the same gap
  ([HrmOptionListEditor.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/HrmOptionListEditor.js),
  [FieldSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/FieldSettingsPanel.js)).
- **Settings → Table now asks module, then screen, then vertical — the same path Fields asks, from
  the same registry.** It asked module, then vertical, over `OS_MODULES`: a list of the
  application's modules kept only in that file, in its own order, naming `Sales Manager`,
  `Booking & Converts` and `Technical`, which are on no menu. It also filed **Leads & Followups** —
  a *screen*, the one the hamburger keeps under Sales & CRM — as a module of its own, so the single
  thing this panel actually edits was the one entry in the wrong column. Module and Screen are now
  read straight from `SIDEBAR_MODULES` in
  [navigation.mjs](../../frontend/src/constants/navigation.mjs), the registry the hamburger, Sidebar
  Layout, Role Management and Settings → Fields already share, so the two screens in the Overall
  group walk one path and a screen added to the menu appears in both pickers with nothing written
  down twice. `OS_MODULES` is gone with it. Every screen is offered and one that keeps no table
  fields says so, rather than being hidden — the picker and the hamburger describe the same
  application. **Nothing moved in the data**: the canvas is keyed by the vertical alone, which is
  what is saved and read back, so every field already built reopens exactly where it was; the
  module and the screen only decide what may be picked. All three pickers ask before they discard
  an unsaved canvas, where it was two before
  ([TableSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/TableSettingsPanel.js)).
- **Settings → Fields no longer asks the three questions behind Payment Schedule Categories and
  Payment Mode.** *Which category closes a schedule?*, *Which can a schedule hold more than one
  of?* and the three Payment Mode questions (*ask for a reference* / *ask which account* / *ask
  what the payment was*) are gone from those two lists, by request. The `settlesBalance`,
  `repeatable`, `asksForTransactionId`, `asksForAccount` and `asksForDetails` flags those questions
  used to set are untouched on the existing rows and still drive the payment schedule builder and
  the cash entry form exactly as before - only the editing UI for them is gone, so changing which
  category closes a schedule or which payment mode asks for what now needs a code change rather
  than a Settings screen ([moduleFieldLists.js](../../frontend/src/constants/moduleFieldLists.js)).
- **Every list in Settings → Fields is named the same way.** The Field picker names a list by its
  plural and the Add box by one of them — "Designations" / *Add Designation* — which twelve of the
  eighteen already did and six did not: Relationship, Referred From, Shoot Type, Tier, Payment
  Schedule Category and Payment Mode were each named in the singular, so the same dropdown read as
  two different kinds of thing depending on where you looked. All six follow the rule now. Only one
  changed by more than an "s": **Referred From** is **Referral Sources**, since "Referred From" has
  no plural — it is the same list, the same rows, and the lead form still asks *Referred From*.
- **Settings → Fields now asks module, then screen, then field, and the first two are the sidebar's
  own.** It asked two questions - module, then one of its lists - over `OS_MODULES`, Table Settings'
  list of the application's modules. That was a *second* answer to "what modules does this
  application have": it named `Sales Manager`, `Booking & Converts` and `Technical`, which are on no
  menu, ordered them differently from the hamburger, and had no way at all to say *which screen* a
  list takes effect on - so Relationship, asked for on both Add Lead and the Quotation Generator, was
  filed under "Quotation" and invisible to anyone looking from the CRM.
  There are three pickers now - **Module → Screen → Field** - and the first two are read straight
  from `SIDEBAR_MODULES` in [navigation.mjs](../../frontend/src/constants/navigation.mjs), the canonical
  registry the hamburger, Sidebar Layout and Role Management already share. **Nothing about them is
  written down twice**: a module or a screen added to the hamburger appears here on its own, in the
  same order and under the same name. Every module and every screen is offered, including the many
  that have no editable answers - those say so rather than being hidden, so this picker and the
  hamburger cannot disagree about what the studio has.
  Each list now names **the screens it takes effect on** rather than a module, and a list used on two
  screens is offered under both: Relationship and Referred From under Leads & Followups *and*
  Quotations, the payment schedule's Category under Bookings & Converted *and* Projects, Payment Mode
  under Accounts Manager *and* Projects. It is one list, one endpoint and one set of rows either way,
  so editing it from either screen is the same edit rather than a copy. A test holds every screen a
  list names to the sidebar registry, so a screen renamed or retired cannot leave a list stranded
  where no picker can reach it ([FieldSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/FieldSettingsPanel.js),
  [moduleFieldLists.js](../../frontend/src/constants/moduleFieldLists.js)).
- **Settings has an "Overall" group: Table, Fields and Project.** The three screens that configure the
  application itself rather than one part of it - Table builds the fields a module's forms carry,
  Fields sets the answers those fields offer, and Project sets the services, deliverables and
  sizes every project is drawn from - sat in the rail as three unrelated neighbours between User
  Preference and Exclude Phone Numbers, each ending in the word "Settings" like everything around
  them. They are one group now, and the names shorten inside it, because "Overall → Table
  Settings" says settings twice.
  
  Project keeps its own three entries - Services, Deliverables, Size Options - which needed the
  shared rail to nest one level further than it did; it now nests as deep as a form asks for, and
  a narrow screen still flattens every group to the screens inside it. Nothing moved in the code:
  each of the three is still written where it was behind its own permission (Table on
  `SALES / CRM Leads`, Fields on `HRM / Employee Management`, Project on `PROJECT / Projects`), and
  the group is assembled from whichever of them the role can actually see - two entries for a role
  that reaches two, and no group at all rather than an empty one for a role that reaches none.

- **Employment Status is a column in the Employee Repository again.** It was taken off the table
  when it was taken off the employee form, and the two do not follow from each other: HR no longer
  types a status, but it is still what every tab of that screen is organised around, and without the
  column a row could not be read, sorted, filtered or exported by it - the Active tab showed who was
  Active and the All tab could not say. Back in its old place, the column after Employee Name, as
  the coloured status dot it always was, and back in Manage Columns with it. The form is unchanged:
  a status is still set by approval, offboarding and send-back rather than typed
  ([EmployeeTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeTable.js)).
- **A CSV no longer has to carry every column of the table it is imported into.** A column the file
  does not have now imports empty, exactly as a column left blank in it does, and the dialog says in
  amber which ones those will be rather than refusing the file. The old rule - the table's *full*
  set or nothing - was stronger than the thing it protects against: adding **Last Followup Date**
  and **Next followup Date** to Dum Dum invalidated every template anybody had already downloaded,
  so a file called `crm-dum-dum-template.csv`, written by this application, was refused by this
  application with no hint that re-downloading was the fix; and a file kept by hand with the fifteen
  columns a studio actually uses could not be imported at all. Two things are still refused: a file
  with no **Enquiry Name** or no **Phone Number**, there being no lead in such a row, and a file
  carrying another table's own columns and none of this table's - a Qua Qua export picked as Dum Dum
  would otherwise put sixteen shared columns of real data into the wrong vertical, which is what the
  check was added for. That refusal now needs the file to carry *none* of the chosen table's columns,
  so one spreadsheet holding several tables' columns is no longer mistaken for somebody else's
  export ([csvImportService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/csvImportService.js),
  [csvTemplateCheck.js](../../frontend/src/lib/csvTemplateCheck.js)).
- **Today Pending Followups is the calls booked for today that nobody has connected on.** It listed
  three things at once - a call owed today, the whole **Yet to connect** queue, and every lead that
  arrived today - so the number beside the name was not what the name said: *Today Pending Followups
  (673)* on Qua Qua, where the follow-ups actually booked for today numbered 89 and most of the rest
  had no follow-up date at all. It is dated like the other two pending tabs, a follow-up owed today,
  and cut by **Yet to connect** - the calls still to be made, a lead already at Followup having been
  spoken to. Both ways that label arrives are counted: a lead with no status reads Yet to connect by
  derivation, and one imported from a sheet or a file whose own Status column said so carries the
  words as stored text - 78 leads on Qua Qua do, 67 of them with a follow-up date, and cutting by
  the empty string alone would have dropped every one of them
  ([leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js)).
- **A lead synced from a Google Sheet reads Yet to connect until somebody gives it a status, however
  old the enquiry.** Yet to connect and Inactive are not stored - a lead with no status reads as the
  first on the day of its Enquiry Date and the second once that day has passed. A sheet is a feed of
  enquiries the studio has *not* worked yet and is routinely linked days or weeks after they came
  in, so that rule put a whole import straight into Inactive: a sheet of last week's Instagram leads
  arrived with 28 of its 32 rows already filed under "nobody needs to ring these". A synced lead now
  stays in the queue until it is answered. A status the sheet itself states still wins - a stored
  status is read before this rule is reached - and nothing else changes: CSV imports and leads typed
  into Add Lead age exactly as they did. Recognised by `spreadsheet_id`, which the sync writes and
  no other writer does, rather than by `source`, which a sheet's own Source column overwrites. The
  Yet to connect **tab** moves with the Status column, the two being one rule asked as a value and
  as a question ([leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

### Added
- **Absences are off the calendar.** It drew a chip per person per day for every day somebody had
  not marked, which in an ordinary month is most days for most people — a wall of red that buried
  the shoots and meetings the calendar exists to show. Who was absent is a register question, and
  the register answers it properly with a reason beside each one. The calendar now draws the days
  people were here; Present, Late, Half day, Leave, Holiday and Weekly off are unchanged, and so is
  everything else on it. Only this one source stops emitting them, so the Attendance Register, the
  Self Dashboard and every count elsewhere read absences exactly as before
  ([schedule/calendar/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/schedule/calendar/service.js)).
- **The common calendar shows the studio's diary again, and nothing else.** Leave, permission,
  tasks and holidays were added to it and are now withdrawn: it draws meetings, shoots, main events
  and attendance, exactly as it did before. The hierarchy scoping, the source references and the
  access change went with them, so the calendar answers to `SALES / CRM Leads` as it always has.
  Two fixes made alongside them are kept, because neither shows anything new — the calendar's
  Present and Absent are still read from real check-ins rather than from how long somebody had the
  application open, and `getAccessibleEmployeeIds` stays in the data-scope resolver for whatever
  next needs to walk the reporting chart.
- **Four to five hours is a half day, and under four earns nothing.** The rule was a single
  threshold — *worked less than four hours and the day is half a day* — which cannot express what a
  studio means by it. It credited somebody who was on the premises for twenty minutes with half a
  day, and it made **four hours exactly a full day**, the boundary landing on the wrong side of the
  one number anybody would check. It is a band now: at least four hours and at most five is a half
  day, below four is too short to credit and reads as **Absent**, and above five is a full day judged
  as before on when the person arrived and left. Both bounds are settings — *Half day from* and
  *Half day up to* on the Rules tab — and a longest shorter than the shortest is refused on both
  sides rather than quietly read as no band at all. A day too short to credit keeps its check-in,
  its selfie and its times on the record; only the status changes. **Three counts of "present" now
  agree**: a day can be both *has a session* and *reads Absent*, so counting logins would have
  reported somebody present on a day the same response called absent — the register, the day-row
  summary and the totals reading all ask one predicate, which also quietly fixed the register card
  omitting Half day and Early exit from its Present count
  ([attendanceRules.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/attendanceRules.js), migration
  `20260912140000_attendance_half_day_band`).
- **The Self Dashboard reads at five scales, and Lifetime finally means lifetime.** It was fixed to
  the current month with no way to change it — the only range picker on the page governed the table
  below it. It now carries its own: **This week, This month, Last 6 months, This Year, Lifetime**.
  Six months was missing entirely, and *Maximum* was quietly clamped to two years back, because the
  summary endpoint built a row per day and refused any range longer than that. Past a quarter the
  dashboard now asks for **figures only** — a year is 365 rows sent so a screen can print nine
  numbers — and the server counts them with arithmetic over the days somebody actually turned up,
  around 250 a year however far back the question goes. With nothing left to walk there is nothing
  left to clamp, so somebody five years in gets five years. The high-level set is working days,
  **attended %**, present, on time, late, half day, early exit, absent and on leave, with hours
  worked against the target the range owed. Attended counts approved leave as attended, because it
  was agreed, and reads *—* rather than 0% for a range that owed nothing. The calendar and the
  day-by-day list stay for a quarter or less and are replaced by a line saying so beyond it, rather
  than by rows nobody scrolls
  ([attendance/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/service.js),
  [EmployeeAttendancePage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/EmployeeAttendancePage.js)).
- **The attendance Self Dashboard is laid out like the Image Selection dashboard.** Two dashboards
  in one application should not be two house styles. It now sits on the same tinted
  `dashboard-canvas` ground, opens with the same one-line caption saying what is being shown, and is
  built from the same card shell — which moved from `components/image-selection/dashboard/` to
  `components/shared/DashboardCard.js`, since a second copy would have drifted from the first the
  moment either was adjusted. Loading now shows the shared skeleton rather than an empty page.
  **A duplication is gone with it:** On time, Late, On leave and Absent were printed twice on that
  one screen — once by the activity panel and again by the totals strip added alongside the
  calendar. The totals strip owns them now and shows eight figures rather than four; the activity
  panel keeps only what nothing else says, which is days marked, hours worked, where they were
  worked and how many were finished properly. The Login Summary keeps its own range picker, because
  it scopes the table and calendar under it and not the month above them
  ([DashboardCard.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/DashboardCard.js),
  [EmployeeAttendancePage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/EmployeeAttendancePage.js)).
- **An attendance location now says where it was, not just what its coordinates were.**
  `11.351332, 77.691160` tells somebody reviewing a day nothing they can act on. The line now reads
  the address — *Perundurai Road, Erode, Tamil Nadu, 638011* — with how far from which office and
  how precise the reading was underneath it, and the coordinates still shown in small type, because
  they are what the distance was actually measured from and a street name a geocoder picked is not a
  substitute. Resolved through **OpenStreetMap Nominatim**: no key, no billing, and rate limited
  in-process to the one request per second its usage policy asks for. Crucially it is **never on the
  critical path** — the lookup runs after the attendance row is safely written, so a geocoder that
  is slow, rate-limited or down costs an address and never a check-in, and fifty people marking a
  day at half past nine do not queue behind each other. The address is stored on the capture and
  never re-read, so a renamed street cannot rewrite history. Existing captures keep their
  coordinates; nothing is backfilled. `GEOCODING_ENABLED="false"` stops coordinates leaving the
  server entirely, and the screens fall back to the office and distance, which need no external call
  ([geocoding.js](../../backend/src/services/geocoding.js), migration
  `20260912120000_attendance_event_address`).
- **HR can change an attendance record directly, and `/today` names the day's state.** Two messages
  in this module already said *"Ask HR to correct the record"* — for an approved request that has
  started, and for a day that already has a checkout — and nothing stood behind either.
  `PATCH /api/hrm/attendance/:sessionId` does now: `HRM / Attendance Approval` only, times as
  `HH:MM` on the day the session already belongs to, a reason required, the checkout held to being
  after the check-in it closes, and an `ADMIN_EDIT` row appended naming who changed what from what.
  The original captures are never touched — an HR edit is precisely the case the append-only event
  table was built to survive. Separately, `GET /today` now returns a named `state` —
  `NOT_CHECKED_IN`, `CHECKED_IN`, `CHECKED_OUT`, `ON_LEAVE`, `HOLIDAY`, `WEEKLY_OFF` — so anything
  consuming the API branches on one field instead of re-deriving it from the session, and two
  derivations of the same thing cannot drift apart. `MISSED_CHECKOUT` is deliberately never today's
  state: an open session this afternoon is somebody still at work, and past ones arrive in
  `needsAttention`. An unmarked working day reads `NOT_CHECKED_IN` rather than absent, because at
  nine in the morning it is not yet an absence
  ([attendanceRules.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/attendanceRules.js),
  [attendance/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/controller.js)).
- **The two attendance migrations are proven, not just written.** Both were hand-written against a
  schema Prisma validates but had never been executed. They were applied to a throwaway MySQL 8.0
  instance along with the repository's entire migration history — all of it succeeded — and
  `prisma migrate diff` then reported **zero drift** on `attendance_office_location`,
  `attendance_event` and `attendance_quote`. The three index names are now declared in
  `schema.prisma` with `map:` so the schema states the names the SQL actually creates, rather than
  Prisma's longer defaults, which would have read as drift on every future check. Twelve behaviours
  were exercised against that live database: the seeded quotes and their stability for a date, a
  branch with no office being unverified rather than refused, a verified capture at the office, a
  measured 1,112 m miss, enforcement refusing only that case, the one-session-per-day constraint
  raising `P2002`, a past open day surfacing as needing attention, an approved correction closing
  the day at 548 worked minutes with the original capture surviving beside it, and the register
  giving a row to somebody who marked nothing.
- **A short day and an early departure now read as what they are, and the month reads as a
  calendar.** `Half day` and `Early exit` join the statuses, both measured against settings the
  studio already sets — minutes under which a day is half of one, and how far before the shift ends
  still counts as leaving on time. A permission covering the *departure* excuses an early exit
  exactly as one covering the arrival excuses lateness, which the module could say in one direction
  only. A day can be short **and** late **and** cut early at once while the status field says one
  thing, so it says the most consequential: half day, then the arrival, then the departure. Both
  rules stay off until the studio configures them, because this function reads a register of days
  already recorded and a rule that switched itself on would restate months of history overnight.
  **Where** a day was worked — Office, Work from home, Field work — is reported beside the status
  and deliberately not folded into it: a day marked as work from home still has to be able to say
  whether it was late, short or full. The employee's own screen gains a totals strip including
  **working days**, the denominator every other figure is read against and one the module could not
  state before, and a **calendar** of the range, one cell per day, drawn from the same rows the
  table below it reads so the two cannot disagree
  ([attendanceRules.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/attendanceRules.js),
  [EmployeeAttendancePage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/EmployeeAttendancePage.js)).
- **HRM → Attendance Register: the whole studio's day on one screen.** A new menu entry under HRM,
  opened by `HRM / Attendance Approval` — which until now granted real powers and no screen of its
  own. It is a **roster**, not a list of sessions: one row per active employee whether or not they
  marked anything, because *who has not checked in today* is the first question anybody opens it to
  ask and a list of what happened can never answer it. Somebody with no session gets the row the
  calendar gives them — holiday, weekly off, approved leave, or an absence nobody explained. Seven
  counts across the top (employees, present, late, absent, on leave, not checked out, outside
  location), counted before the filters so they describe the day rather than the current view.
  Filter by date, branch, status, or a search over name, ID and department. Double-click a marked
  day to see it in full: both captures with their photographs, the coordinates, the accuracy, the
  distance from the office, the device, and every correction ever asked about that day. The
  photographs are read from the append-only event table rather than the day's current row, so a
  corrected day still shows what was originally submitted beside the correction that changed it, and
  each comes through the permission-checked selfie endpoint rather than a link to storage. Every
  status is decided by the same rules the employee's own screen reads, so the two cannot disagree
  ([AttendanceRegisterPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/AttendanceRegisterPage.js),
  [attendance/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/service.js)).
  Reaches every role holding `HRM / Attendance Approval`. No new permission key — the register is
  what that permission now opens — so nothing needs reseeding, but re-save any role that should see
  it if it does not already hold Attendance Approval.
- **A day nobody checked out of now says so, and can be closed by asking.** An open session was
  reported as *Not logged out* forever, whether it was this afternoon or a fortnight ago — the same
  row meaning opposite things. A past open day now reads **Missed checkout**, and the attendance
  screen carries a banner for each one: *Yesterday's attendance needs attention*, with the login
  time and a **Request correction** button. The module never guesses a checkout — the employee
  proposes the time they actually finished, an approver agrees, and only then is it written onto the
  day, alongside a `CHECK_OUT_CORRECTED` audit row so a corrected checkout is never mistaken for one
  somebody stood somewhere and captured. Corrections and outside-location asks ride the approval
  pipeline the other three request types already use — the approver rules, the event log, the
  Approvals queue — so they are two new **types**, not two new tables, and needed no schema change
  at all. A proposed checkout at or before the login is refused on both sides, because
  `workedMinutes` floors at zero and such a day would otherwise read quietly as nought hours
  ([attendanceRules.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/attendanceRules.js),
  [attendance/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/controller.js)).
- **Checking in shows a short acknowledgement, with a line for the day.** Marking attendance is a
  small daily obligation, and a screen that refreshes in silence gives no sign it worked — which is
  how people end up pressing the button twice. The card names the time, the person and, when the
  location could not be confirmed, how far from the branch office they are. Quotes live in
  `attendance_quote`, several per weekday and editable under Rules → Daily quotes; the one shown is
  derived from the date, so the whole studio reads the same line and it does not shuffle on reload.
  Twenty-one are seeded, three per weekday. A weekday with nothing written shows no line rather than
  failing a check-in ([quotes.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/quotes.js), migration
  `20260912100000_attendance_quotes`).
  Both reach every role holding `HRM / Employee Attendance`; editing the quotes needs
  `HRM / Attendance Approval`. No new permission key, so nothing needs reseeding.
- **Attendance now checks where somebody actually was, and keeps a record of it that cannot be
  overwritten.** The module has recorded a selfie, coordinates and an accuracy on every login since
  it shipped, and compared them to nothing — no branch knew where its office was. Three things close
  that. **Office locations**: each branch gets a point and a radius under Rules → Office locations,
  filled by typing coordinates or by pressing *Use this device's location* while standing at the
  door; a branch with no office recorded has no check at all, which is the honest state rather than
  a failing one. **A verdict on every capture**: the distance is measured on a sphere, a position
  too imprecise to place anybody is recorded and left unverified rather than passed, and the reason
  is stored in words beside the reading. **An append-only `attendance_event` row per capture**,
  holding the photograph, the coordinates and the distance as they were at that moment — the session
  row is the day as it currently reads and a correction will overwrite it, and these are what it
  will be audited against. Refusing an outside capture is opt-in (`Refuse attendance from outside
  the radius`, off by default), and never applies to a branch with no office or a phone that could
  not get a fix — those are gaps in the studio's own setup, not the employee's fault. **Checking out
  now takes its own selfie**, because ending a day is as much a claim about where somebody was as
  starting one, and the checkout is guarded against a double tap by a conditional update rather than
  by a read. Half-day minutes and an early-exit grace join the working-day settings
  ([geofence.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/geofence.js),
  [attendance/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/controller.js),
  [EmployeeAttendancePage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/EmployeeAttendancePage.js),
  migration `20260912090000_attendance_geofence_and_events`).
  Reaches every role holding `HRM / Employee Attendance`; recording offices and switching the
  geofence on needs `HRM / Attendance Approval`. No new permission key, so nothing needs reseeding.
- **Attendance selfies are no longer served from the public bucket.** Every other image in this
  application lives at a public R2 URL, which is the wrong home for a photograph of a named person
  at a place and a time. New captures store an object **key** and are served only by
  `GET /api/hrm/attendance/selfie/:eventId`, which admits the employee it is of or an
  `HRM / Attendance Approval` holder and nobody else — not everyone who may open the attendance
  screen, which is the whole studio. Set `R2_PRIVATE_BUCKET_NAME` to a bucket with no public access
  to make this real; left unset it falls back to the main bucket, where the object is guarded only
  by an unguessable key and the server says so once at startup
  ([objectStorage.js](../../backend/src/services/objectStorage.js),
  [imageStorage.js](../../backend/src/services/imageStorage.js)).
- **A login can now be joined to the person it belongs to, from Administration → Users.** `User` and
  `Employee` are separate records held together by `User.linkedEmployeeId`, and that link is what
  Employee Attendance reads to decide whose day is being marked, what Payment Requests matches a
  requester on, and how Tasks and the org chart find the person behind a login. Until now the column
  had exactly one writer — creating credentials from the Employee Repository — so an account made any
  other way, the first Super Admin included, could never be linked to anybody and was refused by all
  of those screens with no way to fix it. The account's ⋮ menu now carries **Link employee** (or
  **Change employee link**), which opens a searchable list of the repository; an employee already
  answering for another login is listed and disabled rather than hidden, so a name that cannot be
  chosen says why. Clearing the link is possible, for a link made to the wrong person. The Users
  table and its detail panel gained an **Employee record** column reading `Name (CODE)`, or
  *Not linked*. The picker is served by `GET /api/admin/linkable-employees` under
  `ADMIN / User Management` rather than from the Employee Repository's own endpoint, so
  administering users does not also require `HRM / Employee Management`
  ([userEmployeeLink.js](../../backend/src/modules/admin/userEmployeeLink.js),
  [admin/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/admin/routes.js),
  [UserManagement.js](../../frontend/src/components/admin/UserManagement.js)).
  Reaches every role holding `ADMIN / User Management` — `READ` to see the column, `READ_WRITE` to
  change the link. No new permission key, so nothing needs reseeding.
- **A sheet may state an enquiry as one timestamp instead of a date column and a time column.**
  `10-Sep-26 10:57 am` in the Enquiry Date cell now fills both the lead's Enquiry Date and its Added
  Time; `2026-09-10T10:57:00`, `10/09/2026 17:49` and `Sep 10, 2026 10:57 am` read the same way, and
  a 24-hour stamp lands on the 12-hour clock the rest of the CRM shows. A sheet that keeps the two
  columns separate is unchanged - its own **Added Time** still wins - and a date with no time on it
  behaves exactly as before. Which shape a sheet uses is read per **row** rather than set on the
  link, so both work side by side and a sheet halfway through changing from one to the other
  imports correctly the whole way. The time is only taken where the cell genuinely is a date, so a remark
  left in the wrong column ("call back at 10:57") is not read as one. This also settles a
  disagreement inside the date module: MySQL's `STR_TO_DATE` ignores whatever trails its format, so
  the SQL half had been reading `10-Sep-26 10:57 am` as the 10th all along while the JavaScript half
  refused it outright ([leadDates.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadDates.js),
  [googleSheetsService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/googleSheetsService.js)).

### Fixed
- **A check-in recorded the day and then answered 500.** `fillEventAddress`, the deferred address
  lookup added with the geocoding work, called `reverseGeocode` without importing it. The reference
  is inside a function body, so the module loaded, every unit test passed and nothing complained —
  but at runtime it threw a `ReferenceError` **synchronously**, into the try block of the check-in
  handler that had deliberately not awaited it, *after* the attendance row was written. Somebody
  marking their day had it recorded and was told the server had failed. The import is there, and a
  test now asserts that the fire-and-forget helper cannot throw into its caller and that nothing the
  service exports is undefined
  ([attendance/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/service.js)).
- **The WhatsApp notification bell no longer errors on a fresh server, and the "must link
  WhatsApp" rule now actually loads.** Two bugs, both from the same account field:
  `whatsappRequired` was read and written by the WhatsApp chat and employee-credentials
  controllers but had never been added to the Prisma schema, so `whatsappStanding()` failed with
  `Unknown field whatsappRequired` on every poll of the chat status - the client-required gate
  could never take effect. Separately, the notification bell's WhatsApp-replies source queried
  `SalesCrmWhatsAppMessage` directly instead of going through the module's own `ensureTable()`,
  so a server where nobody had yet opened the WhatsApp chat panel (that table is created at
  runtime, not by a migration) failed the whole notifications poll with a "table doesn't exist"
  error. `whatsappRequired` is now declared on `User` in
  [schema.prisma](../../backend/prisma/schema.prisma) (mapping to the `whatsapp_required` column the
  migration already added) and the Prisma Client regenerated, and
  [sources.js](../../backend/src/modules/notifications/sources.js) ensures the table before reading
  it, the same way every other reader of it already does.
- **A sheet's Added Time column no longer rejects every row it appears on.** With the link fixed,
  the first sync of a Dum Dum Instagram sheet reported *"0 created, 0 updated, 32 skipped"* - all 32
  invalid, each on the single issue `addedTime: Invalid date value.` **Added Time** is a clock time,
  "10:57 am", and not a date: `added_time` is free text, Add Lead writes it with
  `toLocaleTimeString`, and the CRM board filters and renders it as a time. The sheet importer's
  validator was the last place still listing it among its date columns, so `new Date("10:57 am")`
  came back NaN and marked the row invalid - a whole sheet turned away over the one column that was
  right. It is validated as what it is; the real date columns are unchanged
  ([googleSheetsService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/googleSheetsService.js)).
- **A sheet's dates are folded to `YYYY-MM-DD` on the way in, so an imported lead is filed under
  the day it actually came in.** `created_day`, `follow_up_day` and `dob_edod_day` are stored
  generated columns that understand ISO and `DD/MM/YYYY` and nothing else, and `flattenLeadColumns`
  exists so that nothing ever writes a shape they cannot read - its own note already listed the
  Google Sheet sync among the writers going through it. It did not: the sheet upsert builds its own
  payload and writes raw SQL, and was the one path that never reached it. So a sheet carrying
  Excel's `10-Sep-26` stored that verbatim, leaving `follow_up_day` and `dob_edod_day` NULL - in no
  Today or Yesterday Pending tab, in neither Newborns nor Birthdays - while `created_day` fell
  through to the row's insert timestamp and filed every row of the import under the day the sync
  ran. Yesterday's enquiries now arrive dated yesterday
  ([googleSheetsService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/googleSheetsService.js)).
- **A sync that skips rows now says why it skipped them.** The result read "32 skipped" and stopped
  there, so a sheet every row of which had been rejected was indistinguishable from one that was
  already imported and had nothing to do. The count is broken down into invalid and duplicate - "32
  skipped (32 invalid)" - in the toast and on the link card, from numbers the sync had been counting
  and discarding all along
  ([SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js)).
- **Linking a Google Sheet imports its rows again, instead of saving a link that imports nothing.**
  Every sheet linked through Settings → Integrations answered *"Sheet linked, but the import failed:
  leadIds.next is not a function"*, and the link stayed - stored, active, and silently importing
  nothing on every 30-second sync after it. The row mapper's fifth argument became an enquiry-id
  allocator when ids moved to the shared counter, and `prepareGoogleSheetCrmImport` was the one
  caller not carried over: it still handed in the old `Set` of ids already taken, which has no
  `.next()`. It now builds the allocator the recurring sync already built, seeded from the counter
  and from the ids on existing leads, so a linked sheet numbers its rows exactly as the CSV import
  and the Add Lead form do. The counter is lifted only once rows have actually been written - the
  same function backs the dry-run preview, and committing there would spend a block of ids on a
  sheet nobody went on to import
  ([googleSheetsService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/googleSheetsService.js),
  [crm-sheet-link-lead-ids.test.js](../../backend/test/crm-sheet-link-lead-ids.test.js)).
- **Settings now asks before it throws away work, whichever way you leave a screen.** The rail
  already asked - leave a section holding an unsaved edit and a dialog said so - and it was the only
  thing that did, which made the question look answered while three other ways out of the same
  screen still discarded the work in silence. Ten fields built in Table Settings and one click on
  the **Vertical** dropdown directly above them: gone, one click away from the dialog that would
  have caught the same move made through the rail. A brand's PDF wording in Quotation Settings and
  one click on the **Terms** tab beside it: gone - and leaving that brand by the rail lost it too,
  because neither Quotation screen had ever told the rail it was holding anything. A half-built
  table and an **F5**: gone, with nothing to stop it. All four ask now, in the rail's own words
  (*Go Back* / *Discard & Continue*), and a refresh raises the browser's own warning while Settings
  is holding a staged edit. Nothing changes for the screens that save as you type - Field Settings,
  the option lists, a WhatsApp number, a quotation term - which stage nothing and so are not
  interrupted ([useUnsavedGuard.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/useUnsavedGuard.js),
  [TableSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/TableSettingsPanel.js),
  [QuotationSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/QuotationSettingsPanel.js),
  [SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js)). No permission
  changes: this is a confirmation in front of edits the reader was already allowed to make, and the
  routes behind every Save are guarded exactly as before.

### Added
- **Picking a branch on an Our Infrastructure collage fills in its State.** Settings → Quotation
  Settings → General → Our Infrastructure reads the State from the branch's own record in
  Organization → Branches the moment it is picked, the same field that screen's PIN-code lookup
  already fills in - so it does not have to be typed, and cannot end up naming the wrong state for
  the branch by mistake. The field stays a plain, editable one afterward: a branch Organization →
  Branches has not recorded a state for yet, or wording the printed page should use instead, can
  still be typed or changed by hand
  ([quotation-brands/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/quotation-brands/service.js),
  [QuotationSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/QuotationSettingsPanel.js)).
- **The Branch field under Settings → Quotation Settings → General → Our Infrastructure is now
  Organization → Branches, not a typed name.** Each collage's Branch picker offers only the
  studio's active branches, and only the ones no other collage has already claimed - so the same
  branch cannot be added twice - while a collage keeps whatever branch it already holds even if
  that branch is since inactive or shared with another row. State stays free text. The list of
  active branches now rides on `GET /admin/general`'s existing response
  ([quotation-brands/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/quotation-brands/service.js),
  [QuotationSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/QuotationSettingsPanel.js)) - no
  new route and no permission change, since that endpoint already answers for
  `ADMIN / System Settings`.
- **Ten more dropdowns are lists the studio maintains, across Assets, Client Storage, Vendors,
  Accounts and Tasks.** Asset Conditions, Repair Severities and Asset Shipment Types under
  **Assets**; Storage Conditions and Storage Shipment Types under **Client Storage**; Vendor Types
  under **Vendors**; Bank Account Types under **Accounts Manager**; Settlements under **Payment
  Requests**; Offer Types under **Leads & Followups** and **Quotations**; Task Types under
  **Tasks**. Every one was an array written beside the field that read it, so a studio that started
  hiring a new trade, or judging a disc by a word the list did not have, could not say so.
  
  Four of them were written out *twice* — Conditions and Shipment Types once in the browser and
  again in the server that validates them, Bank Account Type once in the picker and again as the
  `z.enum` that saves it — so the form could offer a word the server refused. Both sides read the
  one list now.
  
  Two carry their meaning on the row rather than in their spelling, the way a payment schedule’s
  closing category does: **Task Types** asks *which types ask what the work was* (that was the
  literal `"Other"`, compared on both sides), and **Settlements** asks *which settlement means
  money comes back* (that was `"Expense Excess"`, which decides whether approving a request records
  a receipt or a payment). Rename either now and the behaviour follows.
  
  Assets and Client Storage get a list each of Conditions and Shipment Types rather than sharing
  one: the words match today, but a disc on its way to a client is not judged the way a camera on
  the shelf is.
  
  **Workflow states were deliberately left in the code** — task and request statuses, asset
  statuses, the client-storage transitions, entry type Received/Spent, priorities. The application
  branches on those words: renaming COMPLETED would strand every completed task, and a state
  machine’s states are not vocabulary. Every list is seeded with exactly what its array held, and
  every read falls back to it, so an unseeded or unreachable table behaves as the app did before.
- **Payment Mode, Payment Schedule Category and Tier are lists the studio maintains, each under
  the module that owns it.** Settings → Field Settings → **Booking & Converts → Payment Schedule
  Category**, **Accounts → Payment Mode** and **Sales Manager → Tier**. All three were arrays in
  the code, and the Payment Mode one was written out *twice* - once for the cash entry form and
  again in the controller that validates the entry - so a studio that started taking payments a
  new way had no way to say so, and adding it by hand in one place would have got a mode the
  form offered and the server refused.
  
  What made them uneditable was that each carried its meaning in its *spelling*. "Final
  Settlement" was compared against as a literal string in three places - it is the row pinned to
  the bottom of a payment schedule that takes no date and that nothing may follow - and "Partial
  Payment" and "Extras Services" were matched by a regular expression that numbered them as they
  were added (*Partial Payment 1*, *Partial Payment 2*). Renaming any of the three stopped all of
  that working. Which payment mode asks for a transaction reference, which asks which account the
  money moved through, and which asks what it actually was were three more arrays of names.
  
  The meaning is on the row now, and Field Settings asks for it as a question about the list rather
  than as a switch on each entry: *Which category closes a schedule?* takes one answer, because only
  one ever may; *Which can a schedule hold more than one of?* and the three Payment Mode questions
  take a set. The list underneath is names and nothing else, like every other list on that screen.
  So a studio can call the closing row whatever it likes and the schedule still closes on it, and a mode it adds asks
  for exactly what it should on the form **and** is accepted by the server, which reads the same
  list. Every list is seeded with precisely what the arrays held, so nothing already recorded
  stops matching, and every read falls back to those same values if it fails - answering "no
  payment mode is valid" would refuse every cash entry the studio tried to record. The one thing
  refused is removing the last category that closes a schedule, which would leave a schedule that
  never closes; rename it instead. Reading these lists needs only a session, as the branch and
  department lists do; editing them needs **HRM / Employee Management** `READ_WRITE`, which is
  what the rest of Field Settings already asks for, so no role gains anything it did not have.
- **Relationship and Referred From are lists the studio maintains, under Settings → Field Settings
  → Quotation.** Both are asked for wherever a lead's details are taken down - CRM → Add Lead, the
  lead popup and the Quotation Generator's Client Details - and both were written out twice, once in
  `CRMPage.js` and once in `quotationVerticalConfig.js`, so the same field on two screens could
  suggest different things and neither could be added to: a studio running ads on a new platform
  had nowhere to put it. Two tables now, seeded with exactly what the arrays held, and **both screens
  read the one list**, so a source added in Field Settings is suggested on the lead and on the
  quotation alike. The fields stay typed-with-suggestions - the list is offered, and an answer not on
  it can still be typed, which is what keeps every lead filed before the list existed valid.
  Deleting an entry is refused while a lead on the board still holds it, and renaming carries those
  leads with it, the way the other lists on that screen behave; a lead in the trash no longer counts,
  or a source used by nothing but deleted leads could never be removed. A quotation's own copy lives
  in its payload and is left as raised, like everything else on a version already sent
  ([useLeadSuggestionLists.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/useLeadSuggestionLists.js),
  [definitions.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/master-data/definitions.js),
  [moduleFieldLists.js](../../frontend/src/constants/moduleFieldLists.js)).
- **Blood Group and Languages Known are maintained lists now, not arrays compiled into the
  browser.** They were the last two employee dropdowns Settings → Field Settings could not touch:
  the eight blood groups and the seventeen languages lived in `frontend/src/constants/employeeOptions.js`
  and nowhere else, so a studio could not add a blood group its people actually answer, or a language
  its crew speaks. Both join the ten lists already under **Field Settings → Human Resource
  Management**, with the same Add box, search, rename and delete as the rest.
  **They were behind in different ways.** Blood group had no table at all, so `blood_group` is
  created and seeded with exactly the eight the browser held. Languages *had* a table — `language`
  has had one, with a master-data definition and routes, since the HRM foundation — and not one row
  had ever been written to it, so the form went on reading the array regardless of how well the table
  behind it was maintained; only the seed was missing.
  **Deleting an entry still in use is refused and renaming still carries the records**, the way every
  other list on that screen behaves — including for languages, which are several to a record and
  comma separated, so `"Tamil"` is matched entry by entry rather than found inside a longer word.
  `employee.blood_group` was widened from `VARCHAR(10)` to `VARCHAR(50)`: ten characters was sized
  for `AB+` when the values were fixed, and an entry like `AB Positive` would have been cut short —
  the public onboarding submission was capped at ten to match and is now fifty. The public apply page
  is carried both lists with its invite, as it already is for gender, work type, marital status and
  the emergency contact relationship, because a candidate has no session to read the master data
  with. Every read falls back to exactly what the browser used to hold, so a database that has not
  run migration `20260910160000_blood_group_and_language_lists` offers what it always did
  ([definitions.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/master-data/definitions.js),
  [employeeOptionLists.js](../../backend/src/services/employeeOptionLists.js),
  [moduleFieldLists.js](../../frontend/src/constants/moduleFieldLists.js),
  [employeeOptions.js](../../frontend/src/constants/employeeOptions.js)).
- **Settings → Field Settings → Quotation → Shoot Type: the shoots each brand quotes for, with a tab
  per brand.** They were the constant `BRAND_SHOOTS` on the server - fourteen for Dum Dum, thirteen
  for Qua Qua, two for Corp - so a studio that started doing a new kind of shoot under a brand had no
  way to say so short of a code change. Each brand's list is now its own to name, add to and edit.
  **One list, three readers**, which is the point of storing it once: this screen edits it, the
  picture sets in **Quotation Settings → *brand* → PDF Content** are chosen from it, and the
  **Quotation Generator's Add Shoot** now offers a quotation only the shoots its brand quotes for -
  raising one under Dum Dum no longer offers a Naming ceremony. Project Management is untouched and
  still offers the studio's whole vocabulary, and a brand whose shoots have not been set up falls
  back to that same vocabulary rather than leaving somebody unable to add a shoot at all.
  **Worth knowing:** the brand lists used to be a subset of the studio's 34 shoots, held there by a
  test, so a quotation could never name a shoot a project could not be given. Naming them per brand
  ends that link. In practice a project takes its shoots from the quotation that sold it, so the two
  still agree; a project raised with no quotation behind it offers the studio's own list.
  The editor is the one every other list on this screen uses - Add, rename, remove, a search, each
  saving as it is made - which now takes its data source as a prop so a list living somewhere other
  than the HRM master data can borrow it rather than growing a second screen beside it. Editing the
  shoots answers to `ADMIN / System Settings`, the permission that owns Quotation Settings, because
  changing which shoots a brand quotes for changes what every quotation raised under it may contain.
  Migration `20260910140000_quotation_brand_shoots` creates the table and seeds each brand with
  exactly what the constant held, so no picture set loses the shoots it was built from
  ([QuotationShootTypeEditor.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/QuotationShootTypeEditor.js),
  [quotation-brands/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/quotation-brands/service.js),
  [AddShootDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/AddShootDialog.js)).

### Fixed
- **A value on one of the studio's lists can no longer be deleted out from under the records using
  it.** Field Settings deletes permanently and checked nothing first, so removing "Accounts" from
  Departments left every employee filed under it naming a department no list offered. Nothing failed
  and nothing was reported - these lists are held on the employee as plain **text** rather than as a
  link to the row, which is exactly what made the loss quiet: the record still read correctly, and
  the studio simply could not choose that department again or put it back. Deleting is refused while
  anything still holds the value, and the refusal names it, says what is using it and what to do
  instead - `"Accounts" is used by 4 employees, so this department cannot be deleted.` An archived
  employee counts, and is called out separately, because an entry nobody currently works under still
  cannot go while a former employee's record shows it - and that reads as a bug unless it is said.
  Applies to every list Field Settings offers; a test fails if a list reaches that screen without
  declaring where its values are used.
- **Renaming an entry now carries the records with it.** The same fault wearing different clothes:
  the row changed and every record stayed on the old word, the list and the records disagreeing with
  nothing to say so. A rename updates every record holding the old value in the same transaction -
  both move or neither does - and the screen reports how many, because rewriting that word on
  somebody's employee record is not a thing to do silently. Renaming is carried through rather than
  refused so that a typo in an entry already in use can still be corrected.
  The editor's own comment claimed the records "hold its id, not its text", which is the opposite of
  the truth and is why the rename was written as though it looked after itself
  ([master-data/usage.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/master-data/usage.js),
  [master-data/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/master-data/controller.js),
  [HrmOptionListEditor.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/HrmOptionListEditor.js)).

### Added
- **An invite that has gone out can be corrected, and its link survives.** A mistyped address or a
  wrong digit in the phone number used to leave only Cancel and re-invite — which issues a *new*
  link and kills the one already sent. That is right when the link went to the wrong person; it is
  heavy-handed when nobody ever received anything. **Edit Invite** sits in the repository row's ⋮ for
  an invite row, and beside Cancel in the invite history, and edits the invite in place: same token,
  same short code, same URL, so the mail can simply go again afterwards through Send Invite. It is
  offered on invite rows only — an employee whose submission was sent back has a record of their own
  and their invite's details are what they filled the form in against. **Submitted** and
  **Cancelled** invites are refused for the same reasons, in the endpoint as well as the menu; an
  **Expired** one is editable, its expiry being one of the fields, which is how a lapsed invite is
  brought back without a new link. Changing the email or phone on a **Verified** invite returns it to
  **Created**: the candidate proved they hold a value the invite no longer carries, so the corrected
  contact has to be proven in its turn
  ([onboarding-invites/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/onboarding-invites/controller.js),
  [OnboardingInviteDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/OnboardingInviteDialog.js),
  [EmployeeTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeTable.js)).
- **The Employee Repository shows the login username and login email.** Two columns beside Email
  ID, filled from the account linked to the employee and empty where there is none, so the address
  somebody signs in with can be read off the register rather than only from the credentials dialog
  at the moment it was created. Identifiers only — the password is not on the row and never has
  been ([employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js),
  [EmployeeTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeTable.js)).
- **Pairing WhatsApp is no longer rate limited, and administrators are shown nothing about WhatsApp.**
  `POST /session/connect` counted five attempts in five minutes, and the limit was met by exactly the
  people it was not aimed at — somebody *trying* to link, held out of the whole application by the
  gate while the server refused them a code. There is no counter on that route now; the gate paces it
  instead, asking once per visit, never while a session is already producing codes, and never twice
  inside five seconds (down from twenty), with the button counting the wait down rather than refusing
  silently. **Anyone adding a caller to that route must bring its own pacing** — each request is a
  real registration against the studio's WhatsApp number, and there is no server-side guard against a
  loop any more. Separately, a Super Admin or any role with the Administrator tick now sees no
  WhatsApp dialog at all — neither the gate nor the five-minute reminder — decided by one rule both
  read, so neither can be silenced without the other
  ([security.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/whatsapp-chat/security.js),
  [whatsappReminder.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/whatsappReminder.mjs)).
- **Everyone except an administrator must link WhatsApp before using the application.** A CRM working
  a morning of leads with the one channel the client answers on disconnected is the fault this stops,
  and a reminder every five minutes is a thing people learn to dismiss unread — so it is a condition
  rather than a notice. Signing in without WhatsApp linked now meets a pairing dialog that cannot be
  dismissed (no ×, no Escape, no backdrop), holding a live QR until the scan succeeds, at which point
  it closes itself. Three exceptions keep it safe: **an administrator is never held** — a Super Admin
  or any role with the Administrator tick — so somebody can always get in and put things right; a
  named person is released by ticking **Allow without WhatsApp** in Login Credentials → WhatsApp, for
  a lost or broken phone; and the studio-wide WhatsApp switch lifts it for everyone at once. The
  answer is read from the session endpoint on every poll rather than carried on the login, so ticking
  or lifting it takes effect within seconds — the person it releases does not have to sign in again,
  which matters because somebody the gate is holding cannot sign out to collect a new token
  ([WhatsappRequiredGate.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/WhatsappRequiredGate.js),
  [AppShell.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppShell.js),
  [whatsapp-chat/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/whatsapp-chat/controller.js),
  [20260910120000_user_whatsapp_exempt](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260910120000_user_whatsapp_exempt/migration.sql)).

- **Settings → Field Settings → Human Resource Management now maintains ten lists, not four.** It
  could edit designations, departments, verticals and skills; gender, marital status, work type,
  emergency contact relationship, the work locations and the employee statuses were arrays compiled
  into the browser, so a studio could not add a work location, retire a status or record a gender
  its people actually answer. All six join the four, and **Work Base Location and Current Work
  Location share one list**, which is how the employee form has always offered them.
  **The meaning moved with the words, which is the point of the change.** Three of these lists are
  not just labels: which statuses mean somebody is working here decides who may be handed an asset,
  who the CRM offers as a salesperson and who may link a WhatsApp session; which marital statuses
  ask for a spouse decides what both forms collect and what the public endpoint requires; and the
  genders are what that endpoint validates against. Those answers were written into the code -
  `["Active", "in Training", "PIP"]` in **eight** separate files, the literal `"Married"` in four
  more, and a `z.enum` of three genders - so the lists were editable in name only and a rename broke
  behaviour somewhere nobody was looking. Each row carries its own meaning now
  (`counts_as_working`, `requires_spouse_details`) and every one of those places asks the list.
  Adding a second status that also has a spouse, or a gender the form should offer, now works end to
  end rather than being accepted by the form and refused by the server.
  **The candidate's page follows too.** The apply page has no session and cannot read HR's master
  data, so the invite carries the lists with it - without that, HR would edit a list the candidate is
  never offered, which is the drift `employeeOptions.js` exists to prevent.
  Every read falls back to exactly what the code used to say, so a database with these tables empty
  behaves as it did before. Migration `20260910120000_employee_option_lists` creates four tables and
  two columns and seeds each list with the values the browser held, so nothing already answered stops
  matching ([employeeOptionLists.js](../../backend/src/services/employeeOptionLists.js),
  [master-data/definitions.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/master-data/definitions.js),
  [moduleFieldLists.js](../../frontend/src/constants/moduleFieldLists.js),
  [employeeOptions.js](../../frontend/src/constants/employeeOptions.js)).
- **Neither Add Lead nor Import Leads asks for an Enquiry Date.** A lead written down as it comes in
  is that day's enquiry, so both stamp today rather than asking — Add Lead each time the dialog opens,
  which also stops a board left running overnight filing this morning's lead under yesterday, and
  Import Leads with the day the batch is being typed. A lead that needs a different date is backdated
  from the lead popup after it is saved. The CSV import and the Google Sheet sync still read a
  Created Date / Enquiry Date column from their source, because a file can carry dates that are not
  today ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [crmBulkImport.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/crmBulkImport.mjs)).
- **Leads & Followups deletes a whole selection, and deleting a lead is now an administrator's
  permission.** Ticking rows on any of the three verticals offers a red **Delete** beside Update
  Details, and the header tick reaches every lead the board, tab, filters and date range match —
  including the pages never scrolled into view — so clearing a vertical is one confirmation rather
  than one lead at a time. It is behind a new permission, `SALES / CRM Lead Deletion`, because
  running the board and destroying its records are different acts and `SALES / CRM Leads` Read /
  Write used to hand out both. A role without it sees no Delete anywhere on the screen — not the
  button, not the row menu, not the right-click menu, which had been offering it even to a read-only
  reader — and both `DELETE /api/sales/crm-leads/bulk` and `DELETE /api/sales/crm-leads/:id` refuse
  it. **Reseed and re-save the roles**: the seed writes no grants, so until somebody grants it in
  Role Management only a Super Admin can delete a lead
  ([routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/routes.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [permission-catalogue.js](../../backend/prisma/seeds/permission-catalogue.js)).
- **`npm run report:employee-match`, to read that change before deploying it.** It lists every handset
  in the call log against the employee it resolves to, and every handset that resolves to nobody with
  the calls that leave the reports with it. The one way the change goes wrong is a real employee whose
  handset is labelled differently *and* whose contact number the repository does not hold — they match
  on neither — so the script prints the number it looked for; filling that in on their employee record
  is the fix. Reads only ([reportEmployeeMatch.js](../../backend/scripts/reportEmployeeMatch.js)).
- **Leads & Followups tells a CRM when their WhatsApp is not linked, and keeps telling them every
  five minutes until it is.** The WhatsApp session belongs to the person rather than to the studio,
  and a lead's WhatsApp Chat tab does nothing until it is linked - so somebody could work a whole
  morning of leads with the one channel the client is answering on disconnected, and nothing on the
  board said so. The reminder points at the WhatsApp Chat tab of any lead rather than offering to
  pair on the board itself: the pairing screen belongs beside the conversation it is for, and a
  second one would be two to keep in step. **Skip is remembered** for that person in that browser,
  so reloading the board, or leaving it and coming back, does not restart the five minutes - they
  are counted from the skip itself. It waits behind whatever the board has open, a lead popup or
  Add Lead or an import, rather than landing on top of a half-typed follow-up note, and appears
  once that is closed. Linking WhatsApp forgets the skip as well as stopping the reminder, so a
  device unlinked from the phone later is spoken about promptly rather than sitting under a skip
  made that morning. Access is unchanged: the board and the session it reads are both already behind
  `SALES / CRM Leads`
  ([WhatsappLinkReminder.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/WhatsappLinkReminder.js),
  [whatsappReminder.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/whatsappReminder.mjs),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

- **`scripts/backfillClientStorage.js` raises the client storage records for projects that started
  before the feature existed.** A `client_storage` record is only ever created by saving a project —
  `ensureProjectClientStorage` runs from Start Project and from adding a shoot, and from nowhere
  else — so every project already under way when the feature shipped has no record and never gets
  one. Client Storage Management then reads as an empty table on a studio with plenty of projects.
  The script writes the missing records through that same function, so a backfilled record is
  indistinguishable from one raised by a save: the same `DBS-STOMGMT-` numbering and the same
  earliest-shoot attachment. It reports without writing unless given `--apply`, is safe to run
  twice, and never revives a record an employee removed on purpose. The dry run also breaks down why
  a project is not eligible, since an empty table has two causes and only one of them is this — a
  project still "Yet to Start", or answering Storage Needed = No, is correctly absent.
- **Dum Dum's table now carries Last Followup Date and Next followup Date.** Both sit beside Status,
  which is what they qualify. Qua Qua has had them since it was built and Dum Dum had neither, so on
  the board the three pending tabs are read from, the date those tabs sort and filter by could not be
  seen on the row itself. They are the same two columns Qua Qua has, spelled identically, so the date
  filters, the sort and Manage Columns work on them with no further wiring. Next Follow-up Date was
  already editable in the lead panel on every vertical and is unchanged; Last Followup Date remains
  read-only there, because the activity log is what records when a lead was last worked.
  **Two knock-on changes worth knowing:** the Dum Dum CSV template now writes these two columns —
  the template is that same column list — so a Dum Dum file exported before this change is missing
  them and is refused on import until re-exported; and the Google Sheets sync now reads
  `Last Followup Date` / `Next followup Date` headings on a Dum Dum sheet, which it previously
  ignored on every vertical but Qua Qua. Access is unchanged: `SALES:Leads & Followups`.

- **A quotation now opens on the photograph of what it is actually for.** Settings → Quotation
  Settings → *brand* → PDF Content no longer holds one cover and one Thank You photograph for the
  whole brand. It holds **picture sets**: a searchable multi-select of the shoots a pair belongs to,
  that pair of photographs, and an **Add set** button for the next group. Each brand offers only the
  shoots it quotes for - Dum Dum 14, Qua Qua 13, Corp 2 - and every set's dropdown offers only what
  the other sets have not already taken, so a shoot can never belong to two sets. A quotation prints
  the set its **main event** falls in; if the main event is in no set another of its shoots answers,
  and if nothing matches the **first set** prints, which is what makes it the fallback. A brand
  configured before this reads back as exactly that first set, so nothing already uploaded is lost
  and no quotation changes. The pair is resolved once, on the server, and frozen into the version's
  snapshot, so re-pointing a set later never alters a quotation already sent
  ([QuotationSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/QuotationSettingsPanel.js),
  [defaults.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/quotation-brands/defaults.js),
  [service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/quotation-brands/service.js)).
- **A quotation closes on a link to its terms, and the clauses are read on a page of their own.**
  The client's copy printed the Terms & Conditions as small print between the closing lines and the
  infrastructure; the studio's design closes on the link alone. It now does, and following it opens
  `/q/<token>/terms` - the same sheet, the same ground and background, the same mark and name - with
  the clauses set to be read and a link back. They are the version's own, from its snapshot, so a
  client opening a quotation from months ago reads the terms it was sent with. The PDF prints the
  link as a real link, absolute, so a client reading the file can follow it too
  ([PublicQuotationTermsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicQuotationTermsPage.js),
  [PublicQuotationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicQuotationPage.js)).
- **Each shared section of a quotation now has a switch.** Settings → Quotation Settings → General
  carries an on/off switch beside **Additional Notes**, **Officially Trademarked Brand**, **Journey
  & Legacy** and **Our Infrastructure**. Off, that whole card is left off the client's copy while
  everything typed under it is kept, so a section can be prepared or paused without losing its
  wording or pictures. Every version freezes the switches it was raised under, so a quotation
  already sent keeps the sections it was sent with, and a switch nobody has touched prints as it
  always did. Migration `20260909210000_quotation_section_switches` adds one nullable JSON column
  and drops nothing
  ([QuotationSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/QuotationSettingsPanel.js),
  [PublicQuotationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicQuotationPage.js)).
- **A brand's Terms can now be edited, added to and removed, standard clauses included.** A brand
  with no terms of its own printed the studio's three standard clauses and offered only **Add
  term** - the three are the template's, so there was nothing stored to change. **Edit** and
  **Delete** now sit on each of them, and **Edit the standard terms** beside Add term: pressing any
  of those copies all three onto the brand in the order they print, after which each is edited,
  hidden, reordered by drag or deleted like any term somebody typed. Quotations already sent keep
  the terms they were sent with
  ([QuotationSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/QuotationSettingsPanel.js)).
- **Quotation Settings → General holds what every brand's quotation shares.** The Additional Notes
  card (its heading, and a standard note that prints when a quotation carries none), the Officially
  Trademarked Brand card, Journey & Legacy (headings, intro, the India map and the timeline) and
  Our Infrastructure (headings, tagline and the collage per branch) read the same on every brand,
  so they are now kept once - on the studio's quotation settings row, migration
  `20260909200000_quotation_general_pdf_content`, two nullable JSON columns, nothing dropped - and
  edited on the General screen. Each brand's PDF Content tab keeps only the brand's own pictures
  (cover, Thank You) and words; a brand can no longer hold the shared ones, and
  whatever a brand row still stored for them is ignored. Every version snapshots the combined
  answer as it stood when it was raised
  ([QuotationSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/QuotationSettingsPanel.js),
  [quotation-brands/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/quotation-brands/service.js)).
- **The client's copy of a quotation is now the studio's reference proposal design, and every
  picture and word on it is configured per brand.** `/q/<token>` and the PDF printed from it - now
  one continuous 1080px sheet rather than A4 pages, as the reference is - open on a cover
  photograph and a quote, then the brand's mark and name, a navy proposal card with the client's
  name and the quotation's details, one card per event with its date, place, duration, shoot type,
  who takes care of food, accommodation and travel, and its services and deliverables with
  quantities, the storage note, the navy pricing card with the GST and validity lines, Additional
  Notes, the trademark card, Journey & Legacy over the India map with the active branches as
