  location chips, the closing lines and terms, Our Infrastructure as a collage per branch, and the
  Thank You photograph with the footer paragraph, branch list, phone, website, social handle and
  copyright. **Nothing on it is written into the code.** The name is the Global Name (Organization →
  Branding Logo, the bundled name when blank); the mark is the Global Logo (the bundled mark when none is set);
  the website and phone come from the quotation's branch (Organization → Branches, the head office
  standing in); the locations are every active branch; and Settings → Quotation Settings → **PDF
  Content** now holds, per brand, the cover, Journey & Legacy background
  (the India map in the reference) and Thank You pictures, the infrastructure collages (branch, state, up to twelve photographs each), the Journey
  & Legacy timeline (year, title, detail, place - the reference's seven milestones until a brand
  writes its own) and every heading, caption and sentence on the page. One template for every
  brand - Dum Dum, Qua Qua, Corp and any added later - with no branch on a brand code anywhere.
  **History is untouched:** everything the page prints is frozen into the version's snapshot the
  moment it is raised, so a version already sent keeps its pictures, name, website and words
  whatever Settings later says; a version raised before the pictures were snapshotted prints
  today's pictures and its own words. Migration `20260909180000_quotation_brand_pdf_assets` adds
  one nullable JSON column, `sales_quotation_brand.pdf_assets`, and drops nothing; the pictures go
  through the same upload path as the Global Logo - a picture may be up to 20 MB when picked and
  is resampled in the browser (2400px, about 2.5 MB for the large pictures) before it is sent.
  Quotation creation, editing, versions, pricing, GST, links, permissions and the register are
  unchanged
  ([PublicQuotationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicQuotationPage.js),
  [quotationTemplateContent.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/quotationTemplateContent.js),
  [QuotationSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/QuotationSettingsPanel.js),
  [quotation-brands/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/quotation-brands/service.js),
  [quotationPdfService.js](../../backend/src/services/quotationPdfService.js)).
- **The Quotation Generator quotes for three brands - Dum Dum, Qua Qua and Corp - from one screen,
  one register and one PDF.** The form's first choice is now labelled **Quotation Brand** (the same
  three answers the Vertical radio always offered), and it decides what the sections below offer:
  the brand's own services and deliverables, its packages (applied to one shoot in a move, every
  line still editable), its terms and the words on the
  client's copy. Switching brand asks first when it would clear client details or remove lines the
  new brand does not offer, and keeps the shoots. All of it is configured from **Settings →
  Quotation Settings** - General (the studio rates and which brands are offered) plus one screen per
  brand with Services, Deliverables, Packages, Pricing, Terms and PDF Content tabs - on
  `ADMIN / System Settings`, the permission the new `/api/sales/quotation-brands/admin/*` routes
  enforce; reading a brand's configuration answers for `SALES / Quotation Management` READ, so
  whoever raises quotations can load it. A brand that has configured nothing offers the whole
  project catalogue at the studio rates and prints the page exactly as before, which is what every
  existing quotation and every new Dum Dum one gets until somebody changes it.
  **History never moves:** each version freezes the brand it was raised under into its own
  `brandSnapshot` - name, the Global Logo, terms, wording, GST rate - and
  every line carries the price it was added at, so raising a service from ₹30,000 to ₹35,000
  reprices only the next quotation, retiring a service leaves it on the quotations that have it, and
  the public page and the PDF read the snapshot and never today's settings. The register gains a
  small **brand badge** column and **All / Dum Dum / Qua Qua / Corp** tabs over the same table;
  quotation numbers are unchanged. Migration `20260909140000_quotation_brands` adds
  `sales_quotation.quotation_brand` (backfilled from each version's own payload, Dum Dum where it
  named nothing) and the brand master tables, and drops nothing. Roles: a Super Admin has it all;
  grant `ADMIN / System Settings` to whoever should configure brands - no reseed is needed, since
  no new permission row was added
  ([QuotationGeneratorPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationGeneratorPanel.js),
  [QuotationSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/QuotationSettingsPanel.js),
  [lib/quotationBrand.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/quotationBrand.mjs),
  [quotation-brands/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/quotation-brands/service.js)).

- **`DateField` answers a date and a time, so the CRD document uses the studio's calendar rather
  than the browser's.** The event start and end were the only two native `datetime-local` inputs in
  the application, and they were native for one reason: the shared calendar had no time, so it could
  not express the question. A browser picker sitting beside eight of the app's own reads as a
  different application, looks different in every browser, and takes none of the bounds
  `restriction`/`minDate`/`maxDate` express. `withTime` adds a time row above the calendar, committed
  with the day as `2026-09-08T22:30` — the shape the document already stored, so nothing had to be
  migrated. A day picked without touching the time takes midnight rather than refusing to close, and
  clearing the day clears the whole value, never leaving a time with no date. Nothing changes for the
  callers that do not ask for a time. `readOnly` arrives with it, losing the calendar icon along with
  the calendar and taking `TextField`'s read-back grey, so the read-back start date matches the
  fields beside it ([DateField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/DateField.js),
  [PublicCrdDocumentFormDumDum.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicCrdDocumentFormDumDum.js)).
- **The client's CRD form names the booking it is for.** Both documents — Dum Dum and Qua Qua — now
  open with **Event ID** beside **WhatsApp Group Name**, on one line, prefilled from the project and
  read-only. The Event ID was in the page header only, which a client scrolling a long form never
  sees again; the two answer the same question, "which shoot is this?", one exactly and one readably,
  so they share a line rather than stacking into two full-width boxes above everything the client is
  actually asked. Never required and never asked for, so it cannot block a submission and does not
  move the completion percentage; a document raised by hand before its project exists shows an empty
  box rather than inventing an id
  ([document.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/document.js),
  [documentQQ.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/documentQQ.js),
  [PublicCrdDocumentFormDumDum.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicCrdDocumentFormDumDum.js),
  [PublicCrdDocumentFormQuaQua.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicCrdDocumentFormQuaQua.js)).
- **Forgot Password is a code on your mobile, and nothing else.** The studio's people know their own
  number and do not all read the address their login was created with, so the email field is gone
  from that screen entirely — not behind a tab, not behind a link. It asks for your registered
  mobile, sends a six-digit code, and shows the time left as a clock; **Resend code** waits until the
  two minutes have run out, because a resend while a code is live is how somebody ends up holding two
  and typing the wrong one. **Use a different mobile number** is offered only once a code is out,
  which is the one thing that goes wrong here. A correct code is exchanged for the same reset token
  an emailed link carries, so `resetPassword` stays the one place a password is set.

  The number comes from your **employee record** — the one given during onboarding — or from the
  login itself for an account that is not a person. That second path is what makes code-only
  recovery safe: the studio's Super Admin has no employee record, so with no email route it would
  have been permanently unable to reset its own password. `user.mobile`
  ([20260909180000](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260909180000_user_mobile/migration.sql)) is where
  that number goes, set with `npm run set:user-mobile -- <username> "+91 …"`; the employee's number
  wins wherever there is one, so it is never a second copy to keep in step. `POST`/`PATCH
  /api/admin/users` accept it too, held to the application's own phone rule — a number in a shape no
  gateway will take is a login that looks recoverable and is not.

  The emailed-link endpoint is left on the server, unreferenced by any screen, as the last way back
  in for an account whose number has not been set.

  Six digits is a million guesses, so the flow is built to refuse: the code is stored only as a
  sha256 hash, one code at a time (asking again spends the last), **five wrong attempts spend it**,
  a number two employees share matches nobody, a non-active account gets nothing, and every
  refusal — wrong, expired, spent, unknown number — reads identically so the form cannot be used to
  ask who works at the studio. The request route is throttled per number and the verify route
  separately and harder, because asking for codes and guessing them are different attacks
  ([authController.js](../../backend/src/controllers/authController.js),
  [authRoutes.js](../../backend/src/routes/authRoutes.js),
  [ForgotPasswordForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/auth/ForgotPasswordForm.js),
  migration [20260909170000](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260909170000_password_reset_otp/migration.sql),
  [password-reset-otp.test.js](../../backend/test/password-reset-otp.test.js)).
- **An SMS gateway can be configured, and nothing is sent until one is.** The application had no SMS
  channel at all — only SMTP and a QR-paired personal WhatsApp account, and automated sending from
  that number is what gets it banned, so codes are deliberately **not** routed through it.
  [smsSender.js](../../backend/src/services/smsSender.js) is built the way `smtpPool` is: credentials in
  the environment, never logged or returned, and `{ sent: false, reason }` rather than a throw when
  nothing is configured. `msg91` is implemented (the usual choice for Indian numbers) and `http`
  covers any gateway taking a key, a number and a message, so a different provider is configuration
  rather than code. **The code is never written to a log**, which a test enforces on both the sender
  and the controller. `GET /api/health/email` now reports the SMS gateway beside the SMTP senders,
  since either can be why nothing arrived. Set `SMS_PROVIDER` and `SMS_API_KEY` to switch it on — see
  [.env.example](../../backend/.env.example).
- **Adding, renaming or removing a Field Settings entry says so.** A screen with every change saved
  as it is made has nothing to press and nothing to grey out, so an entry that was added and an
  entry that was refused looked identical — the box cleared either way. All three actions now answer
  on the application's own snackbar (`FloatingNotice`), naming what happened —
  "Production Manager added to Designations", "Data Manager renamed to Data Lead",
  "Intern removed from Designations" — and a failure says so in the same place. It is the component
  Employee Settings already raises for these same four lists, so both ways into them behave alike. A
  list that could not be *loaded* stays on the panel's notice line instead, where it does not fade:
  it describes the state of the section rather than something somebody just did, and the empty state
  under it would otherwise read "nothing in this list yet"
  ([HrmOptionListEditor.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/HrmOptionListEditor.js)).
- **`GET /api/health/email` says whether this server can send mail.** "The password reset never
  arrived" was unanswerable from outside the machine: every cause lives in the environment — no
  `SMTP_*` configured, a revoked App Password, a sender in cooldown after being refused — and the
  reset form must answer the same thing regardless, or it becomes a way of asking which addresses
  have accounts. This reports the configured senders and how each is faring, and `?verify=true`
  authenticates against each one. Guarded by `ADMIN / System Settings` `READ`, because it names the
  studio's sending addresses; no password is ever returned ([server.js](../../backend/src/server.js)).
- **Field Settings can be searched.** Each of the four HRM lists — Designations, Departments,
  Verticals, Skills — now has a search box in its header, beside the count, in the same arrangement
  the Branches section next to it already uses. It filters what is already loaded, so it answers on
  the keystroke rather than on a round trip, and while it is searching the count reads "3 of 30
  shown" so a filtered list never reads as a list that has lost entries. Adding clears it, or the
  entry just made would be filtered out of the list it was added to, and it is cleared when the
  Field dropdown moves so a search typed against Departments does not hide most of Designations
  ([HrmOptionListEditor.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/HrmOptionListEditor.js),
  [field-settings-list.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/field-settings-list.test.mjs)).
- **The onboarding link can be copied from the dialog that hands it over.** "Onboarding Invite
  Created" showed the link in a read-only box with no way to take it: the only Copy Link button
  appeared in the footer *after* the invite email had failed, so the ordinary path was to select an
  eight-character token and retype it. There is now a copy icon at the right-hand end of the field
  itself, where the value is — a bare glyph like every icon control in the OS, which becomes a tick
  for a moment when the link is on the clipboard and says so to a screen reader as well. It copies
  through [lib/clipboard.js](../../frontend/src/lib/clipboard.js) rather than `navigator.clipboard`
  directly, which matters on the VPS: the async Clipboard API exists only on https and localhost, so
  the direct call threw over plain http and reported *Unable to copy link.* for a link that was on
  the screen the whole time. The failure message now says what to do instead. `TextField` grew an
  `action` slot for it — one control inside the field's border, with the input taking the padding so
  a long value scrolls under it rather than behind it
  ([OnboardingInviteDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/OnboardingInviteDialog.js),
  [TextField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/TextField.js),
  [onboarding-invite-copy-link.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/onboarding-invite-copy-link.test.mjs)).
- **Production Manager and Branch Partner are designations the studio can use.** They are offered by
  Intended Designation on an onboarding invite and by Designation on the employee record, which are
  the same list. Added as rows in
  [20260909150000](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260909150000_add_production_manager_and_branch_partner/migration.sql)
  rather than only in the code, because that list is HR's own: every screen reads the `designation`
  table through master data and falls back to `designationOptions` only when the table is empty — so
  a role added to the constant alone appears on no screen at all. Both halves are now held together
  by [designation-options.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/designation-options.test.mjs), which fails if
  either list names a designation the other does not
  ([employeeOptions.js](../../frontend/src/constants/employeeOptions.js)). The migration is
  `INSERT IGNORE` on the code, so it changes nothing where HR has already added one by hand.

- **Both onboarding popups now carry a message ready to send.** Creating an onboarding invite, and
  creating a login, each end on a dialog holding the finished wording — addressed to the person, with
  the link or the credentials in it — and a **Copy message** button. Before this the invite offered
  the bare link and the login offered three labelled values, leaving whoever was sending them to
  write the sentences around them every time. The box is editable, so a word can be changed before
  sending, and editing it changes only what is copied. Neither message names the studio: what it
  calls itself is a Settings value, and a second copy hardcoded in the wording would be the one that
  goes stale ([ShareableMessage.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/ShareableMessage.js),
  [OnboardingInviteDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/OnboardingInviteDialog.js),
  [CredentialsCreatedDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/CredentialsCreatedDialog.js)).

- **Creating a login now shows the credentials, with a copy of them.** Like creating an onboarding
  invite, finishing *Create User & Send Email* opens a dialog holding the **username**, the **login
  email** and the **temporary password**, with **Copy details** at the bottom. Until now it answered
  "User created and credential email sent." and nothing else — so the one moment the studio held all
  three details passed without showing them, and if the email never arrived there was no way back to
  them: the server does not return a password, so the temporary one was unreadable anywhere
  afterwards. That is most useful exactly when the mail did not go out, which is not rare — the send
  is refused outright whenever the studio's Gmail account has spent its daily quota. The dialog is
  raised by the repository page rather than the form panel, because the panel closes the instant
  onboarding finishes and this has to outlive it
  ([CredentialsCreatedDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/CredentialsCreatedDialog.js),
  [EmployeeVerificationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeVerificationPage.js)).

- **Edit Employee now carries the Login Credentials page, beside the record's own details.** The
  same page HR meets when reviewing an onboarding submission, doing the other half of the job. Edit
  showed the details and nothing else, which reads an account as something issued once and never
  looked at again: an employee taken on before this flow existed had no way to be given a login
  without going round through Administration, and the roles, cover and leave approval recorded
  beside one could be read nowhere on the screen that owns the person.
  It decides for itself which job it is doing. **No account yet** — the create form, exactly as
  onboarding shows it. **An account already** — the sign-in identity is *stated* rather than
  offered: username and email as facts, the password never shown at all, because changing an
  identity belongs to Admin → User Management, the one screen that owns accounts, and a password box
  here would be a second way to take an account over. What the account may **do** stays editable:
  **Roles**, **Alternative** cover and **Leave Approval**, saved together in one transaction since
  roles live on the account and the other two on the employee.
  Two endpoints carry it — `GET` and `PATCH /api/hrm/employees/:id/login-credentials` — each behind
  its own `ADMIN / User Management` guard in the controller, like the draft endpoints beside them:
  the router's blanket pass exists for departments and branches, and who may sign in as whom is not
  reference data. The `PATCH` accepts no username, email or password at all, so a browser sending
  them changes nothing. No new permission and no reseed: `ADMIN / User Management` already existed
  and already governed this page.
  **Save Changes saves both pages.** They are one panel and one press: an edit made on Employee
  Details and then saved from Login Credentials wrote only the account, and closed — through the
  credential save's own callback, which is a programmatic close and so walks past the unsaved-work
  prompt that guards the ×. The details went with nothing said. The record is written first now and a
  refusal stops the press there, on the page that refused; the button reads **Save All Changes** when
  both halves have work, and one notice covers the pair.
  ([EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js),
  [useCredentialForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/useCredentialForm.js),
  [employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js))
- **Bookings & Converted can duplicate a project that was sold at no quotation.** The row menu's new
  **Duplicate** opens Create Project already filled in: the shoots, services and deliverables of the
  project being copied arrive as the new one's own pending plan — every row editable, because a
  duplicate is the same package sold to somebody else and the dates, venue and quantities are what
  the person is there to change — along with its vertical, branch, remarks and Signed Quotation.
  Three answers are asked for again. **Enquiry ID** and **Client Main Number** name who it is being
  sold to. The **WhatsApp group name** goes with them for a harder reason: it is a generator row the
  save *claims*, the source project claimed it already, and carrying it would have come back 409
  "no longer available" on a form that looked complete. **Payment Received opens empty** — the
  advance is a cash entry, and a copy must never credit the studio twice for one payment.
  Offered only on a project with no quotation: one sold on a quotation is copied by duplicating that
  quotation and booking from it, so the copy is priced against a quotation of its own instead of
  naming a document raised for another client's enquiry. It asks for no new permission — duplicating
  books a project, so it answers for `SALES / Sales Manager` `READ_WRITE`, exactly as New Project and
  Edit do, and is absent rather than refused without it. Project Management's Projects lists the same
  records to plan them, not to sell a second one, so it is not offered there.
  ([SalesManagerPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/SalesManagerPage.js),
  [projectBooking.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/projectBooking.js),
  [quotationProjectPlan.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/quotationProjectPlan.js),
  [ProjectDataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/ProjectDataTable.js))

- **Next Follow-up Date opens on today's date**, on all three verticals, in Add Lead and in Bulk
  Import. A lead being written down is one somebody is about to work — that is what the Interested it
  opens on means — and the next call is almost always the same day it was taken. Left blank it was a
  field answered by hand on every lead, and the ones nobody answered are the leads that appear on no
  Pending Followups tab and are chased by nobody. Read when the form is opened rather than when the
  page was built, so a board left open overnight still offers today, and filled only where it is
  blank, so a date somebody typed — or one cleared by marking the lead Rejected — is never replaced.
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [crmBulkImport.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/crmBulkImport.mjs))
- **Leads & Followups → ⋮ → Bulk Import: several clients typed at once, onto one vertical.** Add
  Lead asks one client everything its vertical knows how to ask, which is right for the lead somebody
  is about to work and wrong for twenty names off an event or a call with a planner, and the CSV
  Import beside it only helps where a file already exists. This asks the few fields that make a lead
  findable and assignable — Enquire Name, Contact, Assigned Branch, Assign To, Next Follow-up Date
  and a Description — plus Date of Birth (or) EDOD on Qua Qua, which is the one thing that vertical
  cannot plan a shoot without. The vertical is chosen once at the top and **opens on the board being
  read**, the same behaviour Add Lead has. Each client is one line and nothing scrolls sideways: the
  fields share the row's width in proportion to what each answer needs rather than each claiming a
  width the row has to find, so the line is always exactly as wide as the card, and below a wide
  screen it folds to two columns rather than shrinking past legibility. Adding a client is typing
  rather than pressing: a blank numbered line sits at the end and filling it grows the next one
  behind it, and blank lines are dropped rather than written as nameless leads. One request writes
  the batch.
  ([BulkImportLeadsDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/BulkImportLeadsDialog.js),
  [crmBulkImport.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/crmBulkImport.mjs),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [crm-bulk-import.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/crm-bulk-import.test.mjs))
- **Work Hours is now Call Hours plus Application Usage, with both halves shown beside the total.**
  The column was the application alone, and it undercounted precisely the people it was read about:
  they spend their day on the phone, and a salesperson on a forty-minute call is not touching the
  browser — so the browser recorded nothing and the timesheet read that forty minutes as idle. The
  call half is what Callyzer reports for that person on that day, and a day spent entirely on the
  phone with the application never opened is now a working day with a row rather than no row at all.
  It is effort across two systems rather than wall clock and can exceed the length of a day: a call
  taken with the application open counts in both, because both are true and nothing records which
  minutes overlapped — so Day Span stays wall clock and Idle stays the gaps between the
  application's own stretches. The two sides are matched on the person's name, the only key there is
  between a Callyzer handset and an ERP login; a name on one side with no match on the other still
  gets a row showing that half, which is the honest way to be wrong about a join with no key.
  ([service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-reports/service.js),
  [crmReports.js](../../frontend/src/lib/crmReports.js),
  [crm-reports.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/crm-reports.test.mjs))
- **The CRD link can be sent to the client on WhatsApp, with the message already written.** Send
  Link now offers Send on WhatsApp beside Copy Link and Open Form: it opens WhatsApp on the client's
  number with the link and a short note ready, and the person presses send. It is never an automated
  or unattended send — that is what gets a studio's number banned — and it is the same click-to-chat
  pattern the Image Selection gallery share already uses. Absent where the project holds no number,
  rather than shown and refused.
  ([CrdDocumentsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CrdDocumentsPage.js))
- **Leads & Followups has a date range on its header, immediately left of the Nebula icon: Today,
  Yesterday, This Week, This Month or two days picked by hand.** One question asked once over
  whichever board is on screen; the only way to ask it before was to type into the Date column's
  filter, which matches text and so can say "August" but never "the last seven days". It narrows the
  rows, the total under the table and the counts on the tab labels together, so a tab never promises
  rows it will not show, and "select all" covers what is on screen rather than everything the tab has
  ever held. All Time is where it starts and where it goes back to — a range switch with no way back
  is a filter that cannot be cleared. It appears only where it means something: on the Call Box that
  is the All tab alone, since the other four are queues to work through rather than periods to report
  on and narrowing "the calls nobody answered" to this month hides the ones still outstanding from
  last month; and on the three verticals every tab except Today New Leads, Today Pending Followups
  and Yesterday Pending Followups, which are already a date — a range on top of one of those is two
  answers to one question, and the honest result of asking it is an empty board. Ahead of Nebula
  rather than among the actions after it, through a new `leadingActions` slot on the module header:
  everything from Nebula rightwards does something, and this only changes what is being looked at.
  ([crmDateRange.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/crmDateRange.mjs),
  [DateRangeSwitch.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/DateRangeSwitch.js),
  [ModulePageHeader.js](../../frontend/src/components/layout/ModulePageHeader.js),
  [leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js),
  [callBoardService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/callBoardService.js),
  [crm-date-range.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/crm-date-range.test.mjs))
- **A lead put in somebody's name now tells them so, on the bell.** All three ways of doing it raise
  it — the Add Lead form with a CRM chosen, an edit that changes the CRM, and the bulk Update Details
  over a selection — and the notice opens that lead. A save that does not move the CRM raises
  nothing, so the board's Update no longer re-announces an assignment with every remark and status
  change, and nobody is told about their own action, so taking a lead for yourself is silent. Until
  now a handed-over lead simply appeared on somebody's board and whether they noticed depended on
  whether they happened to look. The CRM records who is working a lead as a *name* rather than as a
  user id, so the person is matched on it — the same compromise the Reports module and the bell's own
  follow-up notices already make, and the same one caveat: two logins under one name would both be
  told. ([leadNotifications.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadNotifications.js),
  [crm-lead-assignment-notice.test.js](../../backend/test/crm-lead-assignment-notice.test.js))
- **An administrator's bell is the studio's rather than their own.** A reader the data scope does not
  confine — a Super Admin, or anybody whose branch reach is not narrowed — now sees the whole floor's
  follow-ups, unanswered WhatsApp replies and unread calls, each line naming whose work it is; a bell
  narrower than the boards beneath it would be the one place their view stopped. Decided by
  `dataScope.seesEverything`, asked per source's module, so somebody unconfined in Sales and confined
  elsewhere gets the studio's CRM notices and their own of everything else — and so that no
  `req.user.role === "SUPER_ADMIN"` test is written into a controller, which would be a second answer
  to a question the data scope already answers and one no role could be granted.
  ([sources.js](../../backend/src/modules/notifications/sources.js),
  [dataScope.service.js](../../backend/src/services/dataScope.service.js))

- **The studio names the system its own people are given access to.** Settings → Branding Logo asks
  for a **Login Credential Name** beside the Global Name, and the email that hands a new employee
  their username and first password uses it — in the subject, in the sentence saying what the
  account is for, on the sign-in button and in the sign-off. That name was written into the email
  template, so every studio running this application told its new employees the same thing whatever
  they call it themselves. Left empty, the email still reads exactly as it did: a blank must never
  reach somebody as a hole in the first email they get. The name is read when the email is sent
  rather than at start-up, so a change in Settings is in the next one
  ([emailTemplates.js](../../backend/src/services/emailTemplates.js),
  [StudioBrandingPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/StudioBrandingPanel.js)). Migration
  [20260908200000](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260908200000_studio_login_credential_name/migration.sql)
  adds the nullable column.
- **An unfinished branch is said once, not under every empty box.** Pressing Save on a branch form
  with blank required fields put a red message under each of them — ten boxes of the same sentence,
  which read as a form full of mistakes rather than a form not yet finished. It is now one line in
  the snackbar, "Fill in all the mandatory fields", with the boxes left as they were; the asterisk
  beside each label already says which are needed. What a *filled* box has got wrong is different —
  a PAN in the wrong shape, an email that is not one — and still says so under the field itself,
  because that is specific and no snackbar can point at it
  ([BranchesPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/BranchesPanel.js)).
- **Claiming the head office asks first, and names who loses it.** The Head office switch on a
  branch is the one control on that form that decides something about a *different* branch: only
  one branch may hold it, so turning it on takes it from whoever has it, and the person doing it
  may not know who that is. The switch now opens a confirmation that says so by name — "Tiruppur TN
  is the head office today" — and does not move until it is confirmed, so a mis-click changes
  nothing. Turning it off asks nothing: that takes it from nobody
  ([BranchesPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/BranchesPanel.js)).
- **A branch address is kept in its parts.** Settings → Organization → Branches asked for the
  address as one box of text, which could be shown and nothing else. It is now Address Line 1,
  Address Line 2, City, State and Postal Code, so a document can print the city or the state on its
  own and two branches in the same city can be told apart by what is above it. Typing a six-digit
  PIN code fills the city and the state from the India Post lookup this application already uses on
  the vendor form, and fills only a box that is empty — a form that corrects what somebody has
  typed is worse than typing two words. Both stay editable, and a PIN that matches nothing simply
  fills nothing. Migration
  [20260908190000](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260908190000_branch_postal_address/migration.sql)
  moves whatever each branch has today into the first line and then drops the old column, so no
  branch loses its address ([BranchesPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/BranchesPanel.js)).
- **A branch can hold two phone numbers.** Settings → Organization → Branches asks for an
  **Alternate Phone** beside the first one, for the landline next to the mobile or whoever answers
  when the first does not. It is the same field twice: optional like every other branch detail,
  cleaned of the spacing and dashes people type, and held to the one phone format this application
  checks every number against, in the browser and again on the server. The rule is now written once
  in the branch schema rather than twice, so the second number can never drift to a looser standard
  than the first. Migration
  [20260908180000](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260908180000_branch_alternate_phone/migration.sql)
  adds the nullable column, so every branch that exists is untouched
  ([BranchesPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/BranchesPanel.js),
  [admin/branches](../../backend/src/modules/admin/branches/validation.js)).

- **A branch may carry a Web URL.** It sits beside the email and is optional, like the email and
  the second phone number: most studios have one site for every branch, and a branch is
  reachable without one. Stored exactly as it is written, with or without a scheme — adding one
  would put a link on a document that the studio did not write — and checked only loosely, enough
  to catch a sentence typed into the box. Migration
  [20260908210000](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260908210000_branch_website_url/migration.sql)
  adds the column, nullable, so every existing branch is untouched.
- **A branch is disabled, never deleted.** The row menu offers **Disable** where it offered Remove,
  and **Use Again** on a branch already disabled. A disabled branch stops being offered wherever a
  branch is chosen and gives up the head office if it held it, while every user, asset and past
  record that belongs to it keeps it and stays readable. There is no delete left to reach: the
  endpoint is gone, along with the counting that used to decide between deleting a branch and
  closing it ([branches/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/admin/branches/routes.js)).
- **A branch is given its code rather than having one derived.** Branch Code is the first field on
  the form, is required, and is stored exactly as it is written — the case, the spacing and the
  punctuation are the studio's to choose, and only the spaces around it are dropped. No two branches
  may hold the same one, compared the way the database compares text, so the same code in another
  case is still the same code. It used to be minted from the name, which meant the studio could not
  decide what its own branches are called in the systems that read a code.
  An existing branch's code may be changed as well, and two things outside this screen go on
  matching the old one until they are told otherwise: the database seed updates branches by code,
  and the asset importer accepts a code in the Branch column of a spreadsheet. What is still refused
  is emptying a code or taking one another branch holds
  ([branches/validation.js](../../backend/src/modules/admin/branches/validation.js)).
- **A branch must carry what a document needs before it can be saved.** The name, the address with
  its city, state and PIN code, a phone number, the GSTIN and PAN, and the whole bank block are all
  required now: those are what an invoice or a receipt from that branch has to print, and a branch
  missing any of them is one nobody can bill from. The second address line, the alternate phone and
  the email stay optional, each being a second way to say something the branch already says. The
  browser names every missing field at once and the server refuses the same save, and an edit may
  still change one field at a time but may not empty a required one
  ([branches/validation.js](../../backend/src/modules/admin/branches/validation.js)).
- **A second branch cannot reuse a GSTIN, a bank account number or a branch name.** Each is a fact
  about one place: two branches sharing a GSTIN means one of them is registered as somewhere it is
  not, and a register with two branches of the same name cannot be used, since every picker in the
  application shows the name and nothing else. The message names the branch that already has it and
  appears under the field itself. PAN and IFSC are deliberately left shareable: a PAN belongs to the
  company, so every branch of one studio has the same one, and an IFSC names the *bank's* branch
  ([branches/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/admin/branches/service.js)). A closed branch still
  holds its own, so its GSTIN cannot be handed to a new branch.
- **The branch form no longer asks whether a branch is open.** Whether a branch is open is not
  something anybody sets by hand: it is open from the day it is added, and Remove closes it when
  users, assets or past work still point at it. The toggle offered a second way to say that and a
  way to reopen a branch by accident while editing its bank details. A closed branch still reads as
  closed on its row, and editing one leaves it closed
  ([BranchesPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/BranchesPanel.js)).
- **The branch register has a search box.** It narrows the list as it is typed, across everything a
  branch is written down as: its name, its code, the address, the GSTIN and PAN, both phone numbers,
  the email and the whole bank block. A query of digits is matched against the digits of every
  field, so a number read off a statement with spaces in it finds the branch it was stored without
  them ([branchName.js](../../frontend/src/lib/branchName.js)). The whole register is already in the
  browser, so nothing is asked of the server; the count beside it reads "1 of 6 branches" while a
  search is on, and a search that finds nothing says so rather than offering to add a branch.
- **Settings → Organization → Branches: the studio keeps its own branch register.** Add Branch opens a
  form for the branch name and address, how to reach it, what it is registered as (GSTIN and PAN)
  and where it banks (account holder, bank, account number and IFSC), plus whether it is the head
  office and whether it is open. Only the name is required — a branch is filled in over time, and a
  half-finished one still has to be usable — and every identifier is checked against its real shape
  in the browser and again on the server, so a PAN with a digit where a letter belongs is caught
  before it reaches a client's accountant
  ([BranchesPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/BranchesPanel.js),
  [admin/branches](../../backend/src/modules/admin/branches/service.js)).

  This adds to the branch every part of the application already points at — a user's home branch,
  an asset's location, the branches a role reaches — rather than inventing a second idea of one, so
  a branch added here is immediately offered wherever a branch is chosen. Two rules are the
  server's: exactly one branch may be the head office, and claiming it takes it from whoever held it
  in the same write; and a branch that users, assets or past work still point at is **closed**
  rather than deleted, so those records keep the branch they were saved against. Migration
  [20260908160000](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260908160000_branch_registered_details/migration.sql)
  adds the columns, all nullable, so every existing branch is untouched.

  The endpoints answer to `ADMIN / System Settings`, the same permission that decides whether the
  Organization section is offered at all. Reading the branch list for a *picker* is unchanged and stays
  open to any signed-in session through `GET /api/hrm/branches`: choosing a branch is everybody's,
  maintaining the register is an administrator's.
- **An Expired Projects card on the Image Selection dashboard.** It counts the galleries whose link
  has stopped working while the client still had picking to do — the far end of the life Link
  Expiring Soon watches the near end of, and a project is on one or the other, never both. A link
  that expires after the client has finished is nobody's problem and joins neither, which is what
  keeps the count from filling up with every old project in the end; a gallery the studio revoked
  is not counted either, since the studio pulled that link itself. Pressing the card narrows Recent
  Projects to those galleries, and `?attention=expired` is a filter the Projects page takes as a
  deep link like the rest ([dashboard/classify.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/dashboard/classify.js)).
- **The client gallery has a Deselect All button.** It sits in the footer between the completion
  bar and Review & Submit, and releases every photo the client picked for the selection item they
  are working in. It asks first, saying how many that is, and is absent once the item is submitted
  - nothing can be unpicked then. One request rather than one per photo
  (`POST /api/public/image-selection/galleries/:token/selection/clear`), so clearing hundreds of
  picks on a phone is a single round trip; it takes the PIN session and re-checks the submitted
  lock exactly as unticking one photo does.

- **Image Selection has a dashboard.** `/image-selection/dashboard` — where Image Selection opens
  from the sidebar, and the first half of the module header's Dashboard | Projects switch — shows every Image Selection project as it
  stands (Album Proofing is a separate section and never counted), with what the chosen range added
  (This Month by default; Lifetime, This Week, 3 Months and 6 Months beside it) — projects
  created, photos added, photos picked, items submitted — under ten headline cards (Total
  Projects, Yet to Upload, Client Sending Not Done, Client Not Started, Client In Progress, Client
  Submitted, Yet to Download, Link Expiring Soon, Expired Projects, Downloaded), each with an arrow saying how far
  it has moved since the start of the range — where the number stood at the end of last week, last
  month, or three or six months ago, reconstructed from the dates the rows carry; Lifetime has no
  start and so no arrow — Client Selection Progress (the clients 85% to 99% through their
  selection, which the range does not scope), Storage & Processing (what it added to the store, with the studio-wide
  totals beneath), and Recent Projects in the standard table. Every card, stage and attention row filters the
  Recent Projects table on the page itself — press again, or Total Projects, to let go — and only
  a project leaves the page; the stages are the sidebar's own progress rungs, Completed being
  the archived list. The Projects page accepts the same views as `?status=` / `?attention=` deep
  links and shows one as a chip in its sidebar. One request feeds the page
  ([dashboard/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/dashboard/service.js)), counts are
  grouped in the database, and no photo row, folder or bucket is read. Under IMAGE_SELECTION /
  Project Management like the rest of the module, so every role that reaches Projects reaches it;
  New Project is offered to `READ_WRITE` only. Migration `20260908100000_gallery_client_activity`
  adds three nullable stamps to the gallery publication — first opened, last opened, last client
  activity — written by the public gallery so the attention rows have something to read; every
  existing publication reads as "not recorded" rather than "never". Migration
  `20260908120000_project_selected_downloaded_at` adds one more, on the project: when the selected
  originals were last taken out, stamped by the server copy-out and by Download Selected finishing
  in the browser (`POST /projects/:id/gallery/selected/downloaded`), and judged against the latest
  submission so a later submission puts the project back on Yet to Download.
  The page sits on a tinted ground with every card lifted on a shadow, so the panels read as
  panels rather than as one white sheet with lines drawn on it; the tint is `.dashboard-canvas` in
  [globals.css](../../frontend/src/app/globals.css), which names its own value per appearance so it
  stays a step behind the cards in both dark themes as well as in light.

- **A salary review now says how it went, and the list shows it in colour.** Each review carries
  **Shine** or **Shadow**, chosen beside the Review Date, and the review list marks it twice over:
  a named chip in a new Outcome column, and the row's own colour — Shine tints the row green with a
  green edge, Shadow tints it rose with a dark red edge — so a record can be read down at a glance.
  A review nobody marked carries neither, which is why there is no third middle option: unmarked
  already means "nobody said". **The increment amount is no longer required**: reviews are held
  without a figure, and demanding one meant typing a `0` that then counted as a real increment of
  zero in the employee's totals. An empty box is stored as null and adds nothing; a typed `0` is
  still a `0`, and the list shows the two as `—` and `0`. **A review may also be dated on any day**
  — the restriction to today-or-earlier refused the scheduled reviews the studio records, and even
  refused the future due date *Record this review* handed it
  ([employeeReviewConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/employeeReviewConfig.js),
  [AddReviewDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/AddReviewDialog.js),
  [EmployeeReviewList.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeReviewList.js),
  [migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260909110000_review_outcome/migration.sql)).
- **Onboarding no longer throws away work you were part-way through.** Change anything on **Review
  Employee Details** or the **Login Credentials** page and closing the panel now asks — *Save
  draft*, *Discard*, *Keep editing* — instead of discarding it silently. On Review, Save draft is
  the save its own footer button makes: the corrections are kept and the submission stays under
  review, nobody is approved. The Login Credentials page gains the same **Discard changes** and
  **Save Draft** pair, and Save Draft now keeps **the whole page** — username, login email,
  temporary password, roles, branch, both alternatives and the leave approvers — so the page
  reopens exactly as it was left. It is a draft and not an account: nothing is validated, no `User`
  row is created, no email is sent, and no access is granted until **Create User & Send Email** —
  which clears the draft in the same transaction, so an account never sits beside a staler copy of
  itself. Reading or writing a draft needs `ADMIN / User Management` write, the same permission
  creating the account needs, because it holds the password the account will start on; for that
  reason it is stripped from every ordinary employee response and travels only through its own
  endpoint ([credentialDraft.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/credentialDraft.js),
  [useCredentialForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/useCredentialForm.js),
  [EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js),
  [controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js),
  [migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260909100000_login_credentials_draft/migration.sql)).
- **A quotation books its own project, without leaving the register.** A quotation nothing has been
  booked from now offers **Create Project** in its row menu, and the form opens on the Quotations
  page itself with the quotation, its enquiry, the client's number, the branch, the vertical and the
  quoted amount already filled in — and with the shoots, services and deliverables the client agreed
  to pay for brought across as the project's plan, shown rather than editable. The studio was
  reading a quotation, going to Bookings & Converted, and retyping what it had just read. The entry
  is offered on the whole chain rather than the row: a quotation whose earlier version already
  became a project has been booked, and offering it again would invite a second project against one
  negotiation. It is also absent for a role that may raise quotations but not book projects — the
  form and its save are `SALES / Sales Manager`, as they are on Bookings & Converted, which is the
  same panel and the same save shared rather than a second one
  ([ProjectBookingPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/ProjectBookingPanel.js),
  [projectBooking.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/projectBooking.js),
  [QuotationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationPage.js),
  [SalesManagerPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/SalesManagerPage.js)).
- **Message Template in Settings, and the receipt it writes for every payment received.** The studio
  was retyping the same acknowledgement after each receipt, copying the amount, the shoot and the
  outstanding balance across by eye. **Settings → Message Template → Cash Received** now holds that
  wording, with the facts left as `{placeholders}` — `{payment amount}`, `{payment date}`,
  `{shoot name}`, `{payment mode}`, `{outstanding balance}`, `{payment id}` — and the panel lists
  every placeholder it can fill, marking the ones this wording uses. Recording a payment then hands
  the message back filled in: **Save & Next** and **Save & Close** both open a dialog with the
  finished text and a **Copy message** button, editable first for the odd word. The outstanding
  balance is the project's own arithmetic — the quotation plus its approved extras, less everything
  received, this payment included — computed on the server, which is also where the wording is read,
  so the accounts screen needs no settings permission to show a message it did not write. Money
  spent gets no message, and neither does a studio that has deleted the wording: the dialog stays
  shut rather than opening on a body of bare placeholders. A placeholder nothing fills is left in
  the message exactly as typed, and the dialog says so — visible in the one place it will be read
  ([messageTemplate.js](../../backend/src/services/messageTemplate.js),
  [receiptMessage.js](../../backend/src/modules/accounts/cash-entries/receiptMessage.js),
  [MessageTemplatePanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/MessageTemplatePanel.js),
  [ReceiptMessageDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/accounts/ReceiptMessageDialog.js),
  [migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260908150000_message_templates/migration.sql)).
- **Every quotation carries a name.** `Quotation - Vertical - Branch - Client Name - Venue` —
  *Quotation - Dum Dum - Erode TN - Shanmugam & Parimala - Kongu Mahal* — stamped on the version
  when it is raised and shown as a **Quotation Name** column on the register, beside the id. The id
  says exactly which document; the name says which one you are looking at without reading four other
  columns. **The client is whichever name that vertical asks for** — the couple for Dum Dum
  (both halves or neither, since "Shanmugam &" reads as a mistake), the baby or mother for Qua Qua,
  the company for Corp — falling back to the shared Enquire Name, which is the one field all three
  require. A part the quotation does not have is dropped rather than left as an empty segment, and
  the word *Quotation* always leads so no row is nameless. It is **stored rather than composed on
  read**: a quotation is never rewritten, so what a version was called when it was sent stays what it
  was called, and rebuilding it would rename a document already in a client's inbox the moment a
  venue was renamed in master data. **Run `node scripts/backfillQuotationNames.js --apply`** to name
  the versions raised before the column existed — it only writes rows that have none, so a second
  run does nothing ([quotationName.js](../../backend/src/services/quotationName.js),
  [backfillQuotationNames.js](../../backend/scripts/backfillQuotationNames.js),
  [migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260908140000_quotation_name/migration.sql)).
- **A task can be handed over with its files.** Assign a Task now has an **Attachments** section —
  the brief, the reference shot, the sheet the work is done from — and the task itself shows them:
  pictures as a grid of thumbnails, everything else as rows, each with a **⋮** carrying
  **Download**. Whoever attached a file, and whoever set the task, also get **Remove**. Anybody who
  may open a task may open its files; adding and removing belong to its two people alone, so
  somebody reading the studio's list through `HRM / All Tasks` can download every file and change
  none — the same ownership rule the four verbs keep. Images, PDFs, Word, Excel and PowerPoint
  files, CSV, text and ZIP up to 15MB each are accepted, judged by the file's own extension rather
  than by the type the browser claims, since a declared `image/png` is no guarantee about an `.exe`.
  The files are stored as private objects in R2 under the task's own code and streamed through the
  login, so there is no URL to a studio file that works without one, and attaching or removing one
  writes an activity entry naming it. What the task keeps is one `attachments` JSON column — the
  name, type, size, bucket key and who added it when, never the bytes — so a file is read with the
  task it belongs to and nothing else has to be joined; each entry takes an id one higher than the
  list has ever reached, and every write locks the task's row first, so two people removing two
  different files cannot put each other's back
  ([TaskAttachments.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/TaskAttachments.js),
  [attachments.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/tasks/attachments.js),
  [migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260908130000_task_attachments/migration.sql)).
- **Every referral now carries a Referral ID.** `DBS-REF-000001`, one run of numbers for the whole
  studio — a referral is one employee introducing another, and neither belongs to a vertical or a
  branch. It is the first column on **HRM → Employee Referral** and the first field in its info
  panel, so the register is quoted by a code rather than by a row nobody can name. Minted from the
  studio's one identifier module inside the transaction that saves the referral, so a failed save
  never spends a number. **On a database with referrals already in it, run
  `node scripts/renumberIdentifiers.js --only=EMPLOYEE_REFERRAL --apply`** to number them — oldest
  joining date first — and leave the counter where the live path carries on from; until then those
  rows read `-`. That `--only=` flag is new, and narrows a run to the schemes it names so filling in
  a new column cannot renumber anything else
  ([identifiers.js](../../backend/src/services/identifiers.js),
  [renumberIdentifiers.js](../../backend/scripts/renumberIdentifiers.js),
  [migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260908120000_referral_identifier/migration.sql)).
- **Employee Referral is a screen of its own under HRM.** Who the studio owes a referral to used to
  be a card list inside **Employee Repository → ⋮ → Settings**, which is a narrow column with no
  sorting, no filtering and no export — a register somebody works through, kept where a figure is
  set. It is now **HRM → Employee Referral** (`/hrm/employee-referrals`): one row per referral, with
  the referred employee and their ID, who referred them, the joining date the instalments are
  counted from, the referral amount, a column for each instalment reading its figure and whether it
  is due, overdue or paid, what is still outstanding, and the referral's own status. Tabs for
  **All / Outstanding / Settled**, the table's usual sorting, filtering, column visibility and CSV
  export, an info panel with the full instalment schedule, a running Outstanding total in the
  footer, and **Mark paid** on the row's ⋮ naming the instalment it settles — offered only for one
  still pending, and only with `READ_WRITE`. Paid stays irreversible here; a correction is an
  Accounts entry, and the server refuses the reversal
  ([EmployeeReferralsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/EmployeeReferralsPage.js)).

- **The studio's mark in the browser tab, uploaded in Settings and shown the moment it is saved.**
  Settings → Global Logo now takes a **favicon for light tabs and one for dark tabs**, because a
  tab is drawn on the browser's own strip rather than on the application's background — a dark mark
  disappears on a dark tab and a light one on a light tab, and the application's theme has no say
  in it. Upload one and it serves both; upload neither and the tab wears the bundled pair, the
  studio's own mark drawn for each strip. Each slot shows only what was put in it: a light mark
  standing in for a dark tab is not a dark mark, and showing it there would claim it was.
  It reaches **every tab this application serves**, the client's included — the proofing link, the
  gallery, the sign-in page — because it is mounted once in the root layout rather than page by
  page. Saving changes the tab **without a reload**: the same branding answer the top bar reads is
  handed the saved values on the spot. The file is stored exactly as it was picked rather than
  re-encoded, so an SVG stays sharp at 16px and at 180px: PNG or SVG, up to 5 MB, checked in the
  browser and again by the API on the same rules
  ([favicon.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/favicon.mjs),
  [StudioFavicon.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/StudioFavicon.js),
  [StudioBrandingPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/StudioBrandingPanel.js),
  `storeFavicon` in [imageStorage.js](../../backend/src/services/imageStorage.js), migration
  [20260907120000](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260907120000_studio_favicons/migration.sql)).

- **The CRM assistant counts only what the person asking may see.** Its module-level context —
  lead totals, the status and owner breakdowns, follow-ups, arrivals, conversions — narrows by the
  reader's grant on `SALES / CRM Leads`, the same resolver the board uses, on top of the vertical
  already on screen; and it says so in the prompt, so a narrowed total is never presented as the
  studio's. This was the last of the gap [IAM.md](../project-state/IAM.md) described between what
  Nebula reads and what the screen shows: every module now answers with the same rows on both paths.
- **Cash entries, payment requests, tasks and saved group names read the data scope.** Each keeps
  the rule it already had and the scope narrows with it, never past it: a cash entry by its branch
  and the employee who handled the money, with read-one, edit and delete now held to the same rows
  as the list; a payment request still the requester's own until somebody who may decide it looks,
  and that decider then narrowed — and approve, reject, edit and withdraw are held to the same
  visibility as the list, which none was before; All Tasks narrowed by the task's branch and the
  employee behind its assignee, while My Tasks and Tasks Given stay yours whatever the grant says;
  saved WhatsApp group names offered for the branches you reach. The assistant's accounts, HRM and
  project tools now read through the same resolver as their screens rather than the session's raw
  branch list, so Nebula and the screen answer with the same rows. New entries, requests, tasks and
  group names store their branch and owner references as they are saved. With every grant at All
  records nothing changes today.
- **Projects, quotations and CRD documents read the data scope.** The project register keeps its
  existing rule (your branches, the projects you are named on, `All Projects` widening it) and the
  grant's scope narrows with it — now by the branch reference rather than the branch's name — and,
  for the first time, every `/projects/:id/...` endpoint (edit, delete, start, shoots, payments,
  deliverables, allocations) is held to the same rows as the list, as are the assistant's project
  tools. Quotations narrow their list and every record action by the grant on Quotation
  Management; CRD documents follow the project or, when raised on their own, the enquiry's lead.
  New projects and quotations store their branch, vertical and owner references as they are saved.
  With every grant at All records nothing changes today.
- **CRM Leads reads the data scope.** The board, its tab counts, select-all, every per-lead
  endpoint (open, edit, delete, activity, call logs, transcripts, converted history, meetings),
  every bulk action and every lead-joined report and the dashboard narrow by the grant's scope on
  `SALES / CRM Leads`, as one resolved answer per request — so an enquiry id typed into a URL, or
  slipped into a bulk selection, reaches exactly what the board shows, and a report total cannot
  leak what the list hides. Every write path — the app, the CSV import, the Google Sheet poller, the
  Callyzer webhook, bulk assign — keeps `branch_id`, `vertical_id` and `owner_employee_id` in step
  with the text under the backfill's rule. With every grant at All records nothing changes today.
  The duplicate check and the Calls board stay unscoped on purpose (DATA-SCOPE.md, D14).
- **The reference columns are filled, under the approved rules.** Every `branch_id`, `vertical_id`
  and `owner_employee_id` is set from the text beside it — a branch by exact name then by its first
  word (`Erode` → `Erode TN`), a vertical by any of its three spellings, an owner by full name or
  employee id and never a first name — and tasks gain `owner_employee_id`, the employee behind the
  assignee's login. Nothing is guessed: a value that matches nothing stays NULL and is listed by
  `npm run report:data-scope`. The rule lives once, in `referenceMatchers.js`, and a test replays
  every row to prove the migration wrote what the rule says. Migration
  [20260906160000_data_scope_backfill](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260906160000_data_scope_backfill/migration.sql).
- **Every branch, vertical and owner a record holds as text now has a reference column beside it,
  unfilled.** `branch_id` on CRM leads, projects, quotations, cash entries, payment requests, tasks
  and WhatsApp group names; `vertical_id` on projects, group names, CRD documents and employees;
  `owner_employee_id` on leads and quotations. All NULL: nothing is backfilled until the match
  report (`npm run report:data-scope`, read-only) has been read and its rules approved — see
  [DATA-SCOPE.md — Stage 6](../project-state/DATA-SCOPE.md#stage-6-the-match-report-and-what-it-asks).
  Every screen still reads the text. Migration
  [20260906150000_data_scope_reference_columns](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260906150000_data_scope_reference_columns/migration.sql).
- **One resolver decides whose rows a request gets, and Asset Management is the first screen to use
  it.** `dataScope.service.js` turns a grant's scope into the `where` a list needs and a yes/no for
  one record; TEAM follows `Employee.reportsToEmployeeId` to any depth in a single recursive query.
  Asset Management now narrows its list, every record endpoint (read, update, delete, status,
  custody, inspections) and the assistant's asset tools through it — so an asset id typed into a
  URL reaches exactly what the list shows, which it did not before, and Nebula reads the same rows
  as the screen. Existing Asset Management grants are translated to Their branches, which is what
  the screen already did, rather than widened to All records
  ([20260906130000](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260906130000_asset_management_grants_scoped_by_branch/migration.sql)).
- **Every permission grant now carries a data scope.** Beside Read / Read-Write, Role Management
  offers whose rows the grant gets — All records, Their branches, Their team, Their own — once for
  the module or per sub-module under Custom. **Withdrawn later in this release** — see the entry
  above. Stored as `RolePermission.dataScope`, defaulting to All
  records (which is what every existing grant becomes), and merged widest-wins where two roles
  grant the same permission. **Nothing reads it yet**: no screen or AI tool narrows on it until the
  resolver in the next stage lands, so no access changes. Migration
  [20260906120000_role_permission_data_scope](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260906120000_role_permission_data_scope/migration.sql);
  the rule in [IAM.md — Scope](../project-state/IAM.md#scope).

- **Both employee forms now refuse a date of birth under eighteen, ask a married employee for their
  children, and record their qualifications and previous jobs as proper lists.** The public
  onboarding form and the HR employee form ask exactly the same things, so a candidate's answers
  stay editable on the record they create and a submission cannot pass one screen and fail the
  other. The calendar behind Date of Birth stops at the last day that still leaves somebody
  eighteen — those days are never offered — and a date that arrives another way (a submission HR
  sent back, an imported row, a record typed before the rule) is named on the field with how old it
  makes them; the schemas on both endpoints check it again, since the browser is never the guard.
  Saying "Married" reveals a **Children Details** list of name-and-date-of-birth rows, never
  required, and changing the status back takes the children with it the way it already took the
  spouse and the anniversary. **Qualification Details** and **Experience Details** are lists in a
  section of their own between Personal and Family: a qualification is a degree, its branch, the
  college and the year it finished, and a job is the company, where it was, the role, how many
  years, and the experience or relieving letter attached to it. Neither is required — a fresher has
  no jobs to list — and a row nobody filled in is dropped on save rather than complained about,
  while a row somebody started is asked for the part that makes it mean anything.
  Built as one component, not three: `RepeatableDetailsField` owns everything a repeating list
  shares and draws each row's cells through `SchemaFormField`, the renderer both forms already
  describe their fields to, so all three lists are field descriptions in `employeeDetailFields` and
  a fourth would cost a description rather than a component. `DateField`/`CalendarPanel` grew
  `maxDate`/`minDate` so any field can carry the bounds a rule puts on it rather than only "not in
  the past" and "not in the future". Each list is its own table — `employee_child`,
  `employee_qualification`, `employee_experience` — for the reason the salary reviews are one, and
  the whole list replaces the stored one on save; the letters go to document storage beside the
  separation letters, and are deleted again if the save they belong to fails. The repository's table
  and info panel show each list folded to a line, since neither can draw rows. No new routes and no
  new permission: all of it rides the guards `GET|POST|PATCH /api/hrm/employees` and the public
  onboarding token already answer to, so who could reach these screens is unchanged
  ([RepeatableDetailsField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/RepeatableDetailsField.js),
  [employeeDetailFields.js](../../frontend/src/constants/employeeDetailFields.js),
  [fieldValidation.js](../../frontend/src/constants/fieldValidation.js),
  [EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js),
  [employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js),
  [onboarding-invites/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/onboarding-invites/controller.js)).

- **Review Employee Details offers Save Draft, Send Back and Approve & Next.** Its save button was
  called **Update**, which named it as though it finished something: a review is read over more than
  one sitting, and what it does is keep the corrections and leave the submission under review. It is
  **Save Draft** now — the behaviour is unchanged, only the label was wrong. **Send Back** joins it,
  the same act the row menu already offered and asking the same confirmation, since it emails the
  candidate and reopens their form; it appears only where there is a submission to reopen, so an
  employee added by hand — who also starts on HR Onboarding Pending, with nothing behind them —
  does not get a button whose only answer is "nothing to send back". Sending back now closes the
  review panel and re-reads the list, since the submission has stopped being HR's to look at.
  **Approve & Next** is unchanged and is the only one wearing the primary colour, so the button that
  finishes the review is the one that looks like it.

- **The login username is now the employee's name joined by dots, and the login email is the
  employee's work email.** A username was the whole name with the employee id tacked on
  (`john.smith.dbs1`); it is `john.smith`, the name in the order it is written. A middle name or an
  initial keeps its place — `rajesh.kumar.nair`, and `dhanush.m.m` for a given name followed by
  initials — and a one-word name is used as it stands. **Two people sharing a name will now
  collide** — the id was what made the old form unique — and the API refuses
  the second with "A user already exists with this username" so whoever is onboarding picks another;
  the field is editable. The login email no longer falls back to the personal address, which quietly
  made somebody's private email their sign-in: it is the **Work Email ID** or nothing.

- **An employee with no work email recorded gets the one their login was created with.** Onboarding
  asks for the address on the Login Credentials page, and that address is written back as the
  employee's Work Email ID. Only where the field is empty — an employee who already has a work email
  keeps it, whatever the login was made with, since reconciling two addresses that disagree is not
  that call's business. Written after the credential email has actually gone out, not before: a
  failed send deletes the account and reports that nothing was created, and writing earlier would
  have filed the failed attempt's address as the work email and left a retry with a different address
  naming one thing on the record and another on the login. See
  [employeeName.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/employeeName.mjs) and `createLoginCredentials` in
  [employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js).

- **An employee is onboarded with their next salary review already scheduled, six months out.**
  **Next Review Date** is now a field in Personal Details, filled in six months from **Date of
  Joining** the moment one is entered and editable afterwards; recording a review proposes its own
  next date the same way, six months on. Every date is a suggestion — once somebody has typed their
  own, moving the date it was counted from leaves it alone rather than replacing a decision with a
  default. Month-ends clamp rather than roll, so 31 August plus six months is 28 February (29th in a
  leap year), not 3 March.

- **The review list moved to Edit, and splits into Upcoming and Past.** Add Employee and Review
  Employee no longer show it: neither is a moment at which somebody has salary reviews, and the empty
  list with its **+ Add Review** invited HR to invent history for a hire who had not started — they
  ask for Next Review Date instead, which is what onboarding needs. In **Edit** the list is two tabs
  split on the review's own date against today (today counts as past, since the review has been
  held), each carrying a count, Upcoming soonest-first and Past newest-first; opening a row fills the
  review dialog with its values. Once reviews exist the latest one's next date *is* the schedule, so
  the Personal Details field follows it and is shown but no longer editable — two editable answers to
  one question is two answers that can disagree.

- **Fixed on the way: a review date entered at onboarding was thrown away on save.** The server works
  the review fields out from the review rows and spreads them over what the form submitted, and with
  no reviews it returned a flat `null` for **Next Review Date** — so every newly onboarded employee
  was stored with no review scheduled, whatever had been entered. It now keeps the submitted date
  when there are no reviews to derive one from, and an edit that sends no reviews keeps the date the
  employee already had. See
  [employeeReviewSchedule.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/employeeReviewSchedule.mjs) and `reviewTotals` in
  [employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js).

- **Everybody arranges their own sidebar, and it follows them to every device.** Settings → User
  Preference → **Sidebar Layout** is where a person drags the modules into the
  order they want and drags
  the screens inside a module into the order they want within it. A screen never leaves its module:
  Quotations is reordered inside Sales & CRM and cannot be dropped into Project Management, on
  screen because each list is its own drag context and again on the server, which refuses to store
  an arrangement that says otherwise. Dragging is not the only way — every row also has up and down
  arrows, so the order can be set by tap on a phone and by keypress without a pointer. A drag held
  near the top or bottom of the panel scrolls it, so a module can be carried past the fold in one
  gesture instead of being dropped, scrolled to and picked up again — the same edge scroll the data
  table has for its columns, now reading either axis from one shared rule
  ([dragEdgeScroll.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/dragEdgeScroll.mjs), moved out of `components/data-table/`
  so both readers share it). Rearranging is a draft until **Save changes** is pressed, which is
  offered only once something has actually moved and goes quiet again the moment it is saved or
  dragged back to where it started: the sidebar does not change under somebody still deciding, and
  leaving the section with an unsaved draft asks first. The
  arrangement is saved against the account rather than the browser, so signing in on a second
  machine, another browser or the desktop app brings the same sidebar; one person's arrangement
  says nothing about anybody else's, and **Reset to Default** puts it back. The appearance moves the
  same way without moving house: the theme is still chosen from the account menu on the top bar
  where it always has been, but it is now remembered against the account rather than the browser, so
  it follows the person too — the cookie stays, because it is what paints the right palette on the
  first frame before any of this is read.
  Permissions still decide what is in the list — a screen a role cannot open is absent from the
  sidebar and from the editor, and reordering grants nothing. A module or screen added to the
  application later appears in everybody's saved layout automatically, in its default position,
  without disturbing the order they set and without anybody pressing Reset; one they lack permission
  for is left out until the permission is granted, and one whose permission is taken away is hidden
  while keeping its place, so getting it back puts the screen where they left it. There is one
  canonical sidebar registry behind all of it —
  ([navigation.mjs](../../frontend/src/constants/navigation.mjs), holding each entry's id, label,
  route, icon and module) — read by the drawer, by the editor and by Role Management's labels, with
  the merge itself in [sidebarLayout.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/sidebarLayout.mjs) and
  [useSidebarLayout.js](../../frontend/src/components/preferences/useSidebarLayout.js). The server
  keeps a structural mirror of it purely to validate what it is asked to store
  ([sidebarStructure.js](../../backend/src/modules/user/preferences/sidebarStructure.js)), and a test
  fails if the two ever disagree about an id. No migration and no new permission: the layout is one
  more row in the existing `user_preference` table, behind the existing self-service API, where
  `userId` comes from the token and never from the request. Settings is reached the way it always
  was, and the studio's own settings did not open up: an employee opening that overlay would see
  User Preference and nothing else.

- **A quotation or CRD document keeps the logo it was issued with.** The Global Logo is copied
  onto each document's own record the moment it is created — every quotation version, including
  one raised by editing or duplicating, and every CRD link — and is never updated after that.
  Changing Settings → Global Logo later rebrands the sign-in page, the app bar and the client
  gallery at once, but a quotation a client was already sent, and the PDF printed from it, still
  show the mark they were sent with; only documents created after the change carry the new one.
  Documents that predate this carry no snapshot and show the bundled default mark, never whichever
  logo is configured today. One shared helper does both halves for any document type
  ([documentBranding.js](../../backend/src/services/documentBranding.js); `logo_url` on
  `sales_quotation` and `sales_project_crd_document`,
  [migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260905100000_document_logo_snapshot/migration.sql)),
  and the pages render it through `DocumentLogo`, which never reads Settings
  ([AppLogo.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/AppLogo.js),
  [PublicQuotationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicQuotationPage.js),
