  [PublicCrdDocumentForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicCrdDocumentForm.js)). No new
  permission: the field rides on the endpoints each document already had.

- **The sidebar has a search box at the top.** Typing in it narrows the navigation to the screens
  and sections whose names match, opening every group that still has a hit and saying so when
  nothing does; clearing it, or closing the drawer, restores the full list with each group's own
  collapsed state. Only screens the role may already open are searched
  ([AppSidebar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppSidebar.js)).
- **Converted History finds a client's bookings by their number, not by the enquiry id.** The tab
  matched projects on `enquiry_id`, which answers a narrower question than it asks — not "what has
  this client bought" but "what was booked through this exact enquiry row". A booking raised against
  another of the client's enquiries, a project whose `enquiry_id` was imported or corrected, or a
  lead whose id was reissued after a deletion all came out the same way: "No project has been created
  from this lead yet" for a client who plainly has one. It now matches on the contact number, folded
  to its last ten digits — the same rule the Contacts directory folds a person by and the duplicate
  check compares — so "+91 4786958704" on the project and "47869-58704" on the lead are one client
  ([crmLeadService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/crmLeadService.js)).

- **A project in Converted History opens as a record.** The tab named what a lead became and stopped
  there — nine summary columns, and reading what was actually sold meant leaving the lead, opening
  Sales Manager and finding that Event ID again by hand, with the popup closed behind you.
  Double-clicking a row now opens the project in the same record view every project table uses:
  identity, client and branch, event planning, deliverables, the WhatsApp group and the timestamps.
  Enter or Space opens it too, because a double click cannot be made from a keyboard. It is the
  **sale's** reading of a project, not Project Management's — the workflow (shoots, service
  allocations, post production) and Accounts' payments are not offered here, since this tab answers
  the CRM's permission rather than theirs ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [crmLeadService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/crmLeadService.js)).

- **Employee Attendance is an entry in the calendar's own switch.** Alongside All, Projects, Pre
  Production, Post Production and Meetings — so a month can be read as attendance alone, and the List
  view beside it becomes a register: employee, date, status, hours worked, whether the day was
  measured or marked, and the note. Offered to everybody rather than gated on a permission, because
  a person's own attendance is their own record and the server already decides the scope — their own
  days, or the studio's for HRM. Attendance is deliberately left out of the **All table**: it is one
  row per person per day, so a fortnight of a dozen people is three hundred rows that would bury the
  handful of bookings the list is read for. The calendar still shows it under All, where a chip in a
  day cell costs nothing ([meetingSources.js](../../frontend/src/lib/meetingSources.js)).

- **Employee Attendance is a real register now, and it reaches the calendar.** The screen was a
  placeholder with empty tables and nothing behind it. Daily Attendance shows who was here, per
  person per day: when they were first and last seen, the hours worked, and why the day reads as it
  does. Nobody signs in or out — an ordinary day is **derived** from what the application already
  measures, the same heartbeat the Work Hours report reads, so nothing has to be entered for it. A
  register that needed a hundred rows written every week would be right in its first week and wrong
  ever after. Marking a day writes it down and overrides the measurement, which is how approved
  leave, a public holiday, a half day or a shoot with no laptop open get recorded, and the Source
  column says which of the two answered — the difference between a register that records and one
  that asserts. Everybody sees and marks their own days; `HRM` / `Employee Management` sees the
  studio's and may correct anybody's, checked against the id in the request rather than the caller.
  **The same days are drawn on the calendar** beside the shoots and meetings, present *and* absent —
  "who is not in" is asked far more often than "who is". Each chip leads with the person's name and
  is coloured by what it says: red for absent, blue for leave, quiet slate for an ordinary day in.
  Somebody who can only see their own attendance sees their own; somebody who can see the studio's
  sees everyone else's and **not** their own, since for them it is a monitoring view and their own
  row on every day is the one answer they already know. Attendance sorts last within a day, so it
  fills what is left of a cell rather than pushing a shoot or a meeting out of the three a cell
  shows
  ([hrm/attendance](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/),
  [schedule/calendar/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/schedule/calendar/service.js),
  [EmployeeAttendancePage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/EmployeeAttendancePage.js)).

  **This adds a table**, so `npx prisma migrate deploy` is needed on the VPS and `npx prisma migrate
  dev` locally — `20260905120000_employee_attendance`.

- **Escape now closes what is on top, everywhere in the application.** Every overlay - dialogs,
  full-screen forms, dropdowns, calendars, context menus, the settings overlay, the chat panel -
  used to listen for the key itself, which produced two faults that are opposite sides of one
  missing idea. Nested things all fired at once: a dropdown inside a dialog closed the list *and*
  the dialog under it, taking the half-filled form with it. And the overlays nobody had written a
  listener for - Add Lead, the lead popup, Update Details, the delete and convert confirmations, the
  import dialog, the meetings drawer - ignored the key entirely, on the screens people spend the
  most time in. There is one arbiter now and a stack: whatever opened most recently is what Escape
  reaches, and closing it hands the key to the next one down. Anything mid-save, mid-import or
  mid-delete is off the stack rather than swallowing the key, since that work is not cancellable by
  looking away, and an inline cell edit still claims the key for itself. Closing an overlay also
  returns focus to whatever opened it, which matters more now that the keyboard can close things
  ([useEscapeKey.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/useEscapeKey.js)).

- **The topbar bell is the whole application now, not only meetings.** It shows what is waiting on
  the person signed in: follow-ups due today or already late, clients who replied on WhatsApp and
  have not been answered, calls they took that are still nobody's enquiry, and approvals waiting on
  their decision — alongside the day's meetings, which is what it used to be alone. Each source is
  read only if the viewer holds that module's own permission, so the bell can never show somebody
  something they are refused everywhere else, and it is offered to every role rather than to CRM
  roles alone — an approver with no CRM access still has approvals, and under the old gate they were
  shown a bell that could never say so. Every row goes to where the work is done. Sources are a
  registry, so the next one is an entry rather than a change to the bell
  ([notifications/sources.js](../../backend/src/modules/notifications/sources.js),
  [MeetingNotifications.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/MeetingNotifications.js)).

- **Reports & Analytics has a Work Hours tab: the studio's timesheet.** A row per person per day —
  when they started, when they finished, the hours actually worked, the idle time between them, the
  length of the day and how many sittings it took. It reads the same measured heartbeat sessions
  Application Usage does, so the hours are time at *this* application rather than the gaps between
  somebody's saved records: a tab in the background, or one nobody is touching, was never recorded
  and cannot be counted. Application Usage answers "how long over the range" in one number per
  person; this answers "which days, and how long on each". Worked, Idle and Day Span are added up on
  the server so a row cannot disagree with itself, all three sort by time rather than by the words
  they print, and a stretch running through midnight is counted against the day it began — the same
  way Application Usage files it, so the two tabs always agree
  ([service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-reports/service.js),
  [crmReports.js](../../frontend/src/lib/crmReports.js)).

- **Adding or editing a lead now warns when the phone number is already an enquiry, and names it.**
  One phone number is one enquiry: the CRM's idea of "the same client" is the last ten digits of
  their number — it is what the Callyzer webhook matches a delivered call on, what the Call Logs tab
  gathers a history by, and what the lead popup counts as "Enquired : 2 times" — so a second lead on
  a number the studio already holds is the same person entered twice, with their calls, remarks and
  follow-ups split across two records that neither knows about. Nothing said so at the point it
  mattered. Add Lead now checks as the number is typed, showing the Enquiry ID under the Contact
  field, and again on Confirm — the second catches a number pasted in and confirmed before the first
  could answer. The lead popup checks when a number is *edited* onto one another lead has; a lead
  that has always shared a number is left alone, since its header already says so. The warning offers
  the existing enquiry by name and opens it where you are. Going ahead anyway is possible and
  deliberate: numbers are shared more often than the rule allows for — a groom and a bride on one
  phone, a family, an office line — and refusing outright would make those records unsaveable in a
  form somebody had opened to fix something else
  ([DuplicateEnquiryDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/DuplicateEnquiryDialog.js),
  [crm-lead-duplicates.service.js](../../frontend/src/services/sales/crm-lead-duplicates.service.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

- **Every table now remembers how you arranged it, on whatever device you sign in from.** The order
  you drag columns into, the ones you hide in Manage Columns, how many you freeze, how wide you pull
  them, Compact view, and the tab each screen was left on are saved against your **login** instead
  of the browser you happened to use - arrange the Employee Repository at the desk and it is
  waiting, arranged, on the laptop. This also closes two faults in the old behaviour: the
  arrangement was lost whenever browser site data was cleared, and because the browser's keys were
  not namespaced by user, the next person to sign in on a shared machine inherited the previous
  one's layout. A new `user_preference` table holds one row per preference per login
  ([migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260905140000_user_preferences/migration.sql)), reached
  through `GET|PUT|DELETE /api/user/preferences` - self-service beside My Profile, guarded by
  `protect` alone with **no module permission**, since a layout belongs to whoever arranged it and
  no role should be refused the right to remember its own screen. No role needs re-saving for this.
  The browser keeps a cache, namespaced by user id, so a table still draws in the right order on the
  first frame. ([PreferencesProvider.js](../../frontend/src/components/preferences/PreferencesProvider.js))

- **Settings → Referrals now shows the referrals themselves, not just what one is worth.** The
  amount was the only half that had been built: the instalments were recorded against every referral
  and the endpoints to read and settle them were live and guarded, but nothing in the application
  ever called them, so what the studio owed could only be seen in the database
  ([ReferralRegister.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/ReferralRegister.js)). The register
  sits under the figure that produced it — who was referred and by whom, both instalments with their
  due dates and amounts, and what is still outstanding across the list. **Outstanding / Paid / All**
  filters it, an instalment past its date reads **overdue**, and **Mark paid** settles one for anyone
  holding `ACCOUNTS / Payroll Management` at Read / Write. Marking paid is one-way by design: it says
  money left the studio, and the server refuses the reversal, so a correction is an Accounts entry
  rather than a dropdown. Read-only holders see the list and no buttons.

- **Role Management has an Administrator tick that grants every module at once.** Building an admin
  role meant setting a dozen modules to Read / Write one at a time, and missing one was silent.
  **Action permissions** now opens with **Administrator — grant every module at Read / Write**;
  ticking it settles every module, unticking clears them, and each can still be changed individually
  afterwards. It is not a second kind of access: it writes the same permission rows the dropdowns
  write, and they apply in the branches chosen on the role and nowhere else — an administrator of
  Erode is an administrator of Erode. The tick is read back from the permissions rather than stored
  beside them, so a role that has since had one module lowered stops calling itself an administrator
  instead of saying one thing and granting another ([RoleDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/admin/RoleDialog.js)).

- **The project register now shows a person the projects they are actually on.** Every signed-in
  reader of the project screens used to be served every project in the studio; a photographer could
  read a wedding they were not shooting. A project is now shown to the five people it names - the
  CRM, the project manager, the pre- and post-production coordinators and the creative manager -
  matched on their employee id rather than their name, plus whoever created it. The new
  **Project / All Projects** permission widens that to every project, and is meant for an admin or a
  project coordinator: grant it in Admin > Role Management. It widens *who*, never *where* - a role
  scoped to Erode is answered with Erode whether or not it holds the permission, and a role reaching
  no branch is shown nothing rather than everything. A Super Admin is filtered by nothing
  ([projectScope.js](../../backend/src/services/projectScope.js)).

  **This permission is new, so reseed** (`npm run seed`) and then grant it to the roles that need it
  — no role holds it until somebody does. Until then only a Super Admin sees the whole register.
  Covers the Project Management screens and Sales Manager, which read the same endpoint; CRD
  Documents and Image Selection read their own and are not yet scoped.

- **`npm run audit:branches` says which stored branch names are real ones.** The branch on a project,
  a quotation, a lead or a cash entry is free text rather than a reference to the branch list, and
  the studio has been writing it two ways — `Erode` and `Erode TN` are the same place. The script is
  read-only, groups every distinct value per table, and either names the location it maps to or says
  what it nearly matched, so the columns can be turned into real references without a backfill
  quietly dropping the rows it could not place
  ([auditBranchValues.js](../../backend/scripts/auditBranchValues.js)).

- **A task given the type "Other" now asks what kind of task it actually was, and the list shows the
  answer.** "Other" is the one entry on the type list that describes nothing, so a studio that used
  it had a Type column reading "Other" against work nobody could tell apart. Choosing it now reveals
  a **What Kind of Task** box beside the dropdown, required while it is showing, and the list and the
  info panel read "Other — Vendor coordination" rather than the bare word. Changing the type away
  empties the box, so a detail typed and then thought better of is never stored against a type that
  would not show it, and the server refuses a bare "Other" on its own account rather than trusting
  the form to have asked. Existing tasks keep the type they were given and leave the new field empty
  ([taskConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/taskConfig.js),
  [service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/tasks/service.js)).

- **Every records table can import a spreadsheet, the way Leads & Followups always could.** Import
  sits in a **⋮ beside the page's own button** — where Leads & Followups has always had it, and
  where people look for it — and in the table's own ⋮ as well, on **Employee Repository, Asset
  Management, Vendor Management, Client Storage, Accounts, Bookings and Projects**. Download the template, fill in one row per record, upload it: columns may be in any
  order, columns the table does not have are ignored, and a file missing a required column is
  refused by name before a single row is sent. Rows are validated one at a time by *the module's own
  schema*, so a file of two hundred with three bad rows imports the other hundred and ninety-seven
  and names the three by their row number and the column they failed on. An imported record is
  written by the module's own creator — same identifier counter, same branch permission check, same
  audit columns — so it is indistinguishable from one typed into the form
  Bookings and Project Management's Projects are the same record, so both screens offer the same
  import; a booking's shoots are added afterwards, since a variable number of them cannot flatten
  into one spreadsheet line
  ([tableImport.js](../../backend/src/services/tableImport.js),
  [ImportDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/ImportDialog.js),
  [importColumns.js](../../frontend/src/constants/importColumns.js)).

- **Qua Qua newborns and birthdays come back to Yet to connect on their own, and are worked there.**
  A Qua Qua lead has two calls that come round without anybody scheduling them: a **newborn** window
  around the estimated delivery date (30 days before to 5 days after) and a **birthday** window
  before the child's next birthday (2 to 45 days out). Either one opening now lists the lead in Yet
  to connect as well as in its own tab, and setting a status there takes it out — so the tab is a
  queue of calls still to make. That status is kept against the occasion rather than against the
  lead, so the last shoot keeps the status it ended on, and next year's birthday opens with nothing
  against it and returns to Yet to connect by itself. While a window is open the Status column, the
  CNA count and the follow-up date on that lead are the occasion's, which is what the Converted,
  Rejected and Pending Followups tabs then count it under; the Newborns and Birthdays tabs still
  list everything in their window whatever its status. The two windows can never both be open on one
  lead, so a lead always reads exactly one status. Reachable by anyone who already has SALES → CRM
  Leads (`READ` to see it, `READ_WRITE` to set a status) — no new permission, no new route
  ([occasionLeadService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/occasionLeadService.js),
  [leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

- **Onboarding a new employee is one flow with two pages, not two buttons leading to unrelated
  screens.** Employee Repository's separate "Add Employee" and "Create Onboarding Invite" buttons
  are now one **Onboard Employee** button, opening a chooser with a left rail naming the two ways
  in — send the candidate a link to fill in themselves, or enter their record directly. Add
  Employee Directly and a reviewed onboarding submission both now carry a **Login Credentials**
  page in the same panel as **Review Employee Details**: saving (**Save & Next** / **Approve &
  Next**) moves the panel on to it, and once the employee is saved the rail lets either page be
  opened directly — so adding somebody and then hunting for their row to give them a login is one
  job again ([EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js),
  page in the same panel as **Review Employee Details**, rather than closing into a separate
  popup: saving (**Save & Next** / **Approve & Next**) moves the panel on to it, and once the
  employee is saved the rail lets either page be opened directly. A back arrow (now available on
  any `Dialog`, not just `SchemaFormPanel`) returns from either flow's own form to the chooser
  ([EmployeeOnboardingChoicePanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeOnboardingChoicePanel.js),
  [EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js),
  [useCredentialForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/useCredentialForm.js),
  [SchemaFormSectionsBody.js](../../frontend/src/components/schema-form/SchemaFormSectionsBody.js),
  [Dialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/ui/Dialog.js)). `EmployeeCredentialDialogs.js` is
  removed — its logic now lives in `useCredentialForm.js`, shared by the embedded Login
  Credentials page. **Add Employee** and **Create Onboarding Invite** stay two buttons on the
  Employee Repository, as they were: which of the two to use is a decision HR has already made
  before reaching the screen, and a chooser standing between the button and the form told nobody
  anything they did not know. `EmployeeOnboardingChoicePanel.js` is removed with it; the back arrow
  it needed remains available on any `Dialog`.
  Credentials page.

- **The Org Chart tab reads like the studio's chart, not a single reporting line.** Below the
  employee's own card it now also shows **who they work with** — everyone else sharing their
  manager — beside the reports and the chain above them it already drew. Each card carries their
  branch (Work Base Location) and, where they have signed in before, their username, alongside the
  designation and department it already showed; there is still no "View profile" button, since the
  point of every card here is to be clicked into the next person's chart. A reporting line more
  than one level up used to stack a full card per manager and push the person the tab was opened on
  off the bottom of the screen — it now collapses into a row of clickable faces with a
  **Show N more** to expand them, so the whole chart from top to bottom stays reachable from the one
  view no matter how deep it runs
  ([EmployeeOrgChart.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeOrgChart.js),
  [org-chart/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/org-chart/service.js),
  [org-chart/repository.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/org-chart/repository.js)).

- **Every table's columns can be dragged into a new order, and it stays that way.** Drag a header
  left or right past its neighbours and drop it there — a thin blue bar shows which side of the
  target column it will land on. The order is remembered per table (keyed by the table's own name,
  the way a remembered tab already is) and survives a reload; a table's own ⋮ menu gains a
  **Reset Column Order** entry once something has actually been dragged, and disappears again once
  it is used. On by default everywhere, the same way sorting and filtering already are, so nothing
  else changes for a page that never asks for it. A fixed column — a row's expand chevron, a
  table's own ⋮ actions — never moves, and a column added to a table later joins at the end of
  whatever order was last dragged rather than displacing it. The gesture is native HTML5 drag and
  drop, so it does not yet answer to a touch drag on a phone the way column resizing does
  ([DataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js),
  [DataTableMoreMenu.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTableMoreMenu.js)).

- **Send Back has its own email, instead of reusing the onboarding invite.** Employee Repository's
  Send Back reopened a submitted onboarding under the same link but emailed the candidate the exact
  "Complete Your Onboarding" invite a second time — which reads as a mistake, or as the form having
  lost what they already typed. It now sends a distinct template that says plainly HR reviewed their
  details and a few need another look, keeping the same link, deadline and what-to-have-ready list
  ([emailTemplates.js](../../backend/src/services/emailTemplates.js),
  [emailService.js](../../backend/src/services/emailService.js),
  [onboarding-invites/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/onboarding-invites/controller.js)).

- **The W/A Group-Name Generator asks whether the event date is known before asking for it.** An
  **Main Event Date Known** toggle sits where Main Event Date used to, and the date is only asked for
  when it is on. The field previously insisted on a date and told people to "enter approximate date
  if not known", which put a guessed day into a group name that then travelled to WhatsApp as fact.
  With it off the generated name simply carries no date — and drops the separator with it, so a name
  never ends in a dangling `|`. It starts off, matching how Create Project already records
  `mainEventDateKnown`. With it on, the date sits in the space to the toggle's right rather than
  under it, dropping to its own line only where that space runs out
  ([WhatsappGroupNameDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/WhatsappGroupNameDialog.js),
  [whatsappGroupNameUtils.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/whatsappGroupNameUtils.js),
  [whatsapp-group-names/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/whatsapp-group-names/controller.js)).
  No migration: `main_event_date` was already nullable.

- **A quotation is now versioned, and every version keeps its own five-day link.** Sales →
  Quotation raises `QUT039-01` for an enquiry, and every edit after that raises the next version of
  the same chain — `QUT039-02`, `QUT039-03` — rather than rewriting what a client has already been
  sent. Versions are grouped by enquiry: quoting again for an enquiry that already has a chain adds
  to it instead of starting a second one. **Edit (new version)** opens the generator with a
  **Version** dropdown listing every version priced, so the next one can be raised from any of them,
  and **Version history** shows the whole chain with what each version was quoted at. Each version's
  public link opens for five days and then stops; raising a new version does **not** kill the links
  already sent, so a client can still compare the v1 they were given with the v2 that followed. A
  lapsed link is re-enabled for five more days from the share dialog or the version history — the
  same URL, a fresh clock — and the client's page says the link has expired rather than showing them
  a not-found error. The register lists one row per chain, showing the current version, how many
  versions have been raised, and whether its link is live; a chain with more than one version carries
  a **chevron** at the start of its row, and opening it lists the earlier versions right there as
  rows of the register — tinted, indented and ruled down the chevron's column so what was opened is
  never mistaken for more records — each with the same **⋮** menu, so any version can be viewed,
  edited, duplicated or deleted without leaving the table — the menu reads **View**, **Edit**,
  **Versions** (only on a chain that has more than one), **Duplicate** and **Delete**. **Duplicate**,
  on a register row, an expanded version or any version in the history, copies a version onto a
  different enquiry — the shoots, services, deliverables and rates come across and only the enquiry changes,
  and the copy lands as that enquiry's next version with a five-day link of its own while the version
  copied from is left alone. It asks one question, the enquiry, and offers the CRM's ids from all
  three verticals at once — minus the enquiry the quotation is already on
  ([projects/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js),
  [QuotationDuplicateDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationDuplicateDialog.js),
  [DataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js),
  [QuotationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationPage.js),
  [QuotationVersionsDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationVersionsDialog.js),
  [20260903000000_quotation_versions](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260903000000_quotation_versions/migration.sql)).
  Access is unchanged: `SALES / Quotation Management`, `READ` to look and read the history,
  `READ_WRITE` to raise a version or re-enable a link — no reseed is needed.
- **CRD Documents can be created for a client before their project exists.** A Create CRD Document
  button in the header opens a dialog that asks for the vertical and the client - Groom and Bride
  names for Dum Dum, Client Name for Qua Qua - plus a contact number and an optional Enquiry ID,
  and issues that client's own public link on the spot. Every client gets a new link, unlike Send
  Link on a project row, which keeps handing back the one the client is already holding. The
  document appears in the table straight away with no Event ID until a project is started
  ([CreateCrdDocumentDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CreateCrdDocumentDialog.js),
  [crd-documents/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/service.js)).

- **A vertical picker sits beside the CRD Documents heading.** Dum Dum or Qua Qua - the table always
  shows one of the two verticals that collect this document. It narrows the table first and the
  status tabs count within it, so All / Submitted / Yet To Receive / Link Not sent always describe
  what is on screen. The vertical is a column on the table and a field in the Info panel too
  ([CrdDocumentsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CrdDocumentsPage.js)).

- **With no Global Name set, the application names itself.** The top bar and the sign-in page
  show Beam Craft beside the logo where Settings -> Global Logo has been left without a name,
  instead of a mark with a gap beside it. A Global Name the studio has typed is always used in
  preference ([BrandFallbackLogo.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/BrandFallbackLogo.js)).
- **The name beside the logo is the studio's own, or nothing.** The top bar and the sign-in page
  write the Global Name from Settings -> Global Logo; left empty there, they write nothing at all
  rather than a name the studio never typed. The field's own placeholder says what to type instead
  of showing another studio's name. The bundled *mark* still stands in where no Global Logo has been
  uploaded — that is a picture of something, and a blank is worse; a name nobody gave is not
  ([BrandFallbackLogo.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/BrandFallbackLogo.js)).

- **A bundled mark stands in wherever no Global Logo has been set.** The top bar, the public
  quotation and CRD pages and all four places the client gallery shows a logo used to render
  nothing at all until a studio uploaded one, leaving a gap where the brand goes. They now show
  the Beam Craft wordmark instead, in whichever of its two files suits the theme: the black
  lettering on the light ground it was drawn for, the white lettering on Dark and Enhanced Dark.
  Both files are in the page and CSS picks one from the theme already on the document, so the
  right mark is painted on the first frame and follows the appearance picker as it changes
  ([BrandFallbackLogo.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/BrandFallbackLogo.js),
  [StudioLogo.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/StudioLogo.js),
  [GalleryBrand.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/client-gallery/GalleryBrand.js)).

- **The signed-in person's photo sits beside their name in the top bar.** A round avatar leads the
  user control on every screen: the linked employee's photo from the Employee Repository where
  there is one, otherwise the photo kept on My Profile, and initials on a fixed colour until
  either exists. Saving a photo on My Profile changes the bar at once, and on a phone the face
  stands in for the name ([AppTopbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppTopbar.js),
  [EmployeeAvatar.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/EmployeeAvatar.js)).

- **The inactivity timeout is a setting, and it defaults to three minutes.** Settings now has an
  **Application Monitoring** section under Studio Branding with a **Usage Timeout** field, in
  minutes. It was a constant compiled into the browser bundle at two minutes, so changing it was a
  deploy and open tabs disagreed with each other until they were reloaded. It is one row on the
  server now: every browser takes it from the reply to its own heartbeat, so a change reaches every
  signed-in tab within a beat or two without anybody reloading. Bounded 1–240 minutes — below two
  heartbeats a stretch would end between one beat and the next and record continuous work as
  fragments. Readable by anyone signed in (every browser needs it to know when to stop counting),
  writable behind `ADMIN / System Settings`, the same permission Studio Branding answers to. The
  field is a number input with the browser's own steppers, bounded to the range the server enforces,
  and Save is dead while it is empty — clearing the box is how you start typing a new number, not a
  request for no timeout at all.
  ([ApplicationMonitoringPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/ApplicationMonitoringPanel.js),
  [usage/settings](../../backend/src/modules/usage/settings/service.js))

- **Field Settings is its own section, after Table Settings.** The two were one entry: Table
  Settings' Module dropdown offered Human Resource Management, and picking it swapped the field
  builder for a list editor while hiding the Preview, Cancel and Save buttons that had nothing to do
  with it — two different jobs behind one name, one of them borrowing the other's frame. Table
  Settings builds **new fields** onto a vertical's form; **Field Settings** edits the **answers an
  existing field offers** — the designations an employee can hold, the departments there are — and
  saves each entry as it is typed, so it has no Save of its own. It keeps the same two-picker flow
  (Module, then Field), and HRM is now flagged as having no table settings, so Table Settings stops
  offering a module it cannot configure.
  ([FieldSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/FieldSettingsPanel.js),
  [TableSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/TableSettingsPanel.js))

- **Two things in Settings are called what the rest of the application calls them.** "Sales
  Integrations" is now **Integrations**, and the entry under it is **Sales** — the group names what
  these are and the leaf names the part of the application they feed, rather than both saying
  "Leads" from different directions. In Table Settings the Module picker now offers **Leads &
  Followups** rather than "Leads", matching the sidebar and the board's own heading. The stored
  value behind it is still `crm`, which is what every saved custom field is keyed by.
  ([SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js),
  [TableSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/TableSettingsPanel.js))

- **Reports & Analytics → Application Usage has an Inactive Time Track column.** Beside Usage, the
  time somebody was signed in and idle, in hours and minutes. A stretch of use ends when they stop
  touching anything for the usage timeout and a new one begins when they come back, so the gaps
  between a day's stretches are that figure — the span of the day's use, less the time actually
  spent in it, never below zero. Summed **per day** rather than across the range: taken across a
  month the span would run from the first morning to the last evening and count every night and
  weekend as idle. Sorts on the seconds like Usage does, so "46m" files below "2h 53m".
  ([crmReports.js](../../frontend/src/lib/crmReports.js),
  [crm-reports/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-reports/service.js))

- **Fixed: Application Usage was counting time spent in the WhatsApp chat panel twice.** The two
  usage contexts run *concurrently*, not one after the other — the app shell beats as `app` for the
  whole session and the chat panel beats as `whatsapp` alongside it while it is open — so an hour in
  the chat produced an hour in each, and the report summed both. Usage read high, and the new idle
  figure read low for the same reason, since idle is the span *minus* the active time. The report
  now reads the `app` context alone, which already covers the chat because the shell's heartbeat
  never stops while the panel is open; WhatsApp Usage still reads its own context to answer the
  narrower question. ([crm-reports/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-reports/service.js))

- **The whole application was being slowed by one poll.** Every open tab asks
  `GET /api/sales/crm-leads/version` every four seconds to notice when lead data changes, and that
  read was `COUNT(*) + MAX(updated_at)` with no index covering it — **3,450 ms at 500,000 leads,
  per poll, per tab**. Five people signed in was five full scans of the lead table every four
  seconds, which is why every other module felt slow too. A covering index takes it to 248 ms and
  the answer is now cached briefly on the server, so a room full of people costs one query rather
  than one each. The duplicate-phone lookup behind the Callyzer webhook and the lead form went the
  same way: 1,622 ms → **19 ms** on the new `phone_key` index.
  ([migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260903130000_lead_version_index/migration.sql))

- **Leads & Followups is fast at half a million leads.** Measured on a seeded 500,000-lead database,
  before and after, with the same script (`node scripts/benchLeadBoard.js`):

  | | before | after |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | first page of a board (switching vertical) | 4,098 ms | **81 ms** |
  | the twelve tab counts | 6,337 ms | **855 ms** |
  | a page reached by scrolling (offset 50,000) | 7,500 ms | **559 ms** |
  | Today Pending Followups | 5,513 ms | **265 ms** |
  | Qua Qua Newborns | 5,478 ms | **338 ms** |
  | the Calls board | 3,886 ms | **99 ms** |
  | all of the above together | 62,027 ms | **8,241 ms** |

  Every lead field is TEXT written by four different producers, so the board normalised as it read —
  `LOWER(REPLACE(TRIM(branch)))` to decide a vertical, `STR_TO_DATE` to compare a follow-up date.
  A function wrapped around a column has to be evaluated for every row before anything can be
  decided, so no index could be used and **every** board query scanned the whole table — and the
  board ran three of them on every request. Six STORED generated columns now do that normalisation
  once, on write (`board_key`, `status_key`, `created_day`, `follow_up_day`, `dob_edod_day`, plus
  `phone_key`/`contact_key` for duplicate lookups), behind one wide covering index. `board_key` is
  NULL for a deleted lead, so the queries no longer say `deleted_at IS NULL` — while they did, MySQL
  kept choosing the `deleted_at` index and counting one board took 3.5s instead of 62ms, and neither
  a hint nor ANALYZE would talk it out of it. **Switching vertical is now under a second.**
  ([migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260903120000_lead_board_performance_keys/migration.sql),
  [leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js))

- **Scrolling the lead board no longer re-reads everything already on screen.** A refresh used to
  re-fetch every loaded row in 500-row chunks, one request after the next — at 50,000 rows that is
  100 sequential round trips and roughly 100 MB, and the four-second version poll fired it whenever
  anything changed, including your own edit. That is what made the board "load for minutes" and
  never settle; it now reads one page. A scroll append also tells the server it is an append, so the
  total and the twelve tab counts — neither of which can have changed — are skipped rather than
  recomputed per page. ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/controller.js))

- **A table of 50,000 rows no longer puts 50,000 listeners on the document.** `OptionsMenu`
  registered its click-outside handler whether or not it was open, so every row of the CRM board
  held one and every click anywhere in the application ran all of them, each walking the DOM twice.
  It is now registered only while a menu is actually open — one listener, because only one can be.
  Alongside it, the two row callbacks that were rebuilt on every render (`onOpen`, `onContextMenu`)
  are stable again, so `memo()` works and typing one character in a filter box stops re-rendering
  every loaded row; and two linear scans of the whole loaded set per render are memoised. The code
  justifying those as "the React Compiler memoises it" was wrong — it is not enabled in this build.
  ([OptionsMenu.js](../../frontend/src/components/ui/OptionsMenu.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js))

- **The board's speed can be measured rather than argued about.**
  `node scripts/seedLeadsForPerf.js` fills the lead table with 500,000 synthetic leads spread across
  the verticals, statuses and two years of dates (`--remove` deletes them; they carry a `PERF-EID-`
  prefix the application cannot issue), and `node scripts/benchLeadBoard.js` times the nine queries
  the board actually runs. Run it before and after a change and the difference is the answer.
  ([seedLeadsForPerf.js](../../backend/scripts/seedLeadsForPerf.js),
  [benchLeadBoard.js](../../backend/scripts/benchLeadBoard.js))

- **The Callyzer flow can be tried end to end without waiting for a real call.**
  `npm run test:callyzer -- --test-leads` delivers a fixture of eleven calls through the real
  webhook: one per decision it makes — a vertical named on the call, a vertical read from the note,
  one read from a label, a repeat caller who rings twice, a missed call with no disposition, and two
  that name no vertical at all and land on the **Calls** board. Three more are set aside as the
  webhook should: a colleague's number, a call labelled Personal, and one with no number. The sender
  prints the expected outcome per number so the CRM can be read against it, and checks the receipt's
  counts — which count *calls*, not leads, since the repeat caller is one lead with two calls.
  Every number begins `99000`, which no Indian mobile does, so it can never collide with a real
  client and `npm run remove:callyzer-test-leads -- --apply` can find its rows again with certainty
  (leads soft-deleted, call logs removed outright). Delivering to anything but this machine needs an
  explicit `--url … --confirm`, because a webhook delivery creates leads the sales team will see.
  ([callyzerTestBatch.js](../../backend/scripts/callyzerTestBatch.js),
  [testCallyzerWebhook.js](../../backend/scripts/testCallyzerWebhook.js),
  [removeCallyzerTestLeads.js](../../backend/scripts/removeCallyzerTestLeads.js))

- **Leads & Followups has a Calls board, and an untriaged call is no longer filed as a wedding.**
  Callyzer delivers a call, the webhook makes a lead of it, and the router reads a vertical off the
  call's labels, its note, the employee whose phone took it and the saved contact name. When none of
  those answer it refuses to guess — and a blank vertical read as **Dum Dum**, so every call it
  could not place landed in the wedding board beside real wedding enquiries. Those calls now wait on
  a fourth board in the same switch: **Calls**, with its own columns (caller, number, call date and
  time, who attended it, status, remarks) and the tabs that mean something before a vertical is
  known — All, Yet to connect, Today New Leads — each with its count. Double-clicking a call opens
  the **Add Lead** form on everything the call already knows, filled in and editable, with the
  Vertical radio above it to move the caller to whichever of the three they turn out to be. Confirm
  writes that vertical onto **that same lead** rather than creating a second one for a number the CRM
  already holds — so the call log, the transcript and the "Enquired N times" count all stay with it.
  The row then leaves Calls for the vertical it was given. Scoped to Callyzer deliberately: a blank
  vertical on a sheet import or on a typed lead still reads as Dum Dum exactly as it did. Calls
  cannot be a CSV import target and cannot be assigned to a lead — it is a board, not a vertical.
  Needs `SALES / CRM Leads` `READ` to see and `READ_WRITE` to place a call, the same permission the
  rest of the module already answers to; no new permission and no reseed.
  ([leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js),
  [crmBoards.js](../../frontend/src/lib/crmBoards.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js))

- **Meetings' List view now reads the whole studio schedule, not only the meetings.** A switch in
  the header chooses what the table is a list of — **All**, **Projects**, **Pre Production**,
  **Post Production** or **Meetings** — and each brings its own columns: a meeting keeps Enquiry
  Name, Time, Duration, Host and Guests; a project reads as the register does, with Event ID, the
  vertical, the main event date, the coordinator that board is about, its shoot count and its
  project status; All reduces both to Type, Reference, Title, Verticals, Date, Time, Owner and
  Status. A project appears once under All however many stages it is staffed at. Sorting, the
  column filters, Manage Columns, the CSV export and the details panel all follow the source, and a
  project row offers **Open project** through to Project Management for anybody who may read that
  board. Only the sources the viewer's own permissions reach are offered — Projects needs
  `SALES / Sales Manager` **or** `PROJECT / Projects` `READ`, the two stages need
  `PROJECT / Pre Production` and `PROJECT / Post Production` `READ`, and a viewer who reads only
  `SALES / CRM Leads` gets no switch at all and the meetings table they had before. The projects
  register is fetched only where one of those sources is offered.
  ([MeetingsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/MeetingsPage.js),
  [meetingSources.js](../../frontend/src/lib/meetingSources.js),
  [projectStages.js](../../frontend/src/lib/projectStages.js))

- **My Profile is a full page with a tab bar.** The header spans the screen with the photo
  (camera badge to change it), name, username, email and contact number; under it a tab strip —
  Personal Information, Change Password and Org Chart — replaces the earlier dialogs with
  inline forms. Personal Information also states the employment facts HR maintains (employee
  id, designation, department, joining date, work location, blood group, gender), read-only
  with a note saying where they are corrected. The Org Chart tab is offered to every login: an
  employee opens it at themselves, a login with no employee record (the standalone Super Admin)
  opens it at the top of the studio, and both walk it by tapping nodes exactly as the Employee
  Repository's record view does
  ([ProfilePage.js](../../frontend/src/components/profile/ProfilePage.js),
  [user/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/user/routes.js)).

- **The org chart wears faces, and My Profile shows yours.** The reporting chart's cards now
  lead with an avatar — the person's profile photo, or their initials on one of four fixed
  colours picked by a stable hash so everyone keeps their colour everywhere (the shared
  `EmployeeAvatar` is reusable across the OS). The record view's tab is named **Org Chart**
  (was "Emp Chart"), and the same chart now sits on the My Profile page under the profile card,
  centred on the signed-in person and walkable by tapping nodes — served by a self-service
  `GET /api/user/org-chart` route open to any signed-in member, since its nodes carry only
  name, designation, department and photo
  ([EmployeeAvatar.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/EmployeeAvatar.js),
  [EmployeeOrgChart.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeOrgChart.js),
  [ProfilePage.js](../../frontend/src/components/profile/ProfilePage.js),
  [user/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/user/routes.js)).

- **The credential form asks who approves this employee's leaves.** A Leave Approval section
  joins Alternative on Create Login Credentials: one searchable ticked list, capped at three
  approvers picked in order, offering the same colleagues the Alternative slots do — never the
  employee themselves. The Alternative pair's menus now each hide whoever the other slot
  names, so the pair cannot hold one person twice. Approvers are stored on the employee row
  (three new `leave_approver*_employee_id` columns), like the cover pair, ready for the leave
  module to read
  ([employeeCredentialConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/employeeCredentialConfig.js),
  [employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js)).

- **A My Profile page: your photo, your details, and your password, self-service.** The user
  menu on the top bar now opens My Profile (`/profile`): a card with your name, email and a
  round avatar — initials until a photo exists — wearing a camera badge that saves a new photo
  as it is picked. Edit Profile changes the login's username and email (and the name, where no
  employee record supplies it — a linked name stays HR's, maintained in the Employee
  Repository), and Change Password changes your password on the strength of knowing the current
  one, held to the same policy as the reset form. The photo lands on your linked employee
  record — the same field HR sets from the employee form — or, for a login with no employee
  (the standalone Super Admin), on a new `user.profile_photo_url` column, so every account can
  carry one. All of it goes through self-service endpoints under `/api/user` guarded by the JWT
  alone, since they only ever touch the caller's own record
  ([ProfilePage.js](../../frontend/src/components/profile/ProfilePage.js),
  [user/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/user/routes.js)).

- **An employee's photograph is collected on the onboarding link.** The public onboarding form now
  asks for a Profile Photo at the top of Personal Details, stored through the same R2 flow as the
  Aadhaar and PAN proofs it is submitted with — compressed in the browser, uploaded before the
  transaction opens, and discarded again if the submission does not land. It is filed under
  `hrm/onboarding/photo` rather than among the identity proofs, because a person's photograph is
  not one of their documents. Optional, so a missing photo never blocks somebody finishing their
  onboarding. HR can add or replace it later from the employee form, and it shows in the employee
  table, the info panel and the record view
  ([onboarding-invites/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/onboarding-invites/controller.js),
  [employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js),
  [onboardingFormConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/onboardingFormConfig.js)).

- **Public CRD documents now open with known project details prefilled.** The form fills blank
  fields from the project, the saved WhatsApp group-name generator row and the main shoot where
  available, including group name, client contact, groom/bride names, event type, shoot date, place,
  district and state.
  ([crd-documents/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/service.js))
- **CRD Documents can be filtered by delivery state.** The page now has All, Submitted, Yet To
  Receive and Link Not sent tabs, backed by the CRD document rows so Submitted means the client
  actually submitted the public form.
  ([CrdDocumentsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CrdDocumentsPage.js),
  [crd-documents/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/service.js))
- **A project's Payments tab says what the project is signed for.** A Signed Quotation card sits
  above the Extras it is half made of: the initial signed figure, the approved extras, and the
  total the studio would quote back to the client today. Approved extras only — anything still
  waiting is named under the figure rather than folded into it, because an extra nobody has agreed
  to is not money the project is signed for. The total is computed by the server alongside the two
  rows it is made of, so it cannot disagree with them
  ([ProjectPaymentsView.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectPaymentsView.js),
  [projects/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js)).
- **A role being viewed reads as a record, not as a form that will not work.** In the read-only
  role dialog the chevrons are gone from the Branches field and from each module's access dropdown,
  the × is gone from each branch chip, and the ⊘ cursor is gone from every field. There is no menu
  behind a record being read, so an arrow pointing at one only invites a click that does nothing.
  Both shared pickers now tell the two silences apart: `disabled` is a control that is off for now —
  the shoot picker before a project is chosen — and keeps its chevron, while the new `readOnly`
  is a value being read back and drops it, centring what it shows in the space the arrow leaves
  ([Dropdown.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/Dropdown.js),
  [MultiSelectField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/MultiSelectField.js),
  [RoleDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/admin/RoleDialog.js)).
- **Reading a role no longer refuses the pointer.** The ⊘ cursor is gone from the fields of a
  role being viewed — the name, the description, the branches, and each module's access level. Those
  fields are values being read, and the grey fill already says so; a cursor that refuses the pointer
  reads as something being withheld. Dropdowns disabled until an earlier answer is given lose it
  too — their placeholder already names what is missing. An option inside an open menu that really
  cannot be picked keeps it ([Dropdown.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/Dropdown.js),
  [MultiSelectField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/MultiSelectField.js),
  [RoleDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/admin/RoleDialog.js)).
- **A read-only multi-select no longer offers to remove what it is showing.** Viewing a role now
  lists its branches as plain chips: the × on each one is gone rather than greyed out, because a
  faded remove button still reads as a control that ought to work and invited people to try to
  change a role they were only reading. Applies wherever the shared multi-select is disabled
  ([MultiSelectField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/MultiSelectField.js)).
- **The branches a role covers can be searched.** The Branches picker in Add/Edit Role types to
  narrow the list instead of scrolling it, which is the difference between finding one branch and
  reading every branch once a studio runs more than a handful. Search is an opt-in `searchable` prop
  on the shared multi-select, so no other picker changed; ticks already made survive the filter
  ([MultiSelectField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/MultiSelectField.js),
  [RoleDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/admin/RoleDialog.js)).
- **A cash entry can be corrected from the project it was filed against.** Each Cash Received and
  Cash Spent row on the Payments tab carries an edit pencil, opening Accounts' own entry form on the
  whole record — the account it moved through, who handled it, the proof — rather than on the
  handful of fields that tab lists, so a figure typed wrongly is fixed where it is noticed instead
  of being withdrawn and entered again. Shown only to somebody who may write cash entries; the
  Accounts Manager table already offered the same thing from its row menu
  ([ProjectPaymentsView.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectPaymentsView.js)).
- **An extra quotation can be corrected while it waits.** A pending extra carries the same pencil
  and reopens the form it was raised in. Only while pending, and only for whoever raised it or a
  super admin: once the manager has approved it the figure is what the project agreed to sell, and
  changing it afterwards would move that quietly — so the server refuses it and the pencil is not
  drawn. Its proof is left alone unless the form sends one, so an edit never clears files it was not
  asked about. New `PATCH /api/sales/extra-quotations/:id`
  ([extra-quotations/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/extra-quotations/controller.js),
  [ExtraQuotationFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ExtraQuotationFormPanel.js)).
- **Extra work can be quoted, approved by the project's manager, and lands on the project.**
  **Extra Quotation** (`EXT-000001`) records work agreed on top of what a project was sold for — an
  extra shoot, an album nobody quoted. One request is one extra: it names the project, the shoot, a
  service from the studio's own service list, an amount, a note, and an image of what the client
  agreed to. It has its own table rather than being a fifth kind of payment request, because it is
  not one: the four payment requests are the studio paying somebody and approving one writes a cash
  entry, while this agrees work and **writes nothing to the books** — the money arrives later as an
  ordinary receipt. It sits at **Approval pending** until decided, and only the manager named on
  that project, or a super admin, may decide it: approving spend and agreeing to sell more work are
  different authorities. Once approved it appears in the project's **Extras** column and is what the
  summary's **Extra** figure totals — so Extra means work agreed, whether or not the money has
  arrived, rather than cash taken above the quotation. New routes under
  `/api/sales/extra-quotations`. It can be raised from two places, through one panel so the two
  cannot drift: the **+** on the project's own Extras column, where the project is already settled
  and the shoots are narrowed to it, and an **Extra Quotation** button on Payment Requests, where it
  asks which project. That button is shown only to somebody holding `PROJECT / Projects`, since that
  screen is Accounts' and an extra is the project's. A waiting extra shows in the Extras column drawn
  back and marked **Approval pending**, with its proof to hand and Approve / Reject offered only to
  whoever may actually decide it; rejecting asks why. Payment Requests also gains an **Extra
  Quotations** tab listing both halves of the workflow at once — what you raised and what waits on
  your decision — as an ordinary table with the same columns, filters, column menu, resizing, info
  panel and CSV export every other table in the app has, the row menu offering the decision only
  where the server says this reader may make it
  ([extra-quotations/](../../backend/src/modules/sales/extra-quotations/controller.js),
  [ExtraQuotationFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ExtraQuotationFormPanel.js),
  [20260828010000_add_project_extra_quotation](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260828010000_add_project_extra_quotation/migration.sql),
  [projects/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js)).
- **The project's Payments tab is three columns.** Extras, then Cash Received and Cash Spent, then
  the summary — renamed **Project Summary**, since it is now one of three named columns rather than
  the only one. They stack in the same order on a narrow screen
  ([ProjectPaymentsView.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectPaymentsView.js)).

- **Adding an employee goes straight on to giving them a login.** Saving a new employee who starts
  **Active** now opens the "create login credentials" step, the same offer an approved onboarding
  submission already got — adding somebody and then hunting for their row to give them access was
  two jobs where the second follows from the first. "Skip for now" is still a real answer, and the
  row keeps offering it until a login exists. An employee added on a pending status is not offered
  it: the server creates a login only for an Active employee, so the offer would be a form that
  could only be refused
  ([EmployeeVerificationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeVerificationPage.js)).

- **A cancelled or expired onboarding invite can be sent again in one press.** View All Invites now
  offers **Resend Invite** on a cancelled or expired row, beside the Cancel Invite a live one still
  offers; a submitted invite is finished and offers nothing. It creates a **new** invite from the
  same candidate's details rather than reviving the old one — the token is stored hashed with only a
  preview kept, so the original link cannot be reconstructed by anyone, this server included, and
  should not be: cancelling is what somebody does when a link went to the wrong address. The
  cancelled row stays as the record of what happened, and the new link arrives on the same screen a
  freshly created invite does, including the button that emails it. Re-inviting somebody who already
  has a live invite is still refused, in those words
  The list is now one row per candidate rather than one per invite: somebody re-invited after a
  cancellation had two rows, which read as the same person listed twice rather than as one person
  invited twice. Each candidate shows their latest invite — the one there is anything to do about —
  with the attempts behind it counted on the row (“1 earlier”) and folded away under it, recessed
  and carrying no action of their own
  ([OnboardingInviteDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/OnboardingInviteDialog.js)).

- **A project now shows what it has actually earned and cost.** Double-clicking a project row on
  Projects opens the record as it always did, and it now has two tabs — **Details**, everything the
  record already showed, and **Payments**: **Cash Received** lists the money taken in against the
  project, **Cash Spent** what was paid out on it, and a **Payment Summary** beside them gives four
  figures — **Total cash received**, **Total cash spent**, **Total quotation** (the project's
  Initial Converted Value, what the sale was converted at) and **Extra** (whatever has come in above
  that figure — an add-on shoot agreed
  after the sale is paid on top of the quotation, so the money over it is the money for the add-ons;
  floored at zero, since a project paid less than it was sold for has a balance still to come rather
  than an extra). Extra is a slice of what came in rather than a figure beside it, and nothing is derived
  from the four: what a project made is a question for Accounts, who know what else it cost. Each row is stamped with the date the money moved. The rows are Accounts' own cash entries filed against the project, so the
  figures are the ones in the Accounts Manager rather than a second set kept here; the section
  reads them and offers no way to add one, because recording a payment is
  `ACCOUNTS / Cash Management`'s job — pressing **Add** on either list opens Accounts' own entry
  form rather than a second form of the tab's own, with **this project already filled in**: the
  direction comes from the button pressed, the project from the screen, the branch from the
  project's source branch, and money out is offered this project's shoots alone. Withdrawn entries
  are left out. Reading the tab asks for `PROJECT / Projects` `READ` **and**
  `ACCOUNTS / Cash Management` `READ` — the money is Accounts', so opening a project is not by
  itself a reason to be shown what it earned, and somebody holding one permission without the other
  gets no tab bar at all. Add needs `Cash Management` `READ_WRITE` and is absent without it. The
  read is deliberately outside the register read the production boards share, so listing a project
  on a board still shows none of this. New route `GET /api/sales/projects/:id/payments`
  ([routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/routes.js),
  [ProjectPaymentsView.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectPaymentsView.js)).
- **A record view can carry a tab the row itself does not hold.** `RecordViewPanel` takes
  `extraTabs` — `{ id, label, render }` entries, each a tab beside **Details** — which is how
  Payments reaches a panel otherwise built entirely from the fields already in hand. A record given
  none has no tab bar and reads exactly as before. `SchemaFormPanel` gained the `tabs` slot the bar
  sits in, between the title and the section rail: the rail says where in a thing you are, a tab
  says which thing, and the two are no longer one list
  ([RecordViewPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/RecordViewPanel.js),
  [SchemaFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/schema-form/SchemaFormPanel.js)).
- **The cash entry form can be opened with part of it already answered.** `CashEntryFormPanel` takes
  a `preset` of `{ entryType, project }`: both are then read back rather than asked for, the shoot
  picker narrows to that project, and the project travels as a whole record because the picker holds
  only the most recent ones and cannot be relied on to contain it
  ([CashEntryFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/accounts/CashEntryFormPanel.js)).
- **Every release has a number, and the running system will tell you which one it is.** Releases
  are `<major>.<minor>.<build>` — `1.2.41`. Major and minor live in the new `VERSION` file at the
  repository root and are the only part anyone chooses; the build number is the count of
  promotions to `main` since the tag `v1.0.0`, so it advances on its own and cannot be forgotten.
  It is **monotonic** and does not reset, so `1.2.41` is followed by `1.3.42`.
  [app-version.sh](../../scripts/app-version.sh) is the single implementation, and the one the deploy
  runs: `--current` for this commit, `--next` for what promoting `develop` would ship, `--check`
  to confirm `VERSION` and both `package.json` files still agree.
  [deploy.yml](../../.github/workflows/deploy.yml) works the number out before it builds anything,
  bakes it into the frontend bundle, writes a `RELEASE` file on the VPS recording the version,
  commit and time, and — once the health check has passed — tags the commit `v<version>` and opens
  a GitHub Release carrying that version's changelog section. Tagging last means a tag existing
  says that version actually served a request in production, which is what makes
  `git reset --hard v1.2.40` a rollback worth having; it pushes a tag and never a commit, because
  a commit on `main` would retrigger the deploy on itself. `GET /api/health` now returns `version`,
  `commit` and `deployedAt` ([server.js](../../backend/src/server.js)), and the sidebar footer shows
  the version the loaded bundle was built with, which is how a cached bundle gives itself away
  ([AppSidebar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppSidebar.js)). See
  [Versioning](../project-state/DEPLOYMENT-WORKFLOW.md#versioning).
- **The deploy now checks *which* release answered, not just that something did.** The health check
  compares the version the backend reports against the `RELEASE` file the deploy wrote: a PM2
  process that restarted onto the old build is otherwise indistinguishable from a healthy one. On
  a night when nothing was merged nothing is restarted, so a server with no `RELEASE` file warns
  rather than failing — the next deploy that ships writes one.
  ([deploy.yml](../../.github/workflows/deploy.yml))

- **The selected-photos manifest names each photo's storage root.** `selected/manifest` items
  now carry `storageRootId` alongside `originalPath`, so the Image Selection Desktop app can
  resolve a selected master through its own workstation's mapping and copy it locally. Null for
  photos from the browser flows, which keep working unchanged.
  ([gallery/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/gallery/service.js))
- **Desktop batch photo registration.** `POST
  /api/image-selection/projects/:projectId/originals/register-batch` registers photos the
  Image Selection Desktop app scanned: metadata only (relative trail, size, dimensions, capture
  date) — the server never stats a file, because a VPS cannot see a studio NAS. Each photo is
  recorded as a named storage root plus a relative trail (`image_selection_upload` gains
  `storage_root_id`, migration `20260824130000`), deduped per folder exactly as the derivative
  upload dedupes, traversal trails refused, and rows wait in PROCESSING until the desktop posts
  their thumbnail and preview through the existing derivative endpoint. Skipped files report per
  file with a 207, matching the other registration routes.
  ([originals/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/originals/service.js))
- **Storage roots carry their mounted location.** `image_selection_storage_root` gains
  `mapped_path` (migration `20260825150000`) and the CRUD API accepts/returns `mappedPath`, so
  where a root is mounted is stored in the database rather than in the desktop app's local
  settings — surviving reinstalls and visible wherever the root is listed. The server stores the
  path but never opens it; the desktop resolves and reads it.
- **Named storage roots for Image Selection.** `GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE
  /api/image-selection/storage-roots` manage named places the studio keeps originals —
  "STUDIO_NAS_MAIN", "ARCHIVE_HDD_2026" — as the durable identity photo records will point at.
  Only the name and notes live on the server: where a root is mounted on any workstation is that
  machine's own configuration, so remapping a NAS from Z:\ to Y:\ never touches a photo record.
  Names are unique among living roots, deletes are soft (photo rows keep pointing at retired
  roots), and the routes answer for IMAGE_SELECTION → Upload & Gallery. First backend piece of
  the DBS Image Selection Desktop app; table `image_selection_storage_root` (migration
  `20260824120000`).
  ([storage-roots/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/storage-roots/service.js))
- **A linked project's shoots appear only after their shoot date.** A Project Management shoot
  dated 20 Aug joins the Image Selection workspace from 21 Aug: until then its root folder is
  neither created nor listed (subfolders included) and its deliverables stay out of Selection
  Items and the client gallery, while shoots already shot are not held back by a later one. A
  shoot with no date is never held back. Manual projects are unaffected.
  ([shoot-visibility.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/shoot-visibility.js),
  [folders/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/folders/service.js),
  [deliverables/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/deliverables/service.js))
- **A manual project's shoot groups are its root folders.** Selection Settings lists every folder
  created at the project root — "wedding", "pre wedding" — as a shoot-name group automatically,
  each with its own "add a selection item" row, since those folders already are the shoots the
  client gallery files items under. The "Create shoot name" input is gone: a new shoot is made by
  making its folder. A folder whose first item is saved keeps one heading, not two, and a project
  with no folders yet says to create one. Groups whose items were saved earlier still show even
  without a matching folder.
  ([SelectionItemsTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/SelectionItemsTable.js),
  [ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js))
- **A manual project's selection items are grouped under shoot names.** In Selection Settings on
  a manually named Image Selection project, each shoot group — "Pre Wedding", "Wedding" — appears
  as a heading like a linked project's shoots, and offers its own "add a selection item" + limits
  row, so every typed item is filed under a shoot. Item names only need to be unique within their
  group, the group's key travels as the item's shootId — so the client gallery lists each item
  under the root folder matching its shoot name and submits one group at a time — and older
  ungrouped items keep listing under "Other deliverables"; the expand view is unchanged. Stored as `manual_shoot_name` on the config row (migration
  `20260821130000_add_manual_shoot_name`).
  ([SelectionItemsTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/SelectionItemsTable.js),
  [deliverables/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/deliverables/service.js))
- **Editing a manual project re-opens its creation form.** The sidebar's Edit on a manually
  named Image Selection project no longer opens a rename dialog: the main pane shows the manual
  creation form pre-filled with everything the project holds — name, client name, WhatsApp
  number, and the Workspace Tabs choice — under an "Edit Project" heading, with **Save** and
  **Cancel** in place of Create Project. Saving writes all of it in one request; the API applies
  only the fields sent, refuses clearing the client or every tab, and still refuses edits on a
  linked project. The archive/delete confirmations are unchanged.
  ([CreateImageSelectionProject.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/CreateImageSelectionProject.js),
  [projects/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/projects/service.js))
- **A manual project chooses its workspace tabs at creation.** The Create New Project form's
  manual branch gains a required **Workspace Tabs** picker — Photo Selection, Photo Culling,
  Signature Album, at least one ticked, Photo Selection on by default — and the project workspace
  shows exactly the ticked tabs. A linked project is unchanged: its tabs are still earned from
  the Post Production Allocation and the Deliverables table's Image Selection column, and the API
  refuses a hand-picked set on one. The choice is stored on the project row (three nullable
  booleans, migration `20260821120000_add_manual_workspace_tabs`; null means the old default of
  Photo Selection alone), travels into duplicates, and a project created without Photo Selection
  opens straight onto its first ticked tab.
  ([CreateImageSelectionProject.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/CreateImageSelectionProject.js),
  [ImageSelectionProjectWorkspace.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionProjectWorkspace.js),
  [projects/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/projects/service.js))
- **Quotation prices come from settings rather than from the code.** A **⋮** menu beside
  *Quotation generator* opens **Settings**, holding the service rate, the deliverable rate, the
  default discount and the GST percentage. All four were constants in the source, so changing a
  price meant changing code and deploying. A quotation stamps the rates it was raised under into
  its own record, so editing them prices the next quotation and never rewrites one already sent —
  reopening an old quotation prices it from its own numbers, and the client's copy states the GST
  that applied when it was raised.
  [QuotationSettingsDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationSettingsDialog.js)

- **The client's quotation is now the studio's own document, and it downloads as a real PDF.** The
  page at `/q/<token>` was a working invoice; it is now the presented quotation — the logo, the
  couple's name set in a serif, the total with GST stated beneath it, **Event Coverage** as a card
  per shoot with its date, place and duration and its services in two columns, **Premium
  Deliverables** sorting whatever the generator quoted into Photography, Cinematography, Premium
  Albums and Complimentary, then the terms and the closing note. The shoot marked as the main event
  carries a **MAIN EVENT** mark. **Download PDF** now returns a file named after the quotation
  instead of opening the browser's print dialog, from Sales → Quotation and from the public page
  alike. The PDF is that same page rendered on the server, so what a client reads online and what
  they are sent cannot differ. Wording and the deliverable grouping live in
  [quotationTemplateContent.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/quotationTemplateContent.js).
  [PublicQuotationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicQuotationPage.js)

- **The Quotation Generator asks for the vertical first, then the details that vertical needs.** A
  **Vertical** choice — Dum Dum, Qua Qua, Corp — now opens the Identification section, and the
  Client Details below it change to match: Dum Dum asks for the groom and the bride, Qua Qua for the
  baby, a due date and an offer type, Corp for the company, who decides inside it and the budget
  expected. The fields, labels and order mirror **Sales → CRM → Add Lead**, so a salesperson meets
  the same form on both screens. **Enquire ID** sits above them as a required picker that suggests
  the enquiry IDs the CRM already holds for that vertical and still accepts one typed in, for an
  enquiry the CRM has not been given yet. Event Venue, Followed By, Reffered By and Secondary
  Contact Number stay below, whichever vertical is chosen. Switching vertical clears the client
  details and says so first; services, deliverables and shoots are kept.
  [QuotationGeneratorPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationGeneratorPanel.js)
- **Payments belong to Accounts, not to the Employee Repository.** `HRM` carried
  `Payment Request Approval` and `Payroll Management`, so somebody administering employee records
  was being asked, in the same breath, whether they may decide what the studio pays out — two
  different jobs sharing a module because the payment requests code happens to live under `hrm/`.
  Both move to `ACCOUNTS`, beside Cash Management, and `HRM` is left with Employee Management alone.
  The two Accounts screens still answer to different permissions: keeping the cash book and deciding
  what is paid out are separate, even though both are the accountant's. `Payroll Management` guards
  no route yet — it moved rather than being dropped so it is in the right place when payroll is
  built. **Roles granting either under HRM need re-saving.** See
  [payment-requests/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/payment-requests/routes.js).

- **Image Selection is one permission.** `Manage IS Projects` and `Upload & Publish` described two
  answers to one question: shaping a project, uploading the shoot and sending the gallery to the
  client are steps of the same job, done by the same people, and splitting them only allowed a role
  that could arrange folders but not fill them. `IMAGE_SELECTION / Project Management` now covers
  the module — projects, folders, settings, uploads, the gallery and publishing — with `READ` to
  browse folders, search the gallery and open a published link, and `READ_WRITE` for everything that
  changes any of it. `Upload & Gallery` is retired, so it has left the Roles dialog rather than
  lingering as a dead option. **Roles granting it need re-saving**, though anyone holding it
  almost certainly held the other too. See
