  [iam-roles-permissions.js](../../backend/prisma/seeds/iam-roles-permissions.js).

- **The Assets screen is one permission, and answering a request is the only thing held apart from
  it.** `ASSET` described five features where the studio only ever had two answers: everybody who
  works with assets browses the register, records an inspection and asks for equipment, while a
  smaller group decides on what is asked. Asset Inspection and Asset Assignment are retired into
  `ASSET / Asset Management`, which now covers the register, inspections, custody history and
  raising a request — `READ` to browse and read your own requests, `READ_WRITE` to add, edit,
  inspect and ask. Vendors and Client Storage keep their own permissions, being their own screens.
  **Roles granting the retired two need re-saving**, though anyone holding them almost certainly
  held Asset Management too. See [assets.routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/assets/assets.routes.js).

- **Deciding on an asset request is a permission now, and grantable at all for the first time.**
  Approving or rejecting was refused to anybody whose legacy `role` column was not `SUPER_ADMIN` — a
  check inside the controller that no role could satisfy, so the Roles screen could hand out asset
  access all day and nobody but the super admin could ever answer a request. That check is gone.
  Raising a request travels with `ASSET / Asset Management`, which every employee holds; the new
  `ASSET / Asset Request Approval` carries the queue and the decision, and the Accept and Reject
  entries follow it rather than the account's role.

  `GET /api/asset-requests` is scoped to match: without approval it returns **your own requests
  only**. It returned everybody's to anyone who could read it, which was survivable while the
  permission was rare and a privacy leak the moment it is given to every employee — which is the
  point of the split. See
  [asset-requests.routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/assets/asset-requests.routes.js) and
  [asset-requests.controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/assets/asset-requests.controller.js).

- **Project Management is a module of its own, and Sales Manager has a permission of its own.** One
  grant, `SALES / Project Management`, decided five unrelated screens: selling a project, planning
  its shoots, the three production boards, and issuing CRD documents — so giving somebody the
  production boards also gave them the power to delete the sale. It is retired, replaced by
  `SALES / Sales Manager` for the sale itself and a new **`PROJECT`** module carrying Projects, Pre
  Production, Production and Post Production; CRD Documents becomes its own `SALES` feature too.
  What made the split possible is that all of them only *share a read*: `GET /api/sales/projects`
  and the reference lists behind it are reachable by any of those permissions, while each write sits
  behind the screen that makes it. On the Projects screen the row menu now answers to both — "Start
  Project" and "Edit Project" to `PROJECT / Projects`, "Edit Sale Details" and "Delete" to
  `SALES / Sales Manager` — so a project manager can plan a shoot without being able to delete the
  sale it came from. 22 permissions in all. See
  [sales/projects/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/routes.js).

- **Every signed-in screen now answers to a permission, and a read-only grant is read-only on
  screen.** The frontend held two permission checks in the whole app; it now decides three things
  from the roles a person carries. A screen they cannot read is **not listed in the sidebar** and
  refuses a typed URL, naming the permission that is missing. A screen they hold `READ` on opens and
  lists its records with every control that changes one **absent** — no Add, no Edit, no Delete, and
  no row menu where the menu held nothing else — rather than present and refused by the API.
  `READ_WRITE` is unchanged. One table,
  [modulePermissions.js](../../frontend/src/constants/modulePermissions.js), maps route prefix to
  `{ module, feature }`, and the sidebar, the new
  [RouteGuard](../../frontend/src/components/auth/RouteGuard.js) and the landing resolver all read it,
  so a screen cannot be listed in one and forgotten in another. Pages ask what they may do with
  `useRouteAccess()` rather than naming their own permission. Signing in still lands on
  `/employees`; somebody who cannot open it is forwarded to the first screen they can, and told to
  ask for a role only when they hold none. The API still refuses these requests on its own — this
  stops the app asking for what it would be refused, and stops reporting the refusal as an error.
  Every signed-in screen across Assets, Sales, Projects, Accounts, HRM, Image Selection and
  Administration is covered. See [IAM.md](../project-state/IAM.md).

- **Vendors are administered under their own permission, apart from assets.** The Vendor Management
  screen answered to `ASSET / Asset Management`, so nobody could keep the studio's asset register
  and its supplier records apart — one grant opened both. `ASSET / Vendor Management` now decides
  the screen and every vendor record behind it. What made this possible is that the asset status
  dialog needs vendor *names* when an asset goes in for service, which is Asset Management's own
  business: `GET /api/vendors/picker` serves those names — id, code, name, type, and nothing
  commercial — and is reachable by either permission, while the vendor records themselves stay
  behind Vendor Management alone. **Anyone who administered vendors through Asset Management needs
  the new permission granting.** See
  [vendors.routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/assets/vendors.routes.js).

- **Client storage is its own permission.** It was decided by `ASSET / Asset Assignment`, which is
  about lending assets to staff and has nothing to do with a client's media; the screen and its
  endpoints now answer to `ASSET / Client Storage`. **Anyone who reached client storage through
  Asset Assignment needs the new permission granting**, so re-check the roles that had it. Nothing
  outside the screen reads that data, so unlike vendors it needed no endpoint splitting to separate.
  The screens still sharing a permission are the ones sharing an endpoint — the sales project
  register alone feeds Sales Manager, CRD Documents and all four Project Management screens — and a
  screen given a finer permission than its endpoint has would be shown to somebody the API then
  refuses the records to, which is the failure this release exists to end. Separating those means
  splitting the endpoint first, as vendors shows. See [iam-roles-permissions.js](../../backend/prisma/seeds/iam-roles-permissions.js)
  and [client-storage.routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/assets/client-storage.routes.js).

- **The pipeline runs itself: `develop` is checked on every merge, and `main` deploys to Hostinger
  on its own.** Two GitHub Actions workflows replace the by-hand deploy, each firing on a merge to
  its own branch. [ci.yml](../../.github/workflows/ci.yml) runs on a push to `develop` — an approved PR
  landing — installing from the lockfile, generating and validating the Prisma schema, then running
  the backend tests, the frontend lint, the frontend tests, and the frontend build. It reports on
  the integrated branch rather than blocking a merge, so it catches two changes that pass separately
  and break together; red on `develop` is fixed before `develop` is promoted.
  [deploy.yml](../../.github/workflows/deploy.yml) ships once a night at 23:00 IST, Monday to
  Saturday — cron `30 17 * * 1-6`, since GitHub cron is UTC only — rather than on every push to
  `main`, so a day's merges go out together in one quiet window and nothing ships into a Sunday;
  `workflow_dispatch` remains the hotfix path. It re-checks that the
  commit builds, backs up the production database, then over SSH resets the VPS to that exact
  commit, runs `prisma migrate deploy`, seeds IAM permissions, roles and scopes, rebuilds the
  frontend, restarts both PM2 processes, and polls `/api/health` until the backend answers —
  failing the deploy, with logs, if it never does. The IAM seed runs on every release because a
  `{ module, feature }` row added in one exists only in the source until it is seeded, and until
  then the permission it names cannot be granted to anyone; the deploy never runs the full
  `npm run seed`, which would reset the live Super Admin's password. Deploy needs five repository
  secrets and a `production` environment — see
  [DEPLOYMENT-WORKFLOW.md](../project-state/DEPLOYMENT-WORKFLOW.md#continuous-integration-and-deployment).

- **The login credentials email is a designed template, not a wall of plain text.** It now uses the
  same frame as the onboarding invite — the wordmark, the white card, the footer — and sends HTML
  and plain text together. The username and temporary password each sit in a bordered monospace box
  above the prose, because copying them is the whole reason the email is open; a Sign in button and
  the same link in text follow. Two things are said plainly that the old text buried: the password
  stops working once it is changed at first sign-in, and nobody at the studio will ever ask for it.
  See [emailTemplates.js](../../backend/src/services/emailTemplates.js).
- **An employee records the two colleagues who cover their work.** The Alternative section of Create
  Login Credentials names them, and they are stored on the employee row as
  `alternative1_employee_id` and `alternative2_employee_id` — against the person rather than the
  account, so the cover outlives any login. Nobody on a departure status is offered, nobody can be
  their own alternative, and the two slots must name different people. Deleting an employee who was
  named as cover empties the slot rather than being refused. Migration:
  `20260822124303_add_employee_alternatives`.
- **Creating an employee's login is a form of three sections, and it now grants roles.** Create
  Login Credentials opens the standard full-screen form panel with a rail of **Credentials**,
  **Roles** and **Alternative**. Who the account is for — name, employee ID, department, branch — is
  stated in the summary strip above the sections rather than filling a section with fields nobody
  may type in. The branch is derived by matching the employee's work location against the locations
  master data. Credentials is the username, login email and the generated
  temporary password. Roles is the new part: several roles are ticked at once and added together,
  and the account is created carrying them, in one transaction with the user itself. Until now the
  form asked only for a department, offered nothing but "Technical", and produced an account with no
  IAM role at all — one that could sign in and do nothing. The account's `accessDepartment`, still
  read by the WhatsApp module, is now the employee's own department instead of a hardcoded
  "Technical". The form is described in
  [constants/employeeCredentialConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/employeeCredentialConfig.js);
  the roles control is [shared/RolePicker.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/RolePicker.js),
  which the Administration screen's own Roles dialog now uses too — so roles can be added several at
  a time there as well.
- **"Create Login Credentials" is reachable from the employee row menu.** The offer that follows an
  approval can be answered with "Skip for now", and until now that was the only chance to take it.
  The ⋮ menu on an Active employee who has no login yet now opens the same form, landing straight on
  it rather than repeating the "Employee Onboarded Successfully" announcement. The menu knows which
  employees still need one because
  [the employee list](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js) now returns
  `hasLoginCredentials` alongside each row. See
  [EmployeeTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeTable.js).
- **Users & Access, the admin screen for the IAM system.** Under Administration in the sidebar,
  `/admin` opens Users, Groups and Roles. **Users** lists everyone you can reach and adds an account — the password is hashed on the way in and the person is asked to
  change it at first sign-in. **Groups** creates the groups a branch's permissions are handed out
  through, and each group opens onto its members and its roles, where a user joins and a role is
  attached; a user from another branch is never offered, because the API would refuse them anyway.
  Every control is behind the permission it needs, so an administrator who may look
  but not change sees the screen without the buttons. See
  [backend/src/modules/admin/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/admin/routes.js) and
  [frontend/src/components/admin/AdminPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/admin/AdminPage.js).
- **Permissions are fetched once per signed-in session** by
  [PermissionsProvider](../../frontend/src/components/auth/PermissionsProvider.js), which every
  `PermissionGuard` and `usePermissions()` call reads from. A fetch that fails is retried; if it
  still cannot be loaded, guards close and the page says the permissions could not be read rather
  than claiming the person lacks access.

- **Roles are carried by a user directly; user groups are gone.** Administration is two tabs, Users
  and Roles, and a person's access is the union of the roles on them. A role names the branches it
  reaches, which is what the group used to decide, so the group added a hop without adding a choice.
  Roles a user reached through a group were copied onto them by the migration, so nobody lost access
  in the move. The Users table gains an info panel — home branch, roles carried, and the branches
  those roles reach — and a ⋮ → **Manage roles**. See
  [UserManagement.js](../../frontend/src/components/admin/UserManagement.js).
- **A role names the branches it reaches.**
  **Create role** is one scrolling form on the app's schema-form panel, with an Identification
  section — name, description and a **branches multi-select** — and an Action permissions section
  that opens a level at a time: enable a module, then tick the sub-modules it applies to and set
  each to **Disabled / Read / Read / Write**. Everything a role grants applies in *every* branch it
  names, so a group in one branch can carry a role that reaches another — which is how somebody
  works across branches. The branch picker has left the page header: Roles carry their own branches
  and Groups, being per-branch lists, pick their own. Roles and Groups are the app's standard data
  table with a ⋮ menu per row — Edit, Duplicate, Delete on a role; Manage members and roles, Edit,
  Delete on a group — so both get sorting, filtering and CSV export like every other module. See
  [RoleDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/admin/RoleDialog.js) and
  [iam.service.js](../../backend/src/services/iam.service.js).
- **The policy layer was folded into roles.** A policy and a role were two names for "a named set of
  permissions", so there is now one: every policy became a role of its own, and every role absorbed
  the grants of the policies attached to it, most-permissive-wins, losing nothing. `Policy`,
  `PolicyPermission` and `RolePolicy` are gone, and a role's permissions are the whole of what it
  grants.

- **The studio's roles are Super Admin, Branch Manager, Team Lead and Employee**, alongside a Full
  and a Read Only role per module so a group can be assembled from module roles rather than one role
  per job title. Only Super Admin is locked — it is the account that restores everyone else's
  access, so it cannot be edited or deleted, only duplicated. The previous `ADMIN`, `MANAGER` and
  `STAFF` roles are removed, but only while nothing is attached to them.

- **The Customer Identification Document.** The link opens a full CRD at `/crd/<token>`: the event is
  described once at the top — WhatsApp Group Name (filled in from the project), Event Type from the
  existing Shoot Type list, Point of Contact name and phone, Event Time, Referred By — and then the
  same set of questions is asked of each side under **Groom** and **Bride** tabs. Each side gives
  their names, the name to use in designs, Insta ID, date of birth, a photo, contact number, shipping
  address, relatives' photographs, family members, their event window and address link, and anything
  else they would like help with. A progress bar tracks the **whole** document, both sides together,
  so whoever opens the link second can see what is left; a count beside each tab says how many
  answers that side still owes. The document **saves as it goes** and is submitted once at the end,
  because the groom side and the bride side are rarely the same person at the same desk. Submit is
  refused — by the server, not just the browser — until all 33 required answers are present.
  Photographs are compressed in the browser and stored in Cloudflare R2 through the existing
  `storeImage`/`storeImages` services, so the row holds links rather than image data.
  ([document.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/document.js) describes the shape and
  owns the required-field rule)
- **A public CRD document link.** Sales → CRD Documents → a row's ⋮ → **Send Link** now issues a
  real HTTPS link and shows it in the dialog with Copy Link and Open Form. Event ID and Project
  Status travel to the page as read-only context because the Event ID is immutable and the status is
  the studio's.
  The link is issued once per project and handed back unchanged on every later press, so pressing
  Send Link again re-copies the link the client already has rather than invalidating it.
  A submission is stored on the new `SalesProjectCrdDocument` row (migration
  `20260818100000_add_sales_project_crd_documents`) and **never writes back to `SalesProject`**, so
  nothing a client types can overwrite what Sales Manager holds — reconciling the two stays a studio
  decision, and the CRD Documents table still reads its Status from the project's own
  `crdDocumentStatus` exactly as before.
  ([crd-documents/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/service.js),
  [PublicCrdDocumentForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicCrdDocumentForm.js))

- **Originals stay on the studio's own storage.** A photo can now be registered from an
  **Original Storage Path** — a local drive or a NAS share — instead of being uploaded through the
  browser. Registering records where the master file already is; it copies nothing. The existing
  processing worker reads that file to build the thumbnail and preview, uploads only those to R2,
  and never writes to, moves or deletes the source. `POST .../projects/:id/originals/register`
  scans a folder under the configured root, `GET /api/image-selection/originals/browse` lists what
  is in one, and both refuse any location that resolves outside the root.
  ([originals/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/originals/service.js),
  [originals/paths.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/originals/paths.js))
- **Download Original Quality copies on the server.** `POST .../projects/:id/originals/copy-selected`
  resolves the client's chosen photos to their recorded `originalPath`, checks each one is still
  under the configured root and still on disk, and copies it — never moves it — into the
  **Selected Output Path**, filed by project, selection item, shoot and folder. A photo whose
  original has gone is reported as "Original file unavailable" against that photo alone and the rest
  of the copy continues; a partial result answers 207 so the caller reads the failures. The button
  falls back to the previous browser download when a studio has no server paths configured, so a
  gallery whose photos were uploaded through the browser behaves exactly as before.
  ([DownloadSelectedButton.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/DownloadSelectedButton.js))
- **Original Storage Path** and **Selected Output Path** in ⋮ → Project Settings, plus the
  `originalPath` column on `ImageSelectionUpload` that records where a master file lives
  (migration `20260815160000_add_original_storage_paths`). Both settings accept a drive letter or a
  UNC share, and no drive letter is hard-coded anywhere. They are server-side paths, so they have no
  folder picker — the browser cannot see the NAS the backend reads.
- Client previews are now encoded to fit within ~200 KB: quality is stepped down, then the long
  edge, until the encode lands under the ceiling. A 40 MB raw file and a phone snap leave the worker
  at roughly the same weight, and the client gallery still only ever receives the preview.
  ([processing/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/processing/service.js))
- **Selection items on a manually named project.** A project created from a typed name has no Post
  Production Allocation to derive its selection items from, so Selection Settings used to show it an
  empty table and the client had nothing to pick photos for. The table now ends in a row for adding
  one — a name and how many photos the client may choose — and each typed item carries a remove
  button. Only those two answers are asked for: an allocation-derived item takes its quantity and
  shoot from Project Management, and a typed one has neither. The new item is enabled straight away
  and takes the studio-wide default limit when none is entered.
  ([SelectionItemsTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/SelectionItemsTable.js),
  [deliverables/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/deliverables/service.js))
- `POST` and `DELETE /api/image-selection/projects/:projectId/deliverables`. Both are refused with a
  409 on a project linked to Project Management, which reads its items from the allocation, and a
  delete is refused once the client has selected photos for that item — unchecking it withdraws it
  without discarding what was already chosen. Migration
  `20260815140000_add_manual_selection_item_name` adds the nullable `name` column that holds a typed
  item's name; a derived row leaves it null and still reads its name from the deliverable it points
  at. ([deliverables/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/deliverables/routes.js))
- **Expiry Date in Selection Settings.** The rail now carries the day the client's link stops
  working, drawn by the shared calendar field every other form uses. It arrives filled in rather
  than blank: a project with no date of its own shows the studio-wide gallery validity from
  ⋮ → Project Settings — six months unless that was changed — so the field always shows the date the
  gallery would be given. Picking another date and saving stores it on the project, and publishing
  uses it in place of the validity window. On a gallery already with the client the saved date is
  pushed onto the live link as well, so the expiry the panel shows is the expiry that applies.
  Clearing the field hands the project back to the studio-wide window. Past dates cannot be picked.
  ([ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js),
  [publication/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/publication/service.js))
- The `selectionValidUntil` column, which existed on `ImageSelectionProject` and was carried into
  duplicates but never written or read, is now what that field stores — no migration needed. A
  studio-wide change to gallery validity still never moves a gallery already published; only this
  per-project date does, and only when the studio saves it.
- **Client details on Create New Project.** Choosing **Use Manual Project Name** now asks for the
  **Client Name** and **Client WhatsApp Number** alongside the project name — a manually named
  project has no Sales Manager project to read a client from, and this is where it gets one. All
  three are required: creating the project is the only screen that asks, so a manual project created
  without a client would be one whose gallery could never be shared. The API insists on the same
  three, a half-written number is rejected rather than stored as something that cannot be sent, and
  the number is saved in the `+91 9876543210` shape Sales Manager uses. Choosing a saved group name
  asks for neither field and says why: a linked project reads both from Sales Manager, and sending
  them with a `salesProjectId` is refused with a 409 rather than kept as a second copy that could
  drift.
  ([CreateImageSelectionProject.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/CreateImageSelectionProject.js),
  [projects/validation.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/projects/validation.js))
- **Project Root Path.** ⋮ → Project Settings now asks where the studio keeps its projects —
  `D:\DBS-Projects` — and every file the app saves is filed beneath it, under the project it belongs
  to and the module that produced it: `D:\DBS-Projects\John & Priya Wedding\Selected Images\…`, with
  Image Selection, Retouches, Frames, Calendar and Downloads beside it. The path is typed once and
  shared by everyone; **Browse** additionally grants *this* browser access to that folder, which is
  what actually lets the app write there, and the field says plainly whether downloads on this
  machine will reach the Root Path. Change the path later and new downloads follow it — existing
  files are never moved, and the browser is asked to point at the new folder rather than quietly
  filing into the old one.
  ([ProjectRootPathField.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ProjectRootPathField.js),
  [projectStorage.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/projectStorage.mjs))
- One resolver decides every download's location:
  [src/lib/projectStorage.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/projectStorage.mjs). Modules hand it a blob, a
  file name and a module folder; it creates the folders that are missing, de-duplicates names within
  each folder, and sanitises every segment — a project named `..` is filed as `Unsorted` rather than
  written above the root. **Download Selected (Original Quality)** and CSV table exports both go
  through it, and no module constructs a path of its own. With no Root Path configured, no folder
  granted, or a browser without the File System Access API (Firefox, Safari), files fall back to the
  browser's own Downloads folder and the app says why.
  ([DownloadSelectedButton.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/DownloadSelectedButton.js),
  [tableExport.js](../../frontend/src/lib/tableExport.js))
- Migration `20260815090000_add_project_root_path` adds the nullable `projectRootPath` column to
  `ImageSelectionSettings`. `PATCH /api/image-selection/settings` accepts only an absolute Windows,
  UNC or POSIX path with no `.` or `..` step; blank is stored as null and means "no root yet", which
  is exactly how the app behaved before this setting existed.
  ([settings/validation.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/settings/validation.js))
- **Studio-wide Image Selection settings.** ⋮ → Project Settings on the Image Selection title bar
  used to open an empty popup; it now opens a full-screen form asking for the Studio Branding every
  project shares — the studio name and the client-gallery logo, both hard-coded constants in the
  backend until now. Gallery link validity and the default Selection Items limit are stored on the
  same record and are read from it everywhere, but the form does not offer them: they keep their
  stored values of six months and fifty, and a save carries them through untouched. The form is the
  same `SchemaFormPanel` every other Create/Edit form uses, opens from both the Dashboard and the
  Projects page, and needs no project selected. A single project's own settings — its client, its
  validity toggles, its per-item limits — stay in the Selection Settings rail beside the workspace,
  which is unchanged.
  ([ImageSelectionSettingsForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionSettingsForm.js),
  [settings/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/settings/service.js))
- Migration `20260814123338_add_image_selection_settings` adds the `ImageSelectionSettings` table:
  one row, fixed id `default`, so there can never be a second row to disagree with the first. No
  seeding is needed — the service falls back to the previously hard-coded values (6 months, a limit
  of 50, "Don't Blink Studios") until a Super Admin saves the form, so an untouched database behaves
  exactly as before. Reading never writes the row.
- `GET`/`PATCH /api/image-selection/settings`. Any signed-in user can read them, because the
  gallery, the deliverable list and the WhatsApp invite all need the values; only a Super Admin can
  change them, since one save affects every project at once.
  ([settings/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/settings/routes.js))
- The client gallery footer now shows the studio's own name and uploaded logo, carried on the
  gallery meta rather than a second unauthenticated endpoint. A studio that has never uploaded one
  keeps the bundled logo, so galleries published before this look unchanged.
  ([GalleryBrand.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/client-gallery/GalleryBrand.js))
- An Image Selection project created with a manual project name can now be shared with a client.
  It has no Sales Manager project to read the client from, so Selection Settings gained a Client
  Name and Client WhatsApp Number field — both marked required with the red asterisk the rest of
  the app uses — and publishing, the gallery link, the PIN, the expiry date and the WhatsApp
  invite all work from those. Both stay saveable while blank so a project can be set up before
  the client's details are known; it is sharing the gallery that demands them. The fields appear only on a project with no
  Sales Manager link — a linked project still reads its client from that project, and the backend
  rejects a second copy so the two can never disagree.
  ([ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js),
  [publish-share/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/publish-share/service.js))
- Migration `20260814120000_add_image_selection_manual_client` adds nullable `clientName` and
  `clientMainNumber` to `ImageSelectionProject` and widens
  `ImageSelectionWhatsAppNotification.salesProjectId` to nullable, so an invite can be recorded
  for a project that has no Sales Manager project behind it. No backfill is needed: every
  existing project either reads its client from a linked Sales Manager project or has never had
  one recorded.
- A **Save Changes** button in the Selection Settings rail, shown once something has actually
  changed. ([ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js))
- The photo's file name now appears under each thumbnail in both Image Selection galleries, so the
  studio and the client can quote the same photo to each other. It uses the `fileName` the gallery
  API already returns — no backend or database change — and truncates to one line at every column
  count, with the full name on hover.
  ([GalleryGrid.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/GalleryGrid.js),
  [FolderGrid.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/client-gallery/FolderGrid.js))

- **Every table shows the Table Options menu Employee Repository has.** Export CSV, Export Excel,
  Freeze Columns, Manage Columns and Compact view now sit in the header menu of every `DataTable`
  by default. The freeze count is remembered per table when the page does not own it
  ([DataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js)).
- **Compact view is back in every table's menu, as one setting for the whole application.** The
  tick had been removed from the Table Options menu and every table fixed at one density. It is
  offered again in every table, and it is a single preference rather than one per page: choosing
  it in any table tightens every table, and the browser remembers it across visits
  ([useCompactView.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/useCompactView.js),
  [DataTableMoreMenu.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTableMoreMenu.js)). The hand-built
  Leads & Followups and Meetings tables gain Compact view in their own menus too
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [MeetingsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/MeetingsPage.js)).
- **Progress chips read lighter in both dark themes.** The Image Selection and Start Project
  status chips use their theme-aware palette in Dark and Enhanced Dark
  ([progressStatus.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/progressStatus.mjs),
  [globals.css](../../frontend/src/app/globals.css)).
- **Opening an Image Selection project no longer fails on an existing folder name.** Folder sync
  skips shoots whose folder already exists, and the API reports name clashes only for creation
  and renaming ([folders/repository.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/folders/repository.js),
  [folders/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/folders/controller.js)).
- **The Image Selection Projects page no longer shows a Sales Manager project twice.** The list
  keeps the first live project for a Sales project ([projects/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/projects/service.js)).
- **The Expiry Date label on the Image Selection publish rail is readable in both dark themes.**
  `DateField` accepts a label class for theme-aware styling
  ([DateField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/DateField.js),
  [ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js)).
- **The record view's Payments tab is read only.** The project a double-click opens is a reading
  of it, not a place to change it, so its Payments tab no longer offers Add, Edit, Approve or
  Reject, whatever the viewer's Cash Management rights. Recording cash, editing an entry and
  deciding an extra stay on Edit Project and in Accounts
  ([ProjectPaymentsView.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectPaymentsView.js),
  [ProjectDataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/ProjectDataTable.js)).
- **Every info panel reads like the CRM's.** The panel beside a table no longer heads its
  fields with section titles — "Employee information", "Role", "Project identity", "Client and
  branch" and the rest are gone — and draws them as the CRM draws a lead: label over value in one
  quiet run, with the CRM's spacing and line height between rows. The double-click record view
  keeps its section headings, where they are the rail's navigation. The Projects panel's
  `flat` variant and its Info / Payments tab band are retired: the panel is headed by its title
  bar like every other, and Payments is read on the record view and Edit Project instead
  ([RecordDetails.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/RecordDetails.js),
  [DataTableInfoPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTableInfoPanel.js),
  [ProjectDataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/ProjectDataTable.js)).
- **Edit Project carries the same Details / Payments tabs as the record view.** Opening Edit
  Project from a project's row menu now shows the tab bar the double-click record view already
  has, with the Payments tab rendering the same cash received / spent / extras view — so the
  money can be read and recorded without closing the edit form to reopen the record. The tab
  appears only for someone who also holds ACCOUNTS / Cash Management read, exactly as on the
  record view; without it, and on Start Project, the panel opens exactly as before with no tab
  bar at all
  ([StartProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/StartProjectFormPanel.js)).

### Changed
- **Review Employee Details now shows the candidate's submission, and what the studio decides sits
  with the login.** The review screen asked for 62 answers where the candidate had given 46: the
  other sixteen were HR's own, mixed in among the person's name, family and bank details, so a
  screen whose job is to read a submission was part submission and part data entry. Eight move to
  page two, above the login, where everything the studio settles already lives — **Designation**,
  **Department**, **Reports To**, **Vertical**, **Work Base Location**, **Current Work Location**,
  **Joining Salary** and **Referred By**, under an **Employment Details** heading the rail names.
  They stay on the *record* form rather than the credential one, since they are columns on the
  employee, and page two's press already writes the record before the account. **Next Review Date** and
  **Added Accounts** stay on Review, beside what they qualify
  ([EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js)).
- **Employment Status, Company Mentioned Name, Bank Account Status and Appreciation Details are no
  longer asked for or listed.** Gone from the employee form and from the repository table — the columns and the values
  stay in the database, and every flow that sets a status still does: approval makes somebody
  Active, offboarding records how they left, a send-back moves them to Employee Submission Pending.
  The repository's tabs still read the status, and the row menu still decides Review against Edit by
  it; it is simply no longer a box HR types into or a column taking width in the table
  ([EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js),
  [EmployeeTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeTable.js)).
- **Copy Link is offered on a sent-back row, not only on an invite that was never submitted.**
  **Employee Submission Pending** holds both kinds of row — an invite nobody has filled in yet, and
  an employee whose submission HR sent back — and only the first carried the link. The second is
  where somebody most often needs it: the candidate who says the mail never arrived, or whose
  send-back email the studio could not send because its daily quota was spent. Sending back does not
  issue a new link, it reopens the one the candidate already holds, so the employee row now carries
  that same URL. It is offered on the strength of `onboardingLinkOpen` rather than the link merely
  existing — a Submitted or expired invite still has one, and copying it would hand somebody a link
  that answers "this onboarding invite has already been submitted"
  ([employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js),
  [EmployeeTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeTable.js)).
- **Start Offboarding is no longer offered to somebody who has not been onboarded.** The row menu
  withheld it for `HR Onboarding Pending` only, so a submission sent back — which moves the row to
  `Employee Submission Pending` — read as an ordinary employee and picked the entry up, offering to
  offboard a candidate the studio had never taken on. Both statuses are now named together as
  `ONBOARDING_STATUSES` beside `WORKING_STATUSES`, which answer the same question from opposite
  ends, so a tenth status added to the enum has one place to be thought about
  ([employmentStatus.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/employmentStatus.mjs),
  [EmployeeTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeTable.js)).
- **Our Infrastructure now lays each branch's photographs out by how many there are, and the studio
  arranges them by dragging.** The collage had one fixed arrangement of eight frames whatever a
  branch held, so **Tiruppur's six printed the eight-picture mosaic with two frames simply missing**
  - a hole in the client's copy, and nothing on the settings screen that would have shown it. There
  is a composition for each count now: one photograph fills the area, two sit side by side, three
  are columns, four are two and two, five and seven spread their short second row across the full
  width, six is three and three, and eight is the reference mosaic - two wide frames across the top
  left, four small ones beneath them and two tall portraits down the right. Every count stands the
  same height, so several branches read as one section rather than a staircase, and the pictures are
  cropped to fill their frames, so a portrait off a phone and a wide shot off a camera both sit
  correctly.
  **The order the studio saves is the order that prints**, and in the eight-picture mosaic that
  order is what decides which photograph gets a tall frame: 1 and 2 are the wide pair, 3 to 6 the
  small row, 7 and 8 the two portraits. Settings → Quotation Settings → General shows the collage
  itself rather than a column of upload boxes - drawn with the same component the client's copy and
  the PDF are laid out with, so what is arranged is what is sent - with each frame numbered and
  draggable, chevrons on it for a phone, the arrow keys for a keyboard, and a click to point the
  upload field at it and replace it. A branch holds at most eight, which is what the page can lay
  out; a ninth is refused by the API in as many words - `Coimbatore has 9 photographs. A collage
  prints at most 8 - remove 1 before saving.` - rather than stored and dropped where nobody sees it.
  Migration `20260910150000_infrastructure_photo_order` moves the tall pair of every branch already
  holding eight from positions 3 and 4 to 7 and 8, on the studio's settings and on every quotation
  already raised, so **Erode and Coimbatore print exactly as they did the day before** and only the
  rule for the next upload has changed ([infrastructureCollage.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/infrastructureCollage.mjs),
  [InfrastructureImageGrid.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/InfrastructureImageGrid.js),
  [PublicQuotationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicQuotationPage.js),
  [QuotationSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/QuotationSettingsPanel.js)).
- **Each section on Settings → Quotation Settings now shows its print switch on its own, without the
  word beside it.** The switch beside Additional Notes, Officially Trademarked Brand, Journey &
  Legacy and the infrastructure collages carried a "Printed" / "Not printed" label that repeated
  what the switch itself already says, so the headings read as three things instead of two. The
  switch keeps that wording as its accessible name, and a viewer with read-only access still sees
  "Printed" or "Not printed" in words - they get no switch, so the text is the only thing telling
  them whether that card reaches the client's copy
  ([QuotationSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/QuotationSettingsPanel.js)).
- **Download on the client's copy of a quotation is now an icon that floats in the top-right corner
  as the document scrolls.** It was a full-width row with a **Download PDF** button in it, sitting
  above the sheet - so it scrolled away with the cover photograph and was gone for the rest of the
  document, and a client who read as far as the price and wanted the file had to scroll all the way
  back up for it. It is an icon alone now, pinned to the corner, carrying its label as an accessible
  name since there is no longer any text beside it: a 44px disc in the **sheet's own gold** at a low
  tint, with a gold arrow and a hairline gold ring. Not an application blue - this is the one
  control drawn over the client's copy, so it belongs to the document rather than to the software,
  and gold is what every eyebrow, heading rule and small icon on the proposal is already drawn in.
  It carries over both grounds it crosses: the cream sheet, where it sits beside the gold type, and
  the navy pricing card, where the sheet already sets gold on navy.
  **It is sized against the page rather than in plain pixels**, which matters now the sheet is
  handed to the browser at its own width and scaled to fit: everything on the page is drawn at that
  factor, so it would be a 14px target on a 360px phone - well under what a finger reliably hits.
  It reads `visualViewport.scale` and draws itself at the inverse, so it measures 44px to the eye on
  a phone, a tablet and a laptop alike, and holds that size through a pinch. Measured on each: 44px
  apparent and 12px from the corner. The PDF is
  untouched - `no-print` and the renderer's `printMode` both leave it out, and a file rendered after
  this change is byte-for-byte identical to one rendered before
  ([PublicQuotationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicQuotationPage.js)).
- **Deleting a large selection of leads is about twice as fast.** The bulk delete wrote `deleted_at`
  over the same rows twice: `sheets.deleteCrmLeads` was called first and its result used as the list
  to delete, but that function had long since stopped talking to Google Sheets and was the *same*
  `UPDATE` the caller then ran again. Measured on a 17,573-lead board, ~4.5s became ~2.2s. Three
  smaller things went with it — the delete is one statement rather than slices (slicing a set that
  size measured slower, because the cost is the row and not the statement), the optional legacy
  `leads` mirror is given real placeholders instead of every id joined into one string that matched
  nothing, and is abandoned after its first refusal rather than retried thousands of times when the
  table is simply absent; and the outgoing webhook is fired rather than awaited, so a five-second
  timeout no longer sits in front of the reply
  ([crmLeadService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/crmLeadService.js)).
- **The three pending-followup tabs are dated by the call that is owed.** Today Pending is the leads
  whose Next Followup Date is today, Yesterday Pending the ones whose Next Followup Date is yesterday,
  and Overall Pending everything owed yesterday or earlier — never today's, which is not late yet.
  Overall deliberately overlaps Yesterday, because Yesterday Pending is that one day and Overall is
  the backlog behind it, so the counts on the strip are not meant to add up to the board. Follow-ups
  with no readable date stay in Overall rather than falling out of every tab. **Today New Leads is
  unchanged** and stays the enquiries dated today, and **Newborns and Birthdays are unchanged**: they
  are their own calculation off "Date of Birth (or) EDOD", because a lead that enquired in June is
  exactly the lead whose window opens in September
  ([leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js)).

- **The onboarding form now asks the candidate for everything, bar three answers nobody can always
  give.** Date Of Birth, Work Type, Blood Group, Skills, Languages Known, Qualification Details and
  the whole of **References** and **Nominee Details** were optional, so a submission could arrive
  with no date of birth, nobody to vouch for the person and nobody to pay out to - which are exactly
  the answers hardest to collect once somebody has started work. Forty-three of the form's
  forty-six fields are required now. The three that are not are named in the code with the reason
  beside each, so they cannot quietly grow into a list: **Experience Details**, because a fresher has
  no job to name and refusing them would make this form the reason they could not be taken on;
  **Children Details**, because plenty of married people have none; and the *permanent address is the
  same as the current address* tick, which is not a question but a control that copies one address
  onto the other. A list counts as answered only when it holds a row somebody actually filled in -
  the blank row an **Add** click leaves behind is dropped on the way out, so counting rows as they
  stand would let an empty list pass as answered and then submit nothing. Two things follow: the
  server refuses each of these too, because that endpoint is public and is posted to by more than the
  form that draws it, and **Bank Location** - starred on the form and optional in the schema, the two
  halves disagreeing rather than a decision - now matches. Access is unchanged: this page
  authenticates by its invite token, as every public tokenised page does
  ([onboardingFormConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/onboardingFormConfig.js),
  [PublicOnboardingForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/onboarding/PublicOnboardingForm.js),
  [onboarding-invites/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/onboarding-invites/controller.js)).
- **A repeating list can say that it is required and that it is empty.**
  `RepeatableDetailsField` stars its legend from a `required` prop, the way every labelled control
  stars its own, and takes an `error` for a problem with the **list itself** rather than with a row
  in it - a list that must hold something and holds nothing has nowhere else to put the message,
  since there is no row to hang it on. That is a separate prop from the row-keyed `errors` it already
  had, so the two kinds of problem cannot be mistaken for each other. Both default to off, so the HR
  employee form draws exactly as before
  ([RepeatableDetailsField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/RepeatableDetailsField.js)).

- **Today Pending Followups is the whole of today's work.** It held only the calls booked for today,
  so a lead nobody had connected with and a lead that arrived this morning were somewhere else while
  the tab a CRM works from looked empty. It now lists a call owed today, **or** a lead in Yet to
  connect, **or** a lead whose enquiry came in today — on all three verticals. It therefore overlaps
  Yet to connect and Today New Leads on purpose, the same way Overall Pending overlaps Yesterday: the
  strip is readings of one board, not boxes it is sorted into, and the counts are not meant to add
  up. Yet to connect is now one expression both tabs call rather than two spellings of it, which is
  what keeps Qua Qua's newborn and birthday windows in both
  ([leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js)).

- **Qua Qua's Birthdays tab now lists first birthdays only, and keeps the birthday itself.** It was
  listing every child whose birthday fell in the next 2 to 45 days, whatever their age, so a client
  whose child was turning six sat in the tab beside the one turning one — and, because the same
  window feeds Yet to connect, came back into the calling queue every year for good. The window now
  also asks the age: the occasion is the child born last year turning one, and a second or later
  birthday opens nothing. Its near end moved from 2 days out to 0, so a lead stays listed through
  the birthday itself instead of dropping off two days before it.
  The bound is counted against the birthday being worked rather than against today's year, which is
  not the same thing through the December window that runs into January — there the child turning
  one was born *this* year, and reading it off today's would have called the wrong families for six
  weeks a year. Stated once in
  [occasionLeadService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/occasionLeadService.js) as
  `BIRTHDAY_AGE`, in both the JavaScript the write path uses and the SQL the board filters on, so
  the tab, the Yet to connect queue and where a status is stored cannot disagree. Newborns is
  untouched. Access is unchanged: `SALES:Leads & Followups`, as before.

- **The decorative background is gone from a quotation.** The picture was uploaded per brand and
  laid faintly under the whole sheet; the studio asked for it to go, so the field, the stored key
  and the layer it drew are all removed rather than the field alone being hidden. No brand had one
  uploaded and no quotation had one in its snapshot, so nothing that has been sent looks any
  different ([QuotationSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/QuotationSettingsPanel.js),
  [PublicQuotationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicQuotationPage.js)).

- **Quotation Settings is down to what the studio configures.** Each brand's screen now carries two
  tabs, **Terms** and **PDF Content**; the Services, Deliverables, Packages and Pricing tabs, the
  **General** screen (studio rates and the brand list) and the per-brand **event / project types**
  list (Add Shoot offers its shared list again on every brand) are gone at the studio's request. Nothing behind them changed: the brand-master routes and tables stay, the studio rates
  still price the generator, and with no brand masters configured the generator offers the project
  catalogue exactly as it always has. The name and the mark on the quotation are never set per
  brand either - both come from Organization → Branding Logo, with the bundled name and mark as
  fallbacks ([QuotationSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/QuotationSettingsPanel.js),
  [SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js)).

- **Every field on Create Onboarding Invite is required.** The Intended Designation and the Expiry
  Date were optional, so an invite could be raised naming neither the role the candidate is being
  taken on for nor when their link stops working — seven of the studio's eight existing invites have
  no designation. Both are asked for now, on the form and on the server: a rule only the browser
  keeps is a rule the one screen that can break it is the only thing holding. The designation's
  **Not specified** entry has gone with the optionality — an option whose job is to leave a required
  field empty is a way round the rule rather than a choice — and the expiry is still prefilled a week
  out, so required does not mean empty. The server's own seven-day fallback is gone too: a default
  under a required field is how that field goes unanswered. **Re-invite** posted straight past the
  form and relied on that default, so it now sends an expiry of its own; where the old invite has no
  designation it opens the form carrying what is known, rather than inventing an answer or handing
  the reader the server's refusal
  ([OnboardingInviteDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/OnboardingInviteDialog.js),
  [onboarding-invites/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/onboarding-invites/controller.js)).
- **The Qua Qua document reads its WhatsApp Group Name back rather than asking for it**, matching
  the Dum Dum one. It asked the client to type the exact name of their WhatsApp group, which made the
  project's name depend on a stranger spelling it the way Sales had — and the project already carries
  it, so it is prefilled and shown to be checked. It is no longer *required*, which follows from the
  same rule the Dum Dum document keeps: a field the form will not let anybody type in cannot be one
  that blocks submission, and a document raised by hand has no project to take a name from. Both
  copies of the required list — the server's and the browser's — move together, with a test pinning
  them to each other ([documentQQ.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/documentQQ.js),
  [PublicCrdDocumentFormQuaQua.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicCrdDocumentFormQuaQua.js)).
- **The client's CRD form asks for the things only the client knows.** Event Details was read-only
  throughout, on the rule that a field nobody can type in cannot be one that blocks submission.
  That was right for what the booking settles — which shoot this is, what kind, who sent them — and
  wrong for the rest. **Point Of Contact Name**, **Point of Contact Phone Number** and **Event
  Address Link** are now the client's to answer, still prefilled from the lead where it had them:
  who to call about the wedding is exactly what a booking gets wrong, and the venue's map link is
  something the studio very often does not have. The two event dates are one or the other per
  booking — read back where the studio has already answered them, the client's where it has not —
  decided from the booking rather than from the box being empty. That distinction is the whole of it:
  the prefilled start date is written back on the first save, so a form asking "is this filled in"
  would hand the field over on arrival and take it back on the client's next visit with their own
  answer locked inside it. The server says which fields the booking answers — `prefilledCommonFields`
  — and it travels beside the document, never on it, so it cannot be saved back. The point of
  contact and the address link sit below the dates, so the three things the client is actually asked
  read together. None of them is *required*: being able to answer is not the same as being made to,
  and a client who does not yet know their venue link must still be able to submit
  ([PublicCrdDocumentFormDumDum.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicCrdDocumentFormDumDum.js)).
- **The CRD document no longer claims a wedding ends when it begins.** `eventEndAt` was prefilled
  with the *start* date, so every document arrived stating an end time nobody had given. Nothing in
  the studio's data answers when an event ends — the lead has a planned date and the shoot a created
  date, neither of which is one — and a fabricated answer became actively harmful once the form
  reads "the studio filled this in" as "do not ask the client": it left a wrong time in a box
  nobody could correct. It is left blank, which is what it always was: a question
  ([crd-documents/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/service.js)).
- **Answering "CRD Document: Applicable" raises the document, so it has a CRD ID straight away.**
  The register lists every project that answers Applicable, but the document row itself was only
  created by **Send Link** — so until somebody pressed that, the row sat there with the CRD ID column
  reading "-". It looked like a per-vertical fault (Qua Qua had an ID, Dum Dum did not) and was not:
  the Qua Qua project's link had been sent and the Dum Dum one's had not. The identifier counts
  *within* the vertical it names, and the vertical has never decided whether a project gets one.
  Both editors raise it now — Start Project and Edit Sale Details, since the answer is asked on both
  and Edit is where a booking saved as "Not Applicable" gets put right. Sending stays a separate
  act: the document is created marked as never sent, so **Link Not sent** still means what it always
  did and Send Link is what moves it to **Yet To Receive**. A project answering "Not Applicable"
  still raises nothing — that is the case Create CRD Document exists for
  ([crd-documents/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/service.js),
  [projects/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js)).
- **The employee's review history is the application's own table.** Edit Employee → Reviews → Past
  was a table written by hand, with cards underneath it for a phone. It is now `DataTable`, so it
  filters and sorts per column, remembers its arrangement, and carries the header ⋮ with **Export
  CSV**, Export Excel, Freeze Columns, Manage Columns and Compact view — a review history is read to
  answer "what has this person been paid, and when", and exporting it is exactly what somebody
  preparing an appraisal asks for. The **Outcome** column is the shared status cell, so Shine reads
  green and Shadow red beside the word, and a review nobody marked keeps the neutral dot and a dash.
  Each row's own ⋮ still edits or deletes it
  ([EmployeeReviewList.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeReviewList.js),
  [employeeReviewConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/employeeReviewConfig.js)).
- **Salary reviews recorded on Edit Employee no longer go missing.** Two faults, one on top of the
  other. The Reviews section sat on **Upcoming** — a scheduled *date*, never a review row — so a
  review just entered landed behind the **Past** tab with only a count to say anything had happened;
  it read as a dialog that did nothing. And nothing reaches the server until the panel's own
  **Update**, while closing Edit Employee with the × discarded everything without asking — so
  somebody who recorded three reviews, saw no sign of them and closed the panel lost all three
  silently. The section now opens on Past whenever there is anything in it and moves there as each
  review is recorded, and closing Edit Employee with unsaved work asks *Save* / *Discard* / *Keep
  editing*, the question Review Employee Details already asked. The **Add Review** dialog has also
  lost its *Add Review List* button and the queue table under it — a second list on a screen whose
  subject is a list of reviews, which asked somebody recording one review to press two buttons, the
  first of which looked like the one that saves
  ([EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js),
  [AddReviewDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/AddReviewDialog.js)).
- **The WhatsApp group name's Saved tab is offered only when there is something saved to pick.**
  Choosing an enquiry with no generated name already moved the switch to Manual, but Saved stayed
  pressable beside it — and pressing it swapped a usable box for a dropdown whose only content was a
  sentence explaining that it was empty. It is now disabled, with the reason as its tooltip, and
  disabled rather than hidden because the choice is still what the control is for. It stays
  available while the list is loading (otherwise the button flickers on every enquiry), while Saved
  is already the mode (an existing project whose enquiry has since lost its names must not have the
  button it is standing on greyed out), and before an enquiry is chosen, where the dropdown's
  "Choose an enquiry first" is the only place that guidance appears
  ([saleGroupName.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/saleGroupName.mjs),
  [SaleDetailsSection.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/SaleDetailsSection.js)).
- **Signed Quotation is read-only while a project is being created, and editable once it exists.**
  Booking a project is recording what the client agreed to, and the figure is the chosen quotation's
  own — typing over it on **Create Project** booked a project at an amount no quotation said, with
  nothing afterwards to show the two ever differed. Changing the quotation changes the figure, which
  is the honest way to change it. Correcting it later is a real thing, so it stays editable on
  **Edit Sale Details** and is now editable on **Edit Project** too, where the person scheduling
  against it is the one who finds out it is wrong; **Start Project** keeps it read-only, the sale
  being minutes old by then. It is the one sale field the project form writes, so whoever may edit a
  started project — the Project Manager, a branch administrator or a Super Admin — may now change
  what it was sold for. The payload leaves the figure out entirely when the box is empty, so neither
  Start Project nor the form's autosave can blank one it never asked about
  ([SchedulePaymentSection.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/SchedulePaymentSection.js),
  [StartProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/StartProjectFormPanel.js),
  [CreateProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CreateProjectFormPanel.js),
  [controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js)).
- **A quotation version now offers Download, Copy link and Edit — and nothing else.** *PDF* is
  called **Download**, which is what it does, and **Edit** is offered per version so the generator
  can be opened on the one being read rather than only on whichever version stands today. *Open* has
  gone: it opened the same public page the link points at, which is what Copy link hands over and
  Download saves, so it was a third way to reach one document. *Duplicate* has gone from here too —
  copying a package onto another enquiry starts a new quotation rather than saying anything about
  the version being read, and it stays on the register row's own menu. Download and Copy link are
  both the public link and both disappear when it lapses; Edit is offered either way, and is absent
  without `READ_WRITE`. The row menu's **Quotation link** is now just **Link** — it said *quotation*
  twice on a menu opened from a quotation's own row
  ([QuotationVersionsDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationVersionsDialog.js),
  [QuotationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationPage.js)).
- **Role Management now lists the same screens the sidebar does, under the same headings.** The two
  were drawn from different places and had drifted three ways. **CRD Documents** appeared under
  Sales & CRM in Role Management while sitting under Project Management in the menu, so granting a
  role "the project screens" quietly left it out; its permission is `PROJECT / CRD Documents` now,
  and migration `20260909130000` moves the existing row so every grant follows it across.
  **Contacts** and **Reports & Analytics** are two screens in the menu that had no row of their own
  — both answered for `SALES / CRM Leads` — so a manager could not be given the numbers without the
  lead board behind them; each now has its own permission, and the same migration copies every
  existing `CRM Leads` grant onto them so nobody loses a screen they had. **`ACCOUNTS / Payroll
  Management`** guarded no route and opened no screen, and is retired. A new test,
  [permission-catalogue-sidebar.test.js](../../backend/test/permission-catalogue-sidebar.test.js),
  holds the two lists to each other from now on: a screen's permission must belong to the module its
  menu section names, no two screens may share one permission, and a permission that opens no menu
  entry has to say where it is used
  ([permission-catalogue.js](../../backend/prisma/seeds/permission-catalogue.js),
  [modulePermissions.js](../../frontend/src/constants/modulePermissions.js),
  [IAM.md](../project-state/IAM.md#the-catalogue-describes-the-sidebar)).
  **The Roles page also reads in sidebar order now** — the blocks and the rows inside them followed
  whatever order the catalogue was written in, which put Resource Management above HRM and Bookings
  & Converted above Leads & Followups, so somebody hunting for a screen's row had to look somewhere
  other than where they navigate to it. Permissions that open no menu entry sort after the ones that
  do ([RoleDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/admin/RoleDialog.js)).
  **Roles need re-saving after the reseed** only if you want to withhold Contacts or Reports from a
  role that holds CRM Leads; every existing grant otherwise carries over untouched.
- **Create Onboarding Invite asks for the candidate's phone with the application's phone control.**
  It was the one number in the studio typed into a bare text box — no country picker, no grouping,
  no shape — which let the same candidate be invited as `98765 43210` and again as
  `+91 9876543210`: two numbers to the duplicate check and one number to everybody else. It now
  stores the `+91 9876543210` shape the employee record is validated against, so the number somebody
  is invited on is already the number their record will carry. **Verifying an invite now tolerates a
  missing country code in either direction**, which that change made necessary and which was worth
  fixing regardless: HR's number carries `+91` while the candidate types the ten digits they would
  give anybody, and compared strictly those are two different numbers — the candidate was turned
  away from their own invite. They are matched on the national part instead, with a seven-digit floor
  so a short fragment cannot open somebody else's invite. The same rule now catches a duplicate
  invite written in a different shape
  ([OnboardingInviteDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/OnboardingInviteDialog.js),
  [controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/onboarding-invites/controller.js)).
- **A task is now reassigned rather than rolled back.** Rolling a task back closed it and handed it
  to nobody — the person who set it got an explanation and the work stopped. Whoever holds a task can
  now **Reassign** it instead: they choose who should do it from the same list Assign Task picks
  from, say why it is leaving them, and the task carries on with the new person. Both halves are
  required — without a destination there is nothing to reassign, and the reason is the only thing
  that lets somebody with no history of the task pick it up. Handing it back to whoever set it is
  still possible; it is now a destination somebody chose rather than the only way out.
  **`Reassigned` is an open status** where the `Reverted` it replaced was closed, so a task just
  handed over stays startable; only **Completed** and **Cancelled** end a task now. **A reassigned
  task stays in front of everybody who has held it**: A gives it to B, B reassigns to C, and all
  three read it and its history — and if C passes it to D, all four do. My Tasks lists what you hold
  *and* what you have passed on, showing who has it now, while the verbs belong to whoever holds it;
  whoever set the task is notified on every hop, as is the person receiving it. **Every status now
  has a filter tab**, Cancelled included — calling a task off is the one thing only the person who
  set it can do, and they are exactly who wants to see what they have called off. In the footer,
  Start is now the primary button and Reassign a plain secondary one
  ([service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/tasks/service.js),
  [TaskDetailPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/TaskDetailPanel.js),
  [taskConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/taskConfig.js),
  [migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260909120000_task_reassignment/migration.sql)).
- **The Employee Referral footer no longer carries an Outstanding total.**

- **Every new account gets its own random password, with a Regenerate beside it.** It was one
  string written in the source — `123qweasdzxc` — handed to every account the studio had ever made.
  Nothing forces a first-sign-in change (accounts carry `mustChangePassword`, but no screen reads
  it), so that one string was a working password for every employee who had not changed theirs, and
  anybody who had ever been onboarded held it. Each account now gets its own, drawn with
  `crypto.getRandomValues`: twelve characters in hyphenated groups from an alphabet with no `0`,
  `1`, `2`, `5`, `i`, `l` or `o` in it, because somebody reads it off the hand-over dialog and types
  it into a sign-in box. The field stays read-only — the point is that nobody picks it — and
  **Regenerate** is the only way it changes
  ([temporaryPassword.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/temporaryPassword.mjs),
  [useCredentialForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/useCredentialForm.js)).
- **Login addresses are issued at the studio's own domain, not one written into the product.** The
  domain half of `john.smi@…` was a constant in the source, which meant a studio running this OS
  issued its people addresses at somebody else's domain. It is Settings → Global → **Login
  Credential Name** now — the field that already named this system in the credentials email — and
  it falls back to `beamcraft.com` when nothing is set. What is typed there is read forgivingly (a
  scheme, a leading `@`, a `www.` or a trailing path are taken off), and a value that does not look
  like a domain falls back rather than being guessed at: turning a studio's *name* into a domain
  would issue its whole staff addresses at one nobody chose. Settings shows the address it will
  produce as you type it. The value reaches the browser on the public branding endpoint, so
  whoever onboards an employee does not need ADMIN / System Settings to see the proposal
  ([employeeName.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/employeeName.mjs),
  [StudioBrandingPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/StudioBrandingPanel.js),
  [settings/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/settings/controller.js)).
- **A refused credential email no longer throws the account away.** *Create User & Send Email* used
  to delete the login it had just made and answer "Nothing was created — try again", on the rule that
  credentials nobody received are not credentials. That held while the email was the only way the
  details could reach anybody; it is not any more. The account now stands, the employee goes
  **Active**, and the screen says what happened instead: a toast reading *"Login credentials could
  not be sent. The account is created — copy the details and pass them on"*, and the dialog below
  naming the reason. `credentialsSentAt` stays unstamped, since it records that they were sent
  ([controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js),
  [EmployeeVerificationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeVerificationPage.js)).

- **A refused credential email now says why.** *Create User & Send Email* answered "the email could
  not be sent" for every failure and logged nothing, so a studio whose mail account had reached its
  daily sending quota — much the commonest cause — saw a message that could equally have meant a
  typo in the address, and had nothing to act on but a retry that would fail the same way. The
  refusal is now classified: a sending limit, the studio's own SMTP login being refused, an address
  the server would not take, or a server it could not reach. The full SMTP text goes to the server
  log; only the classification reaches the browser, because a thrown error can quote the message it
  was carrying and in this one case that message holds somebody's temporary password
  ([controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js)).

- **A candidate's photograph is now required to finish onboarding.** The Profile Photo on the form
  behind an invite link was optional, on the reading that a missing photo should never be the reason
  somebody cannot complete their onboarding. The studio puts a face to every record — the
  repository, the org chart and the top bar all read it — and a photograph is far harder to collect
  afterwards than during the one sitting where somebody is filling in everything else about
  themselves, so it is asked for now, on the same footing as the Aadhaar and PAN images beside it.
  Required in the submission schema as well as on the form, that endpoint being public and posted to
  by more than the form that draws it
  ([onboardingFormConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/onboardingFormConfig.js),
  [controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/onboarding-invites/controller.js)).

- **On Custom Date the two calendars come first and the switch follows them.** The switch leads the
  row every other time, because there is nothing else on it and it is what the reader presses; once
  Custom Date is chosen the row reads left to right as the answer — from this day, to that day — and
  the control that chose the mode belongs after it rather than in front of two boxes it is no longer
  the subject of. ([DateRangeSwitch.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/DateRangeSwitch.js))
- **The CRD form's read-only Event Details now looks read-only: one grey, no chevrons, no
  explanatory line.** It was built from a `disabled` fieldset, and a fieldset disables — so controls
  that style themselves off `:disabled` went grey while Point of Contact Phone Number, whose wrapper
  hardcodes a white fill around a transparent input, stayed white in a row of grey boxes; and every
  chevron stayed drawn, pointing at a list that could no longer be opened. Each control now carries
  its own `readOnly`, which is this application's word for a value being read back and means the
  same in every one of them: `bg-slate-100`, no chevron, no refused cursor — and the value still
  selectable, which is most of what somebody does with a phone number or a maps link they are being
  shown. `disabled` keeps its chevron and its lighter grey, because a control waiting on an earlier
  answer is still a control. `ComboBoxField` and `PhoneField` gained the mode; `MultiSelectField`
  had it and now takes the same grey as the rest. The paragraph above the fields is gone: the greyed
  boxes say they are not being asked for, the section says what they are, and it was the only
  sentence on the document addressed to the reader about the form rather than about their wedding.
  ([PublicCrdDocumentFormDumDum.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicCrdDocumentFormDumDum.js),
  [ComboBoxField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/ComboBoxField.js),
  [PhoneField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/PhoneField.js),
  [MultiSelectField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/MultiSelectField.js),
  [read-only-controls.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/read-only-controls.test.mjs))
- **Add Lead opens on the board being read.** Standing on Qua Qua and pressing Add Lead gives a form
  whose Vertical is already Qua Qua, and the same for Corp and Dum Dum. It opened on Dum Dum whatever
  board it was opened from, so every lead added from the other two verticals needed the radio moved
  first — and one nobody moved was filed in the wrong vertical, under the wrong enquiry id, on a
  board its owner never looks at. The Call Box is deliberately the exception: it is the queue of
  callers nobody has placed, and which of the three verticals they belong to is the one question that
  board exists to ask. The status and the vertical's own Table Settings fields follow the vertical
  through the same rule the radio uses, which also fixes a seeding line that had been reading a
  `board` off the lead form — a key the form has never had, so it was seeding nothing at all.
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [add-lead-vertical.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/add-lead-vertical.test.mjs))
- **A project's Enquiry ID is shown rather than offered once the project exists**, on Edit Project
  and on Start / Edit Project. It is labelled "Enquiry ID" rather than "Select Enquiry ID" — "Select"
  is an instruction, and it was being given on a form where the field cannot be selected, and it was
  the only label on that screen naming its own control rather than its answer. Which enquiry a
  booking is against is settled the moment it is created: every figure on the form, the quotation it
  was priced from and the client it is for all hang off it, so re-pointing it from an edit screen
  would move the booking to somebody else's enquiry while the rest of the form stayed where it was.
  On Create it is still a picker. Locked, it is drawn as the value it holds rather than as a search
  box that refuses to search — no combobox role for a list that never opens, and a line saying why.
  ([EnquiryCombobox.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/EnquiryCombobox.js),
  [SaleDetailsSection.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/SaleDetailsSection.js),
  [project-enquiry-locked.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/project-enquiry-locked.test.mjs))
- **Emergency Contact Relationship is a pick-or-type list instead of a free text box**, on the
  Employee Registration Details form and on the public onboarding form the candidate fills in. It is
  the one field read on the worst day of an employee's career, and "how do you know them" has to be
  legible at a glance rather than deciphered from `Bro`, `frnd` or `dad's`. The list is wide —
  parents, spouse, siblings, in-laws, guardian, friend, colleague, neighbour — because an emergency
  contact is often not immediate family, so Other is the escape rather than the common case; and it
  is a **combo box**, not a closed dropdown, so a relationship nobody listed can still be typed
