  rather than being refused for the sake of tidiness on the one field where recording the truth
  matters most. It is the same `ComboBoxField` the CRM's own Relationship field uses. Both screens
  read one list, so HR can always choose the answer a candidate gave. Every *other* closed select on
  those two forms also now keeps a stored value that is not on its list rather than showing it as
  unanswered, which the shared schema-form field did not do the way the Employee Repository's own
  form already did.
  ([employeeOptions.js](../../frontend/src/constants/employeeOptions.js),
  [EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js),
  [onboardingFormConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/onboardingFormConfig.js),
  [SchemaFormFields.js](../../frontend/src/components/schema-form/SchemaFormFields.js),
  [emergency-contact-relationship.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/emergency-contact-relationship.test.mjs))
- **The navigation drawer is tighter, so more of it fits without scrolling.** The screens under each
  module were the loosest part and are the part that was asked for: the gaps between rows are gone
  and a row's own padding is reduced, so a screen and a group heading are both 28px and rows sit
  flush — a row's hover fill separates it from its neighbours better than two pixels of nothing did.
  The frame came with them: the search box, the nav's padding, the gap between groups and the footer.
  Reducing the rows and leaving the frame is the change that looks like it did nothing — twenty rows
  six pixels shorter is 120px, and the frame is another 100px. A nav of seven groups and twenty
  screens is read by scanning rather than by aiming, and Accounts and Resource Management at the
  bottom were the sections nobody was discovering.
  ([AppSidebar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppSidebar.js),
  [app-sidebar-density.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/app-sidebar-density.test.mjs))
- **Contacts is one row per record instead of one row per person.** A client who enquired for a
  wedding, enquired again for a shoot and became a project is three rows, each under its own id,
  with its own name, its own vertical and its own module — and each opening its own record. The ids
  used to arrive concatenated into one cell, `DBS-EID-DD-ERD-000012, DBS-EID-QQ-HYD-000007`, which
  could not be filtered to either of them, could not be sorted by either, exported as one string,
  and gave the row a single name and vertical where the two enquiries have their own; a double click
  had to pick one, so the second enquiry was reachable from nowhere on the screen. The phone number
  still says which rows are one person — it is the Contact column, it is what a reader filters by to
  bring somebody's rows together, and the list is ordered by it so a person's records arrive next to
  each other rather than a fortnight apart.
  ([service.js](../../backend/src/modules/contacts/directory/service.js),
  [ContactsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/contacts/ContactsPage.js),
  [contacts-directory.test.js](../../backend/test/contacts-directory.test.js))
- **Client Gifts: the first tab and status read "Yet To Sent" instead of "Yet To Receive".** It
  named the wrong end of the journey — a gift sitting in it has not been *sent* yet, and "yet to
  receive" reads as the studio waiting on something rather than owing something. Gifts already
  recorded under the old word read as the new one, so nothing had to be rewritten and no tab or menu
  is left holding rows it cannot move.
  ([service.js](../../backend/src/modules/project-management/client-gifts/service.js),
  [ClientGiftsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ClientGiftsPage.js),
  [client-gift-status.test.js](../../backend/test/client-gift-status.test.js))
- **Moving a gift along re-reads the Client Gifts screen rather than correcting the one cell it
  wrote.** A row carries fields that screen does not write — the project's manager and status, the
  client's name and number, who the gift is assigned to — and a status is very often changed because
  something else about the project has just moved, so those went on showing what they said when the
  page was opened. The footer's "Last updated" was saying the time of that first load however many
  changes had been made since. The row still lands from the write immediately, so the press feels
  the same; the re-read behind it is silent, so the table does not blank under it.
  ([ClientGiftsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ClientGiftsPage.js))
- **The CRD form asks for the event once, under Event Details, and shows that whole section
  read-only.** Event Start, Event End, Event Address Link and "what else can we help you with" were
  asked of the groom *and* of the bride — two answers to each of four questions about one wedding,
  with nothing on the document to say which was right. They are asked once now, after the point of
  contact and with no heading of their own, and the identification fields above them are reordered:
  Referred By follows Event Type, Point Of Contact Name follows Point of Contact Phone Number. The
  whole section is what Sales already knows from the booking, so the client is shown it to check
  rather than asked it. **Nothing in it blocks submission** — a field the form will not let anybody
  type in cannot be one that holds the document up, and Point of Contact Name is routinely blank on
  a wedding lead, which would have stranded the document with no screen anywhere able to fix it.
  What the client must answer is both sides in full and the terms. Documents saved under the old
  shape keep their answers: the event is read up onto the common block on the next read.
  ([document.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/document.js),
  [PublicCrdDocumentFormDumDum.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicCrdDocumentFormDumDum.js),
  [CrdDocumentResponse.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CrdDocumentResponse.js),
  [crd-event-details.test.js](../../backend/test/crd-event-details.test.js))
- **Leads & Followups: Custom Date's two ends are labelled From and To.** They carried the words as
  placeholders, and a placeholder is the first thing a narrow box truncates — so the pair arrived on
  the header as two bare calendar icons with nothing to say which end each was, which is a range
  picker nobody can set. ([DateRangeSwitch.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/DateRangeSwitch.js),
  [DateField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/DateField.js))
- **Sending an onboarding invite closes the dialog and says so on the page behind it.** Sending the
  link is the last thing that card is open for, and it used to stay exactly where it was with a
  disabled button reading "Invite Sent" and nothing left to do but press Close. A failure still
  stays on the card, where the Copy Link fallback is.
  ([OnboardingInviteDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/OnboardingInviteDialog.js),
  [onboarding-invite-sent.test.js](../../backend/test/onboarding-invite-sent.test.js))
- **The Calls board is called Call Box, and the board switch is in the studio's own order: Dum Dum,
  Qua Qua, Corp, Call Box.** The switch alphabetised itself, which put the queue that feeds the three
  verticals above all of them and the vertical most of the studio works every day third. Only the
  label and the order changed: the board is still keyed `Calls`, which is what `CRM_TABLES`,
  `BOARD_TABS` and the page endpoint's `board` parameter all read, so renaming that to change a
  heading would have been renaming a wire format.
  ([crmBoards.js](../../frontend/src/lib/crmBoards.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js))
- **A Call ID is now `DBS-CLID-000009`, and every code minted as `DBS-CID-…` has been rewritten to
  it.** Read out beside an Enquiry ID the old tag was one syllable from it, and a Call ID heard as an
  `EID` sends whoever heard it looking for a lead nobody created. The tag is rewritten in place on
  the first read after deploying — only the tag, never the number, so it is a rename of every code
  and not a renumbering of the log — and nothing is left under the old form, because two formats
  standing side by side on one board is the fault the change was meant to end.
  ([identifiers.js](../../backend/src/services/identifiers.js),
  [callLogService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/callLogService.js),
  [identifiers.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/identifiers.mjs))
- **Call Box → Not Connected is now the calls the studio did not answer, rather than every call of
  zero length.** It held two unrelated things: a number the studio rang that did not pick up is the
  client being unavailable, while a client who rang and got no answer is the studio failing to take
  the call — and only the second is work anybody has to do something about, which is what the tab is
  read for. It is now Callyzer's Missed and Rejected, which are inbound by definition, so the tab and
  the Status column's *Never Attended* say one thing about a row. Unanswered outgoing calls are read
  under Outgoing instead; nothing is hidden, it is filed under the thing it is.
  ([callBoardService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/callBoardService.js),
  [crm-lead-tabs.test.js](../../backend/test/crm-lead-tabs.test.js))
- **The bell gates each kind of stored notice against its own module, instead of gating them all on
  `HRM / Task Management`.** That was right while Tasks was the only module writing notices and
  became wrong the moment the CRM did: a salesperson without the Tasks permission would have been the
  one person never told a lead was theirs, and a task-only reader would have been shown a client's
  name off a lead they cannot open. Each type names the permission it answers for, the unread count
  is taken over the same rows the panel lists, and a type nobody has mapped reaches nobody.
  ([notifications.js](../../backend/src/services/notifications.js),
  [controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/notifications/controller.js),
  [notifications.test.js](../../backend/test/notifications.test.js))

- **Recent Projects has the filter row every other table has.** A box under each header narrows the
  table by what is typed there — a project name, a client, a status. It asks a different question
  from the cards above it, which say what state a project is in rather than which project, and the
  two narrow together: the card first, then what is typed. The count in the footer is whatever
  survived both
  ([DashboardRecentProjects.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/dashboard/DashboardRecentProjects.js)).
- **Recent Projects is fifteen rows tall, and the rest are scrolled to.** The panel shows fifteen
  projects and stops there however many the studio has, so the dashboard below it does not move as
  the register grows; scrolling inside the panel brings the next fifteen, and the head and its
  filter row stay put while it does. There are no page controls
  ([DashboardRecentProjects.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/dashboard/DashboardRecentProjects.js)).
- **A dashboard project row is picked out by one click and opened by two.** Recent Projects used
  to leave the page on a single click, which took a reader off the dashboard while they were still
  reading down it. A click now highlights the row and nothing else, and a double click opens the
  project. It is the gesture the Projects, Accounts, Role Management and User Management tables
  already answer to, so a row here behaves as a row does anywhere else. The Action column went with
  the change: it held one button, repeated on every row, for the one thing the row already does
  ([DashboardRecentProjects.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/dashboard/DashboardRecentProjects.js)).
- **Settings → Global Logo is now Settings → Organization, with two screens under it.** Branding Logo
  is the form that was there — the Global Name, the logo and the favicon, unchanged — and Branches
  sits beside it, empty until what it holds is decided. The heading was named after one of its own
  fields while it also carried the name and the favicon; Branding is what all of it is, and a
  heading with screens under it is what lets the next one be added without renaming anything
  ([SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js)). The logo screen
  keeps its old id, so an unsaved edit is still caught on the way out, a deep link still lands on
  it, and whoever was last on it still returns there. Both answer to the same ADMIN / System
  Settings write the section already did.
- **The dashboard has no View All links.** The one on Client Selection Progress and the one on
  Recent Projects are gone, and so is the shared component behind them. Both went to the Projects
  page, which the sidebar and the module header already reach; on Recent Projects it also read as
  the way to widen a filtered table, which is what the chip beside the heading undoes
  ([DashboardCard.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/dashboard/DashboardCard.js)).
- **Client Selection Progress is now the clients who are nearly finished.** The panel lists the
  projects whose client is between 85% and 99% through their selection, closest to finishing
  first, in place of whatever moved inside the range. That is the list a studio acts on: one
  message to a client sitting at 96% ends the job, where a client at 9% is simply still working.
  A full selection is not on it — nothing is left to pick, and the studio is waiting on the
  submission instead — and neither is a project with no selection limit, which has no percentage
  at all. The range picker deliberately does not reach this panel: a client who stopped a month
  ago is exactly who it is for, and scoping it to the range is what would hide them
  ([dashboard/classify.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/dashboard/classify.js),
  [dashboard/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/dashboard/service.js)).
- **Link Expiring Soon now looks ten days ahead, not seven.** A gallery whose link stops within
  the next ten days is on the card and on `?attention=expiringSoon`, which gives the studio time to
  reach the client, wait for them to pick and extend the link if they cannot — a week left little.
  The window is one constant read by the dashboard and by the Projects page filter alike, so the
  card and the list it opens cannot disagree
  ([dashboard/classify.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/dashboard/classify.js)). Which
  galleries count is otherwise unchanged: still only those the client has picking left to do, and a
  link that has already stopped is on Expired Projects rather than this one.
- **Recent Projects follows the cards on the Image Selection dashboard.** The table a card
  filters now sits directly beneath the cards, so pressing one is seen to do something without
  scrolling past two panels first. Client Selection Progress and Storage & Processing, which
  nothing above them filters, moved down to where the table was
  ([ImageSelectionDashboardPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/dashboard/ImageSelectionDashboardPage.js)).
- **The dashboard's headline cards lead with the figure.** Each of them now reads in two lines:
  what it counts with its change at the top right, then the count itself — "4 Projects", the noun
  agreeing with the number — with the point that change is measured from at the bottom right. The
  change is an arrow and a signed number alone, green up, red down and orange on a flat bar for no
  movement, so a row of nine is scanned by what moved rather than read across
  ([DashboardCards.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/dashboard/DashboardCards.js)).
  Nothing about what is counted changes, and a card still filters the Recent Projects table.
- **Image Selection is one entry in the sidebar again.** Dashboard and Projects are no longer
  listed beneath it. The module header already switches between the two, so the sidebar had put the
  same choice in a second place; Image Selection now opens on the dashboard and stays highlighted
  on either screen ([navigation.mjs](../../frontend/src/constants/navigation.mjs)). Both routes keep
  the permission they had, and nothing about either page changes. An entry's `activePath` is read
  as a prefix now rather than as one exact route, which is how its `href` was already read, so an
  entry that owns more than the page it lands on is lit across all of it
  ([AppSidebar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppSidebar.js)).
- **Sidebar Layout arranges modules, and the whole row is the handle.** Settings → User Preference
  → Sidebar Layout no longer opens each module out into the screens inside it, and the rows have
  lost the icon and the small grip that used to sit at their left: a module is dragged by pressing
  anywhere on it. The grip was an 18px target for a row the width of the panel, and the screens
  within a module were a second level of dragging almost nobody used. An arrangement of those
  screens saved before is kept exactly as it was rather than discarded — saving from this panel
  writes the module order and carries the stored screen order through untouched — and the arrows
  and arrow keys still move a row, which is the only way to reorder by tap
  ([SidebarLayoutPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SidebarLayoutPanel.js),
  [SortableList.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/SortableList.js)).
- **The onboarding page names the studio from Settings.** "Complete your onboarding details for
  Don't Blink Studio." was written into the page, so every studio's candidates read somebody else's
  name. It is the Global Name from Settings → Global Logo now, and the bundled brand where that
  field is left blank — a caption beside a logo may be absent, but a sentence cannot trail off
  ([PublicOnboardingForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/onboarding/PublicOnboardingForm.js),
  `brandNameInProse` in [studioBranding.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/studioBranding.mjs)).
- **Fixed: a page could show branding older than the studio's.** The hook that shares the Global
  Name, Global Logo and Login Slides copied them into component state at the first render and only
  subscribed afterwards, so an answer arriving between the two reached that one reader and no
  later fetch repeated it — it sat on nothing for the life of the page while every other reader on
  the same screen had the studio's. It reads the shared answer directly now
  (`useSyncExternalStore`), which has no such gap
  ([useStudioBranding.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/useStudioBranding.js)).
- **One favicon everywhere, uploaded or bundled.** Settings → Global Logo asks for a single
  **Favicon** rather than one per tab theme, and the file that ships behind an empty field is now
  the Beam Craft mark alone rather than a black-lettered and a white-lettered pair. The pair
  existed because bare lettering disappears on a tab strip of its own colour; the mark carries its
  own white rounded ground, so it reads on a light strip and a dark one alike and there is nothing
  left for `prefers-color-scheme` to decide. `favicon-black.svg` and `favicon-white.svg` are gone,
  the tab component no longer watches the tab theme, and the layout declares one icon. Migration
  [20260908140000](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260908140000_single_studio_favicon/migration.sql)
  carries whichever mark was uploaded over to the new column and drops the two old ones, so a
  studio that had uploaded either keeps it.
- **A client may send a selection item without filling it to its limit.** Review & Submit now
  enables as soon as one photo is picked, so a client who wants seven of the ten offered can send
  seven rather than being made to pick three they do not want. The limit is a ceiling, not a quota:
  picking an eleventh is still refused, the item still locks once sent, and the server applies the
  same one-photo rule the button does
  ([publication/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/publication/service.js)).

- **The referral amount now lives in Settings, not on the referral register.** It is
  **Settings → Employee Referral**, beside everything else the studio configures once; the Employee
  Referral header no longer carries a ⋮ at all, since that menu held exactly one entry and it led
  away from the screen. The section answers to `HRM / Employee Referral` — the permission its own
  routes enforce — rather than to the administrator's `ADMIN / System Settings`, so whoever runs
  referrals can still set the figure without being made an administrator, and it is absent for
  anybody who holds neither. Everything else is unchanged: one amount, halved across the referred
  employee's third and sixth month, and never retrospective
  ([ReferralAmountPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/ReferralAmountPanel.js),
  [SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js),
  [EmployeeReferralsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/EmployeeReferralsPage.js)).

- **The login email is built from the employee's name, and Work Email ID is gone from the forms.**
  Creating a login asked for an address and filed it back onto the record as the employee's Work
  Email ID, so a studio sign-in depended on somebody typing a studio address correctly and on there
  being one at all. The credentials page now proposes `john.smi@dontblinkstudioz.com` — the first
  name, a dot, and the first three letters of the last, from the same rule the username comes from,
  so the two halves of a sign-in agree about the person's name. A last name shorter than three
  letters gives what it has (`dhanush.m.m@…`), a one-word name has no last part and no trailing dot
  (`madonna@…`), and it stays editable and not unique by construction: two people sharing a name
  collide, the API refuses the second, and it is noticed rather than silently wrong. **Work Email ID
  is no longer asked for** on Add Employee, Review or Edit, nor in the employee import template, and
  the credential no longer writes one back. The column and every address already in it are
  untouched — the meeting dialogs still prefer a recorded work email and fall back to the personal
  one ([employeeName.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/employeeName.mjs),
  [useCredentialForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/useCredentialForm.js),
  [controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js)).
- **The shoot list's ⋮ is gone for good, its scrollbar no longer sits on top of the last row, and
  the small tables lost their menus.** Setting `tableOptions: false` was not enough on its own: the
  DataTable keeps its ⋮ whenever a column layout has been saved under that table's name, so it can
  still offer **Reset column order** — which meant a table that had opted out kept the menu for
  ever because of an arrangement made before it did. The reset is now offered only where the
  columns can actually be dragged, and the three tables embedded in Service Creation — the shoot
  list, and the services and deliverables under each shoot — opt out of reordering as well, since
  neither a column order nor an export is anything a reader came to a form for. Fourteen columns is about two thousand pixels, so the list scrolls sideways wherever it
  is read — and on macOS the overlay scrollbar was drawn over the bottom row, hiding the line
  somebody was scrolling to find. The scroll container now reserves the track
  (`scrollbar-gutter: stable`) and keeps a little room below the last row, so nothing is covered.
  The tables embedded in Service Creation — the shoot list, and the services and deliverables under
  each shoot — also no longer show the DataTable's own ⋮ (Manage Columns, Export CSV, Compact view),
  which is a page-level table's menu and on a five-row table inside a form is a control offering
  things nobody came there for. Separately, the phone layout's card printed **every field twice** —
  two nearly identical blocks, invisible on desktop — and now prints each once
  ([ProjectShootList.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectShootList.js),
  [ProjectServiceCreationSection.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectServiceCreationSection.js)).
- **The converted value moved out of the sale's fields and into Shedule Payment, where it can be
  edited, and Select Quotation no longer repeats the price.** The picker's options read
  `QUT039-03 · ₹98,000`, which made sense while picking a version was also the only way to see what
  it would put in the amount field beside it. That figure is Shedule Payment's **Signed Quotation**
  now, filled the moment a version is picked and shown in full there, so the option said the same
  number twice; it is the version's id alone.

  Create Project asked for **Initial Converted Value** among Project Details, where it sat
  read-only whenever a quotation was picked — a figure to look at in the middle of the fields being
  answered, while the section that actually uses it showed the same number again as **Signed
  Quotation**. The box is gone from Project Details; Signed Quotation at the top of Shedule Payment
  is now the input, still filled from the chosen quotation, with every scheduled amount following it
  as it is typed. **A deliberate loosening**: the figure used to be locked to the quotation, so a
  project can now be booked at an amount differing from the quotation it names — the quotation
  itself is untouched, and correcting that is still a new version of it. Start / Edit Project keeps
  it read-only, since by then the sale is settled, and its read-only sale card no longer repeats the
  figure either — it is Signed Quotation's, further down the same form. The validation message names
  the field where it now lives ([SaleDetailsSection.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/SaleDetailsSection.js),
  [SchedulePaymentSection.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/SchedulePaymentSection.js),
  [CreateProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CreateProjectFormPanel.js),
  [StartProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/StartProjectFormPanel.js)).
- **Changing the quotation on Create Project now changes the shoots.** Picking a second quotation
  added its shoots and left the first one's above them, so the project was booked against a plan
  that was neither quotation — and clearing the quotation, which is what choosing a different
  enquiry does, left the old one's shoots to be booked against the new enquiry. The pending plan now
  follows the selected quotation: its shoots, services and deliverables replace the last one's, and
  go entirely when no quotation is chosen. Anything added by hand survives the switch. Edit Sale
  Details stays additive on purpose — a live project's shoot carries cash entries, payment request
  expenses and client storage records, and its deliverables go with it under `ON DELETE CASCADE`
  ([CreateProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CreateProjectFormPanel.js)).
- **Start / Edit Project states the sale as a card, not as read-only boxes.** The Sale Details
  section drew its values in text inputs nobody could type in, which reads as a row of fields
  somebody has closed to you rather than as facts the screen already knows. It is now one card of
  label-over-value pairs — Project Status, Event ID, Enquiry ID, Quotation, Client Main Number,
  Source Branch, WhatsApp Group Name, Vertical and CRM Name — drawn by the new
  `shared/RecordSummaryCard`. That card is not a new design: it is the one the cash entry form
  already states the project it is filed against with, lifted out of that file so the two are the
  same component rather than the same markup twice
  ([RecordSummaryCard.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/RecordSummaryCard.js),
  [StartProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/StartProjectFormPanel.js),
  [CashEntryFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/accounts/CashEntryFormPanel.js)).
- **A started project can only be changed by its Project Manager or a branch administrator.**
  Anyone holding `PROJECT / Projects` `READ_WRITE` could edit any project at any point in its life.
  Planning is the right question up until a project starts; after that the plan is being worked to —
  shoots rostered, services allocated, money scheduled — so changing it now belongs to the employee
  named as its **Project Manager**, or to a holder of `ADMIN / Branch Administration` (which a Super
  Admin holds along with everything else, so no role check names them). Everyone else keeps View and
  loses Edit: the menu entry is absent, and the twelve endpoints that write to a project's plan
  refuse it as well, so adding a shoot is not a way around pressing Update. Editing the **sale** of a
  started project is untouched — that is Sales Manager's. The guard reads the project through the
  caller's own data scope, so one they cannot see still answers 404 rather than a 403 that would
  confirm it exists ([controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js),
  [routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/routes.js),
  [ProjectsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectsPage.js)).
- **The WhatsApp group name switch picks itself.** Choosing an enquiry with a generated name opens
  on the saved list, and one without opens on the manual box. Before, an enquiry with no name left
  the switch wherever it was — for a new project, on a dropdown reading "No group name has been
  generated for this enquiry", with the way to type one behind a control nobody had a reason to
  press ([SaleDetailsSection.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/SaleDetailsSection.js)).
- **Create Project shows the quotation's shoots and no longer lets them be changed.** Picking a
  quotation brings its shoots into Service Creation; from there the section offered **+ Add Shoot**,
  an edit and a delete on every one of them, so a project could be booked with shoots the client
  never agreed to pay for — quietly, since nothing afterwards compares the two. Those controls are
  now absent on Create Project once a quotation has supplied the shoots, on the list and on each
  shoot's card alike, and the strip above the list says where the shoots came from. **The services
  and deliverables are read-only there too**: the Choose Services and Choose Deliverables pickers,
  Clear all and each row's delete are absent, and the cells print their values rather than offering
  an editor. **A quotation
  carrying no shoots keeps + Add Shoot**, since a price agreed before the days were settled has
  nothing to plan against, and anything added by hand then stays editable. Start / Edit Project and
  the quotation generator are untouched
  ([CreateProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CreateProjectFormPanel.js),
  [ProjectServiceCreationSection.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectServiceCreationSection.js),
  [ProjectShootList.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectShootList.js)).
- **Duplicating a quotation onto its own enquiry now makes a second quotation for it.** It was
  refused — *"use Edit to raise its next version"* — which was the only possible answer while an
  enquiry could hold one chain: the copy could only have landed as the next version of its own. Now
  that each quotation is its own chain it is a plain second quotation for that enquiry, its own row
  with its own number and link, which is exactly what copying the Dum Dum package to start the Corp
  one wants. The duplicate dialog offers every enquiry including the current one, and the note under
  the field says whether the copy is landing on another enquiry or beside the quotation it came from
  ([QuotationDuplicateDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationDuplicateDialog.js),
  [controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js)).
- **A quotation chain is one quotation's history, not the enquiry's.** Raising a new quotation for an
  enquiry that already had one used to join its chain as the next version. That is wrong for the way
  the studio actually quotes: one enquiry is regularly quoted for **Dum Dum, Qua Qua and Corp** at
  the same time — three packages at three prices, none of them a revision of the other two — and
  folding them together made the corporate quotation *version 2* of the wedding one, leaving the
  register showing only whichever was raised last and burying the rest under a chevron. A new
  quotation now starts a chain of its own and gets a row of its own, as does a duplicate onto
  another enquiry; **only Edit adds a version**, which is the one action that can honestly say "this
  replaces what I sent you". One enquiry can now hold several quotations, each with its own number
  and price history, and the project form's version picker offers every version of every one of
  them. Nothing already stored is rewritten: chains merged under the old rule stay as they are,
  since those versions were sent to clients under those ids
  ([controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js),
  [SALES-AND-PROJECT-MANAGEMENT-WORKFLOW.md](../project-state/SALES-AND-PROJECT-MANAGEMENT-WORKFLOW.md)).
- **Login Credentials no longer offers "Skip for now".** The page that creates an employee's login
  led its footer with a button to walk past it, in the position the eye reaches first — so the step
  the page exists for was the second thing on offer. It is gone, and the primary action now sits
  where every other panel in the application puts one. Nothing is trapped by dropping it: the
  employee is already saved by the time this page is reached, the panel's own × closes it, and the ⋮
  on an Active employee with no login still opens the same form whenever it is picked up again.
  **Back to details** is untouched — it appears only while nothing is saved yet, and is a way back
  up the form rather than a way past it
  ([EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js)).
- **The referral amount is set from the register it governs.** It was a section of a settings panel
  behind Employee Repository → ⋮ → Settings; it is now **Employee Referral → ⋮ → Settings**, on the
  register's own header — one panel, one figure, halved across the third and sixth month. A figure is changed
  by whoever is looking at what it produced, and reaching it used to mean leaving the register,
  finding another screen and opening a menu on it. The Employee Repository's ⋮ keeps its invite
  history and import. Each instalment still keeps the amount its referral was made under, so raising
  the figure never reprices one already promised
  ([ReferralSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/ReferralSettingsPanel.js),
  [EmployeeVerificationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeVerificationPage.js)).
- **Employee Referral remembers which tab you were on.** Outstanding stayed selected until the page
  was reloaded, which put somebody working through what is owed back at the top of everything. It
  now uses the same per-login `useStickyTab` every other filtered screen does, so the tab survives a
  reload and follows the login to another device
  ([EmployeeReferralsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/EmployeeReferralsPage.js)).
- **Referrals answer to a new `HRM / Employee Referral` permission, not `ACCOUNTS / Payroll
  Management`.** A permission is granted where the screen it opens lives, and that screen is now
  under HRM. It guards the Employee Referral page, every route under `/api/hrm/referrals`, and the
  Settings entry that sets the amount: `READ` reads the register and the figure, `READ_WRITE`
  changes the figure and marks an instalment paid. **This needs a reseed and a re-grant** — run the
  IAM seed so the permission exists, then open each role in Admin → Role Management and give it
  `HRM / Employee Referral`; the seed writes no grants, so until then only Super Admin reaches the
  screen, including roles that could read the register yesterday. `ACCOUNTS / Payroll Management`
  now guards nothing and stays in the seed, as it was before referrals existed
  ([routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/referrals/routes.js),
  [iam-roles-permissions.js](../../backend/prisma/seeds/iam-roles-permissions.js),
  [IAM.md](../project-state/IAM.md)).

- **The closed project sidebar says what is behind it.** Collapsed, Image Selection showed a
  chevron halfway down an empty strip — enough to say "there is something over here", not what.
  The rail now carries the `AccountTree` mark inside it, at the top where a heading would be: a
  label for a column too narrow to hold a word, drawn in the grey the application gives anything
  not to be acted on. It is deliberately **not** a control — no hover, no press, out of the tab
  order — because the chevron beside it already opens the panel, and a second button doing the one
  job is the handle said twice. The mark shows on the closed rail only; open, the panel names
  itself ([ProjectSidebar.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ProjectSidebar.js)).

- **A quotation now follows its enquiry, so whoever books the project can read what it was sold on.**
  One enquiry is quoted, re-quoted and eventually booked, and rarely by the same person — so a
  quotation is yours if you may read the lead it was raised against or the project it became, as
  well as by its own owner and creator.
- **Dropdowns offer everything; lists show your own.** A picker exists to link one record to
  another, and whoever is doing the linking is usually not the person the other record belongs to —
  so the project, shoot, lead and quotation-version pickers offer every option again, and the form
  never offers a choice it will then refuse. The registers behind them stay narrowed to your own
  work. On the project form, the version dropdown lists every quotation raised for the enquiry being
  booked, so the quoted shoots come across whoever wrote them.
- **Meetings and WhatsApp conversations follow the lead they belong to.** The Meetings page, the
  Schedule calendar and the assistant's calendar line now show the meetings on leads you may open,
  plus any meeting you booked yourself; the Add Meet picker offers only those leads. Every WhatsApp
  path — opening a thread, sending, media, marking read, the history, forwarding, and the live
  stream that pushes replies — refuses a lead that is not yours, with the same "not found" the CRM
  board gives. Before this, anybody holding `SALES / CRM Leads` could read every conversation in the
  studio. ([leadScope.js](../../backend/src/services/leadScope.js))
- **Client Storage shows you the discs you are part of, not every disc in the studio.** A hard disc
  is yours if you are holding it, if you handed it to somebody, if you raised the record, if
  somebody who reports to you holds it, or if it belongs to a project you are on — reached through
  the record's project link and through its shoot. The tab counts and the "last updated" note are
  narrowed with it, so a count never reports rows the table will not show, and the shoot picker
  offers only shoots from projects you may read. Reaching a branch still shows you nothing on its
  own; that needs `ADMIN / Branch Administration`.
  ([client-storage.service.js](../../backend/src/modules/assets/client-storage.service.js))
- **A record that hangs off a project is now seen exactly when the project is.** Project
  attachments, extra quotations, client gifts and shoots carry no names of their own, so they follow
  the project they belong to instead of being open to anyone holding the module permission — which
  is what they were, so a screen that listed nothing of yours still named every project in the
  studio. The Schedule calendar draws only the shoots and main events of projects you may read, and
  the project and shoot **pickers** on Cash Management, Payment Requests and the extras form offer
  only those too. A project you may not see is *not found* rather than *forbidden*, so a child
  screen cannot be used to confirm it exists. Approving a payment request still files its cash entry
  against the project that request named, whether or not the approver can read it.
  ([projectScope.js](../../backend/src/services/projectScope.js))
- **One permission decides whose records you see, not one per module.** The project screens had
  their own: `PROJECT / All Projects` lifted the name requirement for projects alone, so somebody
  named on a single project was answered with every project in the branches their role reached — and
  nothing on screen said which rule was answering. It is retired. The project register now follows
  the same rule as assets, leads, cash and tasks: the projects naming you (CRM, project manager,
  either coordinator, creative manager, creator), your reports' projects, and — for a holder of
  **`ADMIN / Branch Administration`** — everything in the branches reached. A role that needs
  branch-wide sight is granted that one permission, which widens every module at once. Roles holding
  the retired permission lose it with the migration; re-save them with Branch Administration if they
  are meant to see a colleague's work.
  ([projectScope.js](../../backend/src/services/projectScope.js),
  migration `20260906200000_retire_all_projects_permission`)
- **Branch administration is a permission now, and every record records who created it.** Reaching a
  branch no longer shows you anything: it says where you may file records and which branches a
  picker offers. Whoever *created* a record is one of the names the rule reads, so a lead nobody has
  been assigned yet stays visible to the person who entered it, and an asset in stock to whoever
  registered it. To see the whole of a branch — colleagues' work included — a role needs the new
  **`ADMIN / Branch Administration`** permission, which is granted from Role Management like any
  other; `PROJECT / All Projects` still does the same for the project register alone. `created_by_user_id`
  was added to CRM leads, quotations and tasks (migration
  [20260906190000_created_by_on_scoped_tables](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260906190000_created_by_on_scoped_tables/migration.sql));
  every other scoped table already recorded it. **Grant `Branch Administration` to whoever runs a
  branch, or they will see only the work their own name is on.**
- **One rule now decides whose records you see, and it puts your name first.** A record is yours if
  your name is on it, or if the name on it belongs to somebody who reports to you at any depth;
  where nobody is named on it, the branches your roles reach decide instead. An asset in
  Visakhapatnam assigned to somebody whose roles reach only Erode is theirs to see, and a manager
  sees their team's work wherever it is — but an asset assigned to a colleague is *not* yours just
  because you share a branch. `PROJECT / All Projects` is the one permission that lifts the name
  requirement inside the branches reached. The per-grant setting added earlier in this release (All records
  / Their branches / Their team / Their own) is **withdrawn** along with its Role Management
  dropdown: it was a setting that could be set wrong, and there was nothing it could usefully say.
  Two module rules survive on top and are narrowed rather than widened — a payment request is the
  requester's own until somebody who may decide it looks, and a task is always readable by the two
  people it is between — and `PROJECT / All Projects` is still what adds the branch to the project
  register. Migration
  [20260906170000_drop_role_permission_data_scope](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260906170000_drop_role_permission_data_scope/migration.sql);
  the rule in [DATA-SCOPE.md](../project-state/DATA-SCOPE.md#the-rule).
- **The CRM lead's vertical column is called `vertical` in the database.** It was `branch` and never
  held one — the lead's branch is `assigned_branch` — and a column named `branch` holding "Dum Dum"
  had to be explained to everyone who opened the database. `board_key`, its index and the four
  Workbench views are dropped and recreated over the new name; the four boards return the same rows.
  Migration
  [20260906180000_rename_crm_lead_branch_to_vertical](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260906180000_rename_crm_lead_branch_to_vertical/migration.sql).

- **Nothing in the CRM calls the vertical a branch any more.** The lead record's `branch` field is
  `vertical` in every payload (leads, duplicates, meetings, reports, notifications, the AI context);
  the board the list is read through is `board` (`GET /api/sales/crm-leads?board=` and `/ids?board=`,
  where it was `branch`); Table Settings, Google Sheet links, the CSV import and the report filters
  say `vertical`; the CRM assistant's request (`POST /api/sales/crm-ai/crm/chat[/stream]`) names
  the vertical it is scoped to as `vertical`; and the frontend's helpers follow (`verticalOptions`, `resolveVertical`,
  `normalizeVertical`, `boardTableColumns`, `BOARD_TABS`). The physical column followed later in
  this release. No screen changes.

- **A CRM lead's vertical now references the `vertical` table.** `sales_crm_lead.vertical_id` is a
  nullable foreign key beside the free-text column (which never held a branch - the lead's branch
  is `assigned_branch`). Nothing is backfilled yet; the text is still what every screen reads, so
  the four boards return exactly the rows they did. The `vertical` table gains the two short codes
  the studio prints - `document_code` (WED / QQ / CORP) and `identifier_code` (DD / QQ / CORP) -
  set for the three sales verticals, so those stop being hard-coded maps. The empty `branch`
  columns on `sales_crm_dynamic_column`, `sales_google_sheet_link` and `sales_crm_lead_watch`,
  which hold a vertical, are renamed `vertical`. The four `vw_crm_leads_*` Workbench views, broken
  since the August snake_case rename, work again and show `Vertical ID`. Migration
  [20260906110000_crm_lead_vertical_reference](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260906110000_crm_lead_vertical_reference/migration.sql);
  design in [DATA-SCOPE.md](../project-state/DATA-SCOPE.md).
- **The branch table is now called `branch`.** The `location` table only ever held the studio's
  six branches, and everything pointing at it already called the column `branch_id`, so the table,
  its Prisma model (`Location` → `Branch`) and the master-data key (`locations` → `branches`,
  served at `GET /api/hrm/branches`) now agree. `GET /api/hrm/locations` stays as a read-only
  alias for any caller not yet updated. No rows change and no behaviour changes; the columns
  named `location` on other tables (a bride's, a bank's, a shoot's) are not branches and are
  untouched. Migration [20260906090000_rename_location_to_branch](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260906090000_rename_location_to_branch/migration.sql).

- **The topbar bell carries the task notices and the waiting-on-you ones together.** Two branches
  built a bell at once — one storing task notifications as rows, one deriving notices from
  follow-ups, WhatsApp replies, unread calls and asset approvals — and they are complementary
  rather than rival, so the panel now draws all three halves: waiting on you, then tasks, then the
  day's meetings. The endpoint's guard moved off the router and into the controller: gating the
  whole of `/api/notifications` on `HRM / Task Management` was right while tasks were the only
  writer and became wrong the moment it carried other modules — a role with the CRM and no Tasks
  would have had its own notices refused, a 403 on the topbar of every screen. Each source is now
  gated on its own module, tasks included, and a role holding none gets an empty bell
  ([notifications/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/notifications/controller.js),
  [AppNotifications.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppNotifications.js)).

- **The calendar's attendance source is separate from the Employee Attendance register.** They
  answer different questions and had collided in one directory. `hrm/attendance` is the studio's
  register — login and logout, leave, permission, approvals, holidays and shift rules — and answers
  per employee. The calendar needs everybody's days across a range, keyed by login, in a couple of
  queries, and derives them from measured application usage where nothing was written down; that
  now lives at `schedule/attendance` with no route of its own, since the calendar is its only
  caller. An HTTP endpoint there would have been a second attendance API beside the real one
  ([schedule/attendance/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/schedule/attendance/service.js)).

- **Relationship on a CRD document is picked from a list rather than typed from scratch.** The
  Family Details rows in the Dum Dum document now offer Father, Mother, Brother, Sister, Son,
  Daughter, Husband, Wife and Other, filtered as the client types — so the common answers are
  spelled one way instead of fifteen. Anything the list does not have is still accepted: typing
  "Family friend" keeps it, because a form the client fills alone should not make them choose the
  nearest wrong answer
  ([PublicCrdDocumentFormDumDum.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicCrdDocumentFormDumDum.js)).
  Its own list, not the `relationshipOptions` the CRM and quotations share: those answer how the
  person enquiring relates to the couple (Self, Bride, Wedding Planner), which is a different
  question from what a relative is to them. Nothing is stored differently — the field is the same
  free text it always was. The rows line up while they are at it: the last column is a stated width
  rather than being sized to its own button, so a row carrying a 36px × and a row carrying + Add
  Member no longer divide the remaining space differently, and the Name box no longer sits 6px below
  its neighbour because of the space it reserved under a label it does not have.

- **A gallery folder card says how many photos are selected in it, not a ratio.** "3/5 Selected"
  on a card read as that folder's own quota, when the limit belongs to the selection item across
  every folder and is already stated in the heading above; the card now reads "3 Selected"
  ([FolderCards.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/client-gallery/FolderCards.js)).

- **One logo, everywhere, with the bundled mark as its permanent default.** Every surface that
  shows the studio's mark — the sign-in page, the app bar, the client's selection gallery, a
  quotation on screen and in the PDF printed from it, and the CRD document form — now renders it
  through a single [AppLogo.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/AppLogo.js): the Global Logo from
  Settings where one is configured, and the bundled Beam Craft mark where none is. That mark is
  also the answer when a configured logo fails to load, so no surface can show a broken image or
  an empty space where the logo goes. Each module states how **tall** the mark is and how **wide it
  may get** — the app bar 40px tall capped at 160, sign-in 96/260, the gallery 56/144 (40/112 on its
  mobile bar, 80/176 for the welcome badge), a quotation 64/240, print 48/200, the CRD header 68/240
  and 40/160 beside its title. The height is what makes a round badge and a wide wordmark read as
  the same size from screen to screen; the width is a limit rather than a reservation, so a narrow
  logo is not padded out with dead space beside whatever sits next to it, and a wide one cannot
  crowd it. Within the two the logo is contained: scaled to fit, its own proportions kept, centred,
  never stretched and never cropped — so square, portrait and landscape logos in PNG, JPG, WebP,
  AVIF, GIF or SVG all sit correctly.
  `StudioLogo` is gone, its callers moved to `AppLogo`, and the client gallery's `GalleryLogo`
  now passes the branding its token carries into the same component
  ([GalleryBrand.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/client-gallery/GalleryBrand.js)).
- **One number is one enquiry within a vertical, and the CRM now enforces it.** The same contact
  number can no longer be added or edited onto a second lead in the vertical that already holds it —
  that is the same client written down twice, with their calls, remarks and follow-ups split across
  two records that neither knows about. Across verticals it is still allowed and always was
  deliberate: a wedding client who later books a corporate shoot is one number and two enquiries.
  The warning dialog now names the vertical **that already holds the number** in its heading — "This
  number is already an enquiry in Corp", whichever vertical you are writing into — and says the rest
  in one line instead of three; **Save anyway** is offered only where the
  save will actually go through, so a button whose only outcome is a refusal is absent rather than
  present and failing. `POST` and `PUT` hold the rule regardless of what the form checked, answering
  409 with the Enquiry ID that already has the number. Verticals are compared normalised, so a lead
  filed as "Crop" or "Corporate" still counts as Corp. Bulk and CSV import are deliberately left
  outside it — a spreadsheet that failed halfway on one repeated number would be worse than the
  duplicate ([DuplicateEnquiryDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/DuplicateEnquiryDialog.js),
  [crm-leads/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/controller.js)).

- **A client's WhatsApp reply now appears as it lands instead of up to a second and a half later,
  and a photo no longer takes seconds to show up or go missing altogether.** Three separate faults,
  all of them on the receiving side. The panel found out that a client had replied by re-reading the
  whole conversation every 1.2 seconds; the server now holds a line open and says so the moment
  anything is written, so a reply arrives in about one round trip. Nothing stopped one of those reads
  starting before the last had answered, so a thread that was slow to read got slower the longer it
  stayed open — reads are now one at a time. And an incoming photo or voice note was not written down
  until its file had been downloaded from WhatsApp and uploaded to the studio's bucket, so the
  message waited on both — and a download that hung took the message with it, which is why a picture
  sometimes never arrived. The message is stored first now and the file follows it onto the screen.
  Polling remains as a fallback for a network or proxy that will not pass a stream, and a dropped
  line re-opens itself, so the chat is never worse than it was
  ([events.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/whatsapp-chat/events.js),
  [sessionManager.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/whatsapp-chat/sessionManager.js),
  [WhatsAppChatPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/WhatsAppChatPanel.js)).

- **Rejected sits after Converted in the Add Lead form.** The status list is ordered by how the lead
  popup reads, which groups Rejected with CNA and Invalid — the three that end a conversation. With
  the other two hidden on this form, Rejected was left sitting first, so a row of pills that runs
  from "we are working this" to "we sold it" opened on "we turned them down"
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

- **The Calls board is now every call Callyzer has delivered, and a call has an ID of its own.**
  Callyzer's webhook used to turn every delivered call into a lead, so a wrong number, a two-second
  misdial and a call nobody answered each took an Enquiry ID out of the same run the studio counts
  its real enquiries in — and the board could only show the calls nobody had dealt with yet. A call
  from a number the CRM does not already hold no longer creates anything: it is stored as a call
  under a Call ID of its own (`DBS-CID-000001`, no vertical and no branch), and the Enquiry ID is
  minted only when somebody reads the caller and adds them, for the vertical they choose in that
  moment. A call from a client the CRM already holds is attached to their lead exactly as before.
  Leads & Followups → Calls now carries **five tabs — All, Incoming, Outgoing, Connected, Not
  Connected** — cut from the call itself: Incoming is every call the client made (answered, missed or
  rejected), and Connected and Not Connected split the board by whether anybody actually talked. They
  overlap on purpose, so a missed call is in three of them and the counts add up to more than the
  board holds. Its columns say what a call is — Call ID, Call Date, Call Time, Caller Name, Phone
  Number, Attended By, Call Type, Duration, Status (Connected / Not Connected / Never Attended) and
  Remarks; Contact Repeated and Referred From are gone, both having been a lead's answers shown
  against a call. The **Enquiry ID** column shows whichever code the row has reached — the call's own
  (`DBS-CID-000001`) until somebody adds the caller, that vertical's (`DBS-EID-QQ-CBE-000004`) from
  then on — so one column says both what the enquiry is called and how far along it is. *Never Attended* is a Status value rather than a tab of its own —
  every call it would have held was already in Incoming and in Not Connected, so as a tab it was
  those two read together, while in the column it says the one thing they do not: this call did not
  connect because nobody in the studio picked it up. Double-clicking a call opens the Add Lead form as it always did and
  Confirm now **creates** the lead, handing it every call from that number; double-clicking a call
  that has already been added opens that lead instead. Access is unchanged — the board is part of
  `SALES` → `CRM Leads`, `READ` to look and `READ_WRITE` to add, and no new permission was
  introduced ([callBoardService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/callBoardService.js),
  [callLogService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/callLogService.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

  Two things follow from it. **Rejected** is offered in the Add Lead form's Lead Status pills when
  the form was opened from a call and nowhere else — a caller can be turned down in the one place
  the studio is looking at what was said, and the lead it writes lands in that vertical's Rejected
  tab. A lead marked **Rejected or Invalid now asks only for the name and the number** — Assigned
  Branch, Estimated Value and Qua Qua's date of birth are questions about a job, and a lead being
  turned down is not one; requiring an estimate of work nobody is doing meant inventing a number or
  leaving no record at all. It also drops any follow-up date typed before the status was changed,
  rather than booking a call against a lead nobody is chasing. And **Contacts** now reads callers
  from the call log directly rather than getting them as a
  side effect of the webhook creating leads: a number the studio has been rung from is listed under
  its Call ID until the caller becomes a lead, behind the same `SALES` → `CRM Leads` permission
  ([contacts/directory/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/contacts/directory/service.js)).

  **Existing databases need one run of `npm run retire:callyzer-call-leads -- --apply`** in
  `backend/`. It gives every call already in the log a Call ID, then soft-deletes the leads that
  were never anything but a call — Callyzer, no vertical, no status, no follow-up, no notes of their
  own and no quotation, project, meeting or CRD against them — and returns their calls to the board.
  It reports and writes nothing without `--apply`, and leaves alone any lead somebody has worked
  ([retireCallyzerCallLeads.js](../../backend/scripts/retireCallyzerCallLeads.js)).

- **Table Options -> "Reset Column Order" is now "Reset Table Layout".** It used to undo only a
  drag; it now puts back the column order, the hidden columns, the frozen columns and the column
  widths together, because those are now remembered together. It appears once a table has been
  arranged at all, rather than only once a column has been dragged.
  ([tableMenuItems.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/tableMenuItems.mjs))

- **Picking a quotation now settles the sale's client number and source branch too, and closes them
  to typing.** Create Project already took **Initial Converted Value** from the quotation and locked
  it; **Client Main Number** and **Source Branch** were still typed by hand, so a project could be
  saved disagreeing with the very document the client was sent, with nothing to say which of the two
  was right. All three are now filled from the version that is picked and read back rather than
  entered, each with the line the amount already carried: *Set from the selected quotation and
  cannot be changed here.* Correcting one of them belongs on the quotation, as a new version, and
  the project then converts at that.

  A field the quotation does not answer stays open: a quotation raised without a branch cannot
  settle one, so the form still asks. And changing the enquiry now gives up the quotation that was
  picked — it belonged to the enquiry it was raised for, and leaving it selected named a quotation
  from the previous enquiry while this one's values were written underneath it, with the amount
  still locked to a figure taken from nowhere. Each quotation version now carries its own client
  name, number and branch to the picker, so a version raised after the client changed number fills
  in the number that version was sent with
  ([SaleDetailsSection.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/SaleDetailsSection.js)).

- **The Date Known switches are smaller.** `ToggleSwitch` takes a `size`, and the Yes/No pair that
  a date field hangs off — Main Event Date Known in the W/A Group-Name Generator, Shoot Date Known
  in Add Shoot — uses the small one. At full size the track was the loudest thing on a row of form
  fields and pulled the eye off the answers beside it
  ([ToggleSwitch.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/ToggleSwitch.js)).

- **The ⋮ menu sits at the right-hand end of every module's title bar.** Four headers put it first
  instead - Bookings & Converted, Assets, Vendors and the Employee Repository, all of them where
  Import had been added - so the quiet ⋮ led the row and pushed Create Project, Add Asset, Add
  Vendor and Create Onboarding Invite along behind it. Every header now runs its buttons first and
  its ⋮ last, which is what CRM, Accounts and CRD Documents already did, so the primary button keeps
  the position the eye goes to and the ⋮ is always in the same corner. The Employee Repository had
  grown two ⋮ triggers in one header - Import's and the repository's own - and now has one: **Import**
  joined **View All Invites** and **Settings** in the menu that was already there, the same
  conclusion Accounts reached for its own Import and Settings. The convention is written down in
  [ImportMenuButton](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/ImportMenuButton.js) and the
  [frontend README](../../frontend/README.md) so the next header follows it
  ([ModulePageHeader.js](../../frontend/src/components/layout/ModulePageHeader.js)).

- **Social Media Posting starts on NSM in the W/A Group-Name Generator.** The radio opened with
  neither option chosen, so a required answer with an obvious starting point was one more field to
  fill in before the name would build at all. It now starts on **NSM**, the cautious half of the
  pair: a group is marked SM only when somebody actively says so, so no client is labelled as
  agreeing to be posted about by a form that was left alone
  ([WhatsappGroupNameDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/WhatsappGroupNameDialog.js)).

- **HRM has its own Settings, on the Employee Repository's ⋮, and Referrals has moved into it.**
  What a referral pays and the register it produces — who is owed what, which instalments are
  outstanding, and Mark paid — were under **Settings → User Settings → Referrals**, three screens
  away from the form where a referral is actually recorded. They are now **Employee Repository → ⋮ →
  Settings → Referrals**, and are no longer in the application's Settings: one doorway, not two. The
  panel is a rail with one section so far, which is where the next HRM setting goes. **The
  permission did not move with it:** referral money is still `ACCOUNTS / Payroll Management`, the
  same permission its routes are guarded by, so the ⋮ entry is absent for a role that may open the
  repository but may not read what the studio pays out, and `READ` without `READ_WRITE` still reads
  the register with the amount fixed and Mark paid withheld. No new permission and no reseed. See
  [HrmSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/HrmSettingsPanel.js).

- **Create Project now requires the Payment Received section in full.** The advance was optional as
  a whole and required only once begun, so a project could be created with nothing recorded against
  it — which is how a project got saved with the section untouched. Every field the section shows
  must now be answered before **Save Project** goes through, and pressing Save with anything
  outstanding marks each field **Required** where it sits rather than naming one problem at a time
  in a line at the top. Nothing is marked until Save has been pressed once. **Save Project stays
  blue at all times** and no longer greys out at all: it used to go grey while the sale was
  incomplete, the advance unfinished, or an edit unchanged — three unrelated reasons wearing one
  disabled state, none of them named — and a disabled button cannot submit, so the message
  explaining the refusal could never be reached. Pressing it always runs the checks and says what
  is outstanding. It dims only while a save is actually in flight, to stop the same save being sent
  twice, and stays blue for that too. Pressing Update on an edit that changed nothing simply closes
  the form, which is what the greyed-out button used to prevent — and what stops an untouched sale
  being re-sent and refused 409 over a group name the project already holds. Only the fields the answers so far put on screen
  are required: cash asks for neither a reference nor an account, UPI asks for a reference, a bank
  transfer for both. **Somebody without `ACCOUNTS / Cash Management` `READ_WRITE` is unaffected** —
  the section is not shown to them, so they still create the project and Accounts records the money
  afterwards on its Payments tab. See
  [CreateProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CreateProjectFormPanel.js) and
  `cashEntryProblems` in [accountsConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/accountsConfig.js), which
  reads the requirements off the cash entry field list so Accounts' own form and this one cannot
  drift apart.

- **Tasks picks its view from a dropdown beside the heading, leaving the tab row to the statuses.**
  **All Tasks**, **My Tasks** and **Tasks Given** were a tab bar with the status filters as a second
  bar directly under it, so the top of the screen was two near-identical rows and the reader had to
  work out which was which before either could be used. The views are now the dropdown beside the
  title — the way Client Gifts picks its gift and CRD Documents its vertical — and Pending, In
  Progress, Completed and Reverted take the tab row on their own. The status is now remembered
  across a reload as well, under its own key: the view has always survived one, but the status
  dropped back to **All** every time, which is worst exactly where reloading is most natural —
  somebody working through Pending refreshes to see what has moved and is put back at the top of
  everything. Nothing about who sees what changed: All Tasks is still listed only for a role holding
  `HRM / All Tasks`, and the screen still opens on My Tasks. See
  [TasksPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/TasksPage.js).

- **Create Project asks for the advance last, after the payment schedule.** **Payment Received** sat
  third, between Sales Remarks and Service Creation, so the form asked for money before the shoots
  were planned and before Shedule Payment had said what the advance was a share of. It is now the
  form's last section and the rail's last entry, after Shedule Payment. Nothing about the section
  itself changed by that move: it is still create-only and still needs `ACCOUNTS / Cash Management`
  `READ_WRITE` to appear at all. (It is no longer optional as a whole — see the Payment Received
  entry under Changed.) Edit Sale Details never
  showed it and still does not
  ([CreateProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CreateProjectFormPanel.js)).
- **Role Management now calls every screen what the sidebar calls it.** A permission was labelled
  from its own vocabulary while the menu the same person reads used another name for the same
  screen — the role form said "Manage Assets", "Manage Sales Projects" and "Asset Management" where
  the sidebar said **Assets**, **Bookings & Converted** and **Resource Management**. Granting a
  permission and then hunting the menu for a heading that was never there is the confusion this
  removes. The headings and entries are now read from the navigation itself rather than written a
  second time, so they cannot drift apart again, and a test holds the two together. A permission
  that opens no screen of its own — All Tasks, Asset Request Approval, the production boards,
  everything under Administration — keeps the name it already had, because inventing a menu name for
  it would suggest there is somewhere to go
  ([navigation.js](../../frontend/src/constants/navigation.js)).

- **A branch now reads as its place — "Erode", not "Erode TN".** The locations master data spells
  every branch with its state appended, which nobody says out loud and which a list of six branches
  does not need to tell them apart. The state is dropped wherever a branch is shown — the role
  branch picker and Role Management's Reach column, User Management's home branch and branches
  reached, and the Assets branch column and picker. Display only: the stored name is untouched, so
  every branch-scoped query, saved record and generated identifier still matches exactly what the
  database holds, and the state is still on the row in its `code` (`ERODE_TN`). A branch whose name
  ends in something that is not a state — "Head Office HQ" — is left alone
  ([branchName.js](../../frontend/src/lib/branchName.js)).

- **Nebula AI's panel is headed by its name alone, on every screen.** The grey pill that followed it
  — `CRM` on the pipeline, `Assets` on Asset Management, and so on for every module — read as a
  second, smaller product name attached to the first, and only ever repeated the screen already
  behind the panel. The header is now the mark, `Nebula AI`, and the close cross, sitting on one line
  at every width down to 360px. Which module is answering is unchanged everywhere it does work: the
  panel's accessible name, the thread's opening line, and the scoping of the answers themselves
  ([NebulaHeaderButton.js](../../frontend/src/components/ai/NebulaHeaderButton.js)).

- **Every record the studio names now carries one shape of identifier, naming its branch and its
  vertical.** Eleven modules each invented their own — `DBS-DD-EID-4867`, `DBS-EVT-00005`,
  `QT-00001`, `AST-000001`, `VEN-000001`, `SM-000001`, `RCP-000001`, `DBS001` — and none of them
  said where the record belonged. They are now `DBS-<what it is>-<vertical>-<branch>-<number>`, with
  the middle two present only where they mean something, and each vertical counting from `000001`
  of its own:

  | | |
  | --- | --- |
  | Employee Repository | `DBS-1` |
  | Leads & Followups | `DBS-EID-QQ-ERD-000001` |
  | Bookings & Converted (the same record Project Management calls a Project) | `DBS-BK-DD-ERD-000001` |
  | Shoots | `DBS-SID-DD-ERD-000001` |
  | Quotations | `DBS-QO-QQ-ERD-000001` |
  | CRD Documents | `DBS-CRD-DD-000001` |
  | Image Selection | `DBS-VCIS-000001` |
  | Accounts — Spent / Receive | `DBS-MS-QQ-ERD-000001` / `DBS-MR-QQ-ERD-000001` |
  | Asset Management | `DBS-ASMGMT-ERD-000001` |
  | Vendor Management | `DBS-VNMGMT-000001` |
  | Client Storage Management | `DBS-STOMGMT-000001` |

  Branches are coded Erode `ERD`, Bangalore `BLR`, Coimbatore `CBE`, Hyderabad `HYD`, Tiruppur
  `TUP`, Vizag `VIZAG`, and are recognised however the record spells them — "Erode TN", "Coimbatore,
  Tamil Nadu" and "Cbe" all read as the same branch. A record naming no branch takes Erode rather
  than refusing to save, because a lead can arrive from the Callyzer webhook with nothing on it.

  **The branch is shown but not counted by**: a vertical runs one sequence across all of its
  branches, so Dum Dum reads `…-DD-ERD-000001`, `…-DD-ERD-000002`, `…-DD-CBE-000003` — the
  Coimbatore lead carrying on the same run rather than restarting at one. The number then says how
  many enquiries that vertical has taken, and the branch code beside it still says where each came
  from. **Records that already existed have been renumbered**, along with every column that quoted
  them; the shapes listed above are the only ones left
  ([identifiers.js](../../backend/src/services/identifiers.js),
  [identifiers.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/identifiers.mjs),
  [renumberIdentifiers.js](../../backend/scripts/renumberIdentifiers.js)).

- **CRD Documents and Image Selection projects have an identifier of their own for the first time.**
  A CRD document was known only by the project it belonged to and an Image Selection project only by
  its name; both now carry a code the studio can quote, shown as the CRD ID column and under the
  project name in the Image Selection sidebar
  ([schema.prisma](../../backend/prisma/schema.prisma)).

- **A new lead's Enquiry ID is issued by the server rather than the browser.** Add Lead used to work
  one out from the highest number among the leads the table happened to be holding, which was only
  right while every lead shared one run of numbers and the browser held them all. An enquiry id now
  counts within its own vertical and branch, and the browser holds a page at a time
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

- **A quotation has one identifier, not two.** The table showed a Quotation Number beside a
  Quotation ID, and the second was `QT` plus a timestamp in base 36 — a code nothing referred to and
  nobody could read out. Both columns now carry the studio's own identifier
  (`DBS-QO-QQ-ERD-000001`), so the table and the info panel show it once, labelled Quotation ID
  ([QuotationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationPage.js)).

- **The Meetings calendar is headed Calendar, and answers the source switch.** The month grid draws
  shoots and main events as readily as meetings, so in Calendar view the page is headed Calendar,
  and the page is headed **Calendar in both views** — a list of shoots and main events is no more
  "Meetings" than the month grid is, and a heading that changed with the view moved under the reader
  every time they changed how they were looking at the same diary. The topbar control that opens it
  is named Calendar too. The All / Projects / Pre Production / Post Production / Meetings switch now
  sits beside that heading in both views instead of vanishing when the grid is on. Choosing a source narrows the grid
  as well as the table: Projects is a project's shoots *and* its main events, and either production
  stage is the bookings of the projects staffed at that stage. The footer says how many of the
  diary's bookings the chosen source holds. The list's own column filters stay the list's — Host and
  Guests mean nothing to a shoot ([MeetingsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/MeetingsPage.js),
  [meetingSources.js](../../frontend/src/lib/meetingSources.js)).

- **Placing a Callyzer call into a vertical re-issues its Enquiry ID for that vertical.** The
  webhook creates the lead the moment the call arrives, before anybody knows which vertical it is,
  so the id it is minted with reads `DBS-EID-DD-…` — the default. Confirming the vertical on the
  Add Lead form now renames it for the one it turned out to be, so a call placed into Qua Qua reads
  `DBS-EID-QQ-…` instead of carrying Dum Dum's code for the rest of its life. The rename moves the
  lead's key and the ten other columns that quote it in one transaction, and is recorded in the
  lead's own history. A lead moved between verticals by hand later keeps its id — by then it has
  been quoted on a project, a quotation and very likely to the client
  ([crmLeadService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/crmLeadService.js)).

- **Leads & Followups → Calls is one queue: Yet to connect.** The Calls board holds the Callyzer
  calls the router could not place, and every row on it is by definition a caller nobody has
  connected with — it stays a row only until somebody gives it a vertical, at which point it leaves
  for that board. So All (the same list under a second name) and Today New Leads are gone, and the
  one tab is dated by nothing: a call that came in last week and is still unplaced is in it, which
  under the arrival-day rule the verticals use would have left it in no tab on any board. Placing a
  call is unchanged — double-click, pick the vertical, confirm
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js)).

- **The account pill in the top bar is plain until it is pointed at.** The signed-in name and
  initial no longer sit in a permanent grey box; the tint and hairline appear under the pointer
  and on keyboard focus, and the menu behind it is unchanged
  ([AppTopbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppTopbar.js)).

- **The Global Name sits beside the logo in the top bar.** The name set under Settings → Global
  Logo is written to the right of the mark on every signed-in screen, centred beside it in
  Montserrat at 12px, in the bar's own text colour so it reads in every theme; it steps aside on a
  phone. Montserrat is self-hosted through next/font and offered as `--font-montserrat`
  ([layout.js](../../frontend/src/app/layout.js)). The mark and the name share one read of the
  branding ([AppTopbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppTopbar.js),
  [useStudioBranding.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/useStudioBranding.js)).

- **Settings → Studio Branding is now Global Logo.** The section is titled Global Logo, its name
  field Global Name and its logo field Global Logo, in the Settings hub and in the Image Selection
  settings form that edits the same two values
  ([StudioBrandingPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/StudioBrandingPanel.js),
  [formPanelConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/formPanelConfig.js)).

- **Info panel answers read a point larger than their labels.** In every table's Info panel the
  field labels (Employee ID, Employee Name, ...) are a pixel smaller at 13px and the values under
  them a pixel larger at 15px with a pixel more air between the two, so the answer leads and the
  label only names it
  ([RecordDetails.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/RecordDetails.js)). The Meetings
  panel, which draws its own details, takes the same two sizes
  ([MeetingsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/MeetingsPage.js)).

- **Meetings' List view is filtered by four tabs instead of a second dropdown.** **All**, **Today**,
