  **Upcoming** and **Past** sit under the title, each carrying its own count, the way Leads &
  Followups reads — and the count is what pressing the tab will actually show, because it is taken
  after the column filters rather than before them. All is new: a booking whose date is unreadable
  used to be listed under every range at once and is now listed there alone. The Date column's sort
  still follows the tab, soonest-first everywhere but Past. The tab is remembered across a reload.
  ([meetings.js](../../frontend/src/lib/meetings.js),
  [MeetingsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/MeetingsPage.js))

- **The Meetings header reads the way the CRM's does.** The source switch sits against the title,
  behind the same left divider Leads & Followups puts its vertical behind — it names what the table
  below holds, so it belongs to the heading: "Meetings — All". The view switch stays at the end of
  the row beside Add Meet, because changing between a month grid and a table is closer to an action
  than to a description of what is listed. Neither draws a box: `mode="switch"` is a bare label and
  a chevron everywhere else in the app, and both are only given a fixed width, so choosing a longer
  name cannot shift the tabs beneath or nudge Add Meet sideways.
  ([MeetingsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/MeetingsPage.js))

- **The topbar's Meetings button is a calendar.** It opens the studio's diary, so it now wears a
  calendar rather than the group-of-people icon, which read as a directory of staff.
  ([AppTopbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppTopbar.js))

- **One rule decides whether a project is in pre- or post-production.** The Pre Production and Post
  Production boards and the Meetings list read the same two functions, so the boards and the
  schedule cannot come to disagree about which stage a project is in.
  ([projectStages.js](../../frontend/src/lib/projectStages.js),
  [ProjectWorkflowModulePage.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectWorkflowModulePage.js))

- **Edit Sale Details now shows the whole Create Project flow, and its Service Creation really edits
  the project.** Sales Manager's *Bookings & Converted* → **Edit** offered only Project Details and
  Sales Remarks. It now carries **Service Creation** and **Shedule Payment** as well. Service
  Creation reads the project's real shoots, services and deliverables and writes every change back
  as it is made — through the same endpoints Start / Edit Project has always used, including the
  careful shoot deletion that clears the shoot's Image Selection footprint first and refuses when
  the client has already submitted selections from it. That wiring was lifted out of Start / Edit
  Project into [useProjectPlan.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/useProjectPlan.js),
  so the two screens share one plan editor rather than two that drift; Start / Edit Project behaves
  exactly as before. **Payment Received stays create-only**: the advance is recorded once when the
  project is created and corrected afterwards in Accounts or on the project's Payments tab —
  offering it on edit would write a second cash entry on every save
  ([CreateProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CreateProjectFormPanel.js),
  [StartProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/StartProjectFormPanel.js)).

- **Create Project now shows Service Creation and Shedule Payment, the same two sections Start
  Project has.** The shoots-services-deliverables block this form called **Event Planning** is
  renamed **Service Creation**, matching what the rest of the OS calls it, and a **Shedule Payment**
  section follows it — its Signed Quotation tracking the Initial Converted Value as it is typed, so
  the schedule is a share of what the project is actually being sold for. The section itself was
  lifted out of Start / Edit Project into
  [SchedulePaymentSection.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/SchedulePaymentSection.js)
  so both forms render one component rather than two copies that drift. **The schedule is still not
  saved** — the rows reach no payload from either form, and a table of their own is the follow-up
  ([CreateProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CreateProjectFormPanel.js)).
- **Start / Edit Project's "Identification" section is now "Project Setup".** It holds the four crew
  assignments — Project Manager, Pre Production Coordinator, Creative Manager, Post Production
  Coordinator — plus Welcome Gift, Project Validity, Storage Needed and CRD Document. "Identification"
  described none of that
  ([StartProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/StartProjectFormPanel.js)).

- **Create Project offers only the quotations raised for the enquiry, and picking one fills in what
  it was quoted at.** *Select Quotation* stayed empty of an enquiry filter until one was typed, and
  asking for quotations without an id returns every quotation in the studio — so a project could be
  booked against a stranger's figure. With no Enquiry ID the picker now says to choose one first and
  offers nothing. Each option reads `QUT039-03 · ₹98,000`, and choosing it writes that amount into
  **Initial Converted Value** rather than leaving it to be retyped off another screen; the field
  stays editable, so a sale closed a little above or below the quotation is still typed over the top
  ([CreateProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CreateProjectFormPanel.js)).

- **A table row can now carry rows of its own.** `DataTable` takes an optional
  `features.expandable` — `{ getSubRows, canExpand, label }` — and draws a chevron column ahead of
  the first one for the rows that have something under them. Sub-rows are drawn with the same
  columns as their parent, but a row can mark its own tone and indentation so an expanded group can
  read as part of the row above rather than as a second table. The Quotation register uses it to
  open earlier versions under the current one, and deleting a version now removes only that one
  version and leaves the chain standing on the version below it
  ([projects/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/routes.js)).
- **A project shows the vertical its lead belongs to, instead of a dash.** `vertical` was only ever
  written when the WhatsApp group name came from the generator, so a project whose name was typed by
  hand had none - Start Project read "Vertical : -" for a lead the CRM had filed under Qua Qua all
  along. The CRM lead behind the enquiry is now the fallback, translated from its own spelling into
  the stored code, on create, on update and when the projects are read - so projects saved before
  this fix show their vertical without a backfill. It reads as Dum Dum / Qua Qua / Corp rather than
  WED / QQ / CORP, and carries through to the CRD Documents table
  ([salesVertical.js](../../backend/src/services/salesVertical.js),
  [projects/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js),
  [StartProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/StartProjectFormPanel.js)).

- **CRD Documents shows its Status as a coloured dot beside the word.** The Status column drops the
  filled chip for the same dot-and-label cell the Employee Repository uses — green for Submitted,
  amber for Yet To Receive, grey for Link Not sent — so a status reads the same way in every table
  ([CrdDocumentsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CrdDocumentsPage.js)).

- **Global Name is optional, and clearing it clears it.** Settings → Global Logo and the Image
  Selection settings form no longer insist on a name, and emptying the field now saves as empty
  instead of writing the default studio name back over it. The top bar, the sign-in page and the
  client gallery each show nothing in its place; only an install that has never saved these
  settings still reads the default ([StudioBrandingPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/StudioBrandingPanel.js),
  [settings/validation.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/settings/validation.js)).

- **The top bar shows the username, not the employee name.** The user control beside the avatar
  reads the username generated with the login credentials, as the account holds it now; the
  employee's name stays on My Profile and in the Employee Repository. The access token carries
  the username too, so the bar names the account from the first paint after signing in
  ([AppTopbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppTopbar.js),
  [authController.js](../../backend/src/controllers/authController.js)).

- **The top bar user menu is headed by the signed-in email.** The heading over Appearance, My
  Profile and the rest read the display name, which the button beneath already shows; it now
  reads the email the person signed in with, in its own case
  ([AppTopbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppTopbar.js)).

- **The Image Selection project sidebar hides its close chevron until hovered.** Open, the disc on
  the sidebar's edge appears only while the pointer is over the sidebar or it has keyboard focus,
  the same manners as the info panel's collapse handle; the reopen chevron on the collapsed rail
  stays on show, and on small screens the close control is always visible because touch has no
  hover ([ProjectSidebar.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ProjectSidebar.js)).

- **My Profile's Personal Information tab is display-only, two boxes to a row.** The tab now
  states up to ten facts — name, employee ID, email, contact number, username, designation,
  department, date of joining, work location, blood group, gender — in read-only text boxes
  laid out two per row from `md` up and one below it, instead of a single narrow column with
  an editable form, and the Change Password form sits centred beside it. Editing is deferred;
  the note under the grid points to HR. The
  `PATCH /api/user/profile` route and its service function stay in place for when it returns
  ([ProfilePage.js](../../frontend/src/components/profile/ProfilePage.js)).
- **My Profile has a Back button beside its title, and its details sit centred.** The arrow
  returns to the page My Profile was chosen from — noted by the user menu at that moment in
  session storage, since browser history is not that page after a reload — and falls to the
  first screen the role may open when there is none. The control is a new `onBack` slot on
  the shared module header, so any page reached from elsewhere can carry one
  ([ModulePageHeader.js](../../frontend/src/components/layout/ModulePageHeader.js),
  [profileReturn.js](../../frontend/src/lib/profileReturn.js)).
- **The top bar's user button shows the person's name.** It read "User: <email>" for an
  employee and "Super Admin" for the administrator; it now shows the name on the signed-in
  account, falling to the email and then the role only where a login has no name, and the
  phone-sized initials are the name's own rather than "E" / "SA"
  ([AppTopbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppTopbar.js)).
- **The release moved from the sidebar footer into the user menu.** "Release Information" in
  the top bar's user menu opens a small dialog: the version the server is running as a
  headline, its release date, and the first entries of this changelog's top section with a
  count of the rest — read by a new signed-in `GET /api/release`, so the notes are the record
  itself rather than a second copy. A tab on an older build than the server is told to
  reload. The sidebar footer no longer prints the version, and the repository gains the
  `VERSION` file the release script and the deploy already expected
  ([ReleaseInfoDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/ReleaseInfoDialog.js),
  [releaseNotes.js](../../backend/src/services/releaseNotes.js)).
- **The studio logo moved to the top bar, in place of the "DBS Operations OS" wording.** It sat in
  the sidebar's header, which meant it was only ever seen by opening the drawer; it now sits beside
  the hamburger on the bar that is on every signed-in screen, and the sidebar's header row is gone
  with it. The mark links home to the first route the signed-in role may actually open, rather than
  the `/hrm/departments` the sidebar sent everyone to. The drawer opens below the bar so the mark
  and the hamburger stay in view, and the dark themes' white chip behind the artwork followed the
  logo across ([AppTopbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppTopbar.js),
  [AppSidebar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppSidebar.js)).

- **A branch picker now offers only the branches you reach.** Every list of branches in the app —
  the Roles dialog and a user's home branch, Asset Management, the Branch on a cash entry or a
  payment request, a project's Source Branch — is narrowed to the branches the signed-in person's
  roles actually reach, rather than showing the whole studio to anyone with write access. A super
  admin reaches all of them and sees no change.
- **And the server refuses a save that names one you don't.** Narrowing the dropdown alone is
  cosmetic while the request can still be sent by hand, so each of those saves is checked against
  the same reach — by name where a record stores its branch as text, by id where it is a real
  column. Two are worth calling out: nobody can grant a role reach into a branch they do not
  themselves have, or post a user into one, which closes the path by which a branch manager holding
  the Users screen could have granted themselves another branch; and a payment request is checked
  only on the branch a requester actually picks, since the other two are read off a project or an
  employee record and are the answer either way.
  ([iam.service.js](../../backend/src/services/iam.service.js) gains `reachableBranches`,
  `canUseBranchId` and `canUseBranchName`; see
  [IAM.md](../project-state/IAM.md#where-a-branch-can-be-chosen).)

- **`react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` reports as a warning rather than failing the build.** Every
  occurrence is a client-only value that has to be read after mount or hydration breaks — the
  gallery session, the upload dock's position, a resumable batch, an OAuth return — or an async
  load writing its own result. Left as an error it would have had CI push a restructuring of
  hydration and data loading in the public gallery and the CRM page to satisfy a lint rule rather
  than a user-visible problem. It stays on the report, so a genuinely derivable piece of state
  added later is still noticed. See [eslint.config.mjs](../../frontend/eslint.config.mjs).

- **Administration opens on every employee, and Users lists the accounts that can sign in.** The
  Employee Repository arrived on the Active tab, so the onboarding and departed rows were hidden
  until somebody thought to look for them; it now opens on **All**. The Users screen listed every
  account whatever its status, including closed ones that cannot sign in at all; it now lists the
  active ones, narrowed by `GET /api/admin/users` through its new optional `status` query parameter
  so the row count is the count of what is listed. An account carrying no role is still listed, with
  `—` for its roles: this screen is the only place a role can be given, so leaving it out would
  strand an account created with the Roles section left empty. See [IAM.md](../project-state/IAM.md),
  [EmployeeVerificationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeVerificationPage.js)
  and [admin/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/admin/routes.js).

- **The Onboarding Invites dialog keeps one size.** It used to be as tall as the number of invites
  in it, so it jumped between a strip and most of the screen as the list loaded or an invite was
  cancelled. It now opens at a fixed height and the rows scroll inside it, with the header row
  holding its place at the top of the scroll. `Dialog` grows with its content as before unless a
  caller asks for the fixed height with `height="tall"`. See
  [OnboardingInviteDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/OnboardingInviteDialog.js) and
  [ui/Dialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/ui/Dialog.js).

- **Onboarding completes at the credentials, not at the review.** Approve &amp; Onboard (now the
  usual blue, not green) saves the reviewed details and opens the login-credentials form
  directly, but the employee stays on **HR Onboarding Pending** — the server marks them Active
  only when the login is created *and* its credential email actually sends. A failed email now
  deletes the just-made account and reports the failure so HR can simply retry, instead of
  leaving a half-made login that blocked every retry with "already exists" while the employee
  never turned Active. Closing the credentials form leaves the row pending; running Review →
  Approve again reopens it
  ([employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js),
  [EmployeeVerificationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeVerificationPage.js)).

- **Family Details and the full Emergency Contact are mandatory on the onboarding form.**
  Father's and Mother's names and occupations, Marital Status, and Emergency Contact
  Relationship are now required (the contact's name and number already were), and a married
  candidate must give Spouse Name and Wedding Anniversary — enforced by the server as well as
  the form
  ([onboardingFormConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/onboardingFormConfig.js),
  [onboarding-invites/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/onboarding-invites/controller.js)).

- **A pending onboarding can be sent back to the candidate to edit, under the same link.** A
  submission on **HR Onboarding Pending** now offers **Send Back** in its row menu: after a
  confirmation, the invite reopens under its original link (expiry extended so the mail is not
  already dead) and the candidate is emailed that same link. Opening it, they verify as before
  and find the form filled with everything they submitted — images included — to edit and
  resubmit; the resubmission updates the same employee row, so the DBS id survives and no
  duplicate appears. To make the same link mailable again, invites now keep their raw token (a
  deliberate softening of the hash-only design — the token opens one public form, not an
  account); invites created before this cannot be sent back and say so. The row menu's **Copy
  Link** entry — usable only immediately after creation, and otherwise a dead item explaining
  itself — is removed
  ([onboarding-invites/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/onboarding-invites/controller.js),
  [EmployeeTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeTable.js),
  [PublicOnboardingForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/onboarding/PublicOnboardingForm.js)).

- **Choosing an enquiry in Create Project brings its generated group name, and creating the
  project clears the generator's rows for that enquiry.** Group names are saved against an
  enquiry now, so picking the enquiry auto-selects the name the generator prepared for it (the
  saved-names dropdown also shows each name's enquiry id). When the project is saved, the used
  name is claimed by deleting its row — under the same atomic guard the old status flip had, so
  two concurrent creates still cannot take one name — and every other name generated for that
  enquiry is deleted with it; the project keeps the name text and the generator snapshot rather
  than a pointer at a row that no longer exists
  ([projects/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js),
  [CreateProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CreateProjectFormPanel.js)).

- **Enquiry pickers offer the CRM's lead enquiries, and the group-name generator asks for one.**
  The enquiry search behind Create Project listed existing sales projects, so it offered
  event/project ids and a brand-new lead's enquiry could not be found; it now searches the CRM
  leads by enquiry id, client name or number, and a picked lead prefills the client's number and
  branch. The W/A Group-Name Generator gains the same searchable Enquiry ID field (required), so
  every saved group name is filed under the lead it was prepared for — stored on a new
  `enquiry_id` column, with the branch column widened for full branch names. The server also now
  verifies the posted branch is an active location the caller's roles reach, replacing the old
  hardcoded code-list check
  ([EnquiryCombobox.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/EnquiryCombobox.js),
  [WhatsappGroupNameDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/WhatsappGroupNameDialog.js),
  [whatsapp-group-names/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/whatsapp-group-names/controller.js),
  [projects/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js)).

- **The W/A Group-Name Generator's branch picker comes from the database, scoped by role.** The
  Branch dropdown offered a hardcoded list of seven branch codes (HYD, VSKP, …) to everyone; it
  now loads the active `locations` master data, which the server already narrows to the branches
  the caller's roles reach — so it shows full branch names, a CRM only sees their own branches,
  and a branch added in Admin appears without a code change. The generated group name carries
  the full branch name the picker shows. The dialog's rows also line up now: the radio groups
  wore a bigger label and a taller box than the text fields beside them, so every row with a
  radio on one side sat lower than its neighbour; they share the fields' metrics
  ([WhatsappGroupNameDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/WhatsappGroupNameDialog.js)).

- **The sidebar header shows the studio logo instead of the "DBS Operations OS" wordmark.** The
  same Don't Blink Studioz logo the public quotation and gallery pages use now heads the
  navigation sidebar, still linking home. The header's close (×) button is gone — tapping the
  backdrop or navigating still closes the drawer. Because the artwork is dark teal, both dark
  themes set it on a small white chip so it stays visible against the darkened sidebar
  ([AppSidebar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppSidebar.js),
  [globals.css](../../frontend/src/app/globals.css)).

- **Manual projects download shoot-wise, and only shoot-wise.** The workspace header's
  **Download Selected (Original Quality)** used to recognise only Project Management shoot
  folders, so a manually named project waited for the whole project's selection and downloaded
  everything as one archive. A manual project's root folder now pairs with the shoot group of
  the same name — the same pairing the client gallery lists and submits that group under — so
  the download appears as soon as that group is submitted and fetches only that folder's photos,
  filed as Project ▸ Selection item ▸ Shoot ▸ Subfolder (the manifest now reads a typed item's
  own name instead of falling back to "Unsorted"). The whole-project archive is never offered on
  a manual project; a linked project keeps it as the fallback inside a folder with no shoot.
  ([GalleryPublication.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/GalleryPublication.js),
  [gallery/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/gallery/service.js))
- **The CRM branch and `develop` are one line of work again.** The Sales CRM, Meetings, Google
  Sheets sync and table settings work now sits alongside Image Selection, Asset Management and the
  sales/project workflow rewrite. Where both sides had changed the same shared control they were
  combined rather than one side chosen: `TextField`, `DateField` and `Dropdown` keep the new `error`
  state *and* the CRM's own styling; `IconButton` keeps the size and shape scale while no longer
  defaulting `title`, so icon buttons draw no browser tooltip unless one is asked for;
  `OptionsMenu` accepts both the layer-scale `menuZIndexClassName` and the numeric `zIndex` a
  portalled trigger needs, and regained `triggerVariant`; `ModulePageHeader` answers to both
  `titleAddon` and `titleAccessory`. Portalled popovers (the calendar, the dropdown menu) now
  default above the CRM's dialogs, which stack to z-200 — at the previous default they opened
  behind the dialog that had opened them.
  ([shared/](../../frontend/src/components/shared/), [ui/OptionsMenu.js](../../frontend/src/components/ui/OptionsMenu.js))
- **The API client has one implementation again.** `lib/apiClient.js` re-exports
  `services/api/client.js`, and that client now carries the Google-reconnect guard: a 401 raised
  because the linked Google account needs re-authorising no longer signs the user out of the app.
  ([services/api/client.js](../../frontend/src/services/api/client.js))

- Selected originals are filed as **Project ▸ Selection item ▸ Shoot ▸ Folder**. The browser
  download used to insert a `Selected Images` folder between the project and the selection item; it
  no longer does, so the studio gets the project name, then one folder per selection item, then the
  shoot and the gallery subfolder each photo came from. The server-side copy into the Selected
  Output Path already filed them this way, so both routes now produce the same tree.
  ([DownloadSelectedButton.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/DownloadSelectedButton.js))
- The calendar is smaller: 260px wide instead of 320, with 28px day cells, an 11px grid and tighter
  padding. It sits better beside a narrow field such as the Expiry Date in the 300px Image Selection
  settings rail, and the placement estimate was brought down with it so the calendar is still
  positioned against its real size. ([DateField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/DateField.js))
- New `layers.popover` (`z-[140]`) for an overlay rendered into the body rather than beside the
  control that opened it. Leaving its own surface means it no longer inherits that surface's
  stacking, so it has to clear the highest thing it can be opened from — a dialog over a form panel.
  The calendar is the first to use it. ([layers.js](../../frontend/src/constants/layers.js))
- A full-screen form with only one section no longer shows the section sidebar. The rail listed a
  single link to the place the reader was already at and left a grey column down the side of a
  short form; every form with more than one section is untouched.
  ([SchemaFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/schema-form/SchemaFormPanel.js))
- **Download Selected (Original Quality)** now disappears when a project is unpublished. It used to
  appear as soon as the client had sent their selection and stayed regardless of publish status, so
  a gallery that had been withdrawn from the client still invited the studio to pull originals out
  of it. The workspace header goes back to showing the Unpublished pill; republishing brings the
  button back, and the client's selection is kept either way. Toggling Publish Status from either
  the Selection Settings rail or ⋮ → Project Settings updates the header immediately.
  ([GalleryPublication.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/GalleryPublication.js))
- Gallery link validity, the fallback Selection Items limit and the studio name on the WhatsApp
  invite now read from studio-wide settings instead of constants in
  `publication/`, `deliverables/` and `publish-share/`. Both numbers stay defaults rather than
  rules: a gallery already published keeps the expiry it was given, and a deliverable that carries
  its own quantity or override keeps that. The constants remain as the fallback values.
- The Selection Items table is now one component shared by the workspace rail and, at a roomier
  size, anywhere else it is needed — drawn at two sizes rather than copied. Behaviour is unchanged
  in the rail, which keeps its compact idiom through `dense`.
  ([SelectionItemsTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/SelectionItemsTable.js))
- Building the per-project settings form from a project row moved out of the rail and into
  [imageSelectionSettings.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/imageSelectionSettings.mjs) as
  `projectSettingsState`, `settingsFormState` and `isManualProject`. Covered by unit tests.

- **Each Download Selected run saves into a fresh, dated root folder.** Re-downloading used to
  write the new files into the same project folder over the previous run; now every press gets a
  root of its own under the Target Download Path — stamped with the day,
  `John & Priya Wedding - 02-Sep-2026`, and numbered ` (1)`, ` (2)` Windows-style when the same
  name already exists. Only the root is renamed: the Selection item ▸ Shoot ▸ Folder tree inside
  keeps the exact pattern it had. The same rule covers per-shoot downloads, and in a browser
  without a chosen folder the date goes on the ZIP's name with the browser's own download
  numbering as the counter. Other saves (CSV exports, retouches, single files) are untouched
  ([projectStorage.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/projectStorage.mjs),
  [DownloadSelectedButton.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/DownloadSelectedButton.js)).
- **A shoot's download button reads "Download Selected" alone.** It carried the shoot's name
  after a separator — "Download Selected · Pre Wedding" — which only repeated the card the
  button already sits on and stretched the label; the name now lives in the button's tooltip
  instead. The project-wide button keeps its "(Original Quality)" label
  ([DownloadSelectedButton.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/DownloadSelectedButton.js)).
- **The info panel's collapse handle appears on hover; the expand handle stays put.** While
  the panel is open, the circular chevron straddling its edge shows itself only while the pointer
  is over the panel (or reaches the handle itself), instead of sitting on the edge at all times;
  keyboard focus still reveals it. Once the panel is shut to its rail the handle is always on
  show, since it is the one control that reopens the panel. The full-screen sheet on mobile keeps
  its always-visible × — a touch screen has no hover to summon a handle with. Applies to every
  module's info panel, which all share the one shell
  ([InfoPanelShell.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/InfoPanelShell.js)).
- **Studio Branding has moved out of User Settings and into the application's Settings, after
  Project Settings.** It was never a user setting: the studio name and the client-gallery logo are
  one record the whole studio shares, and the routes behind them answer for ADMIN / System Settings
  — so anyone without that permission was being shown a form whose Save the server would refuse.
  It is now the last entry on the Settings rail, offered on the permission that actually governs it.
  The screen itself is unchanged: the same two fields, the same Save, now drawn in the shared section
  wrapper the rest of that rail uses. The panel moved to a file of its own so there is one
  implementation of it rather than a copy left behind
  ([StudioBrandingPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/StudioBrandingPanel.js),
  [SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js)).
- **User Settings has gone from the account menu; Settings is the one way in.** With Studio Branding
  moved out, the User Settings overlay held nothing but the WhatsApp table — which Settings, a line
  below it in the same menu, already lists under User Settings > WhatsApp. It was one screen
  reachable two ways, so the row and the overlay behind it are both removed and the WhatsApp table
  is unchanged in its own file. Note that Settings is not offered to the `EMPLOYEE` role, so that
  role no longer reaches the WhatsApp table from the menu; nothing about linking a WhatsApp changes,
  which each CRM still does from the WhatsApp Chat tab of any lead
  ([AppTopbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppTopbar.js),
  [WhatsAppUsersTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/WhatsAppUsersTable.js)).
- **The navigation drawer now dims Nebula AI along with the page behind it, and the assistant's
  mark is drawn large enough to see.** Opening the drawer over an open Nebula panel dimmed the
  module but left the panel beside it fully lit, as though the two were separate applications; the
  drawer and its scrim now sit above the assistant (106/108 rather than 80/90, still below the lead
  popup and every dialog, none of which can be open while the drawer is reachable), so the whole
  screen dims at once and a press anywhere on the dim closes the drawer — including on the panel.
  On a phone this also puts the drawer over the assistant's full-width sheet instead of under it.
  The mark itself is much larger everywhere it appears, on every module: about 38px of ink in the
  module header's trigger where it was 18px, 34px in the lead assistant's floating button, and 23px
  in both panel headers' discs. The trigger's button grew with it, 36px to 44px, so it is now larger
  than the lettered buttons beside it rather than level with them — a lettered button is mostly
  padding around 14px of text and an icon is the whole control, so matching their boxes was what
  kept the mark small. The header row sizes to its content and takes the extra 8px, and the mark
  still centres on the same axis as the text beside it. Nebula now reads as a control rather than
  as a speck
  ([layers.js](../../frontend/src/constants/layers.js),
  [AppSidebar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppSidebar.js),
  [NebulaHeaderButton.js](../../frontend/src/components/ai/NebulaHeaderButton.js)).
- **Reports reads the clock the way the studio does: twelve hours, in IST.** Every timestamp across
  the module — Call History's Call Time, First and Last Call, Last Attempt, WhatsApp Usage's First
  and Last Message, Application Usage's Last Action and Last Sign-in — printed the twenty-four hour
  clock, so a call at six in the evening read "17:58". They now read "2 Sept 2026, 5:58 pm". The
  locale carries the whole convention, so the hour is unpadded ("5:58 pm", not "05:58 pm") and the
  meridiem is lower case. Every date and time is also printed in Asia/Kolkata rather than the
  browser's zone: the range a report is built from is already the studio's day, so a call made
  after midnight IST is now filed on the report of the day it was actually made on. The Sales
  Dashboard's Last Call column reads from the same helper and follows.
  ([crmReports.js](../../frontend/src/lib/crmReports.js))
- **Project Settings is a group in Settings rather than a panel of its own.** Services, Deliverables
  and Size Options used to open from the Projects board's ⋮ as a second full-screen form over the
  app — settings that lived outside Settings. They are now a Project Settings group in the one
  Settings rail, sitting between Sales Integrations and Pre Production. Nothing about the workflow
  changed: the same rows, the same configure-in-a-dialog step, the same staging across all three
  sections, and the same Close / Save footer, which Settings now carries while the group is on.
  The three sections are one scrolling page with one Save beneath them, exactly as the panel has
  always drawn them — the rail's three entries are anchors that scroll to a section rather than
  screens that replace the page, so the Save is visibly the one Save that writes all three. Each
  table also draws every row it has instead of scrolling inside a 32rem window, so the page scrolls
  once rather than four times. Leaving the group with edits staged asks first. The board's ⋮
  Settings now opens Settings there, the way the CRM's own already does for Table Settings, and the
  group is offered on `PROJECT / Projects` write.
  ([SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js),
  [ProjectSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectSettingsPanel.js),
  [ProjectsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectsPage.js))
- **Settings › Table Settings is the same screen Employee Settings shows, HRM lists included.**
  The overlay fetched and saved the table fields itself, with its own copy of the response
  grouping — a second implementation of one endpoint beside the shared `TableSettingsSection` that
  Employee Settings uses. It now renders that shared section, so both ways in are one screen: pick
  a module, then what varies inside it. Leads offers its three verticals; Human Resource Management
  offers the four lists an employee form draws its answers from — Designations, Departments,
  Verticals and Skills — and edits them in place. The builder is also given the panel's full width
  here, as it has in Employee Settings, instead of the narrower reading column the field forms use.
  ([SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js))
- **A contact opens its record on the Contacts page instead of navigating to another module.**
  Double-clicking a contact used to send the reader to the CRM board or to Project Management with
  the record open there — a look-up mid-something-else answered by taking the list they were reading
  away. The same two records now open over the directory itself: a lead opens the CRM's identical
  lead popup, with its Remarks, Call Logs, WhatsApp Chat, Transcripts and Converted History tabs,
  its Update buttons and its CNA count; a project opens the same record view Project Management's
  own double click opens, Payments tab included. Both read and write through their modules' own
  endpoints, so what is done in them is done to the record, and closing one puts the reader back on
  the row they double-clicked. Back and "Proceed Next" step through the directory's own lead rows.
  ([ContactsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/contacts/ContactsPage.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [ProjectDataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/ProjectDataTable.js))

### Fixed

- **A sent-back onboarding link opens again, instead of showing the candidate a cached refusal.**
  A candidate who opened their link after submitting saw "This onboarding invite has already been
  submitted" — and their browser stored it. When HR sent the submission back, the link went on
  showing that same sentence: the invite was open, the browser was reading its own copy of the
  refusal, and nothing the studio could do would reach them. The cause is that **410 Gone is
  cacheable by default**, and the public onboarding routes sent no `Cache-Control` at all, so the
  browser decided for itself how long the refusal stayed fresh. They now send
  `no-store, must-revalidate` — the 200 needs it as much as the 410, since an invite's state changes
  underneath a link that is meant to be opened more than once. It also explains why the fault looked
  intermittent: only a candidate who had *opened* the link after submitting had anything cached, so
  a fresh invite always worked. **Note for anyone already affected:** the header stops new caching
  but cannot clear what a browser has already stored, so an affected link needs one hard reload
  (⌘⇧R) before it reads live again
  ([onboarding-invites/publicRoutes.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/onboarding-invites/publicRoutes.js)).
- **Send Back and the message it hands over now name the same invite.** The employee list read the
  candidate's submitted invite from an *unordered* query and kept whichever row landed last, while
  `sendBackOnboarding` reopens the newest by `createdAt`. For a candidate holding one submitted
  invite the two agree; for one who was re-invited and submitted twice they could pick different
  rows, so the dialog handed out the link of one invite while the send-back reopened the other — and
  the candidate opened a link still marked Submitted. Both order the same way now
  ([employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js)).
- **The WhatsApp session now says whether *this* CRM is allowed to link at all, not just whether the
  studio is.** There are two switches - the studio-wide one, and one CRM's own, which a Super Admin
  moves in Settings → User Settings → WhatsApp. Both were checked on every write, and only the
  studio's was ever reported, so the browser could not tell somebody who has *not linked* their
  WhatsApp from somebody who is *not allowed to*. The session reply carries `userChatEnabled`
  beside `chatEnabled` now, which is what lets the reminder above stay silent for a CRM who could
  not act on it - being nagged to do something the server would refuse is worse than not being told.
  Additive: everything the reply already carried is unchanged
  ([whatsapp-chat/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/whatsapp-chat/controller.js)).
- **A branch added, renamed or disabled now reaches every branch picker on the screen, without a
  reload.** Settings is an overlay, so the screen it is opened over stays mounted holding the branch
  list it fetched when it opened - and nothing told it otherwise. Add a branch in Settings →
  Organization → Branches, close the overlay, and the CRM's Assigned Branch, a quotation's Source
  Branch, Sale Details' Source Branch, an asset's Location, a user's home branch and the WhatsApp
  group name dialog all went on offering the list they were born with until somebody reloaded the
  page; a renamed branch kept its old name in all of them, and a disabled one stayed selectable.
  Saving a branch now says so, and every picker re-reads its own list. What it announces is a
  **revision**, not the branches: three endpoints answer this question and each answers it
  differently - the whole register, the active master data, and that same list narrowed to the
  branches the signed-in person's roles reach - so a shared list of branches would have to pick one
  of the three and hand it to readers the other two belong to, which is how a picker ends up
  offering a branch its owner may not file against. A number changes nothing about what any picker
  asks for or is allowed to see. Announced only after the write has actually succeeded, so a branch
  the server refused sets nothing re-fetching. Access is unchanged throughout: every picker asks the
  same endpoint it always did, under the same permission
  ([branchRegistry.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/branchRegistry.mjs),
  [useBranchRegistry.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/useBranchRegistry.js),
  [BranchesPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/BranchesPanel.js)).

- **The client's copy of a quotation now reads the same on a phone and a tablet as on a laptop, and
  as the PDF.** It was responsive, so the sheet reflowed to 360px on a phone and 736px on a tablet
  against 1080px on a laptop - three layouts, only the last of which is the one printed into the
  PDF. A client opening the link on their phone and the PDF they had been sent were visibly
  different documents: a five-column proposal card on one, a single narrow column on the other.
  The route now declares the sheet's own width as its viewport, so every device is handed the whole
  1080px design and scales it to fit - about a third on a 360px phone, near full size on a 1024px
  tablet - with pinch-zoom to read the rest, which is how a PDF behaves on a phone. **The PDF is
  untouched**, and provably so: rendered before and after, the two files are byte-for-byte identical
  once the `/ID` and timestamps that differ between any two renders are set aside. Its renderer sets
  an explicit 1080px viewport with no device emulation, and Chrome only honours a meta viewport when
  emulating one. Nothing in the sheet's markup changed - the `sm:` classes are still there and
  simply never apply now. The Terms & Conditions page at `/q/<token>/terms` is the same sheet under
  the same layout, so it follows without being told
  ([q/[token]/layout.js](../../frontend/src/app/q/[token]/layout.js)).

- **A CRM is an active Sales employee, everywhere the board names one.** The CRM column filter, the
  Update Details picker and both Assign To fields offered the Sales roster *plus* every name already
  written on a loaded lead — which sounds harmless and is not, because those names are text a CSV or
  a sheet wrote, not people. The lists filled with people who had left, people who were never
  employees, and misspellings of people who are, and picking one wrote it straight back onto the
  leads. A studio that has not added its Sales employees yet saw a list made entirely of names off
  its own imports. All four now read the roster alone. **The consequence is deliberate:** a lead
  whose CRM is not an active Sales employee cannot be found from that filter, and adding the person
  to the Employee repository is what brings them back. The Assigned Branch filter is unchanged — a
  branch written on a lead is still a branch. The board also stops fetching the roster twice on every
  mount ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).
- **The CRM board you were last reading survives a reload.** It always opened on Dum Dum, so anyone
  working Qua Qua was put back on the wedding board every time they refreshed — which is exactly what
  you do on a board you are working through. It is remembered the same way the tab strip beside it
  already was, so it follows the person to another machine, and the tab is resolved against the
  remembered board's own tabs: a Qua Qua reader lands back on Qua Qua's Newborns rather than on Dum
  Dum's All ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).
- **The notifications panel is capped and scrolls.** Every unplaced call and every overdue follow-up
  is a row, so it ran to hundreds — and because it is a fixed-position menu it simply grew past the
  bottom of the window, with the rows beyond unreachable: the page behind does not scroll it and it
  had no scroller of its own. The rows scroll now and the NOTIFICATIONS heading stays above them
  ([AppNotifications.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppNotifications.js),
  [OptionsMenu.js](../../frontend/src/components/ui/OptionsMenu.js)).
- **An imported lead keeps the date its file gave it, and lands on the tab for that date.** A lead's
  dates are stored as text as their source wrote them, and the application understood `2026-09-09`
  and `09/09/2026` and nothing else. Excel's short date is `09-Sep-26`, so a studio importing its own
  exported CSV stored dates nothing could read: the Enquiry Date column fell back to the moment the
  row was written and showed **today** on every row, while the lead sat in no Today New Leads, no
  Today or Yesterday Pending Followups, and on Qua Qua in neither Newborns nor Birthdays — those
  three filter on columns the database computes from the same two shapes. One list of shapes is now
  shared by the browser, the server and the SQL, and every write path stores `YYYY-MM-DD` whatever
  arrived, so the schema's own computed columns never see a shape they cannot read. **Leads imported
  before this need one pass to correct**: `npm run normalise:lead-dates` in `backend`, which reports
  what it would change and writes only with `--apply` — run it once per environment
  ([leadDates.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadDates.js),
  [normaliseLeadDates.js](../../backend/scripts/normaliseLeadDates.js)).
- **Finishing a CSV import no longer crashes the CRM board.** The import wrote its per-table tally
  under `branches` and the summary dialog read it as `verticals`, so every completed import threw
  "Cannot convert undefined or null to object" while rendering — after the leads had been written, so
  the file imported fine and the screen went white. The two halves name the same field now, and the
  summary reads defensively so a shape that drifts again costs a missing line rather than the board
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).
- **A CRM lead is filed under its own Enquiry Date, on every tab.** Today New Leads, Yet to connect,
  the Status column's Yet to connect / Inactive and the header's date range now all read one day —
  the date the lead's Date column shows — instead of the day the row happened to be written. Those
  are the same day for a lead typed in as it comes in and are not the same day for anything imported,
  synced or entered in a batch, so importing a year of old enquiries put the entire file into Today
  New Leads and Yet to connect at once, and inside any range covering today. An August enquiry now
  lands on August whenever it is entered, and Today New Leads holds today's enquiries and nothing
  else. The follow-up tabs already read the lead's own next follow-up date and are unchanged. The
  Status pill also stops reading the *server's* clock, which was the one date on this board that was
  not the browser's ([leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js)).
- **The Google Sheet sync keeps the date the sheet states.** The sync and the CSV import have two
  mappers over the same columns and they had drifted: the CSV path read a Created Date / Enquiry Date
  column, and the sync stamped the day the run happened over it — so the same spreadsheet kept its
  dates imported as a file and lost them all linked as a sheet. A column headed "Enquiry Date" was
  also being read into a field no table stores and thrown away. Both now keep the source's date and
  fall back to the day of the run only when the source states none
  ([googleSheetsService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/googleSheetsService.js)).
- **A lead with a blank Enquiry Date shows the day it is actually filed under.** The cell printed
  "—" while the board still counted the lead under the day its row was written, so it sat in a day's
  New Leads with nothing on screen saying which day that was
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).
- **A refused email no longer hides the fact that the send-back happened.** Sending an onboarding
  back reopens the invite and moves the employee to **Employee Submission Pending** first, and mails
  the candidate last — but a *thrown* refusal escaped to the generic catch and answered 500 "Unable
  to send this onboarding back", carrying no link. That reads as "nothing happened" when in fact
  everything had, and the screen closed on it, taking with it the message that could have been sent
  by hand. It matters most exactly when it fails: the common cause is the studio's mail quota being
  spent for the day. `smtpPool` returns `{ sent: false }` only when no sender is configured at all
  and *throws* when senders exist and every one refuses, so both shapes are the same answer now —
  502 with the candidate's link, expiry and a reason in words. The dialog closes on it like any other
  finish and the reason goes in a notice — the message is offered to copy *before* Send Back is
  pressed, so whoever is sending it by hand already holds it, and keeping the dialog up afterwards
  asked them to read the same wording twice over a submission that had already moved on. **The
  onboarding invite email had the same fault** and is answered the same way, and its dialog closes on
  a refusal too — the message is on that screen with its own Copy before Send Invite is pressed, so
  staying open asked the reader to read the same wording a second time and dismiss it. The login
  credentials email already reported its reason. So all three of the studio's mails now name a spent daily quota as one rather
  than reporting a server error. The reason comes from `describeMailFailure`, moved out of the
  employees controller into `services/mailFailure.js` now that two modules refuse the same way; the
  raw SMTP text never reaches the browser, since a thrown error can quote the message it was carrying
  ([onboarding-invites/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/onboarding-invites/controller.js),
  [mailFailure.js](../../backend/src/services/mailFailure.js),
  [EmployeeVerificationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeVerificationPage.js)).
- **Deleting a project takes its client storage with it.** A hard disc raised against a project went
  on being listed in Client Storage after that project was deleted — which is what a studio finds
  when the Client Storage tab holds projects no other tab does. A project is *soft*-deleted, so its
  row never actually goes and the foreign key's `ON DELETE SET NULL` never fires, and the storage
  register filters on its own `is_deleted` and never looked at the project's. The records now go in
  the same transaction as the project, alongside the Image Selection presence that already went with
  it, and the reply says how many. They are marked rather than erased, with the same three columns
  the register's own **Remove** writes, so one removed this way reads like one removed by hand and
  stays recoverable — and a record somebody had already removed keeps its own reason. A migration
  clears the records left behind by deletions made before this
  ([projects/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js),
  [client-storage.service.js](../../backend/src/modules/assets/client-storage.service.js),
  [20260909190000_remove_client_storage_with_deleted_projects](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260909190000_remove_client_storage_with_deleted_projects/migration.sql)).
- **The notification bell shows follow-ups again.** Every poll logged
  `Unable to build the follow-up notifications: Invalid time value` and the bell showed no follow-ups
  at all. `follow_up_day` is a MySQL `DATE` and Prisma returns those as a JavaScript `Date`, so
  reading it as text gave `"Wed Sep 09"` instead of `"2026-09-09"`, and the timestamp built from that
  threw inside `notice()`. Because the throw happened inside the source, the wrapper that keeps one
  bad source from taking down the bell swallowed it — so a single unparsed date silently cost every
  follow-up notice. The day is now formatted in SQL, and `notice()` answers `null` for a timestamp it
  cannot read rather than throwing, so a future oddity costs one notice instead of all of them. See
  [sources.js](../../backend/src/modules/notifications/sources.js).
- **Overall Pending Followups is now the follow-ups that are late, not every follow-up there is.**
  It counted by status alone — every lead marked Followup whatever its date — so it was the total the
  other two pending tabs were slices of rather than a tab of its own: a call owed today was counted
  in Today Pending and again here, and a call booked for next week was counted as pending before
  anybody was late on it. It is now bounded at yesterday, so the three tabs divide the outstanding
  work between them (owed today / owed yesterday / owed before that) and a lead sits in exactly one.
  A lead marked Followup whose follow-up date is blank or unreadable still counts here — it is owed a
  call on a day the board cannot name, and that is the one set that would otherwise fall through all
  three tabs. Today Pending and Yesterday Pending are unchanged; they already compared the follow-up
  date against today and yesterday. See
  [leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js). Access is
  unchanged: `SALES:Leads & Followups`.
- **Today New Leads now lists the enquiries dated today, not the rows written today.** The tab
  compared `created_day` — the day the row reached the table — while the Enquiry Date column beside
  it shows the enquiry's own date when the source supplied one. For sheet-synced leads those are
  different days: a sync stamps every row it takes with the day it ran, so pulling in a sheet filled
  the tab with a month of older enquiries, and a lead whose Enquiry Date column read today was
  missing from it. The clause is now the Enquiry Date column's own expression folded to a day
  (`enquiryDaySql` in
  [leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js)), which is the
  same expression the filter and the sort on that column already use — so the tab, the cell, the
  filter and the sort cannot disagree. A lead with no date at all still counts as new on the day it
  was entered; a lead whose date is not readable as a date counts as neither, matching what its cell
  shows. The rest of the board has since followed it onto the same expression — see the entry above,
  which takes Yet to connect, the Status pill and the header's date range off `created_day` too.
  Access is unchanged: `SALES:Leads & Followups`.

- **A password reset code now reaches you on a development machine.** Asking for a six-digit code
  locally produced a form that counted down two minutes against a code that had gone nowhere: it was
  minted, stored and dropped, because `SMS_PROVIDER` and `SMS_API_KEY` are unset on every machine
  that has not bought a gateway, and the screen answers the same way whatever happens so that it
  cannot be used to ask which numbers belong to the studio. Nothing past the code box could be
  reached at all. With `NODE_ENV="development"` and no gateway configured,
  [smsSender.js](../../backend/src/services/smsSender.js) now prints the code on the server console in a
  box saying plainly that nothing was sent. It is the only place in the application allowed to print
  a live code, so what it refuses is the point: any other `NODE_ENV` — **including an unset one**, so
  that a VPS which forgot the variable falls on the safe side — prints nothing and still answers
  `{ sent: false }`, `SMS_DEV_ECHO="false"` turns it off, and a configured gateway is always used in
  preference to it. `GET /api/health/email` reports it as `sms.devEcho`. Delivery to a real phone
  still needs a gateway: set `SMS_PROVIDER` and `SMS_API_KEY` (see
  [.env.example](../../backend/.env.example)) — never the WhatsApp integration, which is a QR-paired
  personal account that gets banned for automated sending.

- **A CRD document raised by hand now has a CRD ID.** The register's first column was blank for
  every document created from **Create CRD Document** — the row arrived with the client's details
  filled in and nothing to quote it by. Two separate faults made the same empty cell: `createDocument`
  never took an identifier at all, and the register never read one off a standalone row even when it
  had one. Only two paths ever wrote a code — issuing a project's own link, and a project adopting a
  hand-raised document when it is later started as "Applicable" — and a hand-raised document reaches
  neither, since that button exists precisely for a project answered **Not Applicable** and such a
  project never adopts. So the code stayed null for good. It shows most on Qua Qua, whose projects
  are the ones being answered Not Applicable and then given a document by hand. The identifier is
  now minted as the document is created, from the same per-vertical run an issued link takes, and
  `scripts/backfillCrdDocumentCodes.js` fills in the documents raised before this
  ([crd-documents/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/service.js),
  [backfillCrdDocumentCodes.js](../../backend/scripts/backfillCrdDocumentCodes.js)).
- **`renumberIdentifiers` can name a hand-raised CRD document's vertical.** It read the vertical off
  the document's *project*, which is null for exactly those documents — so the rows most likely to be
  missing a code were also the ones it could not number. It reads the document's own `vertical` first
  and falls back to the project's ([renumberIdentifiers.js](../../backend/scripts/renumberIdentifiers.js)).
- **A project deleted in Project Management now leaves Image Selection too.** Its Image Selection
  project used to stay in the sidebar for ever, still showing the client's name on the dashboard,
  with a published gallery link that still worked. It could not be tidied away either: a linked
  project refuses to be renamed, deleted or re-addressed in Image Selection because all three
  belong to Project Management, so one whose project had been deleted answered every instruction
  with "change it in Project Management" about a project that no longer existed. Archiving was the
  only way out. Deleting the project now takes it, in the same transaction and in both sections -
  the Projects page and Album Proofing - and the gallery link answers 404 from that moment. The
  delete is soft on both sides, exactly as the project's own is: the photographs, the folders and
  the client's selections are all still there. Purging those is Image Selection's own delete of a
  manual project, which makes somebody confirm the client's submitted selections by hand first, and
  a project delete is not that confirmation
  ([projects/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js)).

- **Create Project no longer offers the Call Box's calls as enquiries.** The picker was filling its
  page of fifty with `DBS-EID-DD-ERD-000260`, "Unknown" and a bare mobile number — one row per wrong
  number and misdial — and every one of them pushed a real enquiry off the list. Those rows are the
  Callyzer webhook's, from when it minted a lead for every call delivered to it; they belong to the
  **Calls** board rather than to Leads & Followups, and the picker's one rule has always been that it
  offers that board and nothing else. It now excludes them by `board_key`, the same stored column the
  CRM's own boards are read by, so the two cannot drift: a call becomes offerable the moment somebody
  adds its caller to a vertical, because that is what mints a real enquiry for it
  ([projects/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js)).
- **Contacts holds contacts again, not every number that has ever rung the studio.** The Call Box's
  callers were reaching it twice over — through the leads that old webhook created, and through a
  source reading the call log directly — so a misdial, a courier and a wrong number arrived
  identically, under a Call ID with no name, ahead of the rows anybody wanted. Neither is read any
  more. A caller becomes a contact by being **triaged**: somebody adds them to a vertical on the
  Calls board, that mints a real enquiry, and it is the enquiry that is listed, with the name and
  vertical a person actually filled in. Nothing is lost meanwhile — the number is on the Calls board,
  which is where it is worked, and on the lead's Call Logs tab afterwards
  ([contacts/directory/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/contacts/directory/service.js)).
- **A person on Reports & Analytics is somebody in the Employee Repository.** The **Employee** filter
  offered every row in the repository, and a row exists from the moment HR sends an onboarding invite
  and stays after somebody leaves — so half-finished invites and everyone who has ever left sat in
  the dropdown ahead of whoever the studio was actually reporting on. It is the working statuses now
  (Active, in Training, PIP, not archived), read from HR's own `WORKING_STATUSES` rather than
  restated, so the filter and the repository's Active tab cannot disagree.
- **And the tables under it, not only the dropdown.** Employee Analysis, Work Hours and the call half
  of WhatsApp Usage grouped the call log by `employee_name` — whatever each handset was labelled in
  the Callyzer app — so they listed people the filter above them refused to offer: "Manjula DBS"
  beside "Manjula Donkena", "Deploy Check" beside both, one row per label anybody had ever typed into
  a phone. A handset is matched to its employee by the repository's **contact number** first, which
  is the key that survives a phone being renamed — so one person on two handsets is now one row,
  under the studio's own spelling — and by exact **name** second. A handset matching neither is
  nobody, and its calls are on no person's row. The **call** reports (Summary, Lead Analysis, Never
  Attended, Not Picked Up, Call History) are untouched and still count every call: they are about the
  work rather than the people, and dropping a call from them because of who held the phone would make
  their totals disagree with the CRM's own call history
  ([crm-reports/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-reports/service.js)).

- **The three Callyzer clean-up scripts run again.** `retireCallyzerCallLeads`, `routeCallyzerLeads`
  and `removeCallyzerTestLeads` still named `sales_crm_lead`.`branch`, which migration
  `20260906180000` renamed to `vertical` — so each failed with *Unknown column* on every correctly
  migrated database, which is production, and the one-time clean-up they exist for could not be run
  at all ([scripts/](../../backend/scripts/)).
- **The password reset countdown reads `01:59` down to `00:00`.** It ran `2:00` to `0:00` — a clock
  one digit narrower for its last minute, so the whole line nudged sideways as it passed a minute
  left, on the one screen where somebody is watching it tick. Both halves are padded now, and it
  starts a second below what the server granted: `02:00` was already untrue by the time it rendered,
  the code having been minted, sent and answered first. The countdown therefore reaches zero a
  moment *before* the code expires rather than after, so **Resend code** is never offered against an
  OTP the server would still take. Display only — the two minutes the server holds are unchanged
  ([ForgotPasswordForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/auth/ForgotPasswordForm.js)).
- **Double-clicking a lead no longer crashes the CRM.** The popup threw
  `branchOptions is not defined` the moment it opened: Assigned Branch read a name that lives in
  `CRMPage`'s scope, and `LeadPopupModal` is a different component, so the whole screen came down
  with a `ReferenceError`. The three Add Lead forms reading the same name were fine, because those
  really are inside `CRMPage` — which is why it only broke on the popup. `LeadPopupModal` reads the
  studio's branches itself now rather than being handed them, because it is also rendered by
  `LeadPopupHost`, which the Contacts directory opens a lead through and which knows nothing about
  branches: a prop would have fixed one caller and left the trap
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).
- **A second `ReferenceError` found with it, in Start Project.** `StartProjectFormPanel` had
  `vertical={sale.groupType || sale.vertical || …}` and there is no `sale` in that file — the shoot
  dialog threw as it opened. The `project?.` fallbacks beside it were always the right source
  ([StartProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/StartProjectFormPanel.js)).
- **A name nobody defined now fails the build.** `eslint-config-next` leaves `no-undef` off — it is
  written for TypeScript, where the compiler asks the same question — so on this JavaScript codebase
  nothing was asking it at all, and both crashes above sat in the tree unreported. It is on
  ([eslint.config.mjs](../../frontend/eslint.config.mjs)), with the browser and Node globals declared so
  it can tell `window` from a typo. Neither bug was the kind tests catch: both are in JSX that only
  renders once somebody opens the thing.
- **The bundled logo can no longer be painted twice.** `BrandFallbackLogo` puts both of its files —
  black lettering for a light ground, white for a dark one — in the document and leaves the choice to
  CSS, which is what paints the right one on the first frame with no JavaScript. The two rules
  covered `light`, `dark`, `enhanced-dark` and the attribute being absent, and **hid neither** for
  anything else: an empty attribute, or a value nobody had taught the stylesheet about, showed both
  marks stacked, one ghosting the other. The light rule is written as "not either dark theme" now, so
  between them the two rules cover every possible state of the attribute rather than the four that
  were expected ([globals.css](../../frontend/src/app/globals.css)).
- **Every control the lead popup names is one its renderer draws.** The general form of the
  `assignedBranch: "board"` bug: a control name with no matching case falls quietly through to the
  plain text box at the end of the chain, so a field that must be a picker becomes free text and
  nothing says so. Pinned by
  [crm-branch-options.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/crm-branch-options.test.mjs).

- **Send Back asks its question with the message to send, and works from the review panel again.**
  Two fixes to one flow. Pressing **Send Back** while reviewing a submission dimmed the screen and
  showed nothing: the question is raised by the page rather than by the panel — the panel closes as
  part of answering it, so it cannot be the thing that asks — and at the default dialog layer that
  put it at `z-[100]` underneath the panel's `z-[110]`, so the action could only be completed from
  the row menu. It sits at `dialogOverPanel` now. And the confirmation itself now carries the
  message, the same shape the onboarding invite and the login credentials end on: the finished
  wording addressed to the candidate, their link and its expiry in it, in an editable box with a
  **Copy message** button beside **Cancel** and **Send Back**. It was a bare "are you sure?" that
  showed the wording only afterwards, in a second dialog — the message readable only after the
  decision it belonged to. Offering it first is also what makes the honest case work: when the
  studio's mail quota is spent the send-back still reopens the submission and only the email fails,
  so the dialog now stays open saying why, with the same message still on it, instead of closing and
  taking the one remaining way to reach the candidate with it. The link rides on the employee row
  from the invite behind it, so nothing is fetched when the dialog opens
  ([SendBackDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/SendBackDialog.js),
  [onboardingSendBack.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/onboardingSendBack.mjs),
  [employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js)).
- **Dragging a module in Sidebar Layout now shows a line where it will drop, not a lit-up row.**
  Settings → User Preference → Sidebar Layout highlighted the whole row under the pointer while a
  module was carried, which said which row the drag was over but not which side of it the drop
  would take — the same hover could mean above or below, and the answer was only found out by
  letting go. [SortableList](../../frontend/src/components/shared/SortableList.js) now draws a 2px
  line in the gap above or below that row, by which half of it the pointer is in, the way the data
  table marks the side of a column, and the drop lands in the gap the line marks — `dropIndex` in
  [lib/sidebarLayout.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/sidebarLayout.mjs), tested, turns the gap into the
  index so the two cannot disagree.
- **A reassigned task now reaches the bell it was reassigned to.** The bell gates each stored notice
  against the module it is about — `TYPE_PERMISSIONS` in
  [services/notifications.js](../../backend/src/services/notifications.js) — and a type absent from that
  map reaches nobody, deliberately, so that a module which has not said who its notices belong to
  cannot leak them. `TASK_REASSIGNED` was written by the task service but never added to the map:
  the two people either side of a reassignment were told nothing, while the row sat in the table.
  It answers for `HRM / Task Management` like the four task types beside it. The map's own test
  walks every type in `TYPES` and fails on one that can be written and never read, which is how this
  surfaced.

- **Add Employee no longer refuses a form with nothing marked on it.** Saving a completed employee
  could answer "Fill in the required fields" while every starred field held an answer. Three separate
  things were doing it. **Emergency Contact Relationship** is required, and its control had no way to
  show a problem at all — it was the one field on the form that could block a save and stay silent,
  so `ComboBoxField` now takes an `error` the way every field beside it does. Ticking **Permanent
  address is the same as the current address** only copied the address once, at the moment it was
  ticked, so the ordinary order — tick the box, then type the address — left Permanent Address empty
  while the screen said the two were the same; while it is ticked, the two now stay the same. And the
  message itself said "required" whatever was wrong, so a mistyped PAN or a half-finished
  qualification row was reported as a missing answer: missing and malformed are now told apart and
  named — "Fill in Blood Group; check PAN Card Number" — and Save scrolls to the first outstanding
  field rather than leaving the reader eight headings away from it
  ([EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js),
  [ComboBoxField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/ComboBoxField.js)).
- **The Employee Repository no longer carries a Sl. No. column.** A row number counts positions in a
  page rather than describing the person in the row: it renumbers itself the moment the table is
  sorted, filtered or paged, and that table is all three constantly — so "the third one" meant a
  different employee on every visit and could not be quoted to anybody. The number people do quote,
  the Employee ID, is a column of its own. Dropped from **Manage Columns** with it, since a key left
  in the visibility map is a tick box hiding a column that is not there, and the row offset that fed
  it goes too. Accounts, Payment Requests, Tasks and Employee Reviews keep theirs — this is dropped
  at the table, the way Employee Attendance already dropped it, rather than removed from the shared
  builder. ([EmployeeTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeTable.js))
- **Every email now goes to the employee's personal address, never to the login address.** The login
  address is something the application works out from the person's name
  (`firstname.abc@dontblinkstudioz.com`) — an identifier, not a mailbox anybody reads — and two
  mails were being posted to it:
  - **The credentials email.** The account was created, `credentialsSentAt` was stamped, and the new
    employee never learned they had a login. (The onboarding invite arrived because that one always
    went to the candidate's own address.) It now goes to their personal email, and the login address
    is **stated in the body** beside the username, being something they are told rather than
    something they already knew.
  - **The password reset link.** Worse, the account was looked up *by the login address only*, so
    somebody typing the address they actually know matched nothing and was told "if the address is
    known, a link is on its way" — for a link that was never sent. The lookup now accepts either
    address and the link goes to the personal one.

  An account with no employee behind it — an administrator's — keeps its own address, which for
  those is a real one
  ([controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js),
  [authController.js](../../backend/src/controllers/authController.js),
  [emailService.js](../../backend/src/services/emailService.js),
  [emailTemplates.js](../../backend/src/services/emailTemplates.js)).

- **Employee pickers offer only the people actually working here.** Reports To, Alternative cover and
  Leave Approval on the employee form were fed straight from the repository's own list — which asks
  for everybody, correctly, because its table has a tab per status — so every dropdown offered people
  still onboarding, people whose own form was not in yet, people on long leave, and in Reports To
  people who had been Relieved, Absconded or Fired. Naming one of them records a decision against
  somebody who cannot act on it, and the record then reads as though they were chosen.
  Who counts as working here is now written once in
  [employmentStatus.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/employmentStatus.mjs) — `Active`, `in Training`, `PIP`,
  the same three the backend means and the same three the repository's **Active** tab is built from,
  which now reads it too so the tab and the dropdowns cannot disagree. Invitation rows are dropped
  with them: an invite is somebody who has not filled the form in yet. The repository table itself
  still asks for everybody, and must — a row that never arrives lands in none of its tabs, and HR
  could not review an onboarding submission at all.
  ([EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js),
  [useCredentialForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/useCredentialForm.js))
- **Employee Referral showed an empty page, and naming a referrer failed outright.** Two faults, one
  hiding the other. The route at `/hrm/employee-referrals` imported the register and then rendered
  nothing — `export default function Page() {}` with no `return` — so the screen was blank whatever
  the data said. Behind it, naming somebody under **Referred By** answered `500`: the referral's
  `referral_id` column and the code that writes it were both in place, but the field was never added
  to the Prisma model, so the client refused every insert and no referral was ever created. Both are
  fixed, and a referral now appears the moment a referrer is named — `DBS-REF-000001`, the amount
  halved across the referred employee's third and sixth month
  ([page.js](../../frontend/src/app/(app)/hrm/employee-referrals/page.js),
  [schema.prisma](../../backend/prisma/schema.prisma)).
- **A duplicate asks for its own Enquiry ID instead of opening with the field locked and empty.**
  The picker is drawn as a settled value rather than a search box when the form already holds an
  enquiry — but it was asked as "was this form prefilled at all", and on Create Project a prefill is
  a converted lead, a quotation *or* a duplicate. The first two arrive naming their enquiry, so the
  lock was right by accident; a duplicate arrives with it deliberately blank, because the point is to
  sell the same package to somebody else, and so opened with the one field the person was there to
  answer locked around an empty string. It now locks only when there is an enquiry to show.
  ([CreateProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CreateProjectFormPanel.js))
- **Save Project stopped refusing in silence.** Pressing it a second time did nothing visible: the
  panel remembers a dismissed notice by its **message**, and every refusal sets the same string — so
  once the first one had been dismissed, by hand or by its own five-second timer, the identical
  message never counted as new and the notice stayed hidden. The form went on refusing with nothing
  on screen to say why, which reads as a broken button rather than as a field still wanting an
  answer. The press itself now gives the dismissal up, in
  [SchemaFormPanel](../../frontend/src/components/schema-form/SchemaFormPanel.js), so this is fixed for
  every form built on the panel rather than for the one where it was noticed — a form that forgets to
  clear its own error before submitting cannot go quiet again.
- **Signed Quotation is typed where no quotation answered it, and read-only where one did.** The
  rule now turns on the quotation rather than on which form is open. **With a quotation**, the figure
  is that document's and what the client was sent, so Bookings & Converted shows it and does not
  offer it — on Create Project and on **Edit Sale Details**, which until now let a quotation's amount
  be retyped by somebody `PROJECT / Projects` was never meant to reach. Changing the quotation is how
  the figure changes. **With no quotation** — New Project from nothing, a CRM lead that named no
