  budget, a duplicate — nobody has written the number down anywhere, so the box is open and the money
  is entered there; it was closed for exactly those bookings, which left the amount unrecordable and
  refused the save with "Complete required fields: Signed Quotation" against a field with no way into
  it. Correcting it afterwards stays Project Management's, on Projects → Edit Project, behind the
  permission it already had. It is no longer required to book: a project saved without one opens with
  nothing to schedule against and says so under the field.
  ([CreateProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CreateProjectFormPanel.js),
  [SaleDetailsSection.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/SaleDetailsSection.js),
  [SchedulePaymentSection.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/SchedulePaymentSection.js))

- **A submitted selection item's checkbox is green again in Selection Settings.** It was coloured
  through the native `accent-color`, which this application never uses: `globals.css` draws every
  checkbox itself, setting `appearance: none` and filling a checked box from `--control-accent`.
  The native accent was therefore inert and the box stayed the default blue however green the style
  said it was. [SelectionItemsTable](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/SelectionItemsTable.js)
  sets that variable instead, so the fill, the border and the focus ring turn together and follow
  the appearance themes. Anything else colouring a checkbox with `accent-color` has the same fault.

- **WhatsApp Chat works again: it was refusing every lead in the application.** Opening the panel on
  a lead answered "That is not a valid CRM lead id.", which read as the linking itself failing. The
  route guard checked the id against `/^DBS-[A-Z]{2}-EID-\d{1,12}$/` — the shape an enquiry id had
  *before* it carried its vertical and its branch, `DBS-DD-EID-4867` — so every id the studio has
  minted since, `DBS-EID-DD-HYD-000024`, matched nothing. The same stale pattern was in the CRM media
  store, where it decides the folder a client's files are filed under; that one failed silently,
  because an id it cannot place is deliberately answered with "" so a message is never lost over its
  attachment — so every WhatsApp attachment and every remark file belonging to a current lead had
  nowhere to go. Both now read `IDENTIFIER_PATTERN` from the service that mints identifiers, so
  neither keeps a copy of the format to forget the next time it changes, and a test holds them there.
  ([security.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/whatsapp-chat/security.js),
  [crmMediaStore.js](../../backend/src/services/crmMediaStore.js),
  [enquiry-id-guards.test.js](../../backend/test/enquiry-id-guards.test.js))
- **The date range's tab counts agree with the tabs again.** The strip above the board is counted in
  one pass, and the range was applied to that whole pass — including the tabs that do not carry it.
  So with a range chosen on All, the Call Box's Incoming tab read "2" and then showed 6 rows the
  moment it was opened, and the three dated lead tabs did the same whenever the range excluded
  today. The number beside a tab's name is a promise about what pressing it will show, so the range
  now goes inside each tab's own count, on exactly the tabs that carry it — the rule the client uses
  to decide whether to *offer* the switch, mirrored on the server which decides whether to *apply*
  it, and held together by a test so the two cannot drift.
  ([leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js),
  [callBoardService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/callBoardService.js),
  [crm-date-range.test.js](../../backend/test/crm-date-range.test.js))
- **The remove icon on Bulk Import's last row does something when pressed, instead of nothing.** The
  dialog keeps a blank row at the end so the next client can be typed without pressing anything —
  and that row cannot be removed, because the form puts another one straight back. It was still
  offering a remove button, so pressing it appeared to do nothing, over and over, with no way to
  tell that from the form being broken; and it is the one row a person will try to remove, because
  it is the empty one. The button is now absent there, which is this application's rule for a
  control the reader may not use. The rows also carry keys of their own rather than their positions:
  removing one used to hand its React instance to the row that moved up into its place, so a
  half-typed phone number appeared to jump to the client below. And the card is as tall as what it
  holds rather than a fixed height, which had left two thirds of it empty under a single client.
  ([BulkImportLeadsDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/BulkImportLeadsDialog.js),
  [crmBulkImport.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/crmBulkImport.mjs))
- **`POST /api/sales/crm-leads/bulk` mints the Enquiry ID each record does not bring, instead of
  writing leads with a blank one.** `lead.id` is optional on that endpoint's schema, exactly as it is
  on the single create, because an enquiry id counts within its own vertical and branch and only the
  counter knows where that pair has got to — the single create minted one and this did not, so every
  caller that left it out wrote rows with a blank `enquiry_id`. That is the column the whole module
  is keyed by: the board's row key, the Call Logs join, the activity log, the duplicate check, the
  link a notification opens. Minted one at a time from each record's own vertical and branch, since
  a batch can span all three and a reserved range belongs to one counter. Leads written this way now
  also get their creator stamped, a `CREATED` row in the activity log and a notification to the CRM
  they name — everything a lead added one at a time already got.
  ([controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/controller.js),
  [crm-bulk-create.test.js](../../backend/test/crm-bulk-create.test.js))
- **The bell shows its stored notices again — task assignments, task answers — instead of an empty
  panel under a badge that counted them.** `useTaskNotifications` read the reply's `data` as an array
  of notices, which it has not been since the endpoint grew its second half: it answers
  `{ data: { notifications, notices, sources } }`, so the array test failed on every poll and that
  half of the bell rendered nothing while the badge, counted separately from `meta.unreadCount`, went
  on saying how many there were. Renamed to `useStoredNotifications` with the fix, since the store
  carries every module's notices now rather than only the Tasks module's.
  ([useStoredNotifications.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/useStoredNotifications.js),
  [AppNotifications.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppNotifications.js))
- **A CRD link opens the client's form again, instead of answering them "Project not found."**
  Every document raised from a project — which is every one Send Link issues — refused the client
  the moment the register was scoped: the public page resolves the project behind the token through
  `subjectForDocument`, which called the access-scoped reader without an access rule, and an absent
  rule is deliberately answered with a filter matching nothing. There is no signed-in caller behind
  a CRD link to resolve one from; the token is the credential, and the studio-side readers had
  already checked their own access before reaching it. The project is now read unscoped there, so
  the form opens, saves and submits as it did. Documents raised by hand were unaffected, having no
  project to look up. ([service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/service.js),
  [crd-public-link-scope.test.js](../../backend/test/crd-public-link-scope.test.js))
- **A section switch beside a module heading is the width of its own longest option, so its menu
  lines up with it.** The switch was held at a fixed 160px and its menu measured from that box, so
  on Client Gifts — "Welcome Gift" in a box sized for "Shoot Completed Gift" — the list hung a
  finger's width past the chevron and read as belonging to nothing. The width now comes from the
  options themselves: the control still never resizes as you choose from it, the menu is exactly its
  shape, and the gap that was padding the chevron away from its label is gone. Client Gifts also
  lists Welcome Gift first and Shoot Completed Gift second, the order the two are owed in, rather
  than alphabetically. ([Dropdown.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/Dropdown.js),
  [dropdownMenu.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/dropdownMenu.mjs),
  [ClientGiftsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ClientGiftsPage.js))
- **Add Lead saves again, instead of writing the lead and then reporting that it failed.** Every
  lead added by hand came back "Unable to add CRM lead." while the lead itself was created:
  `stampCrmLeadCreator` was called by both create paths and imported by neither, so the request
  threw on the statement *after* the lead was written and the controller turned that into a 500.
  Pressing Confirm again then answered "already holds this contact number", and each attempt left
  another lead behind. Leads written during the fault carry no creator, which is one of the columns
  the data scope reads — so whoever entered one may not see it unless their name is on it another
  way. A test now fails if either create path calls a `crmLeadService` helper it did not import.
  ([controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/controller.js),
  [crm-controller-imports.test.js](../../backend/test/crm-controller-imports.test.js))
- **The Calls board is the callers nobody has added yet, and it shows Call IDs and nothing else.**
  A call now leaves the board the moment somebody adds its caller to a vertical, for that vertical's
  own board and that vertical's enquiry id — so what is on Calls is what is outstanding, its Enquiry
  ID column is always the call's own `DBS-CID-…` code, and one caller is not two rows on two boards
  each looking like work nobody has done. The board had been holding every call ever delivered, on
  the reasoning that "what did we do on the phone today" was otherwise unanswerable; it is
  answerable where the client is, because the caller's calls follow them to their lead and the Call
  Logs tab reads the whole history there. A call from a number the CRM does not already hold still
  creates no lead, and leads the old webhook left behind are still cleared by
  `scripts/retireCallyzerCallLeads.js`, which reports before it writes.
  ([callBoardService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/callBoardService.js),
  [crmBoards.js](../../frontend/src/lib/crmBoards.js))
- **A caller added to a vertical actually leaves the Calls board, however their calls were stamped.**
  Three calls from one number sat on the board under their Call IDs after the caller had been added
  to Corp, and adding them again changed nothing: the calls carried `DBS-DD-EID-35950`, an id from a
  format no lead has had for a year and no lead at all. Two sides were reading one fact two ways —
  the board judged the claim properly, found no lead and kept the row, while `linkCallsToLead` judged
  it by "is the column empty", found it full and skipped the row, so a call naming a dead id was
  unlinkable for good. There is one rule now, `callerIsALeadSql`, asked of the call's own link and of
  the caller's number, and both sides take it from there. Calls already stranded are repaired on the
  first read after this deploys, re-pointed at the lead their caller was first added as — a number
  may be one enquiry in each vertical, and its call history stays with the first — so their history
  appears on that lead's Call Logs tab rather than nowhere.
  ([callLogService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/callLogService.js),
  [crm-call-placement.test.js](../../backend/test/crm-call-placement.test.js))
- **The Calls board's tabs can be selected again, and so can Qua Qua's Newborns and Birthdays.**
  Clicking Incoming, Outgoing, Connected or Not Connected left the strip on All: the tab a CRM
  reader is on is remembered across a reload, and the remembered value was read back through the
  default board's tab ids alone — so a tab no other board shares was stored and then handed
  straight back as All, and the board could never leave it. The accepted ids now come from the
  board on screen. A remembered tab still cannot decide which board the page opens on, because the
  board is not remembered and always starts at Dum Dum; switching boards now writes the tab it
  lands on, so one board's tab cannot reappear on another that happens to share its name.
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [crm-boards.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/crm-boards.test.mjs))
- **Image Selection storage roots were reachable by nobody but a Super Admin.** The router carried
  two guards, and the first named `IMAGE_SELECTION / Upload & Gallery` — a permission that had been
  merged into Project Management and so existed in no catalogue. A guard naming a permission nobody
  can hold refuses everybody, silently, and it ran before the correct guard below it. The dead line
  is gone ([routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/storage-roots/routes.js)).
- **Nine permission sub-actions could never be ticked.** The five asset ones (record an inspection,
  flag for maintenance, assign, return, approve a request) and the four image ones (upload, publish,
  share, delete) were filed under `Asset Inspection`, `Asset Assignment` and `Upload & Gallery`,
  three permissions that had been merged away, so the seed skipped them without a word. They are
  filed under the permissions that absorbed them, and a scope naming a permission that does not
  exist is now reported rather than skipped
  ([permission-catalogue.js](../../backend/prisma/seeds/permission-catalogue.js)).
- **The task status tabs show their counts again.** *All*, *Pending*, *In Progress*, *Completed* and
  *Reverted* each carry the number behind them, on whichever of the three views is open. They were
  blank because the status was being sent to the server: the browser only ever held the rows for the
  status it was showing, so every other tab had nothing to count. A view is now fetched whole and
  filtered in the browser — which costs nothing, since the endpoint does not paginate and the *All*
  tab was already fetching everything, and switching status no longer touches the network at all.
  *All* counts cancelled tasks too: they have no tab of their own but are still in the list
  ([taskConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/taskConfig.js),
  [TasksPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/TasksPage.js)).

- **Downloading a quotation PDF is roughly twice as fast, and no longer prints the wrong page.**
  The renderer waited for the page's network to fall quiet (`networkidle0`), which asks a different
  question from "is the quotation on screen" and answered it wrong in both directions: quiet arrives
  before the page has asked for the quotation as easily as after — a measured render produced a
  14KB PDF of the **"Loading quotation…"** state — and anything holding a connection open, such as a
  dev server's hot-reload socket, means quiet never comes and the render waits out its whole
  thirty-second timeout. The page now says where it has got to (`data-quotation-state` — `ready`,
  `expired` or `error`, and nothing at all while it loads) and the renderer waits for that, then for
  the logo and the fonts specifically. Measured against a real quotation: **5.0s → 2.7s** cold and
  **2.2s → 1.6s** warm, and the cold render is now the actual document rather than a loading screen.
  A page that settles on an error is a failed download with the reason in it rather than a PDF of
  an error box. Chromium is also started at boot alongside the AI model, so the first download after
  a deploy does not pay its ~1s launch, and it is closed on shutdown so a restart no longer leaves a
  browser behind ([quotationPdfService.js](../../backend/src/services/quotationPdfService.js),
  [PublicQuotationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicQuotationPage.js)).
- **A quotation raised once can be opened, copied and saved as a PDF.** Open, Copy link, PDF and
  Duplicate live under each version in the **Versions** dialog and nowhere else, and the row's ⋮
  offered that dialog only on a chain with more than one version — on the reasoning that a list of
  one version is the row you are already looking at. True of the list, and wrong about the point:
  a quotation with a single version had no way to reach its own client link from the register at
  all. The entry is now on every row, named **Versions** where there is a history and **Quotation
  link** where there is one version, opening the same cards either way. The dialog already drew a
  single version correctly; it now words itself for one and sizes to it rather than standing 30rem
  tall around a single card
  ([QuotationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationPage.js),
  [QuotationVersionsDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationVersionsDialog.js)).
- **A screen is called what the sidebar calls it.** The menu said **Quotations** and the page
  heading said Quotation, because the name was written in two places and only one was ever
  corrected — the kind of mismatch nothing breaks over and nobody can report. The heading of any
  screen the sidebar lists now comes from the registry in
  [constants/navigation.mjs](../../frontend/src/constants/navigation.mjs), the same file the drawer,
  the layout editor and Role Management already take their names from, so the two cannot drift
  again. Pages the menu does not list — My Profile, Administration, the Pre Production boards, a
  record opened from a table — keep the titles they set for themselves: the lookup matches a route
  exactly, never by prefix. Assets also lost a stray trailing space in its heading
  ([ModulePageHeader.js](../../frontend/src/components/layout/ModulePageHeader.js)).
- **Confirming a task action closes the task panel.** Rolling a task back or cancelling it asks in a
  dialog; the dialog closed and the panel behind it stayed open on the task, so the confirmation
  read as though it had not worked. A verb that ends a task — **Mark Complete**, **Roll Back**,
  **Cancel Task** — now closes the panel and leaves the reader on the list they were working
  through, which has just been refreshed with the answer. **Start** still keeps it open: that verb
  finishes nothing, and Mark Complete has appeared in the same footer
  ([TasksPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/TasksPage.js)).
- **The bell lists your task notifications again.** The badge counted them and the panel under it
  read *No task notifications.* — the notices were in the database, sent by the server, and dropped
  by the browser. The endpoint used to answer with the stored rows as `data`; it grew a second half
  when the bell took on follow-ups, WhatsApp replies, unread calls and approvals, and now answers
  `{ data: { notifications, notices } }`. The tasks half went on asking `Array.isArray(data)`, which
  is false for every reply the server sends, so it read an empty list every time while the count
  beside it kept coming from `meta.unreadCount` and stayed right. Both halves now read the reply
  through one place, [lib/notificationFeed.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/notificationFeed.mjs), which
  also accepts the older shape so a deployment's few minutes of new-browser-against-old-server does
  not empty the bell ([useTaskNotifications.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/useTaskNotifications.js)).
- **Start Project and Edit Project show the sale, and no longer let it be edited.** The six sale
  fields — Enquiry ID, Quotation, Client Main Number, Source Branch, WhatsApp Group Name and Initial
  Converted Value — sat at the top of the form as a working enquiry search, group-name picker and
  quotation list, so the sale a whole plan and its payment schedule were priced against could be
  changed while assigning a coordinator. They are now **read-only text boxes** in the same
  two-column grid and the same pairing, on the shared field's grey read-only ground, with the
  project's Status, Event ID, Vertical and CRM Name in the strip above them. That replaces the four-line summary card the form used to show a
  viewer without the sale's permission, which read as a footnote rather than as the figures the plan
  is built on. The form now writes only the plan: `PATCH /api/sales/projects/:id` is never called
  from it, and it no longer loads the generated group names or the SALES employee list. Changing a
  sale is Sales Manager → **Edit Sale Details**, which is unchanged
  ([StartProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/StartProjectFormPanel.js),
  [ProjectsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectsPage.js),
  [SALES-AND-PROJECT-MANAGEMENT-WORKFLOW.md](../project-state/SALES-AND-PROJECT-MANAGEMENT-WORKFLOW.md)).
- **Settings asks before it throws an edit away — on the way out as well as on the way past.**
  Closing the overlay with something typed and not yet saved lost it without a word: the X, the
  Close button and the embedded Project Settings all went straight through. Leaving a section
  asked, but only from three of them, each with its own wording. Now every section that holds an
  edit until a Save is pressed — Table Settings, Sidebar Layout, Project Settings, Global Logo,
  Login Slide and Application Monitoring — reports it, and the same question is put whichever way
  you leave: **Unsaved changes will be discarded**, *Go back if you want to save your settings.*,
  with **Go Back** and **Discard & Continue**. Go Back keeps the edit exactly where it was.
  A panel clears its own flag as it unmounts, so a section that is no longer on screen never warns
  about an edit nobody made; sections that save as they go — a WhatsApp number as it is added, a
  linked sheet by its own dialog, an option list entry as it is typed — say nothing, because they
  have nothing to lose ([SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js),
  [StudioBrandingPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/StudioBrandingPanel.js),
  [LoginSlidePanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/LoginSlidePanel.js),
  [ApplicationMonitoringPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/ApplicationMonitoringPanel.js)).

- **The Global Logo no longer holds a gap open beside the Global Name.** The mark's width cap was
  written `min(<cap>px, 100%)` — the variant's limit or the surface's width, whichever was smaller.
  It painted correctly and *measured* wrong: a percentage max-width is ignored while the browser
  works out how much room an element asks for, so the top bar reserved the logo's uncapped width
  and the cap only applied afterwards. A wide wordmark (300×65 drawn 40px tall wants 185px, capped
  to 160) left 25px of empty box behind it — a hole between the logo and the Global Name that no
  spacing class accounted for, and one that grew with the shape of whatever logo was uploaded. The
  cap is a plain length now, honoured in both passes: measured on the bar, the gap went from 36px
  to the 12px its own classes ask for. Every variant's box is already narrower than the surface it
  sits in, and login and the top bar were checked at 360px — no logo exceeds its surface, no page
  scrolls sideways ([AppLogo.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/AppLogo.js)).
- **The migrations now build the same schema on any database, not only on one created the way ours
  was.** Six tables did not state their collation and inherited whatever the database defaulted to;
  MySQL 8 defaults a new database to `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` while the studio's is
  `utf8mb4_unicode_ci`, so on a fresh database those six came out different from every other table
  and the first migration to join one of them to another failed with *Illegal mix of collations*.
  It ran on every machine the studio owns and failed only in CI, whose whole job is to catch this.
  CI now creates its database in the studio's collation before applying anything, a migration
  converts the six tables on any database where they already drifted, and a test fails if a new
  `CREATE TABLE` is added without saying which collation it is in.
  (migration `20260906210000_pin_table_collations`, [ci.yml](../../.github/workflows/ci.yml))

- **A project belongs to one section of Image Selection, and the queries say so.** The Projects page
  and Album Proofing share `image_selection_project`, told apart by `album_proofing` — but the
  filter that kept them apart shipped with the Album Proofing feature, so with that work out of the
  tree the Projects page listed **every** row and the studio saw album projects it never created in
  its sidebar. The flag, the section-scoped uniqueness of the Sales link
  (`@@unique([salesProjectId, albumProofing])` — one Sales Manager project may back one project in
  each section) and the `albumProofing: false` pin on every Projects-page read now stand on their
  own, so neither section can see the other's rows whatever else is or is not present.
  `findProjectByName` was pinned too — it was the one read that still crossed, quietly enough to
  miss: an album called `manual` made the Projects page think that name was taken, so a duplicate
  came out `- Copy 2` and a Sales sync skipped a project. A separation held together by remembering
  is one query away from gone, so `test/image-selection-section-separation.test.js` reads the
  repository and fails on any project read that does not name its section
  ([projects/repository.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/projects/repository.js),
  [folders/repository.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/folders/repository.js)).

- **A picker no longer offers people who have not started.** Assigning an asset, naming a manager or
  choosing a person for client storage offered everybody in the repository, onboarding candidates
  included — so an asset could be, and was, assigned to somebody mid-onboarding. The pickers ask for
  the people actually working here (Active, in Training, PIP); the repository screen still lists
  everybody, because it has a tab per status.
- **An asset's manager can see the asset they manage.** Naming somebody the manager of an asset had
  no effect on what they could see: only the person it was *assigned to* counted, so an asset
  managed by one person and held by another was invisible to its manager. Both people on an asset
  count now.
- **A search could replace the visibility rule instead of narrowing within it.** Three read services
  — assets, cash entries and projects — spread the rule's `where` beside a search that sets its own
  `OR`, which overwrote the rule and returned rows the caller may not see. The rule goes under `AND`
  in all of them, where nothing can replace it.

- **The CRM assistant no longer fails when it is opened on a vertical.** Renaming the CRM's
  "branch" to "vertical" left one reference behind, so building the assistant's context threw a
  ReferenceError for any session that named a board — introduced and fixed within this release.
- **Today's project count on the assistant's calendar line is no longer always zero.** It compared
  vertical names ("Dum Dum") against columns holding `WED` and a branch name; it matches the
  vertical reference now.
- **The assistant's HRM tools answer for everyone, not only a Super Admin.** `hrm_headcount`,
  `hrm_search_employees` and `hrm_get_employee` narrowed employees by a `branchId` that `Employee`
  did not have, so Prisma refused the query for any branch-scoped caller; a Super Admin's reach is
  null and never built the filter, which is why nobody saw it. `Employee.branchId` now exists as a
  real reference (NULL until the branch backfill), the tools read it first and the work-location
  text as a fallback, and somebody reaching no branch is answered with no employees rather than
  everyone whose branch is not yet set. Migration
  [20260906140000_employee_branch_id](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260906140000_employee_branch_id/migration.sql).
- **Enquiry prefill on a quotation receives the lead's branch, not its vertical.** `GET
  /api/sales/enquiries` sent the vertical as `sourceBranch`, which the quotation form checks against
  branch names, so the prefill never matched anything. It now sends `assignedBranch`.
- **A Google Sheet's "Nearest Branch" column no longer lands on the vertical.** One of the two
  header maps still filed it there, so a city could fold a lead onto Dum Dum; it is `assignedBranch`
  in both maps now.

- **The Upcoming tab on an employee's reviews is no longer always empty.** It split the review rows
  by date, and a review row records an appraisal that has *happened* — the dialog that creates one
  refuses a future date — so every row landed under Past and Upcoming could never hold anything. The
  two tabs are a schedule and a history, not two halves of one list: **Upcoming** now shows the one
  date nobody has acted on, the employee's **Next Review Date**, with **Record this review** opening
  the dialog with that date already filled in, and marks it when the date has gone by. **Past** is
  the reviews actually held, newest first.

- **Onboarding closes itself once the login has been created and sent.** The Login Credentials page
  stayed open on a screen whose only remaining control was **Close** — every field spent, the
  employee already moved to the Active tab behind it — which made a finished job look unfinished. It
  now closes on its own. What that page reported is said in the notice instead: an account created
  with **no roles** can sign in and do nothing, and now says so rather than taking the warning away
  with the page it was written on. **Approve & Next is unchanged** and still moves on to Login
  Credentials rather than closing — closing there would leave the employee on HR Onboarding Pending
  with no way to sign in, which is the step it exists to reach.

- **The Login Credentials page fills in the username and the email again.** Both are worked out from
  the employee record, but the page is only reachable once that record has been saved — and the form
  read it once, when it was still nothing. React runs a state initialiser exactly once, so the two
  fields were computed from an absent employee and stayed empty for the whole flow: a work email
  typed on Review Employee Details saved correctly and then arrived at Login Email blank. The fields
  are **derived** from the record now rather than copied into state when some effect happens to
  fire, so there is no moment at which the record exists and the field is empty. State holds only
  what a person has typed, and a field stays theirs once touched — including one they deliberately
  emptied. Cover and the leave approvers were blank for the same reason and read the same way.

- **Approve & Onboard, and any employee edit, no longer fail with "Validation failed".** The studio
  mints an Employee ID as `DBS-1` — hyphenated and unpadded, deliberately, so it reads without
  leading zeros — while the API's update schema demanded `DBS001`, a shape nothing has ever
  produced. Saving an employee hands their stored id back with the rest of the record, so every
  Update and every **Approve & Onboard** was refused, naming a field nobody had edited. The schema
  now accepts the id the generator actually mints, and the older unhyphenated ids already on the
  repository with it. A test now asserts that what the generator produces is what the validator
  takes, so the two cannot drift apart again.

- **Asset Management's title bar had two identical ⋮ triggers**, and the Employee Attendance range
  picker listened for Escape itself — so one press closed both the picker and whatever it was
  sitting inside. The ⋮ goes last, once; the picker goes through the shared Escape arbiter like
  every other dismissible thing
  ([AssetManagementPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/assets/AssetManagementPage.js),
  [EmployeeAttendancePage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/EmployeeAttendancePage.js)).

- **Removing a service, a deliverable or a size option value works again, and so does
  disconnecting a Google account.** All four endpoints failed with a 500 before reaching their own
  code: the three deletes still called a `onlySuperAdmin` helper that was removed when access moved
  to the route guards (the routes already require read-write on PROJECT / Projects), and the Google
  disconnect handler used a service function it never imported
  ([projects/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js),
  [google-oauth/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/google-oauth/controller.js)).

- **A CRD document link opens again.** The public CRD page crashed on its first render with
  "useNotice is not defined": when the form was split by vertical, the shell that picks the form
  kept eight lines of the old form's state — a notice hook and refs it never imported and never
  used. They are gone ([PublicCrdDocumentForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicCrdDocumentForm.js)).
- **Call Time reads on the 12-hour clock, with am and pm.** Callyzer sends a call's time as
  "17:49:41" and the Calls board showed it as it arrived, so Call Time was the one column in the
  application on a 24-hour clock — sitting beside leads whose Added Time has always read "10:08 am".
  It now reads "05:49 pm", in the cell, the info panel and the CSV export alike, and the fallback
  stamp on a lead's Call Logs tab reads the same way. The column filter is unchanged: the picker
  above it already showed AM/PM and still matches what it always did
  ([clockTime.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/clockTime.mjs),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

- **A dropdown menu is the width of the control it hangs from.** The switches in the page headers —
  the calendar's source, its view, the CRM board, the section switch on Admin, Employees and
  Employee Attendance — were as wide as whatever happened to be selected, while their menus had a
  160px floor of their own. With a short selection the two disagreed: "All" hung a list nearly three
  times its own width, overhanging it to the right and starting left of it, and choosing "Employee
  Attendance" then made the control itself grow, shifting the header around it. The switch now fills
  the box it is given and carries the same floor its menu does, so control and list share one width
  and one left edge whatever is chosen — and a header stops moving as you read it. The chevron still
  sits directly after its label rather than against the far edge of that wider box
  ([Dropdown.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/Dropdown.js),
  [dropdownMenu.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/dropdownMenu.mjs)).

- **One person is one chip a day on the calendar, and the day scrolls to the rest.** Three faults on
  one screen. Somebody with two logins came out as two chips on the same day, and where one account
  had been used and the other had not they *contradicted each other* — "Dheveesh P Present" directly
  above "Dheveesh P Absent" — with nothing on either to say which was which; they are folded by
  person now, and the least absent answer wins, because if any of somebody's logins shows them
  working then they were working. A day cell drew three chips and printed "+2 more" underneath,
  naming what was missing and giving no way to reach it — the cell now scrolls, so every booking is
  reachable and the month grid keeps its shape whether a day holds one or twenty. And the source
  switch was sized for "Post Production", so "Employee Attendance" wrapped onto two lines and left
  the list ragged. The **Today** button is gone from the calendar toolbar
  ([schedule/calendar/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/schedule/calendar/service.js),
  [MeetingsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/MeetingsPage.js)).

- **Attendance no longer marks everybody absent for the rest of the month.** A day that has not
  happened has no attendance — not "absent", *nothing*: nobody can have failed to turn up on a
  morning that has not arrived. The calendar asks for the whole month it is drawing, so on the 5th it
  asked for the 1st to the 30th and got a row per employee reading Absent for every day from the 6th
  on — twenty-five days of red burying the shoots underneath. The register now stops at today,
  clamped in the one place both the calendar and the Employee Attendance screen go through
  ([hrm/attendance/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/service.js)).

- **"The calendar could not load attendance" is gone, and the register works before its migration is
  run.** `employee_attendance` is created by a Prisma migration, so between deploying the code and
  running `migrate deploy` the table does not exist — and that read was unguarded, so it took the
  whole source down. On the calendar, which draws attendance as one of its sources, that surfaced as
  an error toast on every screen while the shoots and meetings beside it were perfectly readable. It
  now degrades to what it can still measure, which is almost everything: attendance is derived by
  default, so with no stored rows every day reads as whatever that person's own activity says. Only
  corrections are lost until the migration runs, and *writing* one still fails loudly — which is the
  honest place for a missing table to be reported. The calendar's own default window was also a day
  out at each end: `new Date(y, m, 1).toISOString()` is midnight *local* rendered as UTC, so east of
  Greenwich it sliced to the last day of the previous month
  ([hrm/attendance/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/service.js),
  [schedule/calendar/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/schedule/calendar/service.js)).

- **A booking against a Qua Qua lead is a Qua Qua booking.** Its Event ID came out
  `DBS-BK-DD-CBE-000005` — Dum Dum — sitting beside its own lead's `DBS-EID-QQ-CBE-000001`, so the
  code said one vertical about a job the enquiry beside it said was another. `vertical` is optional
  on a project and Create Project does not always carry it; arriving blank, the identifier fell back
  to Dum Dum, which is the default for a record that names no vertical at all. It now reads the
  enquiry the booking was made against — that id was minted for that lead under that lead's
  vertical, and a booking is what a converted lead becomes, so the two cannot honestly differ. A
  vertical somebody actually chose is never overruled: only a blank is filled, because a booking can
  be sold across verticals and second-guessing that from an id would be the same mistake the other
  way round. The vertical is also **written onto the project** rather than only into its code, which
  is why the Vertical column read "—" on exactly the rows that went wrong — fixing the id alone would
  have left every filter and report still seeing a booking with no vertical. A shoot inside the
  booking reads it the same way, so it cannot land on a different vertical from the project it
  belongs to ([projects/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js)).

- **Reports & Analytics no longer warns about duplicate React keys.** A report row is the result of
  a grouping rather than a record, so what identifies one is whatever it was grouped by - and the
  key took the first field that had a value and stopped. Work Hours is grouped by person *and* day,
  so every employee's row for the same date came out under that date alone and React was handed a
  dozen rows all keyed `2026-09-02`, which it is then free to duplicate or drop. The key is now
  everything that could be part of a grouping, joined, with the row's position only as a last resort
  - a key that is only a position changes under sorting and the row loses its identity every time
  the table is reordered ([crmReports.js](../../frontend/src/lib/crmReports.js)).

- **Editing a lead's phone number now warns under the Contact box, not only when you press Update.**
  The check was there but it only spoke at the save, and only as a dialog — so a number edited onto
  one another lead already holds read as perfectly fine right up to the moment somebody tried to
  save it, which is the latest possible time to be told and the one point at which they have stopped
  looking at the field. Both forms now say it in the same place and the same words: *Already an
  enquiry: DBS-EID-…*, under the box it is about. The dialog offers to go on with the save only when
  a save was actually interrupted — pressed from the line under the field there is nothing to go on
  with, and a button reading "Save anyway" that saved a half-finished edit would be a trap
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

- **The Calls board showed Callyzer's own id for a call instead of an Enquiry ID.** Codes were minted
  when a call was delivered and nowhere else, so every call already in the log had none — and the
  board, Contacts and the info panel all fell back to the raw id, a string like
  `XgnkwlIXrIKYMem-mDNAP7DPkVfFsV3-4IIhEogXFLU`. They are filled in on the first read now, once per
  process, so a database that has been taking calls comes right on its own rather than waiting for
  somebody to run a script. Contacts was worse than wrong: it asked for the calls that *had* a code
  and so listed none of them at all, while the board sat full
  ([callLogService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/callLogService.js)).

- **Double-clicking a call answered "Lead not found."** A call records the lead it became in a plain
  text column with nothing keeping it honest, so a lead deleted since — or removed by the migration
  script — left the call still claiming it. The board took the claim at face value and went looking
  for a lead that was not there. The link is read off a join against a live lead now, so a call whose
  lead has gone reads as one nobody has added and opens the Add Lead form, which is what it is again;
  the Contacts source tests the same way, so the two agree about who is already a lead. The double
  click falls back to that form as well, for a lead deleted between the page arriving and the click
  ([callBoardService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/callBoardService.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

- **The info panel read a call as though it were a wedding lead.** It headed the call's row key
  "Enquiry ID" and answered "Verticals: Dum Dum" — because a blank vertical folds onto Dum Dum, and a
  call has a blank vertical for the whole reason that board exists. It now reads a call through the
  Calls board's own fields, on any call rather than only on one nobody has added yet
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

- **A filter set on one CRM board no longer follows you to the next.** The four boards share column
  ids on purpose, so the sorting and the filter widgets are the ones every board uses — which meant
  an Enquiry ID typed on one board went on quietly narrowing the next one after the switch, with the
  box that would clear it no longer on screen. Switching boards now drops any filter whose column
  the new board does not have, and any status the new board cannot show
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

- **A saved column order can no longer draw one column twice and drop another.** `weaveColumnOrder`
  filtered a stored order against the table's columns but did not deduplicate it, so a repeated id
  filled two slots and pushed the last column off the end - which reads as data missing from the
  screen rather than as a layout fault. It could not happen while the order was written and read by
  the same browser; it can now that the order arrives from the server and may have been saved by an
  older release. ([tableLayout.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/tableLayout.js))

- **A shoot can be added before its date is fixed.** Add Shoot now asks **Shoot Date Known** first,
  and only asks for a Date when the answer is Yes - the same Yes/No toggle with the date beside it
  that the W/A Group-Name Generator already uses for Main Event Date Known, so the studio answers
  this question one way wherever it is asked. A wedding is regularly sold months before the
  family settles the reception date, and the form had no way to say so - Date was required, so
  somebody either invented one or left the shoot off the project until it was known. The question
  opens on **Yes**, so a shoot with a date is filled in exactly as before. Reopening a saved shoot
  reads the answer off the shoot itself rather than a stored flag, so the two can never disagree,
  and the shoot list's inline editor accepts an undated shoot too - correcting a spelling no longer
  demands a date nobody has.

  An undated shoot reads as "—" wherever shoot dates are listed (three date formatters printed
  **1 Jan 1970** for it, because `new Date(null)` is the epoch rather than an invalid date), does
  not mark the project's Main Event Date as known even when it is the main event, and is treated by
  Image Selection as a shoot that **has not happened**: its folders and selection items stay closed
  until a date is put on it, rather than opening the moment the shoot is created. That last one
  reverses what `shootDayHasPassed` answered for an empty date, which was safe only while a shoot
  could not be saved without one. `project_shoot.date_created` is now nullable
  ([20260905120000_shoot_date_optional](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260905120000_shoot_date_optional/migration.sql));
  every existing shoot keeps its date and nothing is backfilled. **Run `npx prisma migrate deploy`
  before this reaches an environment**, or saving an undated shoot fails on the NOT NULL column
  ([AddShootDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/AddShootDialog.js),
  [shoot-visibility.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/shoot-visibility.js)).

- **A task now says which department and which branch it is for.** The Tasks screen recorded who a
  task was for and what kind of work it was, but nothing about where in the studio it sat - so the
  studio's whole task list could not be read by branch or by department at all. **Department** and
  **Branch** are now asked for when a task is given, both required, both picked from the studio's
  own department and location master data rather than typed. They are the task's own answer, not the
  assignee's: work crosses those lines often - Erode's editor finishing a Karim Nagar wedding - and
  reading them off the person would file that task under the wrong one of each. Both show as columns
  in every task view and on the task's own record, so they filter and export like any other column.
  Tasks raised before this read blank; a task is never edited, so they stay that way. Stored in new
  `hrm_task.department` and `hrm_task.branch` columns
  ([20260905090000_task_department_and_branch](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260905090000_task_department_and_branch/migration.sql)).
  **Run `npx prisma migrate deploy` before this reaches an environment**, or giving a task there
  fails on the missing columns. No new permission: the fields sit inside `HRM / Task Management`,
  which already decides who may give a task
  ([taskConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/taskConfig.js)).

- **The generated WhatsApp group name can be copied instead of retyped.** The W/A Group-Name
  Generator showed the name it had built in a read-only box and left it there, so the name that
  matters most to get character-perfect was the one being copied by eye into WhatsApp. A copy button
  now sits beside it - the mark alone, no word - and turns into a tick for a couple of seconds once
  the name is on the clipboard. It turns on only once every required field is filled in, the same
  bar Save clears, because until then there is no name to hand over; while it is off it says which
  fields are still missing. Copying works over plain http as well as https, where the browser's
  clipboard API is unavailable, and a copy that fails says so in a snackbar and leaves the box
  selectable to copy by hand
  ([WhatsappGroupNameDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/WhatsappGroupNameDialog.js),
  [clipboard.js](../../frontend/src/lib/clipboard.js)).
- **Employee Attendance is a real record now: logins, leave and permissions live in the database
  and are approved by a person.** Every attendance record used to be held in the browser's
  `localStorage`, which meant it was device-specific, disappeared with the site data, and could be
  rewritten from the console by the person it judged — and that nothing was ever validated or
  decided by anybody. The whole module moves server-side across five new tables
  (`attendance_session`, `attendance_request`, `attendance_request_event`, `attendance_settings`,
  `attendance_holiday`; migration `20260903090000_employee_attendance`). A daily login is stamped
  with the *server's* clock, not the device's, and one login per person per day is enforced by a
  unique index rather than by a handler that can be raced. Leave, permission and on-duty requests
  are decided by any one of the three Leave Approval slots already on the employee's record — the
  first to answer settles it, so nothing stalls when approver 1 is themselves away — or by the new
  `HRM / Attendance Approval` permission, and nobody can decide their own. Approving, rejecting,
  cancelling and re-applying each append to a per-request event log instead of overwriting a status
  ([attendance/](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/),
  [EmployeeAttendancePage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/EmployeeAttendancePage.js)).
  **Pulling this branch needs `prisma migrate deploy`, `prisma generate`, `npm run seed` and a
  backend restart.** The screen answers to a new `HRM / Employee Attendance` permission rather than
  to Employee Management, so grant it in Administration → Roles to every role that should be able
  to mark a day, or those roles will find the page closed.
- **The working day is configurable, and attendance is judged against it.** A new Rules section on
  the attendance screen — for `HRM / Attendance Approval` holders — sets the shift start and end,
  how many minutes of lateness are still on time, what a full day owes, which weekdays are off, the
  longest permission anybody may raise, and a holiday calendar. Statuses and hours targets are all
  worked out from those: a day now reads as On time, Late, Permitted, Not logged out, Absent, On
  leave, Weekly off or Holiday, and a range's target is a full day for each working day it actually
  contains rather than a fixed 42h30 week and 170h month whatever the month held. An approved
  permission that covers the arrival excuses the lateness; approved leave still counts towards the
  target, so taking leave cannot quietly hide a shortfall
  ([attendanceRules.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/attendanceRules.js),
  [hrm-attendance-rules.test.js](../../backend/test/hrm-attendance-rules.test.js)).

- **A project now tracks both client gifts, and Project Management → Client Gifts shows where each
  one has got to.** Welcome Gift was a bare Applicable / Not Applicable answer that nobody owned.
  Start / Edit Project now asks the same question about a second gift, **Shoot Completed Gift**, and
  for each one **who is taking care of it** — an active employee, picked the same way a Project
  Manager is. One person usually handles both, so a tick box under the pair names the Welcome Gift's
  person for the other and keeps them in step. An assignee dropdown stays on screen for a gift
  marked Not Applicable, disabled and cleared, so the form does not reflow as the radio is answered;
  saving an applicable gift with nobody named is refused. The new **Client Gifts** screen lists one
  gift at a time — picked from the dropdown beside the heading, the way CRD Documents picks its
  vertical — with the tabs **Yet To Receive**, **Sent** and **Received by Client** counting within
  it, and every row's actions moving it along. Sent and received dates are stamped by the server as
  the move is made, so a gift cannot be recorded as arriving before it left. New permission
  `PROJECT / Client Gifts`: no role reaches the screen until it is granted in Role Management and
  the role re-saved — Super Admin holds it already
  ([ClientGiftsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ClientGiftsPage.js),
  [client-gifts/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/project-management/client-gifts/service.js)).
- **A CRD document's View shows what the client answered, and that response has a link of its own.**
  ⋮ → **View** on a CRD Documents row used to repeat the row's own fields — the Event ID and client
  name already beside it in the Info panel. It now shows the client's submitted answers: every
  question they were asked and what they put, including the photographs and files they attached, for
  either vertical. A row still waiting on its client says so rather than drawing an empty document.
  From there, **Share Response** issues a read-only link that opens the answers and nothing else —
  a **second token, not the one the client filled the document through**, so forwarding a response
  never hands anybody the form that can still be submitted. The link is issued only when asked for,
  is the same URL every time it is pressed, and refuses a document that has not been submitted
  ([CrdDocumentViewPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CrdDocumentViewPanel.js),
  [CrdDocumentResponse.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CrdDocumentResponse.js) — one renderer,
  so the studio's view and the shared link can never disagree,
  [PublicCrdDocumentResponse.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicCrdDocumentResponse.js) at
  `/crd/response/<token>`,
  [crd-documents/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/service.js)). Migration
  `20260904160000_crd_response_share_token` adds the nullable `share_token`.

- **CRD Documents says where each row came from, and a manual one now names the project it is for.**
  A **Type** column reads **Project** for a row Start / Edit Project raised and **Manual** for one
  raised here from Create CRD Document, in both the Dum Dum and Qua Qua tables. Create CRD Document
  now requires an **Enquiry ID**, chosen from a list rather than typed: the projects whose CRD
  Document was answered "Not Applicable", which are exactly the ones this screen never shows and so
  the only ones that need a document raised by hand. A project already collecting one is refused with
  a pointer to Send Link on its own row, and an Enquiry ID that has already been given a manual
  document drops off the list, so a client is never handed two links to the same event
  ([CrdDocumentsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CrdDocumentsPage.js),
  [CreateCrdDocumentDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CreateCrdDocumentDialog.js),
  [crd-documents/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/service.js)).

- **Dates and phone numbers on both CRD documents use the pickers the rest of the app uses.** Every
  date — the Dum Dum Date Of Birth, and Qua Qua's baby, mother, father and delivery dates — is now
  the shared calendar rather than a bare browser date box, and will not offer a birthday in the
  future or a delivery date in the past. Every contact number is the shared phone field, so the
  country code is **chosen from the list of 185 countries** instead of typed. That last one needed a
  fix behind it: both documents stored only the last ten digits of a number, which silently turned a
  client's own country code into an Indian one — a Singapore or US number was stored wrong. They now
  keep the studio's one stored shape, `+<dial code> <national number>`, and a half-typed number still
  saves as a draft ([crdDocumentShared.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/crdDocumentShared.js),
  [document.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/document.js),
  [documentQQ.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/documentQQ.js)). Dum Dum's Event Start
  and End Date & Time stay as they were: the shared calendar carries no time, and splitting them
  would drop the time an event starts at.

- **The Qua Qua CRD document has its own form, built as a wizard rather than one scrolling page.**
  A Qua Qua public CRD link (`/crd/<token>`) no longer opens the Dum Dum groom/bride form — it now
  asks its own questions across three sections named in a left rail, the same rail Dum Dum's document
  uses: **Identification** (WhatsApp Group Name, First/Last Name, Phone Number, Shoot Type, Branch,
  optional Referred By), **Birthdays**, and **References**. Only the active section is on screen —
  Dum Dum's rail scrolls to a section already in the page, this one switches which section is
  rendered — and a section ahead of the one being filled in is locked in the rail until the current
  one is complete, so **Next** replaces free scrolling as how the client moves forward (**Back** to
  return). A returning client reopening the link lands on the first section still outstanding rather
  than being sent back to Identification. Birthdays asks a different five or six questions depending
  on the Shoot Type chosen in Identification — a baby already born (Baby Shoot through Puberty
  Function) asks for the baby's name and birth date plus both parents'; a baby on the way (Maternity
  Shoot, Baby Shower) asks the parents' details and an Expected Delivery Date instead; Combo Packages
  and Other ask the parents' details plus one date covering either case. References asks an optional
  Special Notes, up to ten uploaded reference files (media or documents, 5 MB each), one optional
  reference link, and the terms & conditions agreement, the same wording and box the Dum Dum document
  already asks for — the one field every category requires. It saves as a draft and submits the same
  way Dum Dum's document does, uploading picked reference files to R2 the way Dum Dum's photos already
  are, with its own completion bar that grows once a Shoot Type is picked and Birthdays' own required
  fields become known. The public form component is split by vertical
  ([PublicCrdDocumentForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicCrdDocumentForm.js),
  [PublicCrdDocumentFormDumDum.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicCrdDocumentFormDumDum.js),
  [PublicCrdDocumentFormQuaQua.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicCrdDocumentFormQuaQua.js),
  [crdDocumentShared.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/crdDocumentShared.js)), and so is the
  document schema it validates against on the server — Dum Dum's is unchanged, Qua Qua's is new,
  and a CRD document with no vertical still falls back to Dum Dum's shape exactly as before
  ([documentQQ.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/documentQQ.js),
  [verticalDocument.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/verticalDocument.js),
  [crd-documents/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/service.js)). Reference files
  reuse the assets module's own document storage rather than a new one
  ([documentStorage.js](../../backend/src/services/documentStorage.js)).
  `RadioGroupField` gained a `layout="column"` option for a longer list of choices stacked one per
  line, which Shoot Type and Branch both use — as a bare list with no box around it, so it reads the
  same way the Jotform original does
  ([RadioGroupField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/RadioGroupField.js)). Only Identification
  is a "Next"-gated step; Birthdays and References turned out to have no dependency on each other; so
  past Identification the document is one continuous scrollable page, the same shape Dum Dum's is
  throughout, and the rail scrolls to a section instead of switching to it. WhatsApp Group Name,
  Branch and Shoot Type prefill the same way Dum Dum's own fields do — from the project this document
  is linked to, or the CRM lead behind its Enquiry ID when it is standalone — but only where the
  match is unambiguous: a lead logged as "Birthday Shoot" or a city outside the six branches, say,
  are left for the client to answer rather than guessed, because a wrong Shoot Type would send the
  client through the wrong Birthdays questions
  ([crd-documents/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/service.js)).

- **A CRD document's browser tab names its vertical.** A Dum Dum link's tab now reads
  "DD-CRD Document | DBS" and a Qua Qua link's "QQ-CRD Document | DBS", instead of the same
  "CRD Document | DBS" for both — so a studio user with more than one open can tell them apart
  ([PublicCrdDocumentForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicCrdDocumentForm.js)).
- **A quotation can now say something its line items cannot.** The Quotation Generator has an
  **Additional Notes** field under the shoots — an inclusion agreed on a call, a caveat about a
  date, anything the services and deliverables have no room for — and it is filled in the same
  place whether the quotation is being raised or an existing one is being edited into its next
  version. The note is client-facing: it prints on the client's copy at `/q/<token>`, and therefore
  in the PDF, as its own **Additional Notes** section directly above Terms & Conditions, with the
  line breaks it was typed with. A quotation with no note prints nothing rather than an empty
  heading. It belongs to the version rather than to the chain, so an earlier version read from the
  version history shows the note it was sent with and not the one written later, and duplicating a
  quotation onto another enquiry carries it across. Capped at 2000 characters in the field and
  again on the server. Stored in a new `sales_quotation.additional_notes` column
  ([20260904170000_quotation_additional_notes](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260904170000_quotation_additional_notes/migration.sql))
  and shown in the register's info panel and record view under **Notes**. **Run
  `npx prisma migrate deploy` before this reaches an environment**, or saving a quotation there
  fails on the missing column. No new permission: the field lives inside Quotation Management,
  which already decides who may raise a quotation and who may read one
  ([QuotationGeneratorPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationGeneratorPanel.js)).
- **Every attendance date was stored a day early.** A calendar day was anchored to local midnight,
  and Prisma writes a `@db.Date` column from the Date's *UTC* fields — so in any zone ahead of UTC
  the instant handed to the database fell on the previous UTC day. A leave applied for on the 10th
  was saved, listed and approved as the 9th, and the same shift hit holidays, a session's work date
  and the day a weekly off was decided against. `startOfLocalDay` is now `calendarDay` in
  [attendanceRules.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/attendanceRules.js) and anchors a day
  to midnight UTC, which is the representation the column actually round trips; a written-down
  `YYYY-MM-DD` is taken literally rather than parsed into an instant and read back out of one.

- **Attendance stopped believing a week-old token about who you are.** `linkedEmployeeId` is baked
  into the seven-day JWT, and the module read it from there — so somebody HR linked to an employee
  after they had signed in went on being told their login is not linked to an employee record, and
  saw an empty list of their own requests, until their token expired. Every such question now goes
  through `linkedEmployeeIdFor` in
  [service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/service.js), which reads the user row.

- **A status in Employee Attendance looks like a status everywhere else in the app.** The module
  drew its own coloured pill over the shared table cell — it already passed `statusStyles` to
  `buildTableColumns` and then overrode the cell it had configured, so attendance was the one screen
  where a status did not match the rest of the app. The override and its duplicate colour map are
  gone; `TableCellValue` draws every status now.

- **Employee Attendance uses the shared Button and IconButton instead of its own copies.** Fifteen
  controls were hand-written `<button>` elements that re-typed the `primary` and `secondary` variant
  strings character for character — including the Approve/Reject pair, which hand-coloured itself
  red or blue rather than choosing a variant. The buttons that remain plain are the ones no variant
  describes: tab toggles, list rows, work-mode selectors and inline text links.

- **A permission that is over the limit says so in the dialog, instead of on the server.** The cap
  was only consulted when the attendance rules happened to be loaded — `settings?.maxPermissionMinutes &&`
  skipped the check entirely when they were not — so a 3h 3m permission met an enabled Raise request
  button and a refusal that only arrived after submitting. Rules that have not loaded now block the
  request with a reason rather than waving it through, the message names the limit and what was
  actually asked for ("cannot be longer than 3h, and this one is 3h 3m"), and the duration stays on
  screen beside it rather than being replaced by it.
  [EmployeeAttendancePage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/EmployeeAttendancePage.js)

- **A login with no employee behind it is told so, instead of being handed a form that always
  fails.** The `admin` account is not an employee, so it has nobody for a day or a leave to be
  about — but [EmployeeAttendancePage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/EmployeeAttendancePage.js)
  drew the camera, the work modes and the quick actions anyway, then raised the same error as a
  toast on every load. It now explains who can link the login and shows nothing it cannot use.

- **Employee Attendance is back in the sidebar.** Its two permissions
  (`HRM:Employee Attendance`, `HRM:Attendance Approval`) were sitting `isActive = false` in the
  database, left retired by a seed run from before the catalogue described them. An inactive
  permission is excluded from a super admin's permission set and cannot be granted to a role, so
  `permissionForRoute("/hrm/employee-attendance")` never resolved and
  [AppSidebar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppSidebar.js) filtered the entry out for
  everybody. [iam-roles-permissions.js](../../backend/prisma/seeds/iam-roles-permissions.js) now
  writes `isActive: true` back on every permission the catalogue names, so re-seeding revives a
  permission that was retired by mistake instead of leaving it off forever.

- **A leave can no longer end before it begins, and a permission can no longer run backwards.** A
  leave whose To date fell before its From date was silently recorded as a one-day leave, and a
  permission whose To time fell before its From time showed a duration of "-" and was raised
  anyway. Both are now refused in the dialog and again on the server, which also refuses a
  permission longer than the configured limit and a request overlapping one already pending or
  approved ([attendanceRules.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/attendanceRules.js)).
- **The attendance selfie no longer claims a face was checked when nothing checked it.** The face
  test fell back to a skin-tone-and-edge heuristic that accepted a warm-lit wall and rejected a
  real face in a dim room. Only the browser's own `FaceDetector` may now refuse a shot; where the
  browser has none, the selfie is captured, stored with `login_face_verified` false, and shown to
  the employee and to HR as unverified rather than passed off as checked
  ([EmployeeAttendancePage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/EmployeeAttendancePage.js)).
- **"Upcomming" is spelled Upcoming, and is worked out rather than stored.** The attendance status
  tab was misspelled throughout. It is also no longer a status anybody sets: an approved leave
  reads as Upcoming until its start date arrives and as Approved from then on, decided per request
  against the current date, so a row cannot go stale in the database.
- **The attendance CSV header matches the table it came from.** The download still said "Day, In,
  Out, Mode" after the columns had been renamed. Both now come from one field list, so a renamed
  column cannot leave the export saying something else.

- **The CRD Documents screen loads again instead of failing with "Unable to load CRD documents."**
  `SalesProjectCrdDocument.projectId` was still declared non-nullable in
  [schema.prisma](../../backend/prisma/schema.prisma) after the standalone-document migration made the
  column nullable, so every list read threw as soon as one standalone document existed — a document
  raised from **Create CRD Document** has no project. The field is now optional, matching the
  database.
- **Dragging a table column to the far side of a wide table now scrolls the table under it.** The
  column order is set by dragging a header, but the table did not move while a column was being
  carried: held against the right-hand edge, the pointer simply sat there while the columns it
  should have been passing stayed off screen. Reaching the end of a wide table meant dropping the
  column short, scrolling by hand, picking it up again, and repeating that three or four times.
  Holding a dragged column near either edge now scrolls the table sideways, faster the closer to
  the edge it is held and at full speed past the edge itself, so a column can be carried from one
  end of a table to the other in one gesture. It stops the moment the column is dropped, or if the
  pointer is dragged off the table altogether — a drag abandoned off the side of the page no longer
  leaves the table scrolled to its end. Every table in the app gets this, since they all render
  through `DataTable`. On a table too narrow for two separate edge bands the bands shrink to a
  quarter of its width each, so there is always somewhere to hold a column still
  ([dragEdgeScroll.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/dragEdgeScroll.js),
  [DataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js)).

- **IAM.md now says who sees which *records*, not only who may open which screen.** A new
  [Who sees which records](../project-state/IAM.md#who-sees-which-records) section maps every module
  against the three things that narrow a list — branch, ownership, and the per-source reads that
  Contacts, the Schedule and Nebula do — and names the screens that narrow by nothing, which is most
  of them. Writing it down turned up a bug: the AI's HRM tools narrow employees by `branchId`, a
  column `Employee` does not have, so `hrm_headcount`, `hrm_search_employees` and `hrm_get_employee`
  throw for everybody except a Super Admin, whose filter is empty and so never touches it. Recorded
  as three `todo` tests in
  [hrm-employee-branch-scope.test.js](../../backend/test/hrm-employee-branch-scope.test.js) rather than
  patched, because narrowing employees means matching branch names against `work_base_location` and
  that is a decision about who may read whose staff records.

- **Image Selection opens again instead of failing with "the column selection_submitted_at does not
  exist".** `ImageSelectionDeliverableConfig.selectionSubmittedAt` had been declared in
  `schema.prisma` with no migration to create the column, so Prisma Client selected a column no
  database built from the migrations had, and every read of the Image Selection project list threw
  `P2022`. The column is now created by a migration
  ([20260904160000_selection_submitted_at_on_config](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260904160000_selection_submitted_at_on_config/migration.sql)).
  Nullable with no backfill — a null reads as "not submitted", which is what every existing row is.
  The identically named column on `image_selection_gallery_publication` already existed and is
  untouched. **Run `npx prisma migrate deploy` on any environment that has not had this applied**,
  or Image Selection stays broken there.

- **Enquiry ID is chosen from the CRM's leads and can no longer be typed in.** Every keystroke in
  the picker used to be written straight to the field, so searching *was* answering: a typo was
  enough to book a project — or generate a group name — against an enquiry that exists nowhere in
  the CRM, with nothing on screen saying so. What is typed now only narrows the list, the value
  changes when a row is picked and at no other time, and closing the box puts back whatever is
  actually selected rather than leaving a half-typed search looking like an answer. The empty state
  says where the rows come from. This applies to both places the picker is used — Create Project /
  Edit Sale Details and the **W/A Group-Name Generator** — since they share the one control.
  **A project can therefore no longer be created for an enquiry with no CRM lead behind it.**
  See [EnquiryCombobox.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/EnquiryCombobox.js).

- **The WhatsApp group name list offers only the names saved for the chosen enquiry.** It listed
  every unused name in the studio, so all but one of the rows on offer belonged to somebody else's
  booking — and picking one of those is how a project ends up carrying a name generated for a
  different client. It now narrows to the enquiry in hand and re-reads as the enquiry changes, the
  same way the quotation list beside it already narrows. With no enquiry chosen it offers nothing
  rather than everything and says to choose one first; where an enquiry has no name generated for
  it, it says so and points at **Enter manually** and the W/A Group-Name Generator. A name saved
  before the generator asked which lead it was for carries no enquiry and so is no longer listed —
  it is typed through Enter manually instead. The search box appears only where there is more than
  one name to search.

- **Changing the enquiry now gives up the WhatsApp group name that belonged to the last one.**
  Choosing an enquiry picks up the name the generator saved against it; choosing a *different*
  enquiry did nothing at all when the new one had no name of its own, so the previous enquiry's name
  stayed in the box. That was not only what it looked like: the form carries the name's
  `whatsappGroupNameId` beside the text, and that id is the generator row the save claims — so a
  stale name was a stale id, and saving would have claimed the name reserved for one enquiry against
  the project being created for another. Re-choosing the same enquiry still leaves a name that was
  picked or typed by hand alone.

- **The WhatsApp group name list closes again.** It is drawn by hand rather than by the shared
  `Dropdown`, because its rows are two lines — the name over the group type, branch and enquiry it
  was generated for — and it had been built with no dismissal at all: picking a name closed it and
  nothing else did, so once opened it stayed open over the rest of the form. It now uses
  `useMenuDismiss`, the same rule every other hand-rolled menu in the app follows, so clicking away
  or pressing Escape closes it. See
  [SaleDetailsSection.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/SaleDetailsSection.js).

- **Start Project pre-selects CRD Document and will not start a project with a required answer left
  blank.** CRD Document was marked with an asterisk, opened with neither option chosen, and was
  checked by nobody — so a project could be started with no answer, and an unanswered project fell
  into a gap: **CRD Documents** lists the projects whose answer is set and is not "Not Applicable",
  and **Create CRD Document** offers only the ones answered "Not Applicable", so it appeared on
  neither screen and its CRD was collected by no route at all. It now opens on **Applicable** —
  pre-selecting that and being wrong leaves a visible row somebody can decide about, where
  pre-selecting the other and being wrong is silent. Start and Update now also stop on every
  outstanding required answer at once — Welcome Gift and Shoot Completed Gift with the employee
  taking care of each applicable one, Project Validity (In Months), Storage Needed and CRD
  Document — and mark each field **Required** where it sits, the way Add Employee does, rather than
  naming one problem at a time in a line at the top. Nothing is marked until the button has been
  pressed once, and the button is never disabled, so the form always says why it refused. The same
  rules are enforced at `PATCH /projects/:id/start-project`, which previously accepted the permissive
  draft shape, and which now answers with the field names rather than "Validation failed"; **Save to
  Draft is deliberately unchanged** and still accepts a half-filled form, since that is what the
  panel autosaves into. See
  [StartProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/StartProjectFormPanel.js)
  and [projectSetupValidation.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/projectSetupValidation.mjs).

- **Sending an onboarding back to a candidate now emails the short link, like every other onboarding
  link does.** The invite list copies `/onboarding/apply/A7K9C2M4` and the invite email sends it,
  but Send Back was still emailing the 64-character tokenised URL — the same candidate, about the
  same form, given a different and far uglier link the second time
  ([controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/onboarding-invites/controller.js)). The rule was
  written out separately at each place that emails a link, so when short codes arrived only one of
  them was updated; it is one function now, and Send Back is no longer refused for an invite that
  has a short code but no stored token — a code is a perfectly good link. The same change drops a
  second copy of the frontend base URL that had a different fallback from the module's own, so with
  `FRONTEND_URL` unset this one mail pointed at the production host while the invite mail beside it
  pointed at localhost.
- **Create Project records the advance in the same sitting.** A **Payment Received** section sits
  between Sales Remarks and Event Planning and asks for exactly what Accounts asks for when money
  comes in, from Category down to Description — amount, received date, payment mode and whatever
  that mode carries, the account it landed in, who received it, the receipt, a description. It does
  not ask which project, who the client is, or which branch: the entry is filed against the project
  being created and reads those off it. Optional as a whole and complete once begun, so a booking
  confirmed before the money arrives still saves and the payment goes on the project's Payments tab
  later. The project is created first and the entry sent after it with the new project's id, so a
  refused payment leaves the project standing and says to record it on the Payments tab rather than
  reopening a form that would create a second project. The field list is derived from Accounts' own,
  and the two forms now share one validator and one payload builder, so neither can come to ask for
  less than the other. Needs `ACCOUNTS / Cash Management` `READ_WRITE`; without it the section, its
  rail entry and its options request are all absent, and editing a sale never shows it
  ([CreateProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CreateProjectFormPanel.js),
  [accountsConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/accountsConfig.js),
