  [SalesManagerPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/SalesManagerPage.js)).

- **A profile photo opens on its own screen, and can be saved from it.** Pressing the avatar on
  My Profile opens the photo full screen with one menu at the top right: Change photo, which
  asks whether the new one comes from the camera or the gallery and only offers the camera on a
  device that has one; Download photo, which saves it; and Remove photo. The download reads the
  bytes from a new signed-in `GET /api/user/profile-photo/download` rather than from the storage
  bucket, because across origins a browser ignores a download link and refuses the fetch — so
  the button always produces a file and never a new tab
  ([ProfilePhotoViewer.js](../../frontend/src/components/profile/ProfilePhotoViewer.js),
  [downloadImage.js](../../frontend/src/lib/downloadImage.js)).
- **A new password may not be one the account has used before.** Changing a password on My
  Profile or through a reset link is refused when it matches the current password or any of
  the last five before it, with a message that says so; the previous passwords' bcrypt hashes
  are kept, newest first, in a new `password_history` JSON column on `user` (migration
  `20260902030000_user_password_history`; pulling this branch needs `prisma migrate deploy` and
  `prisma generate`, then a backend restart)
  ([passwordHistory.js](../../backend/src/services/passwordHistory.js),
  [routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/user/routes.js)).
- **Sales Manager can edit a project it sold.** Every row on Sales Manager now carries
  "Edit Sale Details", opening the Create Project panel in edit mode over the existing record —
  group name, client number, vertical, source branch, converted value and the three sales
  remarks — and saving through `PATCH /api/sales/projects/:id`. It appears only for a role
  holding `SALES / Sales Manager` at `READ_WRITE`, the same permission the endpoint already
  demanded; a read-only role sees the menu it saw before. The shared row menu previously offered
  its actions only when a screen passed all four handlers — the sale's two and the project
  plan's two — which is why Sales Manager, which has no business starting a project, was left
  with a view-only menu; each half now stands on its own
  ([SalesManagerPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/SalesManagerPage.js),
  [ProjectDataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/ProjectDataTable.js)).

- **Application and WhatsApp usage are measured rather than inferred.** The two usage columns were
  worked out from the gaps between somebody's recorded lead actions, with anything over fifteen
  minutes treated as a break — so a person who saved a lead, spent twelve minutes in their email and
  saved another was credited with thirteen minutes of work here.

  The browser now reports instead. It beats every thirty seconds while the tab is **visible** and
  the person has **interacted recently** — a pointer that moved, a key, a click, a scroll, a touch —
  and stops the moment either stops being true, so time behind another window and a tab left open on
  an empty desk both count for nothing. The beat carries no time of its own: the server stamps it,
  because a figure a browser can name is one anybody can inflate from a console. Each beat extends
  the stretch it belongs to (new table `app_usage_session`, migration `20260902140000`), so a
  morning's work is one row that grows rather than a hundred and twenty rows, and a closed laptop or
  a lost connection simply stops extending it. WhatsApp time is its own context, recorded only while
  the chat panel is open, so the two reports remain the same measure read side by side.
  ([usage/heartbeat/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/usage/heartbeat/service.js),
  [usageHeartbeat.js](../../frontend/src/lib/usageHeartbeat.js))

- **Meeting reminders use the application's own snackbar.** The reminder drew a blue panel built for
  itself — its own shape, its own dismiss, its own place on the screen — so the application announced
  a meeting in a way it announces nothing else, and two notices could sit on top of each other at the
  foot of the page. It goes through the page's `showNotice` now: the same bar, the same place, the
  same dismiss and timing as everything else, with Join riding on the snackbar's own action slot.
  ([MeetingReminders.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/MeetingReminders.js))

- **Double-clicking a contact opens the record it came from.** The same gesture the rest of the
  application uses to open a row now works in Contacts, landing in the same places: a contact that
  came from a lead opens the CRM's own lead popup, and one that came from a project opens that
  project's record — the lead first where a person is both. A contact whose records live only in
  modules with no screen of their own (an employee, a vendor, a payee) opens in the application's
  own record panel rather than the gesture doing nothing. Each link is followed only where it would
  arrive: both destinations answer to their own permission.

  The CRM board can now open a lead named in the URL (`/sales/crm?lead=DBS-DD-EID-4864`), fetching
  it on its own rather than looking for it among the rows — the board is paged, filtered and scoped
  to one vertical, so a linked lead is usually not on screen, and a link that works only when the
  lead happened to be loaded is not a link. The board moves to that lead's vertical, so closing the
  popup leaves the reader somewhere that makes sense.
  ([ContactsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/contacts/ContactsPage.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js))

- **The WhatsApp Number saves itself as soon as it is whole.** Every other field on the lead popup
  waits for Update, which is that form's contract and stays that way. This one does not, because
  something else on the same screen depends on it: the WhatsApp Chat panel beside it can only hold a
  conversation with a number the server knows, so a number typed and left unsaved was a chat that
  worked until the popup closed. Written on the tenth digit of an Indian number — the last of
  whatever its country allows — and never before, so nothing is saved on the way to typing
  something.
  ([phoneNumber.js](../../frontend/src/lib/phoneNumber.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js))

- **Calendar bookings link to the record they belong to, and say where they are.** A day chip now
  carries the identifier and the name on one line and the location on a second — "DBS-SID-0003 ZZ
  Dur Shoot / P — Chennai, Tamil Nadu" — rather than a truncated sentence, and a cell shows three
  before it counts the rest so a busy week's row stays the height of a quiet one's. Clicking a shoot
  or a main event now opens **that project's record** in Project Management, which is where a shoot
  is planned, staffed and delivered; meetings still open their own row in the List view. The project
  is named in the URL (`/project-management/projects?project=DBS-EVT-00001`), so the link is
  bookmarkable and reloadable, Back returns to the calendar, and the board switches to All when the
  linked project is not on the tab last used. Followed only where it would actually arrive: the
  Projects board has its own permission, and without it the booking opens in the panel beside the
  calendar instead of being sent to a refusal.
  ([MeetingsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/MeetingsPage.js),
  [ProjectsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectsPage.js))

- **The Meetings calendar is now the studio's calendar.** The month grid drew meetings alone,
  which answered "what is my day" for one person doing one job; a shoot is far more of the studio's
  day and was nowhere on it. It now draws **meetings, shoots and projects' main events** together,
  one colour per kind — meetings keep their blue, shoots are amber, main events green. A shoot
  reads as its Shoot ID, name, place and
  session length ("DBS-SID-0003 · ZZ Dur Shoot · P — Chennai, Tamil Nadu · 1h 0m"), worded by the
  same `formatSessionTiming` the shoot list uses, and every booking names its owner.

  Clicking a meeting still goes to its row in the List view, as it always has; a shoot or a main
  event has no row there, so it opens in the details panel beside the calendar and the month stays
  on screen. The List view is untouched — it is still the CRM's meetings table, with its own
  columns, filters and Add Meet. That does mean the two views no longer share a filter: the list's
  filters are a meeting's columns and none of them means anything to a shoot, so the grid draws the
  whole diary and the Today button now jumps to this month rather than reaching into the table's
  Date filter.

  Served by a new `/api/schedule`, its own module for the same reason the contact directory is one.
  Each source is included only for a viewer holding that module's own Read permission, so a role
  without Project Management sees no shoots and is offered no Shoots switch; a source that fails is
  named on screen rather than leaving a day quietly short of its bookings. Bookings made by the day
  travel as plain `YYYY-MM-DD` text so a 1 September shoot cannot be drawn on 31 August west of
  Greenwich.
  ([schedule/calendar/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/schedule/calendar/service.js),
  [lib/schedule.js](../../frontend/src/lib/schedule.js),
  [MeetingsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/MeetingsPage.js))

- **Meeting notifications on the topbar bell.** The bell was a drawing; it now opens the studio's
  meetings — today's schedule in running order, then the next few days — on every screen rather
  than only on the Meetings page. A badge counts what is **still to come today**, so it clears as
  the day is worked through rather than sitting on a number that never moves, and each row says
  where the meeting stands when the clock adds something the time does not: "in 12 minutes",
  "starting now", "under way", "finished". Selecting anything opens Meetings through the same
  toggle the icon beside it uses. Cancelled meetings are left off entirely.

  It reads the same endpoint and the same rules from `@/lib/meetings` as the Meetings screen and
  the reminder toast, so the bell can never disagree with the page it links to about which day a
  meeting is on. Built on `OptionsMenu`, the application's own popover, so it is portalled, flips
  away from the window edge, closes on an outside click or Escape and stacks above the sticky bar.
  Gated on SALES → CRM Leads like the Meetings icon beside it — the meetings API answers for that
  permission, and a bell offered to a role that would then be refused is worse than no bell.
  ([MeetingNotifications.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/MeetingNotifications.js),
  [AppTopbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppTopbar.js))

- **WhatsApp Chat works on a number that has been typed but not yet saved.** The lead popup holds
  its edits until Update, so the WhatsApp Number field and the WhatsApp Chat panel beside it used to
  disagree: the field showed the client's number and the panel said "This lead has no WhatsApp
  number" about it, and messaging a new client meant saving the lead first. The panel now carries
  what is in the field, and a **whole, valid** number opens the conversation immediately — the
  thread, the composer, sending, attachments and read receipts all address it.

  Nothing else changes. A half-typed number, text, or an Indian number of the wrong length falls
  back to whatever is stored on the lead, so the panel behaves exactly as it did while somebody is
  still typing and a lead with nothing stored stays unmessageable until the number is finished. The
  send path still refuses a number that is not on WhatsApp, and forwarding still looks its target
  lead's number up rather than taking one from the request. The number is validated by the same
  `isValidPhoneNumber` rule the lead forms and the server already use.
  ([whatsapp-chat/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/whatsapp-chat/controller.js),
  [WhatsAppChatPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/WhatsAppChatPanel.js))

- **Meetings List view opens on today.** A second switch beside the view switch narrows the list
  to **Upcoming Meetings**, **Today's Meetings** or **Past Meetings**, and the page opens on
  Today's Meetings — the schedule is read to work the day. The Date column's sort follows
  the choice, soonest first for what is ahead and most recent first for what is behind, because the
  same direction does not read the same way in both; a sort set on any other column is left alone.

  Split by calendar day rather than by the clock, so a meeting that started an hour ago is still one
  of today's rather than dropping into Past the moment it began. The three are a clean partition —
  every meeting has exactly one home, and one with no readable date is shown in all three rather
  than hidden by all of them. The footer count, the CSV export and the header menu's Export follow
  what is on screen, an empty table names which part of the schedule is empty, and opening a meeting
  from the Calendar switches the list to that meeting's own range so the details panel never opens
  on a row that was filtered away. Calendar view is untouched: it answers the same question with the
  month it is on and its own Today button.
  ([MeetingsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/MeetingsPage.js),
  [meetings.js](../../frontend/src/lib/meetings.js))

- **Two more tabs on the leads board: Yet to connect and Rejected.** Yet to connect sits straight
  after All and Rejected closes the row, on all three verticals, so the tabs read left to right
  through a lead's life — the ones nobody has spoken to, then the day's and the outstanding
  follow-ups, then the ones turned down. Both carry counts like every other tab.

  Yet to connect is not a stored status and nothing has to be set for a lead to appear in it: a
  lead with nothing against it reads as Yet to connect on the day it arrived and as Inactive from
  the next day on, and the tab asks that same question — no status, and it arrived today — against
  the day the browser is treating as today. So the tab holds exactly the rows whose Status column
  reads Yet to connect, and empties itself overnight without a job to run. Rejected matches the one
  spelling the Status column folds onto that label.
  ([leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js))

- **Contacts: every phone number in the application, as one directory.** A new screen in the
  Sales & CRM section, between Reports & Analytics and Bookings & Converted, with four columns —
  ID, Name, Contact and Verticals — on the same `DataTable` the rest of the application uses, so
  narrowing is the filter row under the headers and Manage Columns, compact view and Export CSV
  are where they always are. A fifth column, Module, is off by default and one tick away in
  Manage Columns.

  It reads across the modules rather than out of one: HR's employees, the CRM's leads, a sales
  project's client, a quotation's client, an Image Selection project's client, a vendor payee on
  a payment request, a vendor, and whoever a client-storage disc was sold to. One row is one
  person, grouped on the last ten digits of the number — the same `phoneMatchKey` rule the lead
  form's duplicate check already applies — so an employee who also enquired is one contact
  carrying both ids, both modules and both verticals rather than two rows on one number. A
  project's `WED` / `QQ` / `CORP` is translated to Dum Dum / Qua Qua / Corp on the way in, so one
  vertical never reads as two.

  Nothing is stored: there is no contacts table and no form, and the modules' own rows are read
  live, so a number changed in the Employee Repository is changed here. Opening the screen answers
  for SALES → CRM Leads, which is where it sits in the navigation; **what is in it** is checked
  again per module, so a module's numbers are included only for a viewer who may read that module
  elsewhere. Contacts is never a way around another module's guard.
  ([ContactsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/contacts/ContactsPage.js),
  [contacts/directory/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/contacts/directory/service.js))

- **Asset Management and Client Storage open again.** Both threw a `ReferenceError` on first render
  as of the import work: Asset Management read `showingRequests` a few lines above where it is
  defined, and Client Storage passed the ⋮ an import config that was never declared at all. The
  config is now defined below what it reads on the first, and written on the second — `/api/client-storage`,
  the client-storage template, refreshing the list when a file lands — so the screens render and
  their imports work as the other tables' do
  ([AssetManagementPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/assets/AssetManagementPage.js),
  [ClientStorageManagementView.js](../../frontend/src/components/assets/ClientStorageManagementView.js)).

- **Nebula AI answers again instead of showing "language is not defined".** The assistant used to
  detect a question's language, answer in it, and report which one it had used; that layer was
  removed — it cost a second 3.3GB model for a language nobody asked it to use — but four references
  to the variable were left behind in the two functions that return an answer. Nothing declared it
  any more, so every reply that reached its own `return` threw `ReferenceError: language is not
  defined` and the panel showed that in red where the answer should have been. The references are
  gone, along with the now-empty `language` field the stream handlers reported
  ([crmAssistant.js](../../backend/src/services/ai/crmAssistant.js)).

- **Settings → Global Logo saves only what has changed, and the top bar shows it at once.** Save
  changes is disabled until the Global Name or the Global Logo differs from what is stored, so
  opening the section and pressing Save no longer writes an empty name and no logo over the
  studio's - it read "Global Logo saved." with nothing saved - and it disables again once the
  save has landed, and the notice names what was saved - Global Name, Global Logo, or both - rather
  than always saying the logo was. The saved mark and name reach the top bar the moment the save succeeds, instead
  of waiting for the page to be reloaded, and a sign-in page that was already open - in another
  tab, or reached by signing out - picks up a newly saved logo or Login Slide when it next comes into
  view, rather than showing the old set until it is refreshed
  ([StudioBrandingPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/StudioBrandingPanel.js),
  [useStudioBranding.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/useStudioBranding.js),
  [studioBranding.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/studioBranding.mjs)). Settings → Login Slide takes the
  same rule: its Save changes is disabled until a picture is added, replaced, removed or reordered
  ([LoginSlidePanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/LoginSlidePanel.js)).

- **Initial Converted Value locks once a quotation has set it.** Create Project and Edit Sale
  Details let the figure be retyped after picking a quotation, which is a transcription error
  waiting to happen — the project converts at the figure the client was actually sent, not at
  whatever a second screen types over it. It now turns read-only the moment a quotation is
  selected, with a line saying why, and stays editable only while no quotation is chosen — which
  is what still lets a project be created for an enquiry with none
  ([SaleDetailsSection.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/SaleDetailsSection.js)), shared by
  both Create Project and Start Project's own Edit Sale Details.

- **Version history opens from an earlier version too, not only from a chain's current one.** The
  register's own **Versions** action decided whether to offer itself from `versionCount`, which is
  deliberately blanked on an earlier-version row so the count column does not repeat "3 versions"
  down every row of a chain already known to have three — and that same blank quietly hid the menu
  item itself. It now reads the version list riding along on every row of the chain instead, current
  and earlier alike, so the full history opens from wherever the chain is being read
  ([QuotationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationPage.js)).

- **A quotation's vertical is locked once it exists.** Quotation Generator let the vertical be
  switched on every version, not only the first — and a switch clears every vertical-specific field
  filled in so far, which on a later version meant silently wiping answers that already priced real
  services and deliverables. The vertical now sets itself once, at the quotation's first version,
  and stays disabled on every version raised after it, with a line saying why rather than a control
  that simply stopped responding
  ([QuotationGeneratorPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationGeneratorPanel.js)).

- **Discard changes actually discards, in Start Project and Edit Project.** Its footer button
  reloaded the project from the server and left the panel open — which read as "did nothing" next
  to Close, and undid a background draft save by refetching over it rather than by leaving without
  it. It now closes the panel the same way Close and the record forms' own Discard Changes do.
  Start Project labels it **Discard** — Close already sits beside it, so **Discard changes** stays
  Edit Project's, where there is no separate Close button on the same row. The submit button also
  named the wrong action on Start Project: **Update** was already right for Edit Project, and
  Start Project now reads **Start** instead of the generic **Submit**
  ([StartProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/StartProjectFormPanel.js)).

- **Schedule Payment schedules against the quotation, not against a guess.** Its **Signed
  Quotation** figure was recomputed from the Service Creation rows at a flat ₹2,000 a service and
  ₹1,000 a deliverable — two constants in the form — so it was a number the studio had never quoted,
  it moved every time somebody added a service to the plan, and it disagreed with the Signed
  Quotation card on the project's own Payments tab. It now reads the project's **Initial Converted
  Value**, which Create Project fills from the quotation chosen for the enquiry and which is the same
  field the Payments tab totals, so the two cards name the same number by construction. Being stamped
  at conversion, raising a new version of the quotation afterwards no longer silently reprices a
  schedule already agreed with the client. A project with no converted value says so rather than
  laying out a schedule of percentages of zero
  ([StartProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/StartProjectFormPanel.js)).

- **Add Shoot marks its required fields in red, asks Preference only of a wedding, and starts it on
  Both.** Date, Shoot Place, Food, Accommodation, Travel and Preference carried a literal `*` typed
  into their label, so it rendered in the label's own grey while the fields beside them showed the
  red one every other form uses; they now pass `required` and match. **Preference** — groom's side,
  bride's side or both — is a Dum Dum question, and a Qua Qua baby shoot or a Corp shoot was being
  made to answer it; it is shown for Dum Dum alone, and the row of four beside it becomes a row of
  three rather than leaving a gap. It also starts on **Both**, the answer that covers the whole
  event, instead of opening with nothing selected on a field that refused to save without one
  ([AddShootDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/AddShootDialog.js)).

- **Send Back no longer appears on an employee somebody typed in by hand.** Add Employee starts a
  new record on **HR Onboarding Pending**, the same status a submitted onboarding invite lands on,
  and the ⋮ menu offered *Send Back* on the strength of that status alone — so a manually created
  employee carried a button whose only possible answer was "no onboarding submission was found for
  this employee". The employee list now says whether a row has an onboarding submission still
  awaiting review, matched on exactly what the send-back endpoint requires, and the entry appears
  only on those rows ([employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js),
  [EmployeeTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeTable.js)).
- **A booking with an unreadable date no longer sorts as though it were in the future.** Two day
  keys are compared as text, which is right for `YYYY-MM-DD` and quietly wrong for anything else:
  "not a date" sorts after a real date on its first character alone, so a broken record filed itself
  under Upcoming and sat at the top of what is coming. The shape is now checked before the
  comparison. ([meetings.js](../../frontend/src/lib/meetings.js))

- **The top bar logo no longer sits on a white chip in the dark themes.** The uploaded logo is
  drawn straight on the dark bar - its white ground dropped out, its artwork lightened - and it is
  a size larger; the Global Name beside it is regular weight
  ([globals.css](../../frontend/src/app/globals.css),
  [AppTopbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppTopbar.js)).

- **The Project Management Projects page opens again.** The merge of `develop` kept one line that
  rendered the old in-page Project Settings panel after that panel had moved to the Settings hub,
  so the page threw "settingsOpen is not defined" on load. The line is gone; Project Settings
  lives in the hub ([ProjectsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectsPage.js)).

- **Checkboxes and radios are visible again, and a chosen one now looks chosen.** Tailwind's
  Preflight resets `border: 0 solid` on every element, and Safari paints a checkbox or radio from
  exactly that border and background: with no border there was no ring to see, and a radio that had
  been picked looked no different from one that had not — on the cash entry form's Entry Type, and
  everywhere else in the app for the same reason. The components had asked for the ring and been
  given half of it (`border-slate-300` on the shared `Checkbox` sets a border colour; its width was
  zero). Rather than restore the width and leave each browser painting its own control,
  [globals.css](../../frontend/src/app/globals.css) now draws them: `appearance: none` in `@layer base`
  with the box, the tick, the radio's dot, the mixed-state dash, disabled and focus all drawn from
  three `--control-*` values, which the two dark tiers restate. Being in `@layer base` is what keeps
  a utility class winning over it — `sr-only`, which is the only thing holding the toggle switch's
  real input off the screen. The dark tiers' text-field rules no longer claim these controls either:
  they set `background-color: transparent`, which had been erasing the checked fill on a dark page.

- **The navigation drawer stays below the top bar even when the offset that put it there is
  missing.** The drawer was placed under the bar by its own `top: var(--app-topbar-h)`, which holds
  only while that arbitrary-value utility is in the stylesheet the page actually has — a tab that
  had not re-fetched the dev stylesheet had the new markup and the old CSS, so the rule was simply
  absent. With no `top`, the panel anchored to its `bottom-0` and took its content's height instead:
  it grew off the top of the screen, so the nav was cut off at the top and its full height covered
  the left end of the bar — the hamburger just pressed and the studio mark beside it. The drawer and
  its backdrop are no longer positioned against the viewport at all. `AppShell` renders them inside
  the row *below* the bar, and they fill it (`absolute inset-y-0` / `inset-0`), so being below the
  bar is where they are in the layout rather than a number that has to agree with the bar's height.
  The panel's height is now bounded by that row, so a nav taller than the screen scrolls inside the
  panel, and `absolute` rather than `fixed` lets the shell's own `overflow-x-hidden` clip the closed
  drawer off-canvas ([AppShell.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppShell.js),
  [AppSidebar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppSidebar.js)).

- **The navigation drawer opens below the top bar instead of over it.** The drawer was pinned to the
  full height of the viewport with the bar's height as padding, which moved its links down but not
  the panel — its white background, border and shadow still started at the top of the screen and,
  sitting above the bar in the stack, covered the left end of it: the hamburger just pressed and the
  studio mark beside it both disappeared while the nav was open. The panel and its backdrop now
  begin where the bar ends, from the same `--app-topbar-h` the bar's own height is declared from, so
  the bar stays whole, visible and usable with the nav open. Two things follow from that. The
  backdrop **fades** with the panel instead of being switched on and off — it used to appear and
  vanish in a single frame while the panel took 200ms to slide, so one gesture read as two — and
  closed it is now inert rather than merely transparent, out of the tab order and the accessibility
  tree. And **pressing anything outside the drawer closes it, the top bar included**: with the bar no
  longer under the backdrop it was the one place a press landed outside the drawer without closing
  it, so the nav stayed open behind whatever that control did. The hamburger is the exception, since
  it toggles
  ([AppSidebar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppSidebar.js),
  [AppTopbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppTopbar.js),
  [AppShell.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppShell.js)).

- **HR's employee form shows the "same as current address" checkbox the way the candidate set
  it.** The record stores only the two addresses, so the Review and Edit forms always opened
  with the box unchecked even when the candidate had copied their current address into the
  permanent one; it now arrives checked whenever the two addresses match
  ([EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js)).

- **Desktop photo registration crashed with "onlySuperAdmin is not defined".** A merge left
  `registerOriginalBatch` calling a legacy role-check helper that no longer exists (module
  permissions on the route guard access now) and duplicated `copySelectedOriginals` with the
  same phantom call; every desktop upload failed before registering a single photo. Both
  leftovers are removed.
  ([originals/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/originals/controller.js))

- **A page stays on the tab you left it on.** Every filtered screen dropped back to All on reload,
  which is worst exactly where refreshing is most natural: somebody working through a Pending queue
  reloads to see what has changed and is put back at the top of everything. The tab is now
  remembered per page, read on the first render the way the compact-view toggle beside it already
  is, so the page draws on the right tab rather than showing All and switching a moment later.
  Applies to Projects, the three production boards (each remembering its own), the Employee
  Repository, Payment Requests, Accounts Manager, Client Storage, Vendors and CRM. A remembered tab
  that has since been renamed or removed falls back rather than filtering by a label nothing
  matches, and a browser refusing storage simply does not remember. Asset Management already kept
  its tab in the URL and is untouched
  ([useStickyTab.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/useStickyTab.js)).
- **Raising a payment request works again — any kind of it.** Every `POST
  /api/hrm/payment-requests` answered 500. The controller writes `repaymentMonths` — how many months
  a salary advance is repaid over, which the form asks for and the validation requires alongside the
  "Over Several Months" plan — but the column was never added to `PaymentRequest`, so Prisma refused
  the whole write as an unknown field. Exactly the fault that broke cash entries, in a second place.
  Added as a nullable column with no backfill
  ([schema.prisma](../../backend/prisma/schema.prisma),
  [20260828000000_add_payment_request_repayment_months](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260828000000_add_payment_request_repayment_months/migration.sql)).
- **An expense line keeps the project it was sent with.** `expenseRows` listed `projectId` twice and
  the second, always `null`, won — so a line naming a project but no shoot lost it silently. Nothing
  looked broken because `resolveExpenseLinks` fills it from the shoot a moment later
  ([payment-requests/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/payment-requests/controller.js)).
- **A project's record is headed with the project, not the word "Project".** The panel a
  double-click opens read `projectName` / `projectCode`, neither of which is a column `SalesProject`
  has, so every project was headed with the same literal fallback and one could not be told from
  another. It now shows the WhatsApp group name — what the studio calls a project, and what the
  Start Project form already heads itself with — falling back to the Event ID and then the Enquiry
  ID. The Event ID sits under it as the description: the name is what it is called, the identifier
  is which one it is
  ([ProjectDataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/ProjectDataTable.js)).
- **Signing in no longer lands an Image Selection user on a 404.** The route table's
  `/image-selection` row was doing two jobs and only one of them worked: as a prefix it correctly
  guards `/image-selection/dashboard` and `/image-selection/projects`, but there is no page at the
  bare path, and `firstPermittedRoute` hands that same prefix to the landing redirect. Anyone whose
  first held permission was `IMAGE_SELECTION` was sent somewhere that does not exist. The dashboard
  is now listed ahead of the prefix, so the landing answer is a real page and the prefix goes on
  guarding both children
  ([modulePermissions.js](../../frontend/src/constants/modulePermissions.js)).
- **Deleting an employee archives them instead of destroying the record, and they vanish from every
  screen.** It was a hard delete: the row went, taking its reviews and separations with it and
  leaving anything else that named the person pointing at nothing. It now sets `archivedAt`, which
  is the column every "active employees" query in the app was already filtering on — so one write
  removes them from the repository, the crew and handler pickers, the assignment lists, everywhere.
  Including the repository's **InActive** tab: that tab is cut from `employmentStatus` and is where
  somebody the studio parted with belongs, which a deleted person is not. An archived employee can
  no longer be edited, offboarded, given a login, or named as somebody's alternative either — each
  reads as not found, the same answer every list gives
  Their **login is closed with them**, which it was not before: a user and an employee are separate
  records joined only by `linkedEmployeeId`, so somebody removed from the repository went on signing
  in with whatever roles they held. Archiving now sets any linked account to `INACTIVE` in the same
  transaction, which both refuses the sign-in and drops it off the Users screen. Roles stay on the
  account, so reactivating restores what they had
  ([employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js),
  [separations/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/separations/controller.js)).
- **An asset moved to Unassigned is no longer still showing who had it.** Changing the status to
  Unassigned left `assignedPersonId` and `managerId` standing, so the asset read as unassigned and
  as held by somebody at the same time — the screen showed the last person to have it. Both are now
  cleared, and the return is written to the custody log as the handover it is, from whoever held it
  to nobody, rather than being skipped over. Assigning it back out now asks for the **Manager**
  alongside Assigned To and Assigned On — unassigning clears the manager, so the moment the asset
  goes out again is the only moment that puts one back. Optional, as it is when an asset is created,
  and a manager who is not a real employee is refused rather than stored
  ([assets.controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/assets/assets.controller.js),
  [formPanelConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/formPanelConfig.js)).
- **Deleting an employee who still holds an asset says so.** It answered 500 with a raw Prisma
  foreign key error. `Asset.assignedPersonId` and `managerId` are `onDelete: Restrict` on purpose —
  deleting the person an asset is out with would leave it held by nobody — so the assets are counted
  first and the refusal names them: "This employee still has 1 asset assigned to them. Reassign
  those in Asset Management before deleting the record." Any other restrict now reads as a 409 too,
  rather than as a server fault
  ([employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js)).
- **A module in the Roles dialog can be searched for one of its sub-modules.** An open module now
  carries one row across its top — a search box on the left, the three access columns named on the
  right over the columns they belong to — and the search narrows the rows under it, matched on both the name shown and the key it
  is stored under. It narrows what is on screen and nothing else — a sub-module filtered out of
  sight keeps whatever it was set to — and the query is dropped when the module is closed, so
  reopening one never shows a list still filtered by something typed and forgotten. A module of one
  sub-module is not offered a search. Each access column also gained a radio in its head that sets
  every sub-module below it in one move, checked only while the whole column agrees — a mixed set
  leaves all three empty, which is the truth about it. With a search running it sets what the search
  left and not what it hid, so no grant is ever changed out of sight. The module name grows from
  `text-sm` to `text-base` while its sub-modules are on screen, so the open one reads as the heading
  of what is under it
  ([RoleDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/admin/RoleDialog.js)).
- **The Roles screens no longer shout PROJECT and ACCOUNTS.** Both modules were added to the
  permission catalogue after the map of readable module names was written, so they fell through to
  a fallback that only strips underscores and showed their raw keys — in the Roles dialog and in the
  roles table's Modules column alike, because that map had been copy-pasted into both. They now read
  "Project Management" and "Accounts", the names the sidebar uses, and the map lives once in
  [modulePermissions.js](../../frontend/src/constants/modulePermissions.js) beside the route table it
  shares a failure mode with — a module added to the backend and forgotten here reads as its own key
  rather than erroring.
- **Recording a cash entry works again.** Every `POST` and `PATCH` to
  `/api/accounts/cash-entries` answered 500. The controller writes `paymentModeOther` — what the
  payment mode actually was when it was "Other" — which the form asks for, the validation requires
  and the Accounts table shows as "Payment Mode Details", but the column was never added to
  `CashEntry`, so Prisma refused the whole write as an unknown field. Prisma's own message blamed
  `projectId` instead, which is what an unknown key does to its choice of create input, and sent
  the search in the wrong direction. Added as a nullable column with no backfill: entries recorded
  before this were paid by one of the five named modes, none of which carries a detail.
  ([schema.prisma](../../backend/prisma/schema.prisma),
  [20260827000000_add_cash_entry_payment_mode_other](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260827000000_add_cash_entry_payment_mode_other/migration.sql))
- **A panel opened over another no longer puts one form inside the other, and Escape no longer
  closes both.** Every `SchemaFormPanel` renders into `body` through a portal — it is a full-screen
  `<form>`, and a panel opening over another nested one form in another, which is invalid HTML that
  React warns about and the browser may do anything with. React still treats it as a child, so
  events, context and state are unchanged, and nothing moves on screen. Escape is now answered by
  the topmost panel alone rather than by every panel listening at once
  ([SchemaFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/schema-form/SchemaFormPanel.js)).

- **Five IAM tests broke on CI while passing locally, because the local database remembered a
  permission the seed no longer creates.** `setupMultiRoleData` looked up `HRM / Payroll Management`
  by name to have something to grant twice. That permission moved to `ACCOUNTS`, and the seed
  deactivates a row it no longer describes rather than deleting it — so a developer database still
  had it and the lookup succeeded, while CI seeds from empty and got `null`. The suite is about how
  two roles granting the same permission are merged, not about which permission it is, so it now
  takes the first active row in a deterministic order and asserts on the module and feature it
  found. Naming a permission in a test pins the catalogue to it; this one had been pinned for long
  enough to survive being moved.

- **Two write controls were still reachable on a read-only grant, and a third would have crashed.**
  A sweep of every component carrying a row menu or a header action, checked against whether the
  control that opens it is itself gated, found two the earlier passes missed. **Edit and Delete on
  every cash entry** were drawn whatever the viewer held — the Accounts header was gated and its
  table was not. The **WhatsApp composer** sits on a tab inside the CRM lead popup, which opens on a
  double-click, so a read-only viewer could send a message to a client; the conversation is still
  readable and the composer is replaced by a line saying why. The third was found while fixing the
  first: `buildTableColumns` called `renderActions` unconditionally, so a table that passed none
  threw rather than dropping the column. It now omits the actions column when there is nothing to
  put in it, which is what a read-only table wants anyway. See
  [tableCells.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/tableCells.js).

- **A router merged from `develop` guarded against a permission that no longer exists.**
  `/api/image-selection/storage-roots` asked for `IMAGE_SELECTION / Upload & Gallery`, written
  before that feature was merged into Project Management. Left as it was, it named a permission no
  role could hold, so the storage roots behind the originals workflow were reachable by nobody but a
  super admin — a check that passes for exactly one account still looks like a working guard. It now
  asks for `IMAGE_SELECTION / Project Management` like the rest of the module. See
  [storage-roots/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/storage-roots/routes.js).

- **The studio logo shows again on the CRD form and the client gallery.** It had been moved into
  `src/app/`, which Next.js does not serve as a static file, so `/dbs-client-gallery-logo.svg`
  answered 404 and both screens rendered a broken image. It is back in `public/`.

- **Selection Settings is held shut rather than left to fail on Save.** The rail on the left of an
  Image Selection project let a read-only viewer change every toggle, the expiry date and the
  selection limits, and answered with an error only once **Save Changes** was pressed — after the
  work of filling it in. Both halves of the rail are now disabled `fieldset`s, so every control
  inside is genuinely inert, and a refused control shows Material UI's `Block` glyph as the pointer
  itself — `cursor: not-allowed` draws the platform's ordinary arrow with a badge beside it, and the
  arrow is the part that reads as "you can click". `.cursor-restricted` in
  [globals.css](../../frontend/src/app/globals.css) replaces it with the symbol alone, 18px in
  slate-500, hotspot at its centre: grey because being unable to change something is a state rather
  than an error. It is registered with `@utility` so it can be used through a variant, which a plain
  class could not. The cursor is put on the controls themselves — the inputs,
  the buttons and the three toggles — rather than on the region around them: over a heading or the
  gap between rows there is nothing to refuse, and a whole panel reading as blocked says the screen
  is unavailable rather than uneditable. The toggle needed its own answer, being a label wrapping a
  visually hidden checkbox: a disabled fieldset makes it inert but leaves the part you can see
  styled as though it were live, so `disabled` is passed down and `ToggleSwitch` draws its own
  refused state. Publish Status answers to `Upload & Gallery` rather than to the permission the rest
  of the rail is edited under. Save Changes stays on screen and refuses, rather than vanishing, so it is clear
  the panel is read-only rather than broken; **Publish & Share** answers to `Upload & Gallery` and
  does the same. The selection-items list is wrapped as well, because items there are added and
  removed one at a time and never pass through Save. This is the deliberate exception to hiding
  what cannot be used: these settings are worth reading when they cannot be changed, and a rail
  that lost half its rows would read as a broken panel. See
  [ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js).

- **A read-only grant is read-only inside the panels too, not just on the table behind them.** The
  first pass hid the controls that *open* an editor, which is enough where an editor is only
  reachable that way — but the CRM lead panel opens on a double-click as well, so a viewer holding
  READ could open a lead and still be shown **CNA and Proceed Next**, **Update and Close** and
  **Update and Proceed Next**, and could still convert a lead or count a CNA from the status pills,
  both of which post the moment they are pressed. The panel now takes the answer with it: without
  READ_WRITE it reads the lead and offers Back and nothing else. The Image Selection workspace is
  covered the same way and by the permission that actually guards each part — **New Folder** and the
  per-folder menu by `Project Management`, the upload dock and **Publish Gallery**, **New PIN** and
  **Unpublish** by `Upload & Gallery` — while browsing folders, searching the gallery, copying a
  published link and opening it stay whatever is held. Every other module's double-click was already
  read-only: they open `RecordViewPanel`, the Roles dialog opens with `readOnly`, and the payment
  request panel has only Close.

- **Fifty-five hardcoded role checks were overruling the permissions above them.** Below every route
  guard this release added sat a second check on the legacy `User.role` column, refusing anybody who
  was not `SUPER_ADMIN`. The permission was read, passed, and then ignored — so a role granted from
  the Roles screen changed nothing, and the refusal named whichever module wrote the message. That
  is why granting CRD Documents at Read **or** Read / Write both answered *"Only Super Admin can
  manage Sales Manager projects"*: the CRD screen lists projects, and all 37 handlers in the sales
  projects controller carried the check. They are gone there and in twelve other controllers across
  Image Selection, project attachments and WhatsApp group names, and the Image Selection uploads
  router's own role gate is now `IMAGE_SELECTION / Upload & Gallery`.

  Two were not simply removed but repointed, because they guard something real: deciding a **payment
  request** now asks for `HRM / Payment Request Approval` at Read / Write — the permission the module
  is named after, which until now nobody but the super admin could exercise — and creating an
  employee's **login credentials** asks for `ADMIN / User Management`, since making somebody a login
  is User Management's business rather than the employee record's. The payment request list is
  scoped by the same answer, so a requester still sees only their own.

  What remains on `role` is deliberate: the studio-wide WhatsApp switches and disconnecting another
  user's session in [whatsapp-chat](../../backend/src/modules/sales/whatsapp-chat/controller.js), and
  the guard in [admin/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/admin/routes.js) that stops the Super Admin
  account being deleted. Two Image Selection tests asserted the old refusal and now assert the route
  carries its permission instead.

- **Three controls asked for data under a permission their screen does not carry.** An audit of
  every mounted router against what each page calls found three places where the interface was
  governed by one permission and the request behind it by another — the same shape as the toast this
  work began with. The **custody history** in the Asset info panel reads
  `GET /api/assets/:id/custody`, which is `ASSET / Asset Assignment`, and was drawn on every asset
  row whatever the viewer held. **Project Settings** on the Image Selection header saves through
  `/api/image-selection/settings`, which is `ADMIN / System Settings`, not the module's own
  permission. **Settings** on Accounts Manager maintains bank accounts through `/api/hrm`, which is
  `HRM / Employee Management`, not `ACCOUNTS / Cash Management`. Each now asks for the permission
  that actually guards it. The status tabs were audited in the same pass and left alone: every one
  of them filters a single dataset behind a single permission, so hiding a tab would hide records
  the viewer is entitled to. The one tab that crossed a boundary — Asset Requests — was already
  gated.

- **The Quotation screen answers to Quotation Management, which it did not.** `/sales/quotation` was
  guarded in the browser by `SALES / Quotation Management` while `/api/sales/quotations` demanded
  `SALES / Project Management`, so somebody granted exactly the permission the screen is named after
  got the page and a refusal on every record in it. The quotation routes now sit under
  `SALES / Quotation Management` with the rest of that screen. Remark attachments moved to
  `SALES / CRM Leads`, the only screen that uses them, and saved WhatsApp group names — read from
  both Sales Manager and Projects — are reachable from either, with adding one held at Read / Write
  rather than Read. See [sales/projects/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/routes.js).

- **A permission dropped from the catalogue stops being offered.** The IAM seed only ever upserted,
  so a `{ module, feature }` row removed from the source stayed in the database, listed in the Roles
  dialog and grantable — granting nothing, since no route named it any more. The seed now
  deactivates any row the catalogue no longer describes, which both readers already filter on. The
  grants naming it are left in place rather than deleted, so a retirement made by mistake can be
  looked back at. See
  [iam-roles-permissions.js](../../backend/prisma/seeds/iam-roles-permissions.js).

- **CI gets its own database, so the backend job can actually run.** The first pipeline run stopped
  at `prisma validate` with **P1012, "Environment variable not found: DATABASE_URL"**:
  `schema.prisma` declares `url = env("DATABASE_URL")` and validate resolves that before parsing,
  while the value locally comes from the gitignored `backend/.env` that CI rightly does not have.
  Supplying a placeholder alone would only have moved the failure — `test/iam.test.js` upserts
  branches and creates roles and users, so 32 of the 309 backend tests need a real database.
  [ci.yml](../../.github/workflows/ci.yml)'s backend job now runs a `mysql:8.0` service container,
  applies all 80 migrations with `migrate deploy`, and seeds it before running the suite, which also
  proves the migrations still apply from empty. It is never the production database: those tests
  write. [deploy.yml](../../.github/workflows/deploy.yml)'s verify job, which only generates and
  validates and opens no connection, sets a placeholder instead.

- **Originals on a NAS share or a Windows drive are addressed with that storage's own separator.**
  Scanning a folder built each file's path with `path.join`, which follows the separator of the
  server rather than of the storage root. On the Linux VPS a root of `\\DBS-NAS\Wedding` produced
  `\\DBS-NAS\Wedding/Project-001/DSC_4582.CR3` — a mixed path written onto the row and then handed
  back to the filesystem, so registering and copying originals could not work outside a Windows
  host. It now joins with `path.win32` or `path.posix` to match the root, the rule
  [paths.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/originals/paths.js) already followed. See
  [originals/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/originals/service.js).
- **A dropdown at the foot of a very short window no longer opens off the top of the screen.** An
  upward menu is pinned by its bottom edge to sit just above its trigger, and held to a minimum
  height so it never shows half a row. Where there was less room above the trigger than that
  minimum, the two rules together pushed the menu's top edge past the top of the window. It is now
  held down to the window margin, overlapping the trigger a little instead — a row of the field
  costs less than the top of the menu. See [dropdownMenu.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/dropdownMenu.mjs).
- **The test suites pass on any host, so CI can gate on them.** The Image Selection folder tests
  still described the older behaviour where deleting a non-empty folder was refused; deleting now
  takes the folder and everything beneath it, which is what the confirmation dialog has been
  telling the user, and the tests say so — with the one refusal that remains, a Project Management
  shoot folder, now covered too. The dropdown tests read an upward menu's position from the `top`
  it stopped returning when it began anchoring by its bottom edge.

- **The Roles dialog keeps one size, and its Add Role menu is no longer cut off.** Manage roles on a
  user opened a card that grew with every role added, walking the Add control out from under the
  pointer that had just used it; the card is now a fixed height with the roles list scrolling under a
  pinned Add row. The menu of roles to add was clipped by the dialog's own scrolling box, so only
  two or three could be seen — it is now portalled to the body and placed against the room the field
  actually has, the same way `Dropdown` and `ComboBoxField` already did it. That fixes the same
  clipping wherever `MultiSelectField` is used inside a dialog, including Create Role and the public
  CRD form. See [UserManagement.js](../../frontend/src/components/admin/UserManagement.js),
  [RolePicker.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/RolePicker.js) and
  [MultiSelectField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/MultiSelectField.js).

- **Info panels open and close smoothly, in every module.** The panel appeared and vanished on the
  spot because the shell rendered two different `<aside>` elements and picked one by state — a swap
  CSS has nothing to interpolate. It is now one element that transitions: from `lg` up its width
  moves between the collapsed rail and its open width, so the table beside it widens in step, and
  below that it slides off the right edge, which is the honest gesture for an overlay with nothing
  to give room to. The body fades instead of unmounting, so closing does not animate a blank white
  box, and it is `aria-hidden` and inert to the pointer while shut. Anyone who has asked their
  system to reduce motion gets the old instant swap. See
  [InfoPanelShell.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/InfoPanelShell.js).
- **The Technical dashboard is gone, and nobody is routed to one.** A new employee account whose
  access department was "Technical" used to be redirected to `/technical/dashboard` on sign-in and
  pinned there, seeing a sidebar of that one entry; an account on any other department was shown a
  wall saying its access was still being prepared. Neither check read the roles a user actually
  carries, so granting somebody a role widened nothing. The route, the redirect, the wall and the
  Technical-only sidebar are all removed: everyone lands on the Employee Repository, and what a
  person may do is decided by the permissions on their roles — checked by `PermissionGuard` and
  `usePermissions()` on the way in, and by the API on every request. See
  [RequireAuth.js](../../frontend/src/components/auth/RequireAuth.js).
- **Double-clicking a table row reads it; it never opens it for editing.** Every table in the OS now
  answers a double click the same way: the record opens in a new shared full-screen
  [RecordViewPanel](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/RecordViewPanel.js), read only, and
  editing is asked for by name from the row's ⋮ menu — which is also where the rules about who may
  edit what already live. Two tables used to do the opposite: Roles opened an editable permission
  matrix, and Project Records opened the start-or-edit flow, both from a gesture people make while
  trying to select a row. Tables that answered a double click with nothing at all — Employees,
  Users, Assets, Asset Requests, Vendors, Client Storage, Accounts Manager — now open the reading
  view. It draws the same record and sections each module's info panel is already given, so no
  module had to describe its fields twice; fields the record has nothing to say for are dropped,
  because a panel is scanned and this is read top to bottom.
- **"Users & Access" is gone from the sidebar.** Users and Roles are reached from the Administration
  control beside the Employee Repository heading, which is the only place that names all three
  screens and shows which one you are on; the sidebar entry was a second way to the same two
  destinations. Administration in the sidebar is now Dashboard alone. See
  [AppSidebar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppSidebar.js).
- **Module page headers had no padding at all on a phone.** The title started at the very edge of
  the screen and the last button ended at it, on every module. The header is pulled out by `-mx-3`
  to span the full width, and both of its rows used `px-3` — so the whole of that padding went on
  undoing the negative margin and none of it was left to show. Asset Management and the
  Administration screens looked right only because they passed
  `titleContainerClassName="max-sm:pl-2"`, which moved the heading and nothing else. Both rows now
  use `px-6`, so the title, the buttons and the tabs are inset by the same amount at both ends, and
  the two per-page overrides are gone. The `sm` and `lg` sizes already allowed for their own
  negative margins, which is why this only ever showed on a phone. See
  [PageHeaderTopRow.js](../../frontend/src/components/layout/PageHeaderTopRow.js).
- **Page header buttons no longer stretch across a phone.** Add Role and the two Asset Management
  buttons were set `w-full` below `sm`, so a one-word action became a full-width blue bar. They are
  now their own width, and the title bar wraps rather than hard-stacking — a page with one short
  action keeps it up beside the heading where there is room, and a page with three, like Employee
  Repository, still drops them to a line of their own. Which happens is decided by the content at
  the width it is drawn at rather than by a breakpoint. See
  [PageHeaderTopRow.js](../../frontend/src/components/layout/PageHeaderTopRow.js).
- **Four columns in Vendor Management had no filter box.** Skills, Experience, Status and Added By
  each drew an empty cell in the filter row and could not be sorted either. They were defined with
  an `id` and a `cell` renderer but no accessor, and a column the table cannot read a value from is
  one it will not offer to filter — the empty cell was the table saying so. Each now carries an
  `accessorFn` returning what its cell shows, so Status filters on the label you can see ("Active")
  rather than the code behind it. Every other table in Asset Management was already written this
  way. See
  [VendorManagementView.js](../../frontend/src/components/assets/VendorManagementView.js).
- **`iamService.createUser` made every account a super admin.** It hardcoded
  `role: "SUPER_ADMIN"` with a note saying it could be changed later, and a super admin is answered
  `true` by `hasPermission()` before it reads a single permission row — so anything creating a user
  through the service handed out an account that bypassed every check. It now defaults to
  `EMPLOYEE`, and what an account may do comes from the roles assigned to it. The admin route was
  never affected: it writes through Prisma directly. See
  [iam.service.js](../../backend/src/services/iam.service.js).

- **A multi-select given labelled options keyed every row the same.** `MultiSelectField` accepted
  only plain strings — it keyed each row on the option itself and rendered it directly — so passing
  the `{label, value}` pairs every other picker takes made every row key `[object Object]`, which
  React reports as duplicate keys. It now normalises both shapes through the same helper `Dropdown`
  uses, keys on the value and shows the label. See
  [MultiSelectField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/MultiSelectField.js).
- **Table columns rendered blank unless they carried their own cell renderer.** `DataTable`
  normalised every column to `cell: <value or undefined>`, and passing `undefined` there does not
  mean "no opinion" — it overwrites the table's own default renderer, so a column that gave an
  accessor and no cell drew nothing at all. Every existing table happened to pass a renderer on
  each column and so was unaffected; the admin tables did not, and showed the right number of empty
  rows. The key is now omitted when there is nothing to set. See
  [DataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js).

- **Two groups granting different levels resolved arbitrarily.** Where the same permission was
  reached more than once, the old code kept "the first occurrence" — whichever row the database
  happened to return — so the same user could come out `READ` or `READ_WRITE` on different requests.
  The strongest grant now wins, deterministically. See
  [iam.service.js](../../backend/src/services/iam.service.js).
- **Deactivating a role did not withdraw what it granted.** Permission and branch resolution now
  count only active roles.
- **A failed permission fetch was reported as "you do not have access".** The permission set is
  fetched once when the app shell mounts; if that request failed - the API restarting is enough -
  it settled into an empty set for the rest of the session, closing every guard and telling the
  person to ask an administrator for access they already held. The fetch now retries before giving
  up, and a set that genuinely could not be loaded says so and offers Try again, rather than being
  presented as an answer about permissions. See
  [PermissionsProvider.js](../../frontend/src/components/auth/PermissionsProvider.js).
- **A super admin was refused the admin screens.** `GET /api/user/permissions` returned a different
  shape for SUPER_ADMIN than for everyone else — the rows arrived with no `accessLevel`, because a
  permission's own `level` column is a code such as `USER_MANAGE` and says nothing about read versus
  write. Anything comparing access levels read `undefined` and closed, so the one role that can
  always open every screen was shown the "no access" message instead. Both paths now return the same
  shape, with a super admin's rows marked `READ_WRITE`. The server-side checks were never affected —
  they answer for a super admin before reading any row, which is also why no existing test caught
  this; the new one asserts the rows rather than the shortcut. See
  [iam.service.js](../../backend/src/services/iam.service.js).

- **Deleting an Image Selection project now deletes its data.** Delete used to soft-delete the
  project row and leave everything else behind — folders, photos, selections, selection items,
  the published gallery, invite logs, and the image files on the server and in the bucket. It now
  purges all of that in one transaction and then removes the server's files (browser-uploaded
  originals, thumbnails, previews, leftover chunk folders) and the bucket's derivatives. Masters
  registered from the Original Storage Path stay untouched on the studio's own storage. The
  project row itself remains soft-deleted so the gallery link keeps answering 404, linked
  projects still refuse delete in favour of archive, and the delete confirmation now says the
  data is permanently removed instead of "retained safely".
  ([projects/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/projects/service.js),
  [projects/repository.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/projects/repository.js),
  [ProjectActionDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ProjectActionDialog.js))
- **Download Selected's progress counter now matches the saved-photos summary.** The "Saving
  4/33…" counter counted source photos while the final message counted written copies, so a photo
  picked for several selection items — saved once under each — made the totals disagree (33
  counted, "Saved 40" reported). The counter now counts every copy the download will write and
  runs to the same number the summary reports.
  ([DownloadSelectedButton.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/DownloadSelectedButton.js))
- **A download's status message no longer follows you into another shoot folder.** After
  collecting one shoot's selected photos (e.g. Wedding), opening a different shoot folder (e.g.
  Engagement) kept showing the previous shoot's "Saved N original-quality photos into …" message
  next to the new folder's Download Selected button, reading as if that shoot had already been
  collected. The button now starts fresh — idle, no message — for each folder.
  ([GalleryPublication.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/GalleryPublication.js))
- **A fresh clone can start the backend again.** `backend/.gitignore` carried an unanchored
  `uploads` rule, which matched `src/modules/image-selection/uploads/` as well as the upload
  directory it was written for — so that module was never committed, and requiring it crashed the
  server on boot for anyone who did not already have the files locally. The rule is now anchored to
  `/uploads`, and the two places that import the module degrade instead of crashing: the upload
  routes are mounted only when present, and image processing falls back to not publishing progress.
  Both report the gap at startup. Browser uploads stay unavailable until the folder is restored;
  registering originals from an Original Storage Path is unaffected.
  ([.gitignore](../../backend/.gitignore), [server.js](../../backend/src/server.js))
- **`migrate reset` and fresh databases replay the migration chain again.**
  `20260818090000_add_per_shoot_selection_submission` altered `image_selection_deliverable_config`
  — the snake_case name that only exists after `20260819112301_convert_to_snake_case` renames the
  tables a step later — so every fresh replay failed there with MySQL error 1146 and left the
  database half-built. The migration is now a no-op: the column it meant to add is created by the
  snake_case conversion itself, which drops and recreates the table with `selection_submitted_at`
  in it, and databases that already applied the migration are untouched since `migrate deploy`
  never re-runs an applied migration. Same recovery pattern as the duplicate index drop in
  `20260810044848`.
  ([migration.sql](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260818090000_add_per_shoot_selection_submission/migration.sql))
- **Migrations run to completion on a fresh database.** Three migrations independently dropped
  `SalesWhatsappGroupName_status_createdAt_idx`, and MySQL has no `DROP INDEX IF EXISTS`, so the
  second to run failed with error 1091 and stalled the rest. The earliest-dated migration owns the
  drop; the duplicate in `20260810044848_migration_10_8_26` is now a no-op, matching the note
  already left in `20260812150000_add_image_selection`.
- **Asset routes are reachable again.** `/api/assets`, `/api/asset-categories`, `/api/vendors`,
  `/api/client-storage` and `/api/asset-requests` were registered a second time *after* the 404
  catch-all, leaving that copy dead; every router now mounts ahead of the 404 and the error handler.
  ([server.js](../../backend/src/server.js))
- **Info panel summaries keep their line breaks.** A formatter that lists one shoot per line no
  longer collapses into a single run of text.
  ([DataTableInfoPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTableInfoPanel.js))

- A manually named project's photos now appear in the client gallery. The gallery opened the shoot
  folder whose name matched the selection item's shoot, and an item the studio typed has no shoot —
  so nothing was opened and the client was shown an empty project however many photos it held. A
  typed item now opens the project's first folder. A folder holding photos directly, with no
  subfolders under it, is also offered as a card instead of disappearing.
  ([PublicGallery.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/PublicGallery.js),
  [FolderCards.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/client-gallery/FolderCards.js))
- A folder card in the client gallery no longer reports selections belonging to a different
  selection item. It counted every photo the client had picked for anything and showed that against
  the item on screen, so a card could read "2/30 Selected" under a heading reading "0 out of 30
  Images Selected for Retouches" — the two were photos picked for Frames. Each folder now carries a
  count per selection item, and the card, the folder sidebar and the optimistic update while
  picking all read the one being shown.
  ([gallery/repository.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/gallery/repository.js),
  [FolderCards.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/client-gallery/FolderCards.js))
- **Link expires on** under the Client URL now follows the Expiry Date. It reported the publication
  as it was read when the panel opened, so saving a new date left it naming a date the link no
  longer expired on until the project was reopened; the publication is now re-read after a save. It
  reports the stored expiry only — the date in the field above becomes this one when it is saved.
  ([ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js))
- The calendar no longer opens clipped inside the pane its field belongs to. It was positioned
  beside the field, so a field in a scrolling pane — the Image Selection settings rail, a dialog
  body — had its calendar cut off at the pane's edge and scrolled away with the content. It is now
  rendered into the page body at coordinates measured from the field, re-measured while the pane
  scrolls or the window resizes, and it still flips above the field when there is no room below.
  ([DateField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/DateField.js))
- The **Updated:** time is back beside the publish badge in the project workspace tab bar. Once a
  client had sent their selection the header hid that label for good, to make room for the wide
  **Download Selected** button — but it kept hiding it after the gallery was unpublished, leaving a
  lone **Unpublished** badge and no indication of when the project last changed. The label now
  steps aside only while the download button is actually on screen, and returns the moment the
  badge does. It also refreshes every minute instead of freezing at whatever it read when the
  workspace last re-rendered, so "3 mins ago" no longer stays "3 mins ago" for an hour.
  ([ImageSelectionProjectWorkspace.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionProjectWorkspace.js),
  [GalleryPublication.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/GalleryPublication.js))
- Selection Settings no longer disappears on a project created with a manual project name. The
  panel loaded the gallery publication and the selection items together, and the selection-items
  call rejected an unlinked project outright, so a failure in one threw away the other and left a
  single error line where the validity toggles, client link, PIN and expiry date should have been.
  Selection items are now simply empty for a project with no Project Management job, and the two
  calls no longer fail as one.
  ([deliverables/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/deliverables/service.js),
  [ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js))
- The **Selection Validity** and **Allow Photo Selection** toggles now persist. Both wrote to local
  state only — the panel had no control wired to its save function, so the settings were lost on
  the next reload.
  ([ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js))
- Saved client details no longer read back as empty. The Projects page held the project as it
  looked when it was opened and never refreshed that copy, so the settings panel repopulated its
  fields from a snapshot taken before the save. Saving now rebuilds the form from the row the API
  returns and hands that row back to the page, which replaces both the selected project and its
  sidebar row.
  ([projects/page.js](../../frontend/src/app/(app)/image-selection/projects/page.js),
  [ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js))
- The WhatsApp invite message no longer contains blank lines when a project has no event date or
  no selection items.
  ([WhatsAppTemplatePanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/WhatsAppTemplatePanel.js))
- Image Selection screens now fill the app shell instead of stopping short and exposing the grey
  background beneath. The pages sized themselves with `100dvh` minus hard-coded topbar/nav heights,
  which collapsed to a 460px floor whenever those values did not resolve; they now fill the flex
  column `AppShell` already provides. The 460px floor on the project column is gone too — a floor
  is what reintroduces a page scrollbar.
  ([frontend/src/app/(app)/image-selection/projects/page.js](../../frontend/src/app/(app)/image-selection/projects/page.js),
  [dashboard/page.js](../../frontend/src/app/(app)/image-selection/dashboard/page.js),
  [ImageSelectionProjectWorkspace.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionProjectWorkspace.js))
- Image Selection no longer scrolls as a whole page. From `md` up the screen is exactly as tall as
  the shell and scrolling belongs to the workspace panes — folder tree, gallery column, and
  settings panel. Below `md` the sidebar and workspace stack and scroll together in the project
  grid, so nothing is clipped on a phone.

- **The top bar shows the logo uploaded in Image Selection settings instead of a hardcoded
  file.** The signed-in top bar pointed at a bundled `/dbs-client-gallery-logo.svg` that no
  longer ships, so it showed a broken image and ignored the studio's uploaded logo; it now
  renders the shared `StudioLogo` component, which reads the studio-branding setting like the
  public quotation and CRD pages already do
  ([AppTopbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppTopbar.js)).
- **Callyzer call logs dropped fields the delivery was carrying.** Two shapes their webhook really
  sends were being read as nonsense rather than as values: an account that delivers the recording as
  an object (`recording: { file_url }`) stored the literal `"[object Object]"`, and one that sends
  `call_recording_url: false` for a call with no recording stored the word `"false"` — both non-empty,
  so both stopped the search and both would have reached the Call Logs tab as the answer. Only text
  and numbers count now. The note, the recording and the call outcome are also looked for under
  whatever the account names them, at one level of nesting, matched on the field's full path so
  `recording.file_url` reads as a recording while `recording_status` does not — and the delivery's
  own bookkeeping (`sync_status`, `is_deleted`, the transcription's state) can never be mistaken for
  how the call went. Callyzer's published names are still read first.
  ([callLogService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/callLogService.js))
- **The bell's badge stayed lit after the panel had been read.** It counted every meeting still to
  come today, so opening the bell, reading it and closing it left the number exactly where it was —
  the panel saying "you have seen this" while the badge said "you have not". Opening it now marks
  today's outstanding meetings as seen, and it stays clear until a meeting nobody has seen appears:
  one booked since, or tomorrow's list becoming today's. Remembered in the browser, so a page change
  does not light it again, and pruned to what is still outstanding so it cannot grow without limit.
  ([MeetingNotifications.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/MeetingNotifications.js))
- **The bell's panel is half the height and has no footer.** Each meeting was drawn as two rows —
  the time and title, then a separate description line for the client and the countdown — and each
  carried its own padding, so one meeting took the room of two. The second line is part of the row
  now: one entry, one hover, one click target. "Open Meetings" is gone from the foot of it, since
  every row already opens the Meetings screen. `OptionsMenu` grew a `detail` field for this, beside
  the `description` that still draws a line standing on its own.
  ([MeetingNotifications.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/MeetingNotifications.js),
  [OptionsMenu.js](../../frontend/src/components/ui/OptionsMenu.js))
- **Pre Production, Production and Post Production are off the Settings rail.** They were listed
  because the rail was meant to be the map of what Settings would eventually cover, each one drawing
  a dashed box reading "settings will be added here". Three entries that answer nothing are three
  places a reader goes to find out there is nothing there, and they crowded the entries that do. The
  rail lists what Settings actually configures now.
  ([SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js))
- **Settings is reached from one place, not from each module's ⋮.** Leads and Projects each carried
  a Settings entry in their page menu, and the Employee Repository carried one that opened its own
  option lists as a separate panel. All three are gone: Settings is the screen the header opens, and
  a per-module doorway into it made the same rail read as though it belonged to whichever board it
  was opened from. Nothing is orphaned — Project Settings is a group in the Settings rail, and the
  employee option lists are under Table Settings → Human Resource Management. Leads keeps Import in
