  its ⋮, the Employee Repository keeps View All Invites, and the Projects board's ⋮ held only
  Settings so the button is gone with it.
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [ProjectsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectsPage.js),
  [EmployeeVerificationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeVerificationPage.js))
- **Nebula AI wears the studio's own mark.** The assistant was drawn as a geometric letter N, a
  stand-in until there was a real one. It is now the supplied glyph, in the one component every
  place Nebula appears already reads from — the inline mark beside a module's actions, the avatar in
  the panel header, and the floating button — so all three changed together. It keeps `currentColor`
  and no colour of its own, which is what lets it be the studio's blue where it sits on white and
  reversed out to white inside the blue circles, with no variant for either or for the dark tiers.
  The artwork's own canvas is three times the glyph, so it is drawn on the glyph's bounds instead —
  otherwise a 22px button would have carried a 7px mark floating in space.
  ([NebulaMark.js](../../frontend/src/components/ai/NebulaMark.js))
- **Three boards called themselves something the sidebar did not.** The lead board's heading read
  "Leads" under a nav entry saying "Leads & Followups", the sales board read "Sales Manager" under
  one saying "Bookings & Converted", and the reports page read "Reports" under "Reports &
  Analytics" — one module under two names, in the two places a reader looks to know where they are.
  All three headings now say what the nav says.
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [SalesManagerPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/SalesManagerPage.js),
  [CrmReportsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CrmReportsPage.js))
- **Sort Order is gone from Project Settings, and the three lists read alphabetically.** Services,
  Deliverables and Size Options were ordered by a number typed against each row, with the name as
  the tie-break. It was a number somebody had to keep in their head across forty services: adding
  one between two others meant renumbering the rest, an unanswered box sorted a row silently to the
  top, and nothing on screen explained the order the list was in. The column and the field are
  removed from all three tables and all three dialogs, and each list is ordered by its own name —
  case-insensitively, since a capital letter is no reason to file one entry above another. The
  quotation calculator and the extra-quotation list ordered the same services by Sort Order too and
  now match, so the studio never sees one list of services in one order and another in a different
  one.
  ([ProjectSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectSettingsPanel.js),
  [ServiceDefinitionDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ServiceDefinitionDialog.js),
  [DeliverableDefinitionDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/DeliverableDefinitionDialog.js),
  [OptionValueDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/OptionValueDialog.js),
  [quotation-calculator/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/quotation-calculator/controller.js),
  [extra-quotations/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/extra-quotations/controller.js))
- **Compact view is gone from the ⋮, and every table is drawn at one density.** It was a per-page
  preference with a tick in each table's Table Options, remembered in the browser. That made one
  decision fifteen times: a table worth reading one way is worth reading that way on every screen,
  and a studio that turned it on for one board still met the other density on the next, which reads
  as two applications rather than one. `useCompactView` is the one place that answers it now, and
  the toggle wiring is gone from all fifteen pages that carried it. `comfortable` is the answer
  nearly everywhere — the size the application has always been read at. `compact` takes the padding
  *and* the type down a step, which fits more rows on a screen and makes a table harder to read, so
  it is asked for by name by the one screen that wants it: the contact directory, which is a name
  and a number a row and is scanned rather than studied.
  ([useCompactView.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/useCompactView.js),
  [ContactsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/contacts/ContactsPage.js),
  [DataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js),
  [DataTableMoreMenu.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTableMoreMenu.js))
- **Contacts had no ⋮ in its header or on its rows.** The table turned on Manage Columns, compact
  view and Export CSV, but the menu that offers all three is drawn in an `actions` column — and this
  table declared none, so it had the controls and nowhere to reach them from. It has that column
  now, ending the table the way every other one in the application ends: the shared table menu in
  the header, and a row menu on each contact carrying **View**, which opens exactly what a double
  click opens (the only way in on a touch screen, where a double click is the browser's zoom), and
  **Copy Number**, the one thing a directory is opened for that reading it does not finish. Nothing
  is created, edited or deleted here, so nothing else is offered. The column is pinned out of Manage
  Columns — it is the column that dialog is opened from.
  ([ContactsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/contacts/ContactsPage.js))
- **Every ⋮ menu inside Settings opened behind it and appeared to do nothing.** The Settings overlay
  sat at `z-140`, one above the layer scale's `popover`, on the reasoning that the sheet dialogs it
  opens are higher still. They are — but 140 also put it above everything opened from *inside* it: a
  ⋮ menu is `menuOverPanel` (120) and is portalled to `body`, so it was painted behind the panel
  that owns it. Edit and Delete on a service, a deliverable, a size option and on Table Settings'
  HRM lists were all being drawn underneath. Settings is on the shared `formPanel` layer now, the
  same as every other full-screen form, so the whole ladder above it works as it does everywhere
  else — and a date picker's calendar (`popover`, 140) no longer ties with the panel it opens in.
  ([SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js),
  [layers.js](../../frontend/src/constants/layers.js))
- **The table header's ⋮ did nothing in Project Settings either.** Manage Columns, Compact view and
  Export CSV are the same portalled menu, left on the page default of `z-60` because `DataTable` had
  no way to say it was drawn inside a panel. It takes a `menuZIndexClassName` now, and the settings
  tables pass the layer their row menus already used.
  ([DataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js),
  [DataTableMoreMenu.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTableMoreMenu.js),
  [ProjectSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectSettingsPanel.js))
- **Scrolling through Project Settings left the rail pointing at Services.** The three entries are
  anchors on one page, and the highlight only moved when one was pressed — so scrolling down to
  Deliverables left Services lit, the rail describing a page the reader had already moved past. It
  follows the scroll now, naming whichever section's sticky heading is on screen, and the foot of
  the page is Size Options however short that section is. A press still scrolls, and stands the
  highlight down while the move travels so the entry pressed is the one that lights up.
  ([SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js))
- **Both Usage columns in Reports always read 0m.** The measured-usage read behind WhatsApp Usage
  and Application Usage bounded its date range with the bare day — `started_at <= '2026-09-02'` —
  and MySQL reads a bare day as the midnight that *starts* it. Everything recorded during the last
  day of the range was therefore excluded, and since a report opens on today, that was every stretch
  anybody had opened today; a studio whose usage all fell inside its own range read 0m however long
  the application had been in use. It is bounded through the whole of the day named now, the way
  every other range in these reports already was. Verified against the live data: the account that
  read 0m reads 2h 19m across 17 sittings, and 5m of WhatsApp. The rule itself is extracted to
  `dayStart` / `dayEnd` and used by all three query builders, so a fourth cannot bound a range its
  own way.
  ([filters.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-reports/filters.js),
  [service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-reports/service.js))
- **The range line above the Reports tabs could name the wrong days.** "Showing: 4 Aug 2026 to
  2 Sept 2026" was built by reading each chosen day back in the browser's zone, so west of UTC it
  said "3 Aug to 1 Sept" over a report actually built from the 4th to the 2nd — the line describing
  the range disagreeing with the range. It is printed in the studio's zone now, like every other
  date in the module, and the tests run clean in four zones rather than only in IST.
  ([crmReports.js](../../frontend/src/lib/crmReports.js))
- **The repository's one lint error, in Project Settings.** `optionSetIdForValue` was called by the
  save writers a page above where it is declared. Hoisting made it work, but the React compiler
  rule refuses it — a value read before its declaration does not follow that value as it changes —
  so it is declared before them now. `npm run lint` is clean.
  ([ProjectSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectSettingsPanel.js))
- **Contacts double-click did nothing.** The handler was written but never reached the table — the
  prop was left off `DataTable`, so the gesture had nothing to call. Double-clicking a contact now
  opens the record it came from, as intended.
  ([ContactsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/contacts/ContactsPage.js))
- **Nebula AI's replies and its composer were invisible in both dark themes.** Two more surfaces the
  general rules got wrong for the same reason: they map a pale tone to a *sunken* one, which is right
  for a well inside a dialog and wrong for something meant to sit on top of a panel. A reply is
  `bg-slate-50`, which became a shade off the drawer behind it, so an answer read as loose text
  rather than a bubble; the composer is `bg-white`, which became the drawer's own colour, so the box
  being typed into read as no box at all. Both are lifted to the tone this palette already keeps for
  "on top of the panel".
  ([globals.css](../../frontend/src/app/globals.css))
- **Nebula AI's own messages were invisible in both dark themes.** A sent message is drawn as
  `bg-blue-50 text-blue-900`; the dark themes correctly darkened the band and left the words navy,
  so the question you had just asked was a bubble of unreadable text. The theme rules lifted blue
  text only as far as the 700 shade — the 800 and 900 shades are darker still, so exactly the text
  that needed lifting most was the text left unlit. The dark end of blue, indigo, emerald, amber and
  red is now lifted with the rest, which fixes the Nebula thread and any other place a 900-weight
  label sits on a 50-weight tint.
  ([globals.css](../../frontend/src/app/globals.css))

### Removed
- **The ten numbered increment columns are gone from the employee record.** `increment_01`…`_10`
  and their ten dates were a fixed twenty slots that could only ever hold ten raises, replaced some
  time ago by `employee_review` — one row per salary review, with the totals and the review dates
  derived from those rows. Nothing had written to them since: the repository table listed them as
  twenty empty columns and the employee form carried them through a save untouched. They were empty
  on every row of the database this was written against; **check yours before deploying**, since a
  dropped column takes its data with it — the migration carries the query to run
  ([20260910120000_drop_employee_increment_columns](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260910120000_drop_employee_increment_columns/migration.sql)).

- **The brand-master write routes and the last of the old quotation settings dialog have gone.**
  The screens that managed a brand's own services, deliverables and packages were removed at the
  studio's request; the routes behind them stayed, and nothing had called them since. That left ten
  endpoints and ten browser functions nobody could reach, and - worse - a table only they could
  write to whose rows still **replace** that brand's whole service list. One leftover master was
  exactly how Dum Dum's Choose Services came to offer a single line instead of the studio's forty,
  with no screen able to show or clear it. Gone with them: `QuotationSettingsDialog.js`, which
  nothing had imported at all, and `quotation-pricing.service.js`, which only that dialog imported
  and which duplicated `quotation-settings.service.js` endpoint for endpoint.
  **What is deliberately kept:** the tables, and every *read*. `GET /:code/configuration` still
  returns a brand's services, deliverables and packages, and the generator still prices off them
  where a brand has any, so a row already in those tables goes on working exactly as before -
  nothing can create or change one. A brand's **terms** and the shared general content are
  untouched, since Settings → Quotation Settings does still manage those
  ([quotation-brands/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/quotation-brands/routes.js),
  [quotation-brands/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/quotation-brands/controller.js),
  [quotation-brands/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/quotation-brands/service.js),
  [quotation-brands.service.js](../../frontend/src/services/sales/quotation-brands.service.js)).

- **Create CRD Document is gone from the CRD Documents header.** A CRD is asked *of a project*:
  every row on that screen is a booking that owes one, and Send Link on the row is where the
  client's link comes from. A second way in created a document belonging to no project, which then
  had no Event ID, no Enquiry ID and nothing to attach the client's answers back to. Documents
  already raised that way are still listed and still readable. The dialog behind the button has gone
  with it, including the fix earlier in this cycle that had it offer the bookings not yet started —
  that fault is now answered by there being one route rather than two.
  ([CrdDocumentsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CrdDocumentsPage.js))
- **The dashboard's Client Selection Activity chart.** The panel is gone from the Image Selection
  dashboard, and so is everything that existed only to fill it: the day-by-day query over photo
  picks and submissions, the read that found where a Lifetime chart should start, the bucketing
  that folded days into weeks and months, and the `activity` block the response carried. The
  dashboard now goes from the cards straight to Recent Projects, then Client Selection Progress and
  Storage & Processing, and asks the database for one query fewer on every load
  ([dashboard/repository.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/dashboard/repository.js),
  [dashboard/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/dashboard/service.js)). The shared
  `BarChart` went with it: it was written for this one panel and nothing else drew a chart.

- **A cash entry no longer asks for a branch, and now must say what it was against.** The branch
  decides whose books the money lands in, and was a free choice sitting beside the project that
  already knew the answer — so an entry could be filed against one branch's project and booked to
  another. It is read off that project's Source Branch on the server instead, which is the rule a
  payment request has applied to itself since it dropped the same field. What that costs is the
  link: money in must now name a project and money out one of its shoots, in the form and on the
  server, since an entry naming neither has no branch to work out. A spend belonging to no project —
  an office expense, a salary advance — is raised as a payment request, which resolves its own
  branch and writes the entry when it is approved; that path is unchanged and still records entries
  with no project. The Branch column and the info panel still show what each entry stored, the
  options endpoint no longer returns branches, and the IAM branch check now runs on the branch the
  chosen project puts the entry in — including when an edit re-files it against a different project
  ([cash-entries/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/accounts/cash-entries/controller.js),
  [accountsConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/accountsConfig.js),
  [CashEntryFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/accounts/CashEntryFormPanel.js)).

- **Sales Manager sheds its Create Quotation stub and the generated-group-names viewer.** The
  header's Create Quotation button only announced that the flow "will be added next" (quotations
  are made from the Quotation module), and the W/A Group-Name Generator's View Generated Group
  Names button opened a list nobody needed inside the generator; both are gone, along with the
  now-unused `GeneratedGroupNamesDialog`
  ([SalesManagerPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/SalesManagerPage.js),
  [WhatsappGroupNameDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/WhatsappGroupNameDialog.js)).

- **The navigation menu lists only what can be opened.** The five role-named groups below Accounts —
  **Pre Production**, **Production**, **Data Management**, **Post Production** and **Technical** —
  are gone, along with **Inventory Management** under HRM, **Business development associate** under
  Sales and **Accounts L2** under Accounts. Every one of them was a job title rather than a
  destination, greyed out and leading nowhere, and together they buried the working entries under a
  screenful of scrolling. Project Management keeps its own Pre Production, Production and Post
  Production entries, which have real screens behind them.
  [AppSidebar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppSidebar.js)

- **Client Name** and **Client WhatsApp Number** from the Selection Settings rail — they are now
  asked for on the Create New Project form instead (see Added). The rail saves the toggles alone,
  and because an absent field leaves the stored column untouched, a project created before this
  change keeps the client it already has.
  ([ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionSettingsPanel.js))
- The client fields from the Selection Settings rail's form state, and the helpers that only served
  them there: `parseClientPhone` (the rail split a stored number back into two inputs; nothing does
  that now) and `isManualProject`.
  ([imageSelectionSettings.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/imageSelectionSettings.mjs))
- `ProjectSettingsDialog`, the empty two-column popup ⋮ → Project Settings used to open. It was
  rendered with no sidebar and no body, so the menu item showed a blank dialog; the new
  `ImageSelectionProjectSettingsPanel` replaces it and nothing else imported it.
- `--app-topbar-h` and `--is-nav-h` CSS variables from
  [frontend/src/app/globals.css](../../frontend/src/app/globals.css). Nothing hard-codes chrome heights
  anymore, so restyling the topbar can no longer break page height.

- **The workspace gallery's full-screen image popup is gone.** Clicking a photo in an Image
  Selection project's gallery used to open a zoomable full-screen preview; the tiles are now
  plain — the grid itself is the view, with the selection tick and file name as before. The
  client gallery's own viewer is unaffected
  ([Gallery.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/Gallery.js),
  [GalleryGrid.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/GalleryGrid.js)).

### Docs
- **Rule 4 describes the access control the app actually has, and IAM.md says how to add a module.**
  Rule 4 in [CLAUDE.md](../../CLAUDE.md) predated most of this release: it named `PermissionGuard` as
  the frontend answer without mentioning the route map that decides whether a page is guarded at
  all, said nothing about panels reached by a double-click, and claimed every list query is scoped
  by branch — which is true of `User` and `Asset` and of nothing else, since every other table
  stores its branch as free text the filter cannot read. It now says both, forbids the `req.user.role`
  check inside a controller that silently overrules the guard above it, and names the two traps that
  shipped bugs: a guard may only name a permission that exists in the seed, and a screen may not be
  given a finer permission than the endpoint it reads.

  [IAM.md](../project-state/IAM.md) gains a nine-step checklist for adding a module or screen — seed,
  reseed, guard, check the endpoint is not shared, add the route-map row, gate the controls, gate the
  panels the page opens, do not fire the call either, and re-save the roles that should hold it —
  plus what retiring a permission leaves behind. Every step on it has shipped a bug when skipped.

- **Access control is now Rule 4, not something remembered afterwards.** [CLAUDE.md](../../CLAUDE.md)
  asks every change to name the module and feature its access is decided by, and to write the RBAC
  for it on both sides in the same change: `protect` plus `checkPermission` and a
  `buildBranchFilter`-scoped list query on the backend, `PermissionGuard` and `hasPermission` on the
  frontend, and a new `{ module, feature }` row in the permission seed when the change adds one. An
  endpoint anyone signed in can call is an unfinished change rather than a follow-up task.

- **CLAUDE.md now makes RBAC part of every change.** A new "RBAC — Every Feature Ships With Its
  Access Control" section says who-may-use-it is worked out and coded in the same change: new
  backend routers mount `protect` plus `requireModule` (or the finer `checkPermission` guards),
  privileged UI is gated with `PermissionGuard`/`usePermissions`, new `module:feature` keys are
  registered in the IAM seed so Role Management can grant them, and the review checklist gained
  "RBAC guarded". ([CLAUDE.md](../../CLAUDE.md))
- **A developer handoff guide.** [dev-handoff.md](../dev-handoff.md) covers the stack, prerequisites,
  a first-run walkthrough, the Prisma and MySQL setup, every environment variable the code reads
  (including the alias names and defaults that are not in the `.env.example` files), the branch and
  deployment workflow, and a troubleshooting table. It names each credential and where to obtain it
  rather than carrying the values, so it is safe to keep in the repository.

- Added [CLAUDE.md](../../CLAUDE.md): the development workflow, a one-screen architecture map, the three
  standing rules, and the hard constraints. Agents read this first.
- Rewrote [frontend/README.md](../../frontend/README.md) — it was still the `create-next-app`
  boilerplate — with the real code flow, the component inventory to reuse from, the mobile
  responsiveness standard, and the README-maintenance rule.
- Added [backend/README.md](../../backend/README.md): request flow, module layout, shared services and
  workers, mounted API surface, and database rules.
- Added a code-flow diagram and the three Working Rules to the [root README](../../README.md).
- Seeded this changelog.
